# Changelog

All notable changes to Hatsu are documented in this file. Releases follow [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/).

## v0.152.0 — The kazaana claude batch: bounded delegate waits, the host workload budget, the wrap-up's two prerequisites, parks on observed blockers only, and the durable ledger slice

Thirteen pull requests landed on `main` since `v0.142.0` (#479 … #500, 2026-10-25 → 2026-10-26), five of them the `hatsu:kazaana zheref/hatsu` batch of 2026-10-26 (HA-PR-#483, #497, #498, #499, #500, one stack merged bottom-up by the maintainer), one the Codex idle batch's integration (#491, nineteen fragments), and the rest the SDD-100 follow-ups (#462, #479, #484, #485, #487, #489, #490). Their thirty-one `changelog.d/` fragments are folded here in the collation order, each closing with the pull request that landed it; the plugin manifests already read `0.152.0` on `main` (HA-PR-#500), so this release touches the changelog only.

**Net effect at `v0.152.0`:** every delegate wait is bounded and hands back at the bound (#483); heavy local work is admitted through one host workload budget with a `Heavy turn:` line in every brief (#497); the Delivery guard reads per effort (#498); cleanup is offered or run only once every scoped PR reads `MERGED` and the maintainer has declared the sitting satisfied — HR-366 ruled, HR-209's trigger superseded, the idle rows `wrap-up` and `cleanup-plan` at `refuse`, HR-367 reading a closed-unmerged PR as out of scope (#499); an idle run parks on an observed blocker, never on a missing nen verb (#500); every Futon batch drives each delivery to Ready or holds it with three facts (#490); the effort's ledgers ride its PR as one durable comment, with a crash-and-resume proof and per-gate stop markers (#489); a re-injecting attribution hook stops the effort before its first push (#484); nen is repinned to `v0.28.0` with the mirrors in its shape (#485, #491); and the morning desk quotes the ten-PR prose shrink (#487). Where two fragments describe one rule at different moments, the later state is the one that holds: HR-366 is **ruled** (the ha-480 fragment's hanten-round line records the reading that preceded the ruling the same day), and the `--catalogue` spelling of `nen pr converge` supersedes `--requirements-from` in shibari, en and rikugan.

### Changed

- **Every Futon-based batch drives every delivery to Ready, or holds it and names why (zheref/hatsu#488).** `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § *Batch delivery* states the maintainer's instruction of 2026-10-25 once. `en` is started behind every delivery a batch opens and keeps acting on pending checks, owed review rounds, findings and catch-up. Every delivery stands in exactly one state — `driving`, `ready` (the `nen pr ready` verdict quoted at the current head), `merged`, `closed`, or `held-<cause>` (approval, gate — en's sixth stop and hanten prompt included —, credential, cap, handback, stranded), parked durably with `nen run park` (a later merged or closed read wins over the append-only park) and carrying three facts: the prerequisite or bound, the last verdict and its judged head, the resume line. A batch with any held delivery ends **incomplete**, never as a success, `register` not `final`; an attended run stays open while a delivery stands Ready at its prompt; the wrap-up picker waits for the run's end. Under `--idle` a run has one integration branch and one integration-to-main PR, no per-effort PR and no stack; an effort that cannot join parks and is listed not included; separate integration deliveries come only from separate runs. `hatsu:futon` §§ 1, 10, 12, `hatsu:kazaana` §§ 1, 3, 7, `hatsu:en` § 7, `hatsu:dowsing-chain` § 9 and `hatsu:backlog-loop` § 10 cite it; no batch ends on "blocked" any more, and WORKFLOW's stacking default and idle overlap rule now say *attended*.
- **`scripts/futon_effort_record.mts`** gains `resume` — the newest real, unended, end-tracked run whose line nen parses alike (never the spelling), or `--run <id>`, continued under its own id with the launch answers nen stored (re-checked against the idle floor), its recorded terminal and integration branch; exit `3` alone means *no open run, start one*, every refusal is exit `1`; it refuses before calling nen when the manifest is absent (nen would write an empty one that opens the `then` gate), and a moved committed profile is reported, never obeyed — `end`, which futon § 10 records so a finished run is never resumed, and `integrate`, which records the run's one integration branch and refuses a second one, the default branch in any spelling and a name `git check-ref-format` rejects. A malformed or forged sibling record never refuses a start and never is picked. `bind` refuses an idle run, `verify` refuses a claim on an idle or dry run, and `start` names an earlier run typed as the same line. The fixture's stub nen now keeps manifests as nen 0.28 does, and it asserts that the batch endings in futon, kazaana and backlog-loop cite § *Batch delivery*; it is red against the previous script and the previous futon prose.

  *Landed by [#490](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/490) (changelog.d/488-batch-delivery-ready.md).*

### Added

- **The ten-PR shrink line of the prose ratchet (zheref/hatsu#448, AC-4's second half).** `scripts/prose_ambiguity_check.mts --history <n>` (1 to 100) lints nothing and reads git alone. It walks `HEAD`'s first-parent line and keeps the last `<n>` pull requests whose diff touches a canon prose path. A pull request is a `Merge pull request #N` merge or a squash commit whose subject ends `(#N)`; an integration branch's sub-PRs count once, through their delivery PR. For each one it counts the removed baseline rows against its first parent's baseline, exactly as `--base-baseline` counts them, and prints one line per pull request, their sum and the baseline's findings at `HEAD` (SC-2's figure). A prose commit naming no pull request (a rebase merge, a direct commit) and a non-PR merge are counted as skipped (`skippedNonMergeProse`, `skippedNonPrMergeProse`), never as pull requests. The ratchet's start is found once, as the oldest first-parent commit that added the baseline, and nothing older is read; past it, a pull request whose tree or parent has no baseline is flagged `baselineAbsent` and left out of the sum. The walk's end is said: `complete`, `ratchet-start`, `history-start` or `scan-cap` (more than `HATSU_PROSE_HISTORY_SCAN` commits, default 5000). A shallow clone, a `HEAD` with no baseline, `--base`, `--base-baseline` or `--self-test` beside it, a bad count or a bad `HATSU_PROSE_HISTORY_SCAN` is wiring (exit `2`), as is any git failure other than a path absent from a tree. `--json` prints `hatsu.prose-lint.history/v0.1`. `--self-test` now refuses any other flag, and the fixture refuses a start from inside itself, so a regressed guard fails red instead of recursing. Changed paths, here and in `--base`'s scope, are read NUL-split with `core.quotePath=false`, so a non-ASCII path reads as itself. The `canon-lint` fixture builds merge histories for each case.
- **The morning desk quotes it.** `contracts/schedule.json`'s `morning-desk` prompt runs `node scripts/prose_ambiguity_check.mts --root . --history 10` and quotes its last line beside the register's link, stating the counting rule; a non-zero exit is reported as not run. That exact command, never a prefix, joins the row's `alwaysAllow`. `docs/config/schedule.md` § *Permissions* says it is safe only because the desk runs at `origin/main`'s commit; § *Row 4* replaces the *owed* line with the verb, and the table row names the quote. `docs/ab/schedule-install.md` re-records the printout on this branch over `main` `00881fc1d` and records the first live `--history 10`. The CI workflow is unchanged: its guard step is frozen and its per-PR count stays. AC-2's tag trigger stays owed, as the page says.

  *Landed by [#487](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/487) (changelog.d/ha-448-ten-pr-shrink-line-2026-10-25.md).*
### Fixed

- **A hook that re-injects a forbidden trailer on every commit now stops the effort before its first push** ([#470](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/470)). A Cursor cloud agent's environment `commit-msg` hook stamps `Co-authored-by` naming the person who started it on every commit, and a run on a consumer repository published such a commit and disclosed it, leaving a history rewrite only the maintainer may do. `kokusen` § 5 and `aka` §§ 4 and 7 now treat a second read-back of the same refused key as a re-injecting hook: drop the unpublished tip, keep the change, push nothing and stop at G5; publish-and-disclose stays only for a commit published before the run read it. The stop goes through row `harness-attribution-switch` option C, whose option A is now qualified as IDE/CLI only. `aka` § 7 reads `nen/workflow.json` before its pre-push guard and passes `--forbid-trailers-from` where the file lists a non-empty `forbiddenTrailers`, so a listed key is refused mechanically, chained to `nen wc publish`; every exit `2` from the guard is a stop before any push, and the `-by`/`-with` suffix rule stays the agent's read. `scripts/branch_authorship_check.sh` now reads that policy as UTF-8: under the C locale Ruby raised on the file's first non-ASCII byte, so the flag exited `2` on Hatsu's own policy. `scripts/publish_integrity_fixture.sh` gains four combined-mode cases under `LC_ALL=C` (142 cases), one on a fixture policy holding an explicit non-ASCII string so the case does not lean on the live policy's bytes; they fail against the previous guard.
- **`docs/AGENT-ATTRIBUTION.md` no longer claims a consumer has a required PR trailer check.** The check is `zheref/hatsu`'s own `plugin-bump-check` step; a consumer has one only where its own workflow runs it, Tenkai renders none yet and nen has no range verb for it. The doc now quotes Cursor's own documentation, fetched 2026-10-25: cloud agents add `Co-authored-by` to every commit, and no page documents a switch for it (the IDE/CLI *Attribution* toggle covers `Made with Cursor`). `docs/surfaces/cursor.md` carries the five quoted lines in its dated checklist (fourteen URLs now) and `docs/using/cursor.md` the caveat. Trims for `kokusen`'s ceiling: § 4's "Never `git add -A` blind" (§ 8 keeps the rule), P5's "§ 8 still binds in full", and § 5's pointer "`aka` § 7 reads every outgoing commit before the push" (`aka` § 7 and `docs/surfaces/cursor.md` keep it).

  *Landed by [#484](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/484) (changelog.d/ha-470-cloud-hook-trailer-stop-2026-10-25.md).*
### Added

- `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § *Delegate briefs — how a delegate waits* carries the wait block every brief that raises a builder delegate (kazaana § 3's, futon § 4's) copies word for word; Illumi's long watch, en's observation hold, is excluded by name: never `gh pr checks --watch`, an open `sleep` or poll loop, or a background job left running; a wait is one `nen watch until … --max-iterations <n> --interval-ms <ms>`, both flags passed, within the brief's ceiling (9 minutes unless the brief names another, under Claude Code's 600000 ms Bash maximum), the host call's own timeout set above the wait's, a named ceiling staying under the host's maximum and a longer wait split into several bounded waits, each with its own hand-back; at the bound, settled or not, a hand-back of the head SHA, each check and review round as read, and what is still pending. Without `--max-iterations` the wait takes `monitor.maxCycles` (20 × 300 s here, 100 minutes). Kazaana § 3 hands the block to its builder delegate and futon § 4 cites it. Closes #473.
- `scripts/delegate_brief_check.mts` reads the block from WORKFLOW and refuses a brief that drops, rewords or paraphrases a line (exit 1, each line named against the canon actually read; wiring exit 2, a comma, colon or line break refused in the brief's path and a line break in the canon's; `--print` prints the block). Its fixture runs as the new `delegate-brief-guard` lane, gated in `scripts/unit_tests_ci.mts` with its runner-fixture case.

- WORKFLOW § *Idle batches* rule 8: the `decisions` footer row is resolved by the `decisions` run fact as that `contracts/pr-sections.json` footer row records it (#474).
- Prose-diet trims to fit the new citations: kazaana § 3 drops "never raw `gh pr create`" (Authority's not-permitted list still carries it); futon § 6 drops the claimed-effort slot clause it restated from the line above and WORKFLOW § 2 `monitor`. After HA-PR-#490 grew `kazaana`, its § 3 link to `WORKFLOW.md` § *Where worktrees live* is written in the file's plain `WORKFLOW §` form (35 bytes, the same reference) to stay under its 12288-byte ceiling.

### Fixed

- `scripts/mechanical_conflicts_fixture.test.mts` hands nen the same git identity it hands its own `git` calls and writes it into each fixture repository's config, so the `mechanical-conflicts-guard` lane passes on a GitHub-hosted runner, which has none (the lane landed on `main` through HA-PR-#489 and first ran on this branch's catch-up: `git merge` refused with "empty ident name").
- `vitest.config.mts` excludes `**/.trusted/**` from test discovery: CI checks the trusted base out at `.trusted/` beside the head, and vitest's positional filters are substrings, so every `vitest run scripts/<fixture>.test.mts` lane also ran the base's copy of the fixture and judged this tree by it (the `mechanical-conflicts-guard` failures on this PR's unit-tests runs were the base's copy, which lacks the identity fix above).
- `scripts/pr_sections_fixture_check.ts` invokes `scripts/pr_sections_resolve.sh` through `bash`, the wrapper's own shebang: the one call that used `sh` ran under dash on the GitHub-hosted runner and refused the wrapper's `set -o pipefail` (unit-tests run 40379417349, the first run of the `pr-sections-guard` lane on this stack once the mechanical lane passed).

  *Landed by [#483](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/483) (changelog.d/ha-473-delegate-bounded-waits-2026-10-25.md).*
### Added

- `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § *Host workload budget — heavy local work*: the host workload budget, distinct from effort/worktree capacity (`nen loop slots --local-cap`) and remote-provider quotas. Light work is named (a read, git other than a push that starts CI on this host, `--dry-run`, a lint of the changed files, prose checks); anything else is heavy, including a push to a target whose CI runs on this host. The missing admission guard is owed to zheref/nen#536–#540, and every phase that runs a heavy verb reports `host admission: no guard (zheref/nen#536); serialized fallback` (rasengan, breath, tsukuyomi, kokusen, hanten's fixtures and reviewers, murasaki, kotoamatsukami, byakugan, susanoo, delegates). The serialized fallback: capacity fixed at one at this version; the heavy turn is held by the orchestrator, granted to one delegate at a time by a `Heavy turn: granted, workers <n>` / `Heavy turn: withheld` line, returned at a hand-back naming every recorded process group (a silent delegate with no known group is no recorded id, and the run parks) and re-granted in queue order; a reviewer always starts withheld and the orchestrator runs its pending heavy lanes; a pending lane is settled by a re-raise with the turn or by the orchestrator in that worktree; a delegate without the turn commits and publishes only where the push starts no same-host CI; finite workers, 2 unless the maintainer or a brief names another; same-host CI in two reads: a push is heavy only where its target declares a pool on this host, and before any heavy launch, whatever the target, every pool registered on this host is read through `nen runner inventory` (the set: `nen/repos.json` repositories whose `nen/workflow.json` `runners` carry this host's machine code, which is typed, never derived, HR-276). Busy is `parked:budget` at the minutes in an idle run and bounded waits then a G5 stop interactively; unknown (machine code, set or inventory) is `parked:g5-host-ci-unknown` idle and a G5 stop interactively. Overload is host-wide: in an idle run, `parked:g5-host-overload` and light-only for later efforts; interactively, a G5 stop. A retry needs a recorded process group (`ps -o pgid= -p $$` at launch; a group that differs from `$$` or cannot be read is no recorded id: an idle park, an interactive G5 stop) listed empty with `ps -A -o pid,ppid,pgid,command`, plus the maintainer's word on recovery. No stopped claim without that listing, no kill by name or pattern, and stopping any process, the session's own launch included, is the maintainer's G5. Closes #478.
- The host block every builder delegate's brief and every hanten reviewer prompt carries word for word (`<!-- delegate-host:begin -->` … `end`), eight lines including the unbounded-lane line and the record-your-process-group line; a reviewer prompt carries it with `Heavy turn: withheld` and no wait block. `scripts/delegate_brief_check.mts` now checks it beside the wait block, along with exactly one line starting `Heavy turn:`, which must be well formed (a malformed one is named, and a second line beside a valid one fails); `--print-host` prints the block. It checks delegate briefs, not reviewer prompts. The fixture closes on `delegate-brief-check fixture: 52 cases passed`.
- `docs/ab/host-workload-budget.md`: SC-1's four scenarios (overload, orphan uncertainty, same-host CI overlap, healthy serialized recovery) walked through the canon on 2026-10-25, with the recording and listing forms exercised on a bounded orphaned `sleep` (again on 2026-10-25 after `ps -o pgid= -p $$` replaced the echoed `$$`).
- `claude/rules/hatsu.md` rule 11, `claude/agents/kurapika.md` § *The `shu` verbs*, `claude/agents/_review-preamble.md` § 7 and `claude/skills/hanten/SKILL.md` § 4 carry the obligations to every surface. `docs/PROCESS.md` names the residue, including the Codex `AGENTS.md` 32 KiB placement owed to the mirror generator (unfiled), and the heavy-verb turn in § *Running a declared verb*.

### Changed

- **Cleanup is offered or run only when two prerequisites hold together** (zheref/hatsu#480; `docs/ROSTER.md` HR-366, **ruled by the maintainer on 2026-10-26** through the kazaana register's G4 picker — *"Rule it; refuse both"*, `nen stop answer` record `R-2026-10-26-09` — over the correction of 2026-10-25: *"Cleanup should never be offerered if PRs in scope are not merged and the sitting has been determined satisfied by maintainer."*). (1) Every pull request in the sitting's scope reads `MERGED` live — a closed-unmerged PR or an unread merge state never counts — and (2) the maintainer has explicitly declared the sitting satisfied. Readiness, green checks, `en` § 5's merge prompt, a composite's run ending, a merge alone, silence and any default never supply (2); short of both, no wrap-up picker is shown and no cleanup runs, and the report names the missing prerequisite. The rule is owned by `hatsu:cleanup` § 2 and `nen/decisions.json` row `wrap-up`, and every caller now defers to it: `en`'s preamble, § 8 and its hard limit, `build` phase 6, `futon` § 10, `kazaana` § 7, `kamui` § 8, `hiraishin` § 8, `backlog-loop` § 10, `dowsing-chain` § 11, `claude/rules/hatsu.md` rule 9, `claude/agents/kurapika.md`, `claude/agents/shizuku.md`, `claude/skills/README.md`, the top-level `README.md`, `docs/config/decisions.json.md` and WORKFLOW § *Recorded Futon delivery ownership*'s "Two offers" paragraph. This supersedes the 2026-10-13 trigger (HR-209's status reads `superseded by HR-366 (the trigger; the rest stands)`: *at every session delivery — a PR at en § 5's merge prompt or merged, a composite's run ending*); HR-209's words stay as the dated record, with a dated note pointing at HR-366.
- **A PR the maintainer closed unmerged leaves the sitting's scope** (`docs/ROSTER.md` HR-367, a reading of the maintainer's picker answer *"Yes, it leaves"*, record `R-2026-10-26-10`): prerequisite (1) is read over the PRs still in scope, so a closed-unmerged PR neither counts as merged nor holds the wrap-up, while an unread merge state still does. Recorded in `cleanup` § 2, row `wrap-up`, `docs/config/decisions.json.md`, WORKFLOW § *Two offers* and `docs/ab/cleanup.md`.
- **An `--idle` run never offers or runs cleanup, and the idle rows `wrap-up` and `cleanup-plan` are `refuse`** (HR-366 as ruled: `publish-offer`'s shape, the one exception to HR-334's derivation for a starred row). `nen/workflow.json` → `idle.decisions` and `contracts/config-catalogue.json`'s `workflow.idle.decisions` default move both rows from `default` to `refuse`; `scripts/futon_effort_record.mts start` adds them to the floor it enforces at exit 1 (`default` or `hold` on either row is refused), and `futon_effort_record_fixture.mts` proves the four refusals and moves its ask-once probes to `direct-mismatch`. WORKFLOW § 2 `idle` (*Applied*, the `decisions` row) and § *Idle batches* rule 5, `docs/config/workflow.json.md` and row `wrap-up` say so.
- `docs/ROSTER.md` governance 1.56.0 → 1.57.0: 1.56.1 for the id-range wording and the dated note naming HR-366 (PATCH), then one MINOR for HR-366 ruled as a partial supersession of HR-209; HR-367 is a reading and bumps nothing. The section is retitled *Rulings of 2026-10-25 — cleanup only after every PR merged and the sitting declared satisfied*.

### Fixed

- WORKFLOW § *Idle batches* rule 3 no longer reads as a blanket park whenever a step has no Nen verb. A residue note is an ownership fact: each step resolves authority and gates first, then a Nen verb where Nen owns the operation, then the owning skill's native capability or surface adapter, and judgment and authoring proceed. An effort parks only on an observed fact — the named capability absent, an applicable prohibition, an unavailable prerequisite, an observed rejection or an unresolved required human choice — as `g5-capability-unavailable`, `g5-harness-denied` or, for a step whose only route is a hand-run operation, `residue-<skill>`. Improvised shell never replaces a Nen-owned safeguard, a native capability never supplies a human authorization, a hold is one effort's with its evidence named on the register, and the report separates a policy judgment from an observed execution failure. Closes #481.
- `hatsu:kazaana` § 3 says a builder is raised by the surface's native tool under `--idle` as attended, and § Residue that the raise having no verb is never a park; `hatsu:futon` § 12 cites rule 3. PROCESS § *Residue and owned dependencies* says residue names ownership, never availability. The four surface guides name their delegation tool (the Agent tool, `spawn_agent`, a Cursor subagent, `invoke_subagent`) against the same rule.

### Added

- `docs/ab/futon.md` § 2026-10-26: the original false stop of run `469c57416a765d2af7084e0801a59ded` (27 `residue-kazaana` parks, zero PRs) kept as recorded, and nine bounded scenarios evaluated on paper — supported delegation, unavailable delegation, an actual rejection, a deterministic owner unavailable, ordinary judgment, a human gate, an unrelated later rejection, one effort holding while the rest continue, and a true residue park.

### Fixed

- `scripts/pr_body_delivery_check.sh` now reads its expectation with `nen report data … --effort <current branch>`, the shape `hatsu:shibari` § 3 renders the Delivery block with. The ledgers are the repository's, shared by every worktree, and a row's index is its position in what nen returns, so the guard's global read numbered rows across every effort and refused a body rendered per effort ("missing ledger row" / "extra or duplicate body row", HA-PR-#490). The guard moved rather than the skill because the block is the effort's (WORKFLOW § *The effort's ledgers*) and `scripts/pr_delivery.ts` already refuses to guess an effort; a detached HEAD is now refused at exit `2` before nen is asked. shibari § 3 says the guard reads with the same `--effort <branch>`. Closes #492.
- `hatsu:shibari` § 4, `hatsu:en` §§ 4–5 and `hatsu:rikugan` § 3 pass `--catalogue` to `nen pr converge`, where nen 0.28 names `--requirements-from` a deprecated alias; the criteria lane's fixture (`scripts/pr_criteria_fixture.mts`), `nen/contract.json`'s criteria comment, `docs/PR-SECTIONS.md` and `docs/config/plugin-registries.md` say the same, so one call has one spelling (hanten round 1, C-1/C-2); `body-check`, `next-blocker` and `merge` keep `--requirements-from`.
- The guard and `scripts/pr_delivery.ts` derive the effort from `git symbolic-ref --quiet HEAD` with its `refs/heads/` prefix stripped: `--short` prints `heads/<branch>` when a tag shares the name, and that id matches no ledger row (P-1); the guard takes an optional `--effort <branch>` and refuses, at exit 2 by name, a `--repo` checked out on another branch (C-3); `docs/PR-SECTIONS.md` § *Check every write* says `--repo` is the effort's own worktree on its branch; `docs/history/nen-repins.md`'s v0.28.0 note carries a dated pointer to this change (C-4). The fixture gains a same-name-tag case (nen 0.28's `report data` still reads `heads/<branch>` there and normalize refuses the mismatch by name, exit 2, never a global read; the nen fix is proposed at `<core>/.nen/proposed/nen-report-data-branch-ref.json`) and an `--effort` mismatch case.
- shibari § 3's pointer to WORKFLOW § *The effort's ledgers* lost its parenthesis (locator, timestamp, optional phase) at the catch-up of 2026-10-26, a prose-budget trim (shibari at 12281 of 12288 bytes); `docs/PR-SECTIONS.md` § *Check every write* states the cell.

### Added

### Added

- **The durable slice: an effort's ledgers on its pull request (zheref/hatsu#445).** Mukai's new step 8a posts `nen pr ledger post` once the PR exists: one marker-keyed comment holding the effort's phases, usage, hanten findings and proof index, updated in place by a later landing and never duplicated. The comment is public, so three steps come first: `scripts/ledger_findings_prepare.mts` (lane `ledger-findings-guard`) holds the post while a security finding of severity critical or high is not fixed, and writes the findings copy with every absolute path relativised; then the `--dry-run --json` slice passes the pinned gitleaks. Ren § 2a reads it back before a resume (`--restore` only where no local ledger exists), skips the phases recorded as finished and names each in one form, continues an open phase with `nen phase begin --resume`, and refuses by name a phase that ended non-zero instead of beginning it twice; build § 4 cites it. WORKFLOW § *The effort's ledgers* states the verdicts and every record's home by nen version once. Requires nen 0.28 (zheref/nen#516 and zheref/nen#518, released in v0.28.0, the pin since HA-PR-#485).
- **The crash-and-resume proof, ledger half (zheref/hatsu#444).** `scripts/resume_proof.mts` (lane `resume-proof`, gated into `unit-tests.yml`) interrupts ren's and build's phase ledgers after every `begin` and every `end` and the futon run manifest after a park, re-invokes under the resume rule, and asserts one entry per phase, one `resumedAt` on the interrupted phase, a projection equal to the uninterrupted run, a finished ledger byte-identical on re-invocation, `begin --resume` refused at exit 2 while another phase is open, and a phase that ended non-zero refused by name with the ledger unchanged: 34/34 converge at nen 0.26.0, at nen source 0.27.0 and at the released 0.28.0. Each composite's `docs/ab` file carries a dated section; the live publish half was proved on HA-PR-#463 (closed by GitHub on base_ref_deleted; this branch continues as its successor) (`nen pr open` → `existing`, `nen pr ledger post` → `unchanged`).
- **The idle remainder (zheref/hatsu#453).** `nen/decisions.json` gains the `human-gate` G5 row `harness-denied`: the refused command quoted verbatim, the stop marked for the effort, the effort parked as `g5-harness-denied` under `--idle`, never retried another way (it agrees with the 2026-10-23 idle ruling HA-PR-#460 applies). `contracts/idle-permissions.json` lists one exemplar per command shape and per nen verb family an idle batch runs, and `scripts/idle_permissions_check.mts` (lane `idle-permissions-guard`) lists every one the session's Claude Code settings do not pre-allow, or ask or deny, before the first claim; it grants nothing. WORKFLOW § *Idle batches* states the three rules.

### Changed

- **One stop marker per gate, one bell per gate.** `hooks/stop-bell.sh` reads every per-gate marker through `nen stop list --json`, rings each whatever its age (an idle orchestrator's turn spans hours) and clears it with `nen stop clear --gate <g> [--effort <id>]`, so a cleared gate removes only its own marker; a nen without `stop list` falls back to the single `.nen/last-stop.json`, consumed with bare `nen stop clear` (`rm -f` only where no nen runs). It finds the markers on git older than 2.31 and outside git. Jutaisho § 3 marks with `--effort <branch>` only where `nen stop --help` lists it (0.27). A fixture (lane `stop-bell-guard`) holds it, including the real marking form for the nen on PATH.

### Fixed

- **The Delivery engine reads the direct record where nen now writes it.** `scripts/pr_delivery.ts` read `<checkout>/.nen/direct/`, which nen's effort-ledger move (zheref/nen#509) leaves empty; it now reads `<git common dir>/.nen/direct/`, the older home in place while nen still reads it, and refuses a record in both homes by name. Its fixture wrote the record at the old path and so hid the break; it now writes the durable home and asserts all three cases.
- **The turn report renders one effort.** Spiritual Message § 4 passes `--effort <branch>` to `nen report data`: with the ledgers shared by every worktree, a report without it would mix efforts (en, mukai and rikugan render through it).
- **`scripts/spec_family_fixture_check.sh` ran `config_values.sh` through `sh`**, which is dash on the Linux runner and refuses `set -o pipefail` (`Illegal option`); the lane `spec-family-guard` read red on its first live CI run (HA-PR-#489, 2026-10-25) while passing on macOS, where `sh` is bash. It now runs the script with `bash`, as every other caller does.
- **`scripts/pr_delivery.ts` read the git common directory with `--path-format=absolute`** (git 2.31 and later only), so an older git could not read the direct record at all; it now runs plain `--git-common-dir` and resolves a relative answer against the root, as `futon_effort_record.mts` and `hooks/stop-bell.sh` do (Cursor Bugbot on HA-PR-#489).
- **`contracts/idle-permissions.json` omitted mukai step 8a's two pre-post commands**, `ledger_findings_prepare.mts` and the pinned gitleaks scan, so an idle landing would reach them unlisted and park on the first unanswered prompt; both are exemplars now (47 in all; Cursor Bugbot on HA-PR-#489).

  *Landed by [#489](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/489) (changelog.d/resume-durable-idle-2026-10-23.md).*
### Added

- **A spec-derived delivery PR carries a checked `## Spec` section, and shibari stops when an SC is unaccounted (zheref/hatsu#405).** `contracts/pr-sections.json` gains the `spec` footer row (`templates/pr-body/spec.md`), admitted by the new run fact `--spec` of `scripts/pr_sections_resolve.sh` when a closing issue carries `Spec: specs/<nnn>-<slug>@<sha>`, and carrying nen's `spec-rows` requirement whole so `nen pr body-check` fails a missing or empty section; `scripts/pr_sections.ts` links a content requirement to its heading by `heading` and refuses a `rows` requirement on an unconditional row. `shibari` § 4 runs `nen spec coverage`'s delivery form before every create and edit and raises row `spec-coverage` (G5) on anything but `accounted`, and on a nen without the delivery form (exit `2`, no document) stops at a named wiring stop (G5), never opening unchecked; the `Spec:` value is matched whole before any command carries it; `build` names it.
- **Porting readiness quotes the convergence table (part of zheref/hatsu#447; per-scenario keying waits on zheref/nen#525).** `docs/config/spec-repo.md` § *The convergence table at readiness* computes it from `nen spec inventory` at the port PR's head, `nen spec converge --code <target>` and `nen spec parity`; `kamui` § 8, `hiraishin` § 8, `en` § 4 and `rikugan`'s register quote it beside `nen pr ready`. A `missing` or `contradicts` row, or an unsettled target parity row, reads not ready, quoted; a table the verbs could not compute, or one over zero target citations, reads *could not read*, never converged, and holds the PR not ready (stated once, in `spec-repo.md`). Dated proof in `docs/ab/hiraishin.md` § 4.
- **Lane `spec-family-guard`** runs `nen schema check`, `nen repo classify`/`resolve`, `config_values.sh diagnose --domain port`, `nen spec check` and (where the nen carries it) `nen run record write --dry-run` over `scripts/fixtures/spec-family/`, with a red path per read; `scripts/unit_tests_ci.mts` gates it.
- **`hatsu:cleanup` proposes stale run records**: a `done` or `declined` record under `hatsu/specs/` or `hatsu/ports/` whose `at` is older than 30 days.

### Changed

- **`docs/config/spec-repo.md` states the conventions nen reads**: the parity table shape (an `FR` column, one product-code column per member, each cell starting with its state), the tasks grammar (Spec Kit `T<nnn>` and Hatsu's `[x] captured — <cite>` / `[x] delivered — <PR>` / gap lines, `[US<k>]`, `[platform:<p>]` or `### Platform: <p>`, `depends on T<nnn>`, the convergence phase), `<root>/INDEX.md` and `<root>/traceability.md`, and one table of the verbs that read each convention. The run record is written by `nen run record write`; the kamui-folder recapture exception and the owed lines are gone. `kamui` § 3 and `hiraishin` § 4 cite its citation-or-marker grammar; `dowsing-chain` cites each verb. These verbs need a nen past `0.26.0` (zheref/nen#514/#517, unreleased); the contract pin is unchanged.
- **`kamui` § 4 waits with `nen watch until --pr <n> --until merged`** (a `CLOSED` spec PR stops on that read) and § 5 files with `--title-file`, both carried by the pinned nen.
