## v0.142.0 — The SDD-100 delivery and the first idle kazaana batch

Eighteen pull requests landed on `main` since `v0.130.0` (HA-PR-#385 … #476, 2026-10-21 → 2026-10-25), and eleven more (#386, #399, #400, #401, #403, #404, #407, #408, #409, #410, #412) reached it through `gpt/kurapika/mirror-fixture-roots` or `integration/sdd-plan-2026-10-22`; each is cited beside the fragment that describes its work. Their forty-four `changelog.d/` fragments are folded here in the collation order (by fragment name), each entry closing with the pull request that landed it on `main`. Every intermediate version from `0.131.0` to `0.141.2` was never tagged and ships inside `v0.142.0`. The section is folded by hand: `nen changelog collate` anchors only on a `### Unreleased` header and refuses this file's `##` release headings (zheref/nen#446).

**Net effect at `v0.142.0`:** the SDD compliance plan's first delivery is on `main` — the HR rulings sittings (HR-334 … HR-365), the intake examiners, `nen pr converge` as the readiness criterion reader, the canon prose ratchet lint, the clause guard, ruling capture, the idle futon profile and its run manifest, kuchiyose, shell coverage on the focused lanes and the steady-state scheduler rows — and the first live `hatsu:kazaana --idle` batch adds byakugan's unmeasurable-file option, the `decisions` PR-section fact, the sixth stop's `owedRounds[]` reading, plan-unique cleanup ids with a named host for every remote-head delete, and strict UTF-8 decoding in the Development-link guard. The plugin reads `0.142.0` on nen `0.26.0`. Read together, three entries are superseded in part: every fragment line that names an earlier plugin version (`0.131.0` … `0.141.2`) was overtaken by a later merge; the interim `scripts/pr_criteria_check.mts` that the criterion-coverage entry adds (through #401) is retired by the `nen pr converge` entry (#467), so converge is the one reader at this cut; and HR-333, written as a reading by #433, is ruled by #437.

- **The canon prose ratchet lint (HR-13, HR-4).** `scripts/prose_ambiguity_check.mts` (lane `canon-lint`) reads `claude/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `claude/agents/*.md`, `claude/rules/*.md` and `docs/**/*.md`, never `surfaces/**`, against the twelve rules of `contracts/prose.lint.json` (`PL-01` … `PL-12`) and the shrink-only `contracts/prose.lint.baseline.json`, whose `PL-03` (stale nen pin) and `PL-04` (dangling citation) rows are always empty. Exits `0` clean, `1` findings (a stale row, including one whose file the walk no longer reads, and a row grown over the base revision's baseline, summed per key), `2` wiring (a rule id paired with another class than its handler's or missing a key it reads, a repeated baseline key, a marker row that is not ask-once-per-run, a link through a symlink leaving the root); never green over a file it could not read. Rule ids are fixed; the RETIRED exemption is the contract's `retired.rules`; quotes pair within one line and never across a code span; anchors keep `_`; bracket and clause spans are bounded. Its TypeScript fixture, `scripts/prose_ambiguity_check_fixture.mts`, carries the hostile cases (CRLF, a heading inside a fence, folded frontmatter, a symlink leaving the root, a marker naming a human-gate row, an unparseable contract). Owed to `nen canon lint` (zheref/nen#481) and `nen ref resolve --citation` (zheref/nen#482). Page: `docs/config/prose.lint.json.md`.
- **The advisory `canon-lint` check, step one.** `templates/prose-ambiguity-check.yml`, cloned from `prose-size-check.yml`'s hardening, is registered ahead of its file in `scripts/workflow_runner_policy_check.rb` with its guard step frozen and its node pinned; the guard passes the trusted base revision's baseline as `--base-baseline`, so the check refuses growth. Installing it is step two (docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md). The `canon-lint` fixture runs in `unit-tests.yml` through `scripts/unit_tests_ci.mts`, and `scripts/trusted_guard_preflight.rb` derives `.mts` guards (the canon lint and the direct-matrix check run; the readiness verdict is named as not run before a PR exists).
- **Three conventions.** `docs/PROCESS.md` § *An unresolved question in prose* (the one marker shape, the contract's ask-once-per-run `markerRows` only, every human-gate row refused, at most three per skill, a ledger reference, no default); § *Residue and owned dependencies*: every Residue item cites `zheref/<repo>#N` or `unfiled`; `docs/ROSTER.md` § *The ruling template*.
- **Stale pin claims and dangling citations on the tree.** `futon`, `pr-state`, `tenkai`, `limbo`, `docs/WORKFLOW.md`, `docs/PROCESS.md` and `docs/config/plugin-registries.md` stop restating a nen pin; `kazaana`, `kotoamatsukami`, `mukai`, `ren`, `docs/WORKFLOW.md`, `docs/PROCESS.md`, `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md`, `docs/config/plugin-registries.md`, `docs/ab/hatsu-plugin-update.md` and `docs/history/v0.42.0-prose-diet.md` re-aim citations that resolved to nothing. `docs/WORKFLOW.md`'s *Report titles* and *Where the effort is* and `docs/PROCESS.md`'s *AI reviewers per consumer* become headings. (landed by [#402](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/402), through [#412](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/412).)
- **Handbook canon repinned from `zheref/bankai-handbooks` `v0.6.0` to `v0.11.0`.** `nen/repos.json` → `maintained_tools` → `zheref/bankai-handbooks` → `pinned` reads `v0.11.0`, moved by `nen canon pin --write` (tag first, `CON-13`). `hatsu:bankai-handbooks` and `hatsu:bankai-quality` quote the tag their prose was re-read against on 2026-10-22: the always-load set is the same five files; `handbooks/spec/authoring.md` (`SP-{n}`) and the `handbooks/ports/` family (`PT-{n}`: `INDEX.md` plus the six directed pair files of the three product stacks, zero rulings each) load beside the set; each product stack handbook ends in an *Appendix — capture reader* mapping its file suffixes to the spec row kinds; `UZF-21` carries clauses (a)–(f), `UX-13` governs cross-platform adaptation, `SEC-15` binds every lane that reads content it did not author, and `QA-26` is reserved. The review preamble's prefix list admits `SP-` and `PT-`. Both skills record that nen `0.24.0` answers `nen repo scenario` for a `maintained_tools` row that declares a `scenario`, and quote its refusal for one that does not. No canon mirror to regenerate (Hatsu carries none); the three surface mirrors are regenerated at `0.134.0`. Five stale rows leave `contracts/prose.lint.baseline.json`. (landed by [#421](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/421).)
- **Acceptance-criterion coverage is `nen pr converge`'s verdict alone (HR-11; zheref/hatsu#413).** The interim `scripts/pr_criteria_check.mts` and its fixture retire at the nen `0.26` pin: `hatsu:shibari` § 4 runs `nen pr converge --target <owner/name> --pr <n> --requirements-from "$hatsu_root/contracts/issue-sections.json"` after open and edit, `hatsu:en` §§ 4–5 hold its exit `0` as a readiness conjunct beside `nen pr ready` and `body-check`, and `hatsu:rikugan` § 3 quotes it beside the verdict. Closing issues now come from GitHub's closing references (or, off the default branch, the body's closing keywords) rather than the Associated issues table. The catalogue declares no `coverage` block, so nen's defaults apply, and `templates/pr-body/verify.md`, `docs/PR-SECTIONS.md`, `docs/config/plugin-registries.md` and `docs/config/spec-repo.md` name the verb instead of restating its rules. The `criteria` lane stays, on a new fixture `scripts/pr_criteria_fixture.mts`: every reader cites the verb with Hatsu's catalogue, no canon file restates the coverage rule, the catalogue is read through `nen issue criteria --body-file`, the verify template and `verify-content` wiring, and `contracts/hanten-findings.schema.json`'s disposition invariants; `scripts/unit_tests_ci.mts` gates it as its own fixture. Akatsuki's `AC-{epic}-{nnn}` rows (zheref/hatsu#411) are unrecognised by nen `0.26.0`, pinned in the fixture as a documented miss owed to nen. Installed copies get the change by updating to `0.140.0`.
- **Settle round (hanten on HA-PR-#456).** `hatsu:en` §§ 4–5 word the conjunct once (converge's exit `1` is not ready, whatever it blocks on) and carry one G4 reading: `closingSource` `none-on-non-default-base` while the Development guard's `--body` form exits `0` (no closing row, as a stacked *delivers part* PR) is not applicable, ruled by the maintainer as HR-357 (record R-2026-10-24-01, zheref/hatsu#450): reported as such with `closingSource` quoted, never as a pass, and readiness and en § 5's recorded delivery merge proceed on the other conjuncts. `hatsu:shibari` § 4 says when converge runs and what exit `1` asks. The `criteria` lane runs unconditionally in `unit-tests.yml`, catches the restatement shapes it retires in each file's joined text, so a hard-wrapped one too (ROSTER's HR-11 row cut out by its id as the ruling), pins nen `0.26.0`'s raw U+202E in `issue criteria --json` as a miss owed to nen, and cleans its temp directory. `docs/NEN-CONTRACT.md` and `docs/history/nen-repins.md` record the adoption. (landed by [#467](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/467).)
- **Acceptance-criterion coverage is a readiness conjunct, and a constitution-cited finding is critical by shape (HR-11, HR-10).** `templates/pr-body/verify.md` gains, for every repository kind, one `| Criterion | Coverage | Evidence |` row per AC id of each closing issue: `<CODE>#<N>/AC-<k>`, the closed vocabulary satisfied · partial · missing · contradicts · unrequested, and a test name, path:line or scenario. The `verify` catalogue row declares `content: true`, so `contracts/pr-body.requirements.json` gains a `verify-content` row and an empty verify heading fails `nen pr body-check` and `scripts/pr_body_sections_check.sh` alike. `scripts/pr_criteria_check.mts` (`--body` or `--pr`, `--json`) derives each closing issue from the Associated issues table, resolves its code through `nen repo resolve`, reads its `- AC-{n}:` rows through `scripts/gh_token_env.sh`, and exits `1` on a criterion with no row, a `missing` or `contradicts` row, or a closing issue with no criteria (not-verifiable, never a pass); its fixture runs on the new `criteria` lane. `hatsu:shibari` § 4 runs it beside the evidence and Development guards, `hatsu:en` §§ 4–5 hold it as a readiness conjunct beside `nen pr ready` and `body-check`, and `hatsu:rikugan` § 3 quotes it beside the verdict. `hatsu:hanten` § 4's reviewer prompt carries the closing issue refs quoted; the review preamble § 3 and hanten § 5 admit `<CODE>#<N>/AC-<k>`, `CON-{n}` and `HR-{n}` as rule values; hanten § 6 never pushes back a `CON-`/`HR-` finding, only fixes it or defers it to a named amendment PR. `contracts/hanten-findings.schema.json` (`hatsu.hanten.findings/v0.1`) states the disposition enum, the required detail and the tracked ref; its registration with `nen schema check` is owed to nen. The guard is owed to nen as part of `nen pr converge` (zheref/nen#483). hanten, the preamble, shibari and rikugan pay their bytes by deleting restated links and restatements. (landed by [#402](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/402), through [#401](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/401).)
- **`scripts/pr_body_delivery_check.sh` renders the Delivery expectation from Hatsu's own `templates/pr-delivery.md`**, resolved beside the script, instead of `<--repo>/templates/pr-delivery.md`. A consumer carries no such file, so on any non-Hatsu target (zheref/nen's NN-PR-#507 the first seen) the guard exited 2 with `could not read '<target>/templates/pr-delivery.md' (ENOENT)` and could never pass; a target that did carry one would have had its own text checked as the expectation. `shibari` § 3's manual Delivery render now names `"$hatsu_root/scripts/pr_delivery.ts"` and `"$hatsu_root/templates/pr-delivery.md"` for the same reason. The guard resolves its own directory physically (`cd -P`, `CDPATH` cleared), and `scripts/pr_delivery.ts` compares real paths before running, so a call through a symlinked plugin root (`~/.claude/skills/hatsu` is one) no longer exits 0 having written nothing.
- **The guard and its engine are plugin-shipped paths.** `scripts/pr_body_delivery_check.sh` and `scripts/pr_delivery.ts` join `PLUGIN_SURFACE_GLOBS` beside the evidence and Development guards, so a change to either needs a version bump and reaches cached installs; this release is `0.138.1`. `CONTRIBUTING.md`'s copy of the list is re-synced, picking up `scripts/issue_body_check.mts` and `scripts/pr_criteria_check.mts` it had missed. Installed copies get the fix by updating to `0.138.1`.
- The delivery fixture runs the guard against a target with no `templates/`, with a planted template it must ignore, from a plugin root with no template (refused, naming the plugin's path), and through a symlinked plugin root; each fails against `main`'s guard or engine. (landed by [#438](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/438).)
- **`hatsu:dowsing-chain`, the capture skill, wave 1 (HR-12, HR-16).** A product family's sibling repositories read through their build-system membership into evidence rows and written once as a private, living `github/spec-kit` canon in the family's spec repository: the constitution merged first, one folder per capability in waves at G2, every statement ending in `[src: <CODE>:<path>:<l1>-<l2> @ <sha> · <tier>]` or exactly one marker, one bounded clarify sitting, the intake examiners' uncounted challenge (HR-23 … HR-41); then `dispatch` files only what the never-starred picker names at a pinned spec sha, `wave` builds filed marker-free issues through `futon` on the typed call, `recapture` appends a convergence diff, and `resume` continues from `${XDG_STATE_HOME}/hatsu/specs/<run-id>.json`. `docs/config/spec-repo.md` states the five-template grammar, the id, citation and marker grammars, the living-spec model, the pickers, the issue and PR Spec shapes and the run record `hatsu.spec.run/v0.1` once, and cites the SP family's confidence tiers and vocabulary rule (SP-2, SP-3, SP-5) with Hatsu's deltas; `nen/decisions.json` gains eight rows (`spec-capture-scope`, `spec-slice`, `spec-clarify`, `spec-claim-unverified`, `spec-dispatch`, `spec-coverage`, `spec-conflict`, `spec-converge-filing`); offline fixtures under `scripts/fixtures/spec-family/`. The nen spec verb family is owed (zheref/nen#493), so the inventory is prose-made and unverified until it lands; `--dry-run` runs end to end today. The skill joins the dieted list and its three surface mirrors. (landed by [#402](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/402), through [#408](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/408).)
- **An intake examiner can no longer be configured as a hanten reviewer (HR-23).** `config_values.sh set --id workflow.review.scopes` refuses, exit `2` and nothing written, any scope whose `persona` is `satotz`, `lippo`, `buhara` or `bisky` — the set read from the catalogue's `intake.challenger` options, never a second copy — in the TypeScript adapter `scripts/config_values.ts` (BC-11). The adapter now reads the engine's mode after `--hatsu-root`, so `fill` and `diagnose` keep their enrichment when that flag comes first. `hatsu:hanten` § 3 names the four and treats one raised by a hand-written scope as a gap; nen's own refusal for hand edits is zheref/nen#472, and running this lane in CI is zheref/hatsu#397. The `config-values-guard` lane now runs `scripts/config_values_fixture.sh`: the frozen engine's self-test, then `scripts/config_values_examiner_fixture.ts` (the examiner set checked against the agent files and the two prose copies; refusals by name, case, whitespace, zero-width and full-width forms, a path, `.md` or `hatsu:` form, in any scope, under a repeated `--id` or `--value`, a flag's value that looks like a flag, `--hatsu-root` before the mode, and a `NaN` literal the strict parser cannot read — refused, never passed through; a hanten reviewer still written; red on 0.131.0). `docs/config/workflow.json.md` and the catalogue row say so. Plugin bumped to 0.133.1. (landed by [#398](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/398).)
- **futon runs on nen's run manifest (zheref/hatsu#423).** `scripts/futon_effort_record.mts start` opens the manifest through `nen run start --id <run id> --target --repo [--idle-answers]` and only then writes its ownership record, contract `hatsu.futon.run/v0.3` (id, repo, invocation, selector, `startedAt`, `dry`, and the idle profile's `terminal`, `budget`, `source`; no PRs, no parks, no answers). Every opened PR is `nen run append`, every park `nen run park`, the `then` gate and the register `nen run verify`, the budget `nen run budget --max-minutes --max-tokens` with the committed profile's values, and futon § 6's slots `nen loop slots --from-run <run id> --target` — no typed efforts file. The record's `append`, `park` and `status` are retired and refuse by name; a new `profile` prints the ownership record. A run started before this release has no nen manifest, so its `then` gate is held. Park rows are `[a-z0-9-]+`: `g5-<class>` and `residue-<skill>` replace the colon forms nen refuses.
- **`--idle` is nen's to read.** `nen parse futon` reads the token (nen `0.26.0`); the record no longer strips it, and still refuses a doubled `--idle`, a modifier after `then` and any unknown `--` token before it, which nen would fold into the label.
- **The issue shape is nen's (HR-4).** `hatsu:build` § 2, `hatsu:futon` § 2, `hatsu:file` § 4 and `hatsu:emperor-time` § 6 run `nen issue questions` and `nen issue body-check --requirements-from "$hatsu_root/contracts/issue-sections.json"`, stated once in `docs/config/plugin-registries.md` § *The check*. `scripts/issue_body_check.mts` and its fixture are retired; the `issue-shape` lane now runs `scripts/issue_sections_fixture.mts`, which holds the templates to the catalogue and runs the verified nen over them. A vague adjective or technology term on an acceptance row is now an advisory, never a miss.
- **`hatsu:futon <repo>@<selector> --idle --dry-run` (zheref/hatsu#424).** The idle profile's rehearsal in one typed line: every read-only verb runs, nothing is authored, every effort's dry end state is parked by its row (`dry-prompt` inside the local cap, `dry-waiting-slot` past it, or the stop the read predicts), and the record says `dry: true`. The record lifts `--dry-run` off before nen's parse until nen reads it; without `--idle` it is refused. `docs/ab/futon.md` § 2026-10-23 is the proof: 25 issues, 7 authorable in one night. This release is `0.140.1` (0.140.0 collided with a sibling piece on the integration branch).
- **The maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-23 on idle runs** (HR-334, record `R-2026-10-23-01`; WORKFLOW § 2 `idle`): unattended except an act that may not be reversed. Every ask-once row that stars an option now resolves `default` (`harness-attribution-switch`, `host-nen-link`, `cleanup-plan` as this session's deterministic rows, the starred `spec-*` rows); rows that star none hold; `publish-offer` stays `refuse`. The floor is `publish-offer` refuse and `missing-maintainer-choice` at most hold. An idle run always takes the integration-branch shape and never merges into `main`. Every decision taken for the maintainer is listed in the new PR-body footer section **Decisions taken on your behalf** (`decisions`, resolved with `pr_sections_resolve.sh --idle`), in the final brief and in the register's `legendRows`.
- **Review settlement.** futon § 6 sums two slot counts: delivery PRs through `--from-run`, and claimed efforts with no PR through `--efforts` (HR-257). `nen run budget`'s four exit rows are stated. The record copies nen's `startedAt`, refuses a manifest that stores other answers than it sent, and names nen's manifest as the orphan when its own write fails. `bind` refuses a dry run. A dry run's predictions go on the record through a new `predict` verb, never into nen's `parked[]`. futon § 1 lifts `--dry-run` before its echo parse, and § 7 holds every act under `--dry-run`.
- **The maintainer's second ruling of 2026-10-23** (HR-335, record `R-2026-10-23-02`; WORKFLOW § *Idle batches* rule 9): nothing blocks a batch. An issue with an open `OQ-{n}` row, or whose questions cannot be read, is skipped (`nen run park --row needs-clarification` / `g5-shape-unread`) and the queue continues. futon § 10's closing report and the Rikugan register list every skipped issue with its open rows, and the report ends by offering (never starting) the open-question flow: one picker per open row, each answer recorded through `nen stop answer --record` and struck into the issue through `nen issue edit-body --expect-body-sha256`, then read back with `nen issue questions`.
- **Local slots free at `ready+prompted` (zheref/hatsu#425).** The maintainer's pick (HR-353, record `R-2026-10-23-20`) supersedes HR-257's *frees at its local merge* in part and reconciles the 2026-10-13 stacked-slot rule. Every effort, stacked or not, frees its local slot once its PR is Ready and its merge prompt (or, for a stacked PR, its Ready stop) was shown. A claimed effort with no PR holds its slot until then, or until its local merge into the integration branch. `monitor.localSlotFrees` and the catalogue default are `ready+prompted`, and the idle `start` refuses `merged` and `stacked-open`, here or under `idle.localSlotFrees`. futon § 11's cap is now 7 local slots, not 7 worktrees. (landed by [#467](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/467).)
- **Hatsu's shell guards are measured on their focused lanes (zheref/hatsu#148, UZF-19).** Twenty-five of the 38 shell lanes now run their script through `scripts/shell_coverage.mts`: with the pinned Bashcov 4.0.0 (activated at that exact version through RubyGems, so a user gem home off `PATH` still resolves), SimpleCov 1.3.2, simplecov-lcov 0.9.0 and a Bash 5+, the `test` row writes `coverage/<lane>/lcov.info` and nen records its capture, and each lane's new extraction-only `coverage` row (which refuses a report whose recorded file digests no longer match the tree) is read, as byakugan now does, with `nen shu coverage --lane <lane> --touched --base origin/<base> --from-capture`, which runs no fixture. `project.toolchain` declares the five host rows (`ruby` and `bash` verify-only, the three gems `gem-user`), so `nen shu tools --repo . --install` provisions them. Without Bashcov a fixture still runs, once and uninstrumented, says so on stderr and leaves no report, so `--from-capture` refuses for want of a capture rather than reading an old number; `HATSU_SHELL_COVERAGE=0` skips the instrumentation and `require` refuses without it. A POSIX `sh` fixture runs under `sh` first, then instrumented. A trace Bashcov cannot parse captures nothing; a passing run stays green, and a failing one is re-run plain, whose exit decides. SimpleCov is activated at its pin before Bashcov, and each installed `.gem` is checked against the contract's `gemSha256` (nen's `gem-user` installer pins by version only); a mismatch refuses. The gems are on no dependency-audit row: not scanned. A `nen shu tools` exit `5` on these coverage-only rows alone reads *unmeasured, still testable* (`docs/PROCESS.md`, breath § 2c, ten § 1, bankai-quality). The helper refuses any invocation its lane does not declare, a symlinked fixture, and an LCOV source outside the repository; its own fixtures (`scripts/shell_coverage_fixture.test.mts`) run in the `unit-tests-ci-guard` lane. `scripts/shell_coverage.mts` joins `PLUGIN_SURFACE_GLOBS` in `scripts/plugin_bump_check.sh`, because ten's `nen shu tools --repo <hatsu_root>` runs the `bash` row's probe out of an installed copy. The other thirteen seat their `coverage` row with the measured reason instead of a number: in eight the trace of a child shell reaches the output the fixture asserts on (macOS `/bin/sh`, bash 3.2, ignores `BASH_XTRACEFD`; `publish-integrity-guard` among them, which is also red uninstrumented under an empty or C locale and green under UTF-8), three produce a trace Bashcov 4.0.0 cannot parse, and two (`trusted-guard-preflight`, `redaction-tree`) are red uninstrumented on the measuring host and on the untouched base, under UTF-8 too. The `plugin` lane's `coverage` seat now says where shell coverage lives and keeps the TypeScript guards as an unmeasured gap. The 80/85/90 ladder is unchanged.
- **`config-values-guard` runs in CI, step one (zheref/hatsu#397, BC-9).** `scripts/unit_tests_ci.mts` selects the lane whenever `scripts/config_values.ts` exists beside `scripts/config_values_examiner_fixture.ts`, and its fixture proves the selection, the missing-fixture refusal and a red lane failing the run. The runner is the base revision's trusted copy, so the lane runs from the first pull request whose base carries this change; step two is that pull request's `unit-tests` log naming the lane, plus a non-draft probe pull request breaking the examiner fixture that turns the check red; HA#397 closes on that evidence. The lane also declares its `coverage` row: Bashcov measures `scripts/config_values.sh` and its fixture, while `scripts/config_values.ts` stays in the TypeScript gap. (landed by [#467](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/467).)
- **nen `review ledger` owns hanten's review-cycle ledger (zheref/hatsu#216, zheref/nen#346).** `hanten` §§ 1–2b and `breath` § 3b call `nen review ledger ensure|init|decide|record|show|recover-first --json`; the roster and maxima are the target's `review.scopes` alone, with no fallback persona or late-persona exception, so an unstamped ledger missing a declared reviewer's row refuses at exit `2` and is restored by hand. `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § *The review ledger's exits* routes every exit (a missing `review.scopes` to configuration, a nen below 0.26 to the binding, a lock to a re-run) and says how to drain a pre-0.136.0 installed copy before the new writer touches the same ledger: nen's mutex does not exclude the script's OS lock. `scripts/tenkai_adopt.sh`'s review-ledger row asks `nen review ledger show` instead of re-checking persona rows itself, after checking the nen on `PATH` meets the pin and that `review.scopes` is declared. ROSTER HR-244 records the delivery; two readings (the retired lane, HR-287's `leorio` hydration) are owed the maintainer's ruling at this change's G4.
- **Repinned nen to v0.26.0, minimum and floor 0.26.** Absorbed: `shibari` § 4 reads `pr open`'s adoption of an open PR at exit `0`; `file` § 4 reads `issue file`'s `--json` refusals from the `nen.refusal/v0.1` envelope on stdout; `izanagi` § 3 and `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § *`monitor`* name the loop ledger's git-common-dir home. `docs/history/nen-repins.md` § *v0.26.0* lists every v0.25/v0.26 note and its disposition.
- **`scripts/hanten_cycle_ledger.sh`, `scripts/hanten_cycle_ledger_late_persona_fixture.sh` and lane `ledger-guard`.** The ledger's tests are nen's own; the BC-11 freeze (HR-243) ends with the script. (landed by [#422](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/422).)
- **`hatsu:kuchiyose`, the CI tier installer (zheref/hatsu#331; HR-135 … HR-142).** On a repository `tenkai` has adopted, `hatsu:kuchiyose [tier <tier>] [--repo <path>] [--dry-run]` resolves the Akatsuki provider by its code `AK` and its pinned release tag, reads the tier catalogue at that tag, asks the tier and the per-consumer rulings in one picker call (Hidan on or off; Claude billing with the subscription token starred), and lands the caller workflow, the lanes' `nen/gates.json` entries (`wake: "event"`, never `base_reviewers`) and the `nen/workflow.json` → `ci` block as one PR at the consumer's own gate, plus the provider's registry row as a second PR. The day-one ceremony (the `-akatsuki` Apps, their secrets, the Claude secret, the Hidan variable, the required-checks ruleset) is printed one block per step and never performed; `hatsu:kuchiyose verify` reads each lane's state off `nen pr ready --json`, never a check's colour. `nen/decisions.json` gains two human-gate rows (`ci-install-rulings`, `ci-ceremony`); `contracts/config-catalogue.json` gains the `ci` domain and five rows, none defaulted, each `ownedBy: kuchiyose` so `config_values.sh diagnose`, `fill` and `need` never list them and `tenkai` never asks or fills them (`scripts/config_values_owned_fixture.ts`); `contracts/permissions.json` denies the session `gh secret set`/`delete`, `gh variable set` and `gh api` writes to rulesets, secrets and variables; PROCESS and GATE-CONFIGURATION are repinned to nen `0.26`'s `wake: "event"`; `scripts/provider_tag_commit.mts` peels a provider release tag to its commit (an annotated tag's object is never compared with a commit), fixture-held in lane `gh-api-read-guard`; PROCESS's pre-0.26 *until zheref/nen#372* sentence is replaced by the base-read rule; the guide is `docs/using/kuchiyose.md` and the dry run `docs/ab/kuchiyose.md`. The skill joins the dieted list and its three surface mirrors. **Today it stops before writing on every consumer**: the provider has no release tag, no `schemas/tiers.json` and no caller render verb yet (zheref/akatsuki-ai#49, #50), and nen `0.26`'s `nen schema set` cannot add a member to a gates array, so the gates write names that gap rather than hand-editing the file. (landed by [#467](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/467).)
- **#336 Mirror fixture roots** ([#336](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/336)). The focused mirror fixture now compares the roots passed by the shared checker and regenerator, and proves a changed regeneration root is rejected. (landed by [#385](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/385).)
- **#362 Release notes from collated changelogs** ([#362](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/362)). The generated release publisher reads the changelog's own heading level through a TypeScript helper, accepts Nen's `###` sections and existing `##` changelogs, and refuses absent or mixed-level tag boundaries. Process consumers render the helper with Tenkai and need Node 22.18+. (landed by [#385](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/385), through [#386](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/386).)
- `hatsu:okiro` composes a new project's Nen scaffolding, approved setup post-steps, Tenkai adoption and one initial Hatsu commit behind one complete plan and confirmation. Dry runs write nothing; existing trees route to Tenkai, native refusals quote Nen's manual step, and interrupted runs verify ownership before resuming. Repository creation and pushing remain separate owner calls. Kurapika and emperor-time now point new projects to Okiro. Addresses HA#393; native structured handoff remains NN#470's dependency. (landed by [#396](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/396).)
- `nen/workflow.json` → `release.proposalMerge` (`self` · `human`, default `self`): a repository can keep its release-proposal merge with the maintainer. `hatsu:getsuga` § 3a reads it at the merge-time re-check's `<baseNow>` SHA and, on anything but `self`, runs no merge and stops at the declaration gate quoting the line it read (the opt-out Akatsuki's `CON-5` row 5 asks for). Catalogued as `workflow.release.proposalMerge`; `config_values.sh set` refuses a value outside the two. Addresses HA#414.
- `scripts/config_values.sh get --repo <path> --id <catalogue id> [--ref <git ref>] [--json]` prints one catalogued value's effective reading (declared, default, invalid, unset) at a ref or in the working tree; a parent key that is not an object is `invalid` (exit `1`), and an unreadable file or ref, or a symlink or submodule anywhere on the file's path, is exit `2`, never a default. Fixture `scripts/config_values_get_fixture.ts` in lane `config-values-guard`.
- `templates/ports/INDEX.md` defines one row per directed pair file, the wording the rendered `handbooks/ports/INDEX.md` carries, so a fresh `hatsu:bankai-rule` render no longer puts the "pair that carries rulings" sentence back; bankai-rule § 4 says every pair file gets its row, at `0` rulings too. `port-rule-guard` holds the template wording (T16) and, in its own fixture, that a render over a `0`-rulings pair file indexes every pair file once (T15b, with a red case); the reserve guard itself still never compares INDEX rows to files. Addresses HA#406.
- **Handbook canon repinned from `zheref/bankai-handbooks` `v0.11.0` to `v0.12.0`** (commit `5f0b2f4b`, recorded as `pinnedSha` by hand until zheref/nen#506), so Akatsuki's pin can equal Hatsu's (HR-8). The always-load set, the spec handbook and the pair files are unchanged; the `react-uzf-v1` Electron desktop shell (`RC-64`–`RC-69`) and its `QA-7`/`QA-12` rows are new, and `hatsu:bankai-quality`'s tooling table carries the desktop row. History in `docs/ab/bankai-handbooks.md` § *Repin to v0.12.0*.
- **A missing pair file is refused from handbooks `v0.13.0`**, the tag that is to carry `CON-13` as amended by zheref/bankai-handbooks#21 (every directed pair ships its file; unmerged today): `port_rule_reserve.sh --handbooks-tag <pin>` exits `2` there, naming the pair and the tag, and `hatsu:bankai-rule` and `hatsu:bankai-handbooks` read the absence as that refusal. At `v0.11.0` to `v0.12.x`, which `CON-13` still says create a pair file on first use, the create path stands. Fixture T17. The INDEX template's lead stays `v0.12.0`'s rendered text (T16); adopting zheref/bankai-handbooks#21's lead at the `v0.13.0` repin is owed (named on HA#449). (landed by [#467](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/467).)
- **`hatsu:byakugan` § 8 has an option for a touched file no declared lane measures (zheref/hatsu#418).** Option **E** applies where no lane or target in the declaration measures this file's target, on any host. It names the missing capability with the line that shows it absent, reports the file as `not measurable here — <capability>` in the percent column rather than a number, has the PR state it, and leads to a proposal at the declaration gate (G4 in a canon repository, G2 in a consumer one) for a lane or target that can measure the file, or a seat on such a lane. It never leads to a lowered ladder or an added exclusion. ⭐ goes to E wherever it holds, and A now means tests that are writable and runnable on a lane that measures the file. § 8 keeps one cause per option, so snapshot-tested UI, generated code and a lane-bound target are no longer folded into one waiver, and the stop groups its files by applicable option set and asks one picker per group: B plus that group's options, two to four and never padded, exactly one ⭐ by the order E, A, C, D (B never), with the line that would tip it stated. Each file's group is named in the chat table, an answer binds only its group's files, and an unbound file returns to § 8 as a fresh stop. A file shown to be § 9 (b) leaves the § 6 test loop at once for option E. § 9 tells apart (a) a lane that seats coverage, (b) a lane that declares coverage while no declared lane or target measures this file on any host, with (a) taking precedence for its own lane, which reaches § 8's stop, and (c) a host that lacks a capability the declaration expects, reported `unread — <host, capability>`, whose remedy is host repair rather than a declaration proposal. § 5 rule 2 now excludes only file kinds or languages the tool never measures, and § 2's band table carries the three labels, which take precedence over every band. No change to `coverage.minimum`, the bands or any nen verb. (landed by [#476](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/476).)
- **`cleanup` writes its two helper calls out in full (zheref/hatsu#426).** § 4 states the worktree-path admission as `'<Hatsu root>/scripts/cleanup_path_admit.sh' --core '<core>' --base '<branch.base>' --path '<abs path>'`, with where each value comes from; § 3's raise now carries the absolute Hatsu root into Shizuku's prompt, since a subagent inherits no `$hatsu_root`. § 7 states the marker slug once for every scope — a typed `for <CODE>#<N>` keeps its number, a one-effort sitting takes its issue (else its PR), a several-effort sitting takes `<composite>-<CODE>-<YYYY-MM-DD>`, no number takes the branch slug — and the check behind it, `nen ref format --repo '<core>' --code <CODE> --kind <IS|PR> --number <N> --no-glyphs`, whose `<CODE>-<IS|PR>-#<N>` is cut to `<CODE>-<N>`; a non-zero exit keeps the slug and says so. `third-hand` § 2 now cites that rule instead of its own copy, so a sitting's harvest and sweep markers share a name. `agents/shizuku.md` cites § 4 and § 5 instead of restating them. To hold the 12288-byte ceiling, cleanup drops restatements: § 3's per-surface picker list (PROCESS § *Surfaces and pickers* owns it) and Shizuku's tool list, § 4's `docs/ab/cleanup.md` evidence cite (the `.ts` header and `docs/ab/cleanup.md` still cite each other), § 2's composite step numbers, § 6's restatement of § 3's pick record and close, and one hard limit restating § 4's "ownership never assumed". (landed by [#429](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/429).)
- **`unit-tests.yml` runs the tenkai engine's self-test (zheref/hatsu#427, BC-9).** `scripts/unit_tests_ci.mts` selects lane `tenkai-guard` whenever `scripts/tenkai_adopt.sh` exists, so the only Hatsu-side test of `nen review ledger` (`init`, `record`, `show`) runs on same-repository, non-draft pull requests instead of only by hand. The runner now spawns every lane with the workflow-installed nen's directory first on `PATH` and its path as `NEN`, because the workflow installs nen with `--rc none` and the self-test's review cases call `nen` from `PATH`; a binary whose directory contains the `PATH` delimiter is refused at exit `2`. No workflow file changed, so this is a one-step landing: the runner is trusted at the base revision, and the lane first runs on the first `unit-tests` run whose base includes this merge.
- **`tenkai_adopt.sh --self-test` no longer depends on the checkout's nen minimum or on a symlinked temp directory.** Its review-ledger cases hold the running nen to a fixture minimum at its own `major.minor`, with a case for a nen one minor below, so a repin PR that raises `dependency.minimum` stays green on the base revision's nen. Its fixtures resolve their real paths, and `scripts/config_values.ts` resolves both sides of its `nen/workflow.json` containment check and keeps the engine's blocked row for an unparseable declaration instead of exiting `2`, which a real-path temp directory (the Linux runner's) triggered. (landed by [#431](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/431).)
- **Copilot is declared a bot, so `aiReviewers: optional` waives its round (zheref/hatsu#428, closing zheref/hatsu#380).** `nen/gates.json` → `reviewers[]` `copilot` carries `kind: "bot"` and a `$comment` saying why. nen `0.26` waives under `optional` or `off` only a reviewer declared `bot` with an identical declaration (the parsed identity, never a `$comment`) at the PR's base and the default branch's tip. Without the key, every Hatsu PR read `aiReviewers optional waives bot rounds: (none)` and stopped at `rounds-owed` on a Copilot request GitHub never records. Read at the base, it takes effect from the first PR whose base carries it, not on the PR that adds it. `docs/PROCESS.md` § *AI reviewers per consumer* states nen's waiver: a declared bot's round is waived and the verdict reads `ready`. The hanten prompt remains for a round nen did not waive, and its criterion also refuses a `kind: "human"` entry. `nen/decisions.json` row `ai-reviewers`, `nen/workflow.json`'s commentary, `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § `aiReviewers`, `docs/config/gates.json.md` (a `kind` row), `docs/config/workflow.json.md`, `docs/config/decisions.json.md`, `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` § 3 and `en` §§ 5–6 follow. `docs/ROSTER.md` HR-333 records the reading that a waived bot round reads `ready` (hanten settled), owed the maintainer's ruling at this change's G4 (ruled there; see the HR-333 entry). The sixth stop's (e) premise is marked stale and deferred to zheref/hatsu#430.
- **"Owed to nen" retired for the release-unit merge's `aiReviewers` read.** It shipped in nen `0.26` (NN-PR-#463). `getsuga` § 3a and `docs/PROCESS.md` § Residue now say that the verb waives a declared bot's round, that getsuga § 3a merges on it under HR-333, and that a round it does not waive halts as under `required`; `kazaana` § 5 defers the case to getsuga § 3a, and its § Residue drops the owed-to-nen clause. These were zheref/hatsu#380's remaining clauses; its repin criterion was met by HA-PR-#422 (nen `v0.26.0`). (landed by [#433](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/433).)
- **The sixth stop's (e) reads nen's owed list (zheref/hatsu#430, HR-365).** `docs/PROCESS.md` § *The sixth stop*, condition (e), no longer argues from nen `v0.21.0` exposing no owed list. Under `aiReviewers: required` it now holds when the `rounds-owed` row at the judged head carries `owedRounds[]` and every entry's `reviewer` is in the row's `roundQuorum.anyOf`; an entry outside it, whatever its `kind`, or a row without the list (uncomputed) ends at `cap-reached`'s not-ready terminus. That widens the stop: a consumer that enrols a reviewer outside its round quorum reaches it whenever that reviewer is not owed at the judged head, where the declaration-only test (`reviewers[]` ⊆ `any_of`) never let it. The maintainer ruled it on 2026-10-22 through the picker (*"Read owedRounds[]"*); `docs/ROSTER.md` records it as HR-365, which replaces HR-221's (e); HR-221 stays an indexed reading for (a) to (d), its index entry noting the superseded (e) (governance 1.56.0). (a) to (d), the optional/off hanten prompt and nen itself are unchanged. (landed by [#476](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/476).)
- **`cleanup` plan row ids are unique in their plan (zheref/hatsu#435).** § 5 now mints each row's `id` as its class letter (`D`, `P` or `N`) plus one count across the whole plan, never restarted per class or owner group, so the HA-IS-#426 sweep's two `S1` rows (a remote head and a reviewer checkout) cannot recur. § 6 opens with the id check, read word for word: `jq -e -s 'length==1 and (.[0].plan|type=="array") and ([.[0].plan[].id] | all(type=="string" and test("\\S")) and length==(unique|length))' '<marker>'`. The read slurps the file, so a marker holding two JSON documents fails it in either order, and an object `plan` or a whitespace-only id fails too. The exit code routes the stop. Exit `1` or `5` (an id two rows share, a row with no id or a blank one, a `plan` that is not one array, such as a marker whose `plan[]` holds strings) stops before the picker question or its idle default and removes nothing. On `5` the stop names the parse error; on `1` the shared ids come from a companion read, `jq -r -s '[.[0].plan[].id] | group_by(.)[] | select(length>1)[0]' '<marker>'`. The main session raises pass 1 again to mint the plan anew, never renaming a row itself, and runs the read again. On a second stop the main session records `pickerAnswer` `ids-invalid`, which tells it apart from the maintainer's **C**, and Shizuku closes the marker `none-picked` with the ids in the report. Any other exit (`2`, `127`) is a host fact: named, and the marker is left `pending`, never `none-picked`. A plan with no deterministic or proposed row asks no question and has no idle default: Shizuku closes it `already-clean` when the plan is empty and `none-proposed` when it has only named rows. A picked id that names no `plan[]` row, or a named one, runs nothing and is listed unmatched with `result` `partial`, and § 3's close treats a `done[]` id the same way. `pending` joins § 7's `result` list and § 2's offered-again list. The Residue notes that the id read's exit routing assumes jq 1.7 or later. The row shape names "(none on named row)" again. Proved against 13 sample markers and the 17 real markers in a core `.nen/cleanup/`: HA-426 reads `1` (its two `S1` rows), HA-363 reads `5` (string `plan[]` rows), the rest `0`. The check has no nen verb and joins the residue owed to zheref/nen#409. `agents/shizuku.md` states the same rule where it restates the row shape. To hold the 12288-byte ceiling, cleanup drops restatements: § 3's frontier-tier clause (Hard limits keep it), its PROCESS cite in the picker step (the raise paragraph keeps it), § 3's pass-2 detail (§ 6 owns it), § 5's paraphrase of the ancestry command it prints and a repeated literal-path clause, § 6's remote-delete ordering (now stated in § 5 by zheref/hatsu#436) and the description's wording. Settling the review dropped more restatements: § 3's separate pass-2 step, so § 6 alone owns the picker record and pass 2; § 6's "Nothing removed before answer" (Hard limits keep "never deletes before pick"); Hard limits' "never unpicked/named row" (§ 5's named class and Authority keep it) and "never retries wider" (§ 6 keeps it); § 4's "facts, never a class" (the admission header and § 5 keep it) and "(not this sitting's effort)" (§ 1 keeps prior leftovers apart from this sitting); the caches row's "never deleted" (§ 5's named class keeps it); § 7's "(§ 4's `<core>`)" and its "under B" aside (§ 2 keeps it); § 2's "once"; the description's "bytes freed read back"; and the Residue's ", as third-hand's". `agents/shizuku.md` now leaves pass 2's mechanics to § 6: it drops its restatement of the read-backs, the stderr report and `none` (the wider-flag refusal stays under *What you never do*). (landed by [#476](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/476).)
- **`cleanup` names the checkout a remote-head delete runs from when no worktree is left (zheref/hatsu#436).** § 5 had a push shape only for an effort worktree, so on the HA-IS-#426 sweep a merged remote head whose worktree was gone got a row pushing from core, which sits on the trunk and is refused by `hooks/guard-base-branch.sh`. Now every remote-head row, deterministic or proposed, names its host, and `<branch.base>` is never a remote-head row. That includes a merged head whose worktree is kept (dirty, locked or not admitted), and the clean effort-worktree sequence, whose push is now that same row with its own worktree as host, so a moved SHA there has an outcome too. The row first runs `git -C '<host>' ls-remote --heads origin -- 'refs/heads/<branch>'`, which must print only `<sha>\trefs/heads/<branch>` with the SHA pass 1 recorded, else the row runs nothing and is reported. Then it runs `git -C '<host>' push origin --delete -- '<branch>'`, where `<host>` is a literal path: the head's own worktree when § 4's admission admitted it, else another admitted path. An admitted path is never on `<branch.base>` (condition 6 of `cleanup_path_admit.ts`'s header), so the trunk guard lets the push through, and core is never the host. The host goes in the row's evidence. Every push runs before any `worktree remove`, and § 6's pass 2 says so ("every push first"), so the host is still there when its row runs. With no admitted path, the row is **named** (`no admitted checkout`, now in the "Named facts win" list) and never given a command that cannot run as written. Hard limits again refuse, in one bullet, deleting the trunk or an open-PR branch, local or remote, and running an unpicked, named or unvalidated row. The trunk guard hook does not catch a quoted `-- 'main'`, so the skill's own refusal is what holds there; body copies of these rules now defer to Hard limits. `agents/shizuku.md` restores the push-before-removal ordering #435 took out of § 6, names the host and the SHA read, and adds `no admitted checkout` to its named facts. To fit, it cites § 4's ref check instead of spelling it, and drops its parenthetical list of named kinds (§ 5 owns it). To stay under the 12288-byte ceiling, cleanup drops § 4's prunable/surface clause (the admission header refuses both), the `(-D)` gloss in § 6, the Hard limits' repeat of § 4's name rule and § 5's proposed restatement of the stale-trunk `git branch -d` case (the deterministic clause keeps it). Settling the review dropped more restatements: - the description's object list and class names (§ 4's table and § 5 keep them), and its "Never destroys …" sentence (Hard limits keep the pick, the uninspected tree, the trunk and the open-PR branch; § 4 keeps core's ledgers); - the Proposed list's "worktree, branch, remote head with PR closed unmerged or no PR", "merged remote head whose SHA != PR `headRefOid`" and "merged local branch with no worktree" (§ 5's table states each cell); - Authority's "removing core, trunk, locked or open-PR worktree" and "core's `.nen/` ledgers" (Hard limits keep the trunk and open-PR refusal, the admission header refuses core, § 5's named facts hold locked worktrees, and § 4 makes core's ledgers records); - Hard limits' "never removes worktree whose dirt was not listed" (§ 5's proposed row lists the paths); - § 4's "and no command runs on them" (a named row never runs); - the billed row's "(paste absolute root)" (§ 4's opening keeps it); - § 5's "`git worktree prune` (no path)" gloss (the admission header keeps it) and "by `git branch -d`" in the stale-trunk clause (the command precedes it); - § 7's "(§ 2)" after `already-clean`. - In the final round: § 3's pass-1 "Removes nothing" and the named class's "never run" (Hard limits); the table's "(unlocked)" and the reviewer rule's "`locked no`" (named facts win first); the surface row's "for surface's own cleanup" and the billed "(maintainer's act)" (§ 5's named class); "(host/remote used)" in the nature line; the trunk-guard gloss on condition 6; § 4's "An exit but `0` admits nothing" (the admission header is the rule); and the effort sequence's own "only when `git ls-remote` returns PR's `headRefOid`" (now the remote-head row's SHA read). (landed by [#476](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/476).)
- **The Development-link guard decodes UTF-8 with Perl's `Encode` (zheref/hatsu#440).** `scripts/pr_development_link_check.sh` checked encoding by sending `iconv -f UTF-8 -t UTF-8` to `/dev/null`. macOS's `/usr/bin/iconv` fails writing a character device when a valid multibyte character straddles its 1024-byte buffer, so a body was refused as `not valid UTF-8` depending only on where a `·`, `→` or emoji landed, and `shibari` § 4 and `en` § 4 stopped on a body that was fine. The same iconv also passes a code point above U+10FFFF (`F4 90 80 80`) and the lead bytes `F5`–`F7`, which are not UTF-8. The guard now decodes with `Encode`'s `utf8` decoder under `FB_CROAK`, writing no output, and then refuses a surrogate and any code point above U+10FFFF. It refuses a stray, truncated or overlong sequence, a surrogate, a code point above U+10FFFF and a lead byte `F5`–`FF` with the same message as before. Noncharacters such as U+FFFE and U+10FFFF are valid UTF-8 and are read. A body path that starts with `-` is read as a file, never as a `perl` or `cmp` switch. A decoder that cannot run is exit `2`, quoting the decoder's first error line, and is never reported as `not valid UTF-8`. The guard now needs `perl` instead of `iconv`, and `PR_DEV_LINK_PERL` names the perl to run under the same never-a-verdict rule as `PR_DEV_LINK_AWK`. The self-test adds these cases and a sweep in which a `·`, a `→` and an emoji start at every offset from 1000 to 1100. `scripts/unit_tests_ci.mts` now lists the `development-link-guard` lane. `unit-tests.yml` runs that runner from the base commit, so the lane first runs in CI on a pull request whose base already lists it. It does not run on the pull request that adds it, or on that pull request's integration pull request into `main`, so CI cannot confirm it there. No other script has the same `iconv … > /dev/null` shape. (landed by [#476](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/476).)
- **The 2026-10-23 SDD readings sitting is recorded (zheref/hatsu#450).** `docs/ROSTER.md` gains § *Rulings of 2026-10-23 — the SDD readings sitting*, HR-334 to HR-362, every row rendered by `scripts/ruling_record.mts render` from a record exported into `docs/ruling-records/` (27 under effort `HA-IS-#450`, 2 under the futon-batch effort), and `ruling-record` re-renders all 29 clean. What changes in canon: `great-hiker` § 2 runs prose as a `models.<surface>.frontier` subsession (HR-336, reversing the reading HR-326 and superseding HR-100's never-frontier sentence for prose; the skill's Hard limit, WORKFLOW § *models* and the lead persona name the one exception, scoped by HR-362; OPEN-7 closed); Akatsuki `CON-5` row 7 is ratified (HR-361, OQ-11); HR-10's critical-by-shape binds only ruled HR rows and CON ids (HR-337, reversing the reading HR-328; the review preamble § 3 and `hanten` § 6 follow, and the findings schema forces the critical shape on `CON-` ids only, its HR half tracked by zheref/hatsu#466); HR-331 and HR-332 are ratified (HR-338, HR-339), OPEN-5 and OPEN-6 close, and their owed-ruling markers leave § *Sources*, `nen/contract.json`'s `$precedence` and WORKFLOW's precedence restatement; HR-257's slot rule is superseded in part (HR-353, `ready+prompted`). § *Readings* keeps HR-331 and HR-332 with their ratifying row and drops HR-326 and HR-328. Two of the lead's readings, HR-363 and HR-364, sit below the table and in § *Readings*. `models.rule` in `nen/workflow.json` names the one frontier exception, and every prose copy points at WORKFLOW § *models*. Rulings cited by their R-id now cite the HR row, the record kept as evidence. `scripts/ruling_record.mts render` keeps the lead's status and settles cells as markdown, escaping only a pipe. HR-257's landed words are restored, the status carrying HR-353. The governance footer moves to 1.55.1 (29 MINOR, one PATCH). Plugin `0.141.0`. (landed by [#467](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/467).)
- **The clause guard reads HR statuses, citations and the Readings index (zheref/hatsu#452).** `scripts/clause_id_guard.mts` (lane `clause-guard`) gains three checks over `docs/ROSTER.md`, each exiting 1 by name: a definition whose status is not one of ROSTER § *How HR ids are read*'s words — `ruled`, `reading`, or `reversed by` / `superseded by` one or more `HR-m` with one optional balanced parenthesis naming the displaced part and ending the status — or that carries no status at all, or whose table `Status` cell and parenthesis after `**HR-N**` both exist and differ; a bare `HR-N` or a `(#hr-N)` link whose id no row defines, naming the citing line once per id; and a `reading` row with no entry in § *Readings*' index, or an index entry whose row is not a reading, is not defined, or whose label and link ids disagree. A roster with no `## Readings` heading exits 2, never a clean parity. The fixture grows from 24 to 45 cases, every new check with a hostile case that fails by name, and the live roster reads clean on all three, so no ROSTER row changes; the § *Governance* footer's description of the guard follows (Version 1.26.1, a wording PATCH). Plugin `0.139.1`. (landed by [#467](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/467).)
- **The `decisions` PR section resolves for any run that took a decision, not only an idle one (zheref/hatsu#474).** `contracts/pr-sections.json`'s footer row `decisions` (*Decisions taken on your behalf*) now carries `"when": "decisions"`, a new run fact, where it carried `"when": "idle"`. An attended run that applied a default or a ruling (zheref/hatsu#474 AC-1; an idle run's are [HR-334](docs/ROSTER.md#hr-334)'s) no longer has to drop the section or carry a failing strict check. Any positive source includes the section: `scripts/pr_sections_resolve.sh --decisions`, `--idle` (an idle run always pre-answers its rows), or `--decisions-from <nen.run manifest>` whose `idleAnswers` is not empty. The manifest proves only the pre-answers a run was launched with, since nen never updates `idleAnswers` and it is null on an attended run, so a null or empty map adds nothing: beside `--decisions` or `--idle` the flag wins, and alone it refuses at exit 1 rather than reading as "no decision". A default taken mid-run or attended is `--decisions`, passed by the session that took it. Every other `--decisions-from` input refuses at exit 1, whatever else is passed: a non-object, a row id outside `[a-z0-9-]+`, a value other than `default`, `hold` or `refuse`, and a repeated flag. In every file the resolver loads, a missing or unreadable file is reported by path and errno code, and unparseable JSON by path, error class and position, never by an excerpt of its bytes. A run that took no decision resolves no `decisions` requirement, as before. On zheref/hatsu#467's body the strict sections check and `nen pr body-check` now agree; `scripts/pr_sections_fixture_check.ts` proves it on that body's heading skeleton (`scripts/fixtures/pr-sections/ha-467-headings.md`) through the shim for each source, proves the footer on all 24 cells with and without the fact, and proves each refusal. `claude/skills/shibari/SKILL.md`, `claude/skills/tensho/SKILL.md`, `docs/PR-SECTIONS.md`, the section's template comment and `nen/workflow.json`'s `pr.$sections` annotation say when to pass it.

Plugin bumped to 0.137.0 (the two refusals are behaviour). (landed by [#432](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/432).)
- **The idle profile for batches, and the bounds an idle run needs (HR-5; D2, D3, D4, D5, D9).** `nen/workflow.json` gains `idle`: `decisions` (one resolution per `ask-once-per-run` row of `nen/decisions.json`, `default` where the row stars an option, `hold` otherwise; `harness-attribution-switch` and `host-nen-link` held because each stars an act on the maintainer's own harness or host, `cleanup-plan` held because an unattended remote branch deletion can close a stacked child, the five `spec-*` rows held (the starred `spec-capture-scope` and `spec-converge-filing` among them) because `dowsing-chain` is not an idle-run skill, `port-drift` held because its answer is a permanent canon record under the porting ruling, `publish-offer` refused because an idle run never reaches a release), `terminal` (`ready-at-prompt-or-integration`) and `budget` (`minutes`, the enforced bound; `tokens`, advisory and unread until zheref/nen#490 lands). `monitor` gains `localSlotFrees`, fixed at `merged` (HR-257, *Slots and stops*): the one key that frees a local slot at its merge, for every futon run, read by zheref/nen#498's `nen loop slots` (unreleased, not at the 0.24.0 pin; until the repin futon § 6 keeps the effort's `ready`/`prompted` flags false in its efforts file until the local merge); an `--idle` `start` fails on any other value. Each has a row in `contracts/config-catalogue.json` (domain `autonomy`, applied where the block is declared), written through `scripts/config_values.sh set`; `nen schema check` tolerates the block. `docs/WORKFLOW.md` gains the `idle` key table and § *Idle batches*.
- **A floor no file lifts, read from committed canon.** `start` reads the idle block from the committed `nen/workflow.json` of the remote default branch only (`origin/HEAD`, else `gh repo view`'s `defaultBranchRef`; SEC-15), never `HEAD` or a ref `HEAD`'s commit names, never the working tree; a default branch with no `nen/workflow.json` refuses `start` (exit 1, reason printed), and records that commit as `idle.source`. - **A typed `--idle` on `hatsu:futon` and `hatsu:kazaana`.** nen 0.24's `nen parse futon` reads the token as part of an exact label, so it is stripped before parsing. The run records every pre-answered row; a held or refused row, a G5, a *by hand* or *no verb* step, or the budget parks the effort as `parked:<row>` and the run moves on, with no picker shown. The run ends with every PR Ready at its merge prompt or merged into a non-main integration branch. It never merges into `main` and never reaches `getsuga` or `mugetsu`. futon § 12 and kazaana § 1 cite the WORKFLOW section.
- **The run manifest.** `scripts/futon_effort_record.mts` `start` writes contract `hatsu.futon.run/v0.2`. New `append` records `{issue, pr, base, kind, head}` for every PR the run opens, whatever its base, from the live PR. A re-append refreshes the PR's entry and never adds a second one. New `park` records a parked effort and `status` prints the manifest with elapsed minutes. On a v0.1 run `status` prints `manifest: absent`, omits `prs` and exits 2, and futon § 8 and kazaana § 5 treat that as the gate held. Both read the manifest instead of memory. A manifest lock older than ten minutes is a crashed writer's and is cleared. The `futon-effort-guard` fixture proves the manifest cases, the floor, the committed source, `overBudget`, a stale lock and `--idle` typed before `@`.
