The verbs are owed to nen (zheref/nen#476, zheref/nen#477). `emperor-time` drops its stale nen 0.22.0 residue (`nen idea file` takes `--private-names-ignore-file` at the 0.24.0 pin); build, futon and kazaana pay their bytes by deleting restated citations. (landed by [#402](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/402), through [#400](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/400).)
- **The Node dependency audit reads the repository's own lockfile and asks OSV, executing nothing ([#247](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/247), item 1).** `contracts/scans.json` (now `hatsu.scans/v0.2`) → `dependencyAudit.node` was a single `npm audit --audit-level=high` row. That command exits `ENOLOCK` on a pnpm tree, so every adopted Node consumer (each pins pnpm through `packageManager`) was reported *not scanned*. Running the matching package manager instead would execute the reviewed tree's `.pnpmfile.cjs`, `yarnPath` and `.corepack.env`, follow its `.npmrc` registry and send its tokens there. So the row works the way the swift row does: - `packageManager` selects the manager. The one lockfile present selects it only where the field is absent. - The lockfile is parsed strictly: pnpm at lockfileVersion 5, 6 and 9; yarn Berry and classic, by declared markers, split on column-0 headers, with npm aliases read as the real package; npm 1 to 3. A line the parser does not read, a truncated file or entry, or a registry-shaped entry that does not parse is *not scanned*, never skipped. - An entry is queried only when it has no resolved URL, or one on a declared `registryHosts` host. Git, `file:` and foreign tarballs are *unqueried*, and an unqueried entry is never clean. - Every registry `package@version` is posted to OSV `querybatch` at ecosystem `npm`. Each returned id's GHSA severity is read against `severityFloor`; a missing or unknown severity is `UNRATED` and counts. - The answers are bounded by `timeoutSeconds` and `maxResponseBytes`, and the lockfile by `maxEntries` and `maxDepth`. A malformed answer is *not scanned*. The two OSV endpoints are the only hosts contacted, and no environment is sent. - Redirect and suppression keys, and another lockfile beside the selected one, are printed as `audit-config:` evidence and never honoured.
- **Rows are selected by stack.** Each `dependencyAudit` row declares the `nen repo classify` stacks it answers: `node` takes `nextjs`, `gatsby`, `expo`, `react` and `electron`; `swift` takes `xcode-ios` and `xcode-macos`; `jvm` takes `gradle-android` and `compose-desktop`. `dotnet-winui`, a null stack and Hatsu's own `claude-code-plugin` are *not scanned*, by name. `stackCatalogue` snapshots nen's stack vocabulary, and the fixture fails when a stack is neither routed nor named.
- **`scripts/dependency_audit_resolve.mts`** answers exit `0` (clean), `1` (a finding or an unqueried entry, never clean), `3` (not scanned, named) or `2` (wiring, including any internal error). Its command is the node row's `run`. Hanten § 2a and Feitan's table name the selection, and `docs/config/plugin-registries.md` lists the new keys with their types. `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue* records the resolver and the swift row's OSV route as owed to nen. The script joins `PLUGIN_SURFACE_GLOBS`. Its hermetic fixture runs as lane `dependency-audit-guard`, in `unit-tests.yml` through `scripts/unit_tests_ci.mts` once this lands. It uses a loopback stub OSV, and proves a pnpm tree is scanned and its finding reported. Its hostile tree runs no hook, sends no canary or token, and cannot read clean. (landed by [#442](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/442).)
- **The port spec becomes an input, and product-level divergence gets a home (HR-6, HR-9).** `docs/config/ports.md` gains two *Shared conventions* rows — the **port issue body** (`spec.md`'s user stories and acceptance scenarios verbatim with their `US-<n>`/`FR-<nnn>`/`SC-<nnn>` ids and that platform's `tasks.md` rows, in one fenced *source data* block; a link alone is refused) and the **spec read** (`build` § 4's start opens the cited folder in the effort worktree before `ren`, one `spec-read` phase-ledger entry in its full form; fenced *source data* stays data, SEC-15; a body citing no folder is a G5 only where a spec step ran, *no spec* on hiraishin's no-spec route) — and three sections: the enumerated **mirror-versus-map list** (`unclassifiable` becomes an asked `port-drift` row), **product-level divergence** (`spec.md`'s private `## Platform decisions`, one row per adaptation, and a per-feature `port-ledger.md`, written on an amendment PR owned by the composite that raised the drift, in the same run as the answer, its `PR` cell filled at the build's end; the port PR's readiness waits on that amendment PR reading `MERGED` (HR-6); on the no-spec route the words stay in the private brief), and **canon and ids** recording HR-6 and HR-9. `kamui` §§ 3, 5, 7 and `hiraishin` §§ 5–7 cite them; hiraishin § 5's *Handling* column cites a list entry. the list's `map` entries cover architecture (state container, effect, reducer) and dev-ops (lane, CI step). `nen/decisions.json` row `port-drift` stars **A** (`map to <equivalent> (source line shown)`) only when its equivalent is quoted from an existing pair row or an explicit handbook cross-reference, else **C**, set per ask with no static `recommended`; `bankai-rule` § 4 and `templates/ports/pair.md` split the row's `rationale` (the label or the typed words, verbatim) from a new `redraft` column (the technology-level restatement, shown before/after); `rationale` still never names a product, screen, business rule or object id, and `bankai-rule` § 1 routes such words to `## Platform decisions` under a composite with a spec folder, else refuses them for a technology-level restatement. Every dieted skill pays its added bytes by deleting a restatement in the same file. (landed by [#402](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/402), through [#407](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/407).)
- **The product-term guard on public pair files (zheref/hatsu#354, wave 3 of the SDD assessment: porting closes the loop).** `scripts/product_term_check.mts`, TypeScript behind the shim `product_term_check.sh` (BC-11, HR-171; lane `product-term-guard`, run in `unit-tests.yml` through `scripts/unit_tests_ci.mts` once this lands), compares text against the maintainer's own product-term list at `${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/hatsu/product-terms` — a sibling of the redaction ignore file, kept on the maintainer's machine, never in a tree, written only by the maintainer — by token: camel case, `-`, `_`, `.`, `/` and a plain join read as one term, whole tokens never substrings, and a line that could hide a term also read normalised as `private_name_check.sh` reads it, plus combining marks and default-ignorable code points deleted (a confusable letter is the documented miss). An object reference — an object id `<CODE>-<IS|PR>-#<n>` or an invocation-grammar ref `<CODE>#<n>` — in a table row is a hit by shape with no list at all; a `|`-led line inside a fence counts as a row. No list is `missing-configuration` (exit `2`), never a pass; a hit prints an index, never the term, and a path or label naming a term inside a camel-case word is withheld whole. `hatsu:bankai-rule` § 4 runs it on the drafted row and on the rendered pair file and INDEX before any write (a hit in a merged row stops naming its PT id: a superseding row or a `handbook-question`, never an edit), beside the private-name guard, and a hit yields to § 1's product row — the words go to `spec.md § ## Platform decisions` under a composite with a spec folder, else the row is redrafted technology-level and said back; `--dry-run` joins its grammar (the line's last token only, the bare form included) and runs the reads and both guards in a scratch clone, writing nothing outside the session's scratch directory. `docs/config/ports.md` § *The product-term guard* states it once; `docs/PUBLIC-REDACTION.md` § *How it is guarded* and `docs/config/README.md` name the file. The fixture carries the hostile cases, green at bash 5.3 and `/bin/bash` 3.2.
- **One dated dry run per port skill in `docs/ab/`.** `docs/ab/bankai-rule.md` § 6, `docs/ab/kamui.md` and `docs/ab/hiraishin.md` record a `--dry-run` over `scripts/fixtures/port-family/` and `scripts/fixtures/spec-family/` with the exact commands and output, on nen `0.24.0`, 2026-10-22, the way `docs/ab/en.md` records its scenario; a stale dry run is a finding. No fixture slug reached a networked verb; what a live run would read from GitHub is named as not read. (landed by [#419](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/419).)
- **Readings never authorise a publish path (HR-3, HR-20).** `hatsu:aka` § 0's second carve-out, the typed futon or kazaana line's delegated publication, now cites the maintainer's ruling HR-20 first and drops *this run's reading*; `murasaki` § 6, `en` and `mukai` (the catch-up handoff), `futon` § 7, `kazaana` § 3 and its *Authority*, and `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` cite HR-20 beside aka § 0. `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Reading of 2026-10-19* gains one line: a push, merge or release path cites HR-20, the ruling, never the section's reading status. `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Authority every phase shares* states HR-3's rule: such a path cites a ruling-status row, and a path whose only authority is a reading asks once per run (row `missing-maintainer-choice`). `futon` pays its bytes by citing aka § 0 instead of restating its Covered row; `kazaana` by dropping a *Not permitted* entry its *Hard limits* already hold. (landed by [#402](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/402), through [#399](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/399).)
- **Every picker answer is recorded; a ROSTER row is rendered from its record (zheref/hatsu#443, SDD plan wave 1 *ruling capture*, Hatsu half).** `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 4 *Every picker answer is recorded* states once how an ask-once or human-gate picker's answer is written through nen 0.26.0's `nen stop answer --record` before the run acts: the effort's branch as the key, the question and every option with its consequence as shown, `--pick` or the typed words verbatim. A free-text answer stays the maintainer's word (HR-173, kept by the maintainer on 2026-10-23, record `R-2026-10-23-08`); only a reply that does not answer is asked once more, and rows typed by design (`missing-maintainer-choice`, `missing-argument`, `missing-configuration`, `port-drift`) never are, `missing-maintainer-choice` recorded with one reference option. An idle pre-answer is recorded with its source and renders `pre-answered`, never a pick; `direct-mismatch`'s record stays `nen direct answer`. `jutaisho` § 4 cites the paragraph, and the skills that show a picker (build, futon, kazaana, file, emperor-time, dowsing-chain, kamui, hiraishin, getsuga, cleanup, third-hand, en, mukai, mugetsu, bankai-rule, backlog-loop, and the nine `missing-maintainer-choice` askers) record at their own pickers. New `scripts/ruling_record.mts` (`export` through `nen stop answer show` into `docs/ruling-records/<effort>--<R-id>.json` after the private-name guard and a secret-shape pass, `render`, `check`, `preanswer`) with its lane `ruling-record`, run in `unit-tests.yml`. The review preamble § 3 and `hanten` § 6 accept an `R-…` record as a citation only before its row lands. `docs/ROSTER.md` § *The ruling template* renders a recorded ruling's row instead of retyping it. Proof: `docs/ab/jutaisho.md` § *Dated verification, 2026-10-23*.
- **The redaction tree check reads a `.json` file with its string escapes decoded** (`scripts/private_name_check.sh`), so a private name a serializer glued to `\n` or spelt in `\uXXXX` is still a hit; three fixture cases. (landed by [#467](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/467).)
- **`scripts/ruling_record.mts` renders a `<repository> #N` in a record's prose coded (PL-05).** Seven ROSTER rows rendered from ruling records (HR-348..HR-352, HR-355, HR-356) carried `bankai-handbooks #20`, `bankai-handbooks #11` and `akatsuki-ai #69` in their question cell, a bare `#N` the canon prose lint refuses and the baseline may not absorb (HR-13). The record keeps the question as the maintainer was shown it; `render` and `check` now code such a reference `<owner>/<name>#N` when `nen repo resolve` names exactly one repository for the word before it, in the question, option labels, consequences and the picked label, never in the maintainer's typed words. An unknown word and `all` are left as written; a resolve reply off its documented shape is wiring (exit `2`); `render` passes each coded slug through the private-name guard and refuses a private one at exit `1` without naming it. WORKFLOW § 4 now asks for references written coded at ask time, the render coding being the safety net; ROSTER § *The ruling template* step 3 and `docs/ruling-records/README.md` name it as a row's second departure from as asked and `nen/repos.json` as a second render input. Installed copies get the change by updating to `0.141.2`. (landed by [#467](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/467).)
- **The steady-state schedule (part of zheref/hatsu#448; ruling HR-354, record R-2026-10-23-21, effort HA-IS-#450, quoted in the contract's `ruling`).** `contracts/schedule.json` (`hatsu.schedule/v0.1`) holds the four rows on the picked scheduler, *Your own machine*, read as Claude Desktop's local scheduled tasks (a reading, marked): `drift-pass` (quarterly, `great-hiker evolve` over every surface, dry first, nothing filed twice on the same day through `nen issue search`), `recapture` (weekly `dowsing-chain recapture <family> --dry-run`; the tag trigger is owed), `morning-desk` (weekdays, `rikugan for <repo> as register`, the last idle run first) and `ratchet-shrink` (CI, `canon-lint` on every pull request). Page: `docs/config/schedule.md`. Each task carries its own `alwaysAllow` reads and the contract's `permissions.neverAlwaysAllow` writes (SEC-9); `drift-pass` and `morning-desk` run in a Desktop worktree and refuse unless clean at `origin/main`. No row names a release step (HR-5, the floor).
- **`scripts/schedule_install.mts` prints the tasks and registers nothing.** Per task, the fields of Desktop's *New scheduled task* form and the `SKILL.md` Desktop keeps at `~/.claude/scheduled-tasks/<name>/SKILL.md`; exit `0` every placeholder filled, `1` one unfilled (named), `2` wiring, including an undeclared `<token>`; placeholders are read from the contract; `--table` renders the page's row table, which the fixture holds equal to `docs/config/schedule.md`'s. `--family` reaches stdout only. Its hermetic fixture is the `schedule-printer` lane, run in `unit-tests.yml` through `scripts/unit_tests_ci.mts`. Dry run: `docs/ab/schedule-install.md`.
- **`canon-lint` counts the shrink.** Against `--base` or `--base-baseline`, `scripts/prose_ambiguity_check.mts` prints `<n> removed over <base>` in its summary line and `removedBaseline` in `--json` (null with no base), summed per key.
- `great-hiker` § Residue 1 and `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § *Idle batches* name the schedule page. (landed by [#467](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/467).)
- **The SDD plan delivery lands at `v0.133.0`, a MINOR above `main`'s `v0.132.0`** (WORKFLOW § *Stacked pull requests*, rule 3: minor for behaviour). The delivery carries behaviour: the `dowsing-chain` skill, the canon prose lint and clause-guard lanes, and the idle profile. The skill count recounts from disk: fifty-eight skills, fifty-nine mirrored directories with `ten`.
- **Cross-plane alignments from the bankai-handbooks and nen delivery rounds.** `docs/config/spec-repo.md` retires the code-as-tie-breaker line (UZF-21(b), HR-6), gives `spec-clarify` one meaning of round (SP-11 as amended on zheref/bankai-handbooks#12), and says an open question is a marker, never an `OQ-{n}` row, with `AC-{n}` mapped onto the numbered acceptance scenario under a `US-n`. `docs/config/ports.md` names the Platform decisions key the criterion id, never an AC id, and defers the UI map entries to UX-13's adapt list. `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § *Idle batches* says the `then` gate reads Hatsu's run record while the two run manifests stay two files. (landed by [#402](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/402).)
- **`docs/history/nen-repins.md`** — one row per nen pin (version, minimum, nen floor, date, commit, PR) with each repin's adopted verbs and absorbed breaking notes, moved from `nen/contract.json` → `dependency.minimum_semantics`, which now reads one sentence and a pointer.
- **`scripts/plugin_version_refs.sh --check-nen-pin`** — exits `1` when `docs/NEN-CONTRACT.md`'s echoed pin line disagrees with the contract's `dependency.minimum` or `pinned_ref`, `2` when either side is missing, malformed, duplicated, unparseable or resolves outside the root; the `plugin-version-refs` fixture covers each of those and ends on the committed tree's own echo.
- **`hatsu:great-hiker` § 2** routes prose to `models.<surface>.deep` (Cursor's, which is its frontier alias, falls to `fast`), its path table is total with an explicit fall-through row, no longer a Fable subsession its own Hard limits and `models.rule` forbid.
- **`docs/NEN-CONTRACT.md` § *The range*** echoes `nen >= 0.24` installing `v0.24.0`, names the v0.24.0 adoption and floor, and drops a stale skill count.
- **Hand-typed counts** dropped from `README.md`, `claude/skills/README.md` and `docs/surfaces/codex.md`; **`hatsu:futon`'s residue** no longer restates the pin. (landed by [#402](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/402), through [#403](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/403).)
- **One machine-readable precedence order ([HR-1](../docs/ROSTER.md#hr-1)).** `nen/contract.json` gains a top-level `precedence` array — `skill.hardLimit` › `roster.ruling` › `nen/decisions.json` › `docs/WORKFLOW.md` › `docs/PROCESS.md` › `akatsuki.CON` › `handbooks` — ranking canon prose and rulings (a configuration file's value still beats any prose copy of it), with a `$precedence` comment naming each entry and an explicit fall-through. `nen schema check` at the pin tolerates the key and does not validate it: validation and the drift refusal are owed to [zheref/nen#474](https://github.com/zheref/nen/issues/474), and until that repin nothing checks the order. `docs/WORKFLOW.md` restates the order once; `docs/PROCESS.md` points at it. `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Sources* retires the frozen reference implementation's fallback; until zheref/akatsuki-ai is pinned, `CON-{n}` resolves through zheref/bankai-handbooks' `CONSTITUTION.md` at its pin (this run's reading, OPEN-5), and the fall-through and the unlisted texts' ranks are OPEN-6. (landed by [#402](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/402), through [#404](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/404).)
- **`hatsu:shibari` § 4 hands a closing-free stacked sub-PR to `en` (HR-357).** The `pr converge` bullet said never to hand to `en` on exit `1`, while `hatsu:en` § 4 reads converge's exit `1` with `closingSource` `none-on-non-default-base` (a non-default-base PR that closes no issue) as not applicable under HR-357, so a stacked sub-PR closing nothing stalled in shibari. Shibari now runs `nen pr converge --json` and hands over only when that no-closing line is the only blocking line, as `en` § 4 reads it; any other exit `1` is still fixed first (verify table, `edit-body`, re-run; HR-11). Converge's re-runs point at `en` §§ 4–5 and the Development guard's at `en` §§ 4–5 and `sharingan` § 4. The Delivery provenance sentences now cite WORKFLOW § *The effort's ledgers* instead of restating it, to keep the skill under its ceiling. Installed copies get the change by updating to `0.141.1`. (landed by [#467](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/467).)
- **The `canon-lint` check is installed (HR-13, step two).** `.github/workflows/prose-ambiguity-check.yml` is the staged template moved into place with only its header comment rewritten; its name moved from `PENDING_HOSTED_WORKFLOWS` to `PORTABLE_HOSTED_WORKFLOWS` in `scripts/workflow_runner_policy_check.rb`, so its absence is now a refusal, and the self-test judges the installed copy, refuses its absence and refuses nine hostile edits of it. The check stays advisory for the merge: it sits in no ruleset and becomes required only after one tagged release of green runs with a shrinking baseline, by the maintainer's own ruleset edit, while a red run already reads the pull request not-ready (`nen pr ready`'s `CON-32(a)` row counts every reported check) (`docs/PROCESS.md` § *Named residue, by skill*, `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` § *The new-workflow rule*). A `pull_request_target` workflow runs from the base branch's copy, so its first live run is the first pull request opened after this one merged.
- **The nen pin echo is checked on every pull request.** `scripts/unit_tests_ci.mts` selects the `plugin-version-refs-guard` lane whenever `scripts/plugin_version_refs.ts` exists beside its fixture, so `unit-tests.yml` runs `--check-nen-pin` over the pull request's committed tree: a repin that leaves `docs/NEN-CONTRACT.md`'s echoed pin line stale fails the check (the lane and its fixture are the pull request's own, so it catches a careless repin, not one that also rewrites them; the trusted workflows read their pin from `.trusted`, and a trusted frozen step stays the later route). No workflow file changed, so no two-step landing; the runner is the base revision's copy, so the lane runs from the first pull request after this one merged. `docs/NEN-CONTRACT.md` and the lane's own comment no longer say that no CI step runs it.
- **The pre-PR preflight can run the canon lint.** `scripts/trusted_guard_preflight.rb`'s adapter passed the trusted checkout's own `--rules` and `--base-baseline` paths, which sit outside `--root` and which the lint refuses at exit 2; it now copies the two trusted contracts under the root's git-ignored `.nen/` for the run and removes them with it. The gap was latent while the workflow was staged (nothing on `main` derived the lint) and would have reddened the first preflight after this install. Behind it a second one: the preflight's temporary directory is the symlinked spelling `mktmpdir` hands back (`/var` → `/private/var` on macOS) and the lint's entry guard compared that unresolved path with its resolved module URL, so the trusted lint ran nothing and exited 0, a false pass. The preflight now resolves its temporary directory's real path and `scripts/prose_ambiguity_check.mts`'s entry guard compares real paths; its fixture invokes the lint through a symlinked directory and requires the verdict, and the preflight's fixture, which accepted any exit for the lint, now requires exit 0 with the verdict line on the green case, exit 1 with the planted finding and its remedy named on a planted finding, exit 2 on a base without the lint's rules, and a refusal of a trusted lint that exits 0 printing nothing, with no copy left behind on any of them. A red that is the lint's own finding names its remedy (fix the prose or remove the stale row) instead of the two-step landing, which stays the remedy for a guard-shape red.
- **One `PL-01` row leaves the canon prose baseline.** `docs/NEN-CONTRACT.md`'s *by hand at each repin* admission is gone with the CI step that replaces it; `contracts/prose.lint.baseline.json` shrinks by that row. (landed by [#420](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/420).)

## v0.130.0 — Port rulings numbered PT-{n} from one global counter, and the SDD assessment's rulings recorded as HR-1 … HR-34

Two pull requests landed on `main` since `v0.129.0` (HA-PR-#391 and #392, 2026-10-21 and 2026-10-22). Their two `changelog.d/` fragments are folded here in the collation order (by fragment name), each citing the pull request that landed it.

**Net effect at `v0.130.0`:** `docs/ROSTER.md` carries the HR id space — HR-1 … HR-33 from the SDD assessment sitting and its follow-ups, and HR-34, the port rule prefix — and `hatsu:bankai-rule` reserves port ruling ids as `PT-{n}` from one counter across every pair file, the reservation failing closed on every reviewed hostile input and costing no more than the one-file read it replaced. The plugin reads `0.130.0` on nen `0.24.0`.

- **#355 Port rulings are `PT-{n}` from one global counter** ([#355](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/355)). The maintainer ruled on 2026-10-21, recorded in `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-21 — the port rule prefix (HA#355)*: the ports rule prefix is `PT`, and one counter runs across every pair file of the rulings directory. The answer is HR-34, re-confirming HR-9. `templates/ports/pair.md` carries `prefix: PT · numbering: global`. Citations keep the ruled form `<from>--<to> PT-<n> (branch <ref>, unmerged)`. (landed by [#392](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/392), [#355](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/355).)
- `scripts/port_rule_reserve.sh` reads every `<rulingsDir>/*.md` (the `--pair-file`'s own directory) at the tip, at the run's HEAD and working tree, on every sibling `ports-*` branch, and in every open same-repository PR touching one. The next id is one past the highest anywhere. `--check` refuses one id in two pair files, including a tip row in a different file.
- **Exit 2:** a `--pair-file` with an empty, `.` or `..` segment, a leading `./` or a trailing `/`; a `--prefix` other than `PT`; a `--target` whose name is not a segment (`acme/.`, `acme/..`).
- **Exit 3:** any header line (`<!--` naming `prefix:` or `numbering:`, keys in any case, at any indent, in a file or in an open PR's added lines) that is not exactly one `prefix: PT · numbering: global`, with `global` followed by whitespace, `)`, `·` or `-->`; a headerless file is counted. A pair file named out of shape, its path printed. A first cell carrying another prefix (`SW-3`), or `PT-<digits>` that is not a row. Every GFM row form is counted: a pipe-less row, up to three leading spaces, `**`/`*`/backtick emphasis. A row in a fenced example is counted too, a safe over-count.
- **Exit 1, nothing reserved:** a failed git listing, blob or working-tree read.
- **Never read, or not others:** a fork PR is never read and is listed by number only. The run's own push and own PR are not "others" to `--check` when, and only when, they are ancestors of HEAD (the PR by its `headRefOid`); a same-named branch or PR of another run is counted. `--check` also flags one id in two rows of one pair file. A local head tracking `refs/pull/*`, or a remote whose URL is not origin's, is skipped and reported by count, never by name; a remote unset or `.` is read. `--json` escapes every control character.
- Every earlier refusal keeps its exit code, except the fork-PR reads, which are never made (exit 1 → 0). The `port-rule-guard` fixture proves each case (77 checks, at bash 5.3 and at `/bin/bash` 3.2).
- `hatsu:bankai-rule` §§ 2–3 state the rulings-path grammar and the prefix and header refusals. § 3 drops the G5 stop that held the global case. `hatsu:kamui` and `hatsu:bankai-handbooks` lose stale "not landed" and "ruled on its adoption PR" wording.
- The reservation stays linear in rows and costs no more than the one-file read it replaced: one listing per place, one blob batch and one judging pass for every place, whatever the number of pair files.
- The open-PR reads stay residue until zheref/nen#456.
- Plugin bumped to 0.130.0.
- **The SDD assessment's rulings are recorded as HR-1 … HR-33, and the HR id space starts.** `docs/ROSTER.md` gains § *Rulings of 2026-10-20 — the SDD assessment sitting*: the maintainer's sixteen picker answers to Part 9 of the SDD assessment report, each question, the options shown and the pick recorded as the label the picker returned (private repository names placeheld), under stable anchors **HR-1** … **HR-16**, every row `ruled` at the sitting. A second section, § *Rulings of 2026-10-21 — the SDD sitting's follow-up questions*, records **HR-17** … **HR-33**: the six questions the sitting left open (`HR` is *Hatsu Ruling*; OPEN-4's contract and cap; the 2026-10-19 delegated-publication reading ratified as written; `intake.challenger` as a map; Hidan kept per consumer), then the intake cast they led to — in the maintainer's own words a dedicated cast that only challenges at capture intake, isolated from code review (HR-23), the Hunter Exam examiners Satotz, Lippo, Buhara and Menchi (HR-24), every matching examiner, Satotz for anything unmapped and for challenging the Design Direction Menchi drafts, shared machinery, and challenge rounds never counted but run until the brief is fulfilled, its criteria ideally testable, stalled examiners dropping out until none remains and the maintainer is asked, a decline filing the open questions (HR-25 … HR-33). HR-14's challengers, HR-19's cap, HR-21's values and HR-28's ledger are marked reversed or superseded, their wording unchanged; *How HR ids are read* gains the partial and ratified-by cases. **OPEN-4** is closed, and § *Sources* notes that HR-1 retires its upstream fallback. Rulings only: every change the rows settle is owed to its own PR, the intake cast's definitions, ROSTER section, `intake.challenger` map and `emperor-time` changes among them. Plugin bumped to 0.129.1 (overtaken by #392's 0.130.0; `main` reads `0.130.0` at this cut). (landed by [#391](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/391).)

## v0.129.0 — Gemini 3.1 Pro as the hallucination-shaped fallback, read-only GitHub API access restored, the canon guard names no stale version, and Shizuku's second cleanup pass runs on every surface

Four pull requests landed on `main` since `v0.128.4` (HA-PR-#383, #384, #387 and #388, 2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21). Their four `changelog.d/` fragments are folded here in the collation order (by fragment name), each citing the pull request that landed it.

**Net effect at `v0.129.0`:** `hatsu:direct` routes Gemini 3.1 Pro (`VISUAL_ORCHESTRATOR`) as the fallback on the 21 hallucination-shaped Opus reading cells, with the standing terminal-cell rule and the maintainer's four rulings of 2026-10-20 recorded in `docs/ROSTER.md`; Claude Code's permission pack grants an audited read-only GitHub API reader and drops the retired `gh api *` rule; the canon contracts guard names no obsolete nen version; and `hatsu:cleanup` runs Shizuku's second pass through the main session's own picker on every surface. The plugin reads `0.129.0` on nen `0.24.0`.

- **Defined Shizuku cleanup pass 2 consent channel, fixed local branch deletion against fetched base, and anchored cleanup markers** ([#319](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/319)). `cleanup` previously failed to run pass 2 on Claude Code because Shizuku declares no question tool (`tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash`) and subagents cannot receive user turns, while her canon correctly refuses a relayed prompt string as maintainer consent. Under candidate (b), uniform across Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, and Antigravity, Shizuku inventories on pass 1 and writes an initial marker (`plan[]`, empty `picked[]`, `done[]`, `result: "pending"`), the main session presents the picker through the surface's native question tool (`AskUserQuestion`, `request_user_input`, `AskQuestion`, `ask_question`) and records `picked[]` and raw `pickerAnswer` in the marker, the main session executes the picked rows from `plan[]` in pass 2, and Shizuku returns to verify read-backs against the marker, update freed bytes, and close the marker. Local branch deletion in `cleanup` § 5 now runs the ancestry check against fetched `origin/<branch.base>` before non-force deletion (`git merge-base --is-ancestor -- '<branch>' 'origin/<branch.base>' && git branch -d -- '<branch>'`), and explicitly classifies a branch that passes ancestry but that `git branch -d` refuses against a stale local trunk (deterministic once the core trunk is fast-forwarded, or proposed with that reason), never retrying with `-D`. The cleanup marker path is explicitly specified as `<core>/.nen/cleanup/<slug>.json` (with session worktree fallback where isolation blocks core writes), `<slug>` is defined for all scopes, markers are read from both locations, cache removal prints literal absolute paths, and detached release worktrees are classified explicitly via commit reachability from `origin/<branch.base>`. Plugin bumped to 0.120.0 and mirrors restamped (the bump that fragment names was overtaken by later merges; `main` reads `0.129.0` at this cut). (landed by [#387](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/387), [#319](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/319).)
- **#317 Canon guard output** ([#317](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/317)). The canon contracts guard now describes Nen's current invariants without repeating an obsolete version in its header or pass line. (landed by [#384](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/384), [#317](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/317).)
- **#326 Read-only GitHub API access** ([#326](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/326)). Claude Code's permission pack now grants a named TypeScript reader for audited REST GETs and a fixed GraphQL query. The reader rejects caller-supplied methods, endpoints, bodies, and mutation text before invoking `gh`. Installing the pack removes the retired `gh api *` allow rule from older local settings while preserving unrelated entries. Local trusted-guard preflight also runs the newly required private-name fixture. (landed by [#383](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/383), [#326](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/326).)
- **#342 Gemini 3.1 Pro is the fallback on the hallucination-shaped reading cells** ([#342](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/342)). Under the maintainer's four rulings of 2026-10-20, recorded in `docs/ROSTER.md`, `contracts/direct.registry.json` moves the fallback of 21 Opus-primary cells from GPT-6.1 Sol on Codex to Gemini 3.1 Pro on Antigravity (`VISUAL_ORCHESTRATOR`): requirements, analysis, triage, deprecation, prose-authoring, evals, drift-review and parity. The other 19 reading cells and the 24 execution-adjacent Opus cells keep Sol, and Opus stays primary on the four discovery/backlog cells Pro already backed. `VISUAL_ORCHESTRATOR`'s selection text states the measured profile with its source and date, and the standing terminal-cell rule: never an execution-side primary or fallback, never the fallback on an execution-adjacent Opus cell. Four older cells where Pro already backs Sol are named there as open for the maintainer (ruled grandfathered at the merge prompt of 2026-10-20). `VISUAL_FALLBACK` (Flash) is kept for a later in-pool role. `docs/DIRECT-MATRIX.md` is regenerated; a consumer's directed issues change their fallback only on their next `hatsu:direct` sweep. Plugin bumped to 0.129.0. (landed by [#388](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/388), [#342](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/342).)

## v0.128.4 — the redaction fixture runs in CI, `file` stages drafts safely, PR bodies resolve from a section catalogue with a Delivery preamble, the porting trio lands, readiness reads Nen's JSON and unit tests run on pull requests

Seventeen pull requests landed on `main` since `v0.124.0` (HA-PR-#327 … #378, 2026-10-20), #363 — the `v0.124.0` release proposal itself, whose merge commit landed after that tag was cut at its reviewed head — among them. Their fourteen `changelog.d/` fragments are folded here in the collation order (by fragment name), each entry cited with the pull request that landed it; #363, #368 and #376 carried no fragment of their own and are cited where the work they landed is described. Every intermediate version from `0.125.0` to `0.128.3` was never tagged and ships inside `v0.128.4`. The section is folded by hand: `nen changelog collate` anchors only on a `### Unreleased` header, and with this file's `##` release headings it refuses (zheref/nen#446).

**Net effect at `v0.128.4`:** the plugin reads `0.128.4` on nen `0.24.0` (`nen/contract.json` repinned by #374). `surface-mirror-check` runs the hermetic redaction-guard fixture on every same-repository pull request as a frozen step the trusted validator requires, landed in two steps. `hatsu:file` writes every draft with the surface's own file-writing tool and single-quotes the title. PR bodies resolve from one section catalogue by repository kind and work family, and open with a ledger-rendered Delivery preamble. `hatsu:bankai-rule`, `hatsu:kamui` and `hatsu:hiraishin` carry a delivered feature to sibling platform repositories through a spec repository and recorded port rulings. The readiness check reads `nen pr ready --json` through a trusted, head-pinned reader, and a read-only `unit-tests` workflow runs the focused machinery fixtures. `classify.publish` joins `direct.publish`, both at `label+comment` here, with the criteria and direct comments specified in data and no marker literal in the mirrors.

- **`classify.publish`** (`label` | `label+comment`, catalogue id `workflow.classify.publish`): under `label+comment`, `hatsu:classify` § 5b posts one marker-keyed `<!-- hatsu:classify -->` comment per labelled issue carrying the applied `lang/`/`job/` keys with confidence and cited signals, the low keys and any undecidable axis, idempotent on the poster's own marker, never under `ren` or for an inline effort. `file` and `build` name it beside `direct.publish`. Hatsu's own value is `label+comment` for both keys, on the maintainer's request of 2026-10-20 (ROSTER § *Rulings of 2026-10-20 — classify and direct always write back*); the family and consumer repositories set both through their own PRs. Plugin bumped to 0.127.0. (landed by [#369](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/369).)
- **The classify criteria comment is specified in data and carries no issue text** (hanten round 1 on #369, landed after #369 merged at its pre-review head). The whole contract moves to `contracts/classify.taxonomy.json` → `publish` — `classify.publish` read at the default branch's tip (a PR cannot opt itself in), one comment per *classified* issue with unapplied keys marked *not applied* and their reason, signals posted as category ids (`signalIds`) and never as issue text, an injection-suspect row posting keys only, idempotence on the poster's own trailer, a `mktemp` body file outside the checkout, the private-names ignore file, failure never a stop, the sweep picker naming the comments, keys decided against the taxonomy alone (the target's `nen/labels.json` bounds what is applied), a trailer carrying `applied=` and a `-` form so a status change re-posts and an undecidable issue's comment parses, a paginated marker read keeping id, author and last line only, every body line checked against the closed grammar before posting, and a private-name refusal stopping the remaining posts — and classify § 5b points at it, so no marker literal reaches the mirror generator's prefix rewrite (which turned it into `$classify` / `/classify` on Codex, Cursor and Antigravity). The ROSTER rows are marked as this run's reading, row 3 agrees with the skill, the rollout names the sibling PRs and bankai-handbooks as owed; WORKFLOW § 2 gains the `classify` block; `build`, `emperor-time`, `file`, DISCOVERY, PROCESS, the READMEs and the config docs name the key. Plugin bumped to 0.127.2. (landed by [#372](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/372).)
- **`hatsu:direct` § 7 no longer quotes its comment marker as a literal.** The mirror generator rewrites the `hatsu:` invocation prefix everywhere, so the Codex, Cursor and Antigravity mirrors told those sessions to look for `$direct` / `/direct` markers that nobody posts, which could lead to a duplicate public comment. § 7 now points at `contracts/direct.registry.json` → `publish.comment` (`marker`, `idempotent`, `shape`, `versionPattern`, `bodyFile`, `verb`), the same fix zheref/hatsu#372 made for classify, and the mirrors carry no marker literal. A sweep of `claude/` (hanten round 1 on this PR) found three more spans the rewrite bent: `hatsu:netero` in `third-hand`'s Claude Code raise row and `hatsu:shalnark` in `black-voice` now name the plugin's agent, and `mugetsu` § 0's slash-prefixed `/hatsu:mugetsu` (mirrored as `/$mugetsu` and `//mugetsu`) drops the slash; every other `hatsu:` code span is an invocation and is meant to be rewritten. The generator-side fix is zheref/nen#465. Plugin bumped to 0.127.3. (landed by [#373](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/373).)
- Publish the readiness check from Nen's structured JSON verdict in Hatsu and Tenkai-rendered workflows, with the reader installed from the trusted base checkout. (landed by [#328](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/328), [#131](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/131).)
- #131: Admit a trusted, head-pinned TypeScript reader for `nen pr ready --json` beside the current frozen readiness step. The workflow and Tenkai template move to the JSON form in the follow-up installation PR. (landed by [#327](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/327), [#131](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/131).)
- Add the ledger-rendered Delivery metadata preamble to PR bodies, with observed surfaces, model tokens and registry aliases, effort dials and session identity; retain explicit unread values for unavailable facts. Require the Delivery guard before every body write while keeping commit trailers and the final Agent attribution ledger persona-only. Adopt published Nen v0.24.0 for its phase, usage and session ledger APIs. (HA-IS-#321) (landed by [#374](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/374).)
- Run the redaction-guard fixture as a frozen `Redaction guard fixture` step of `surface-mirror-check.yml`, on the trusted checkout with no secret, no private list and no token, so a guard regression goes red in CI; the workflow-policy validator now requires the step, completing the two-step landing. (HA-IS-#337) (landed by [#377](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/377), step one [#376](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/376).)
- **`hatsu:file` § 4 no longer stages the title through a quoted heredoc.** It told the filer to write `title.txt` with `cat > title.txt <<'EOF'`, the construction `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Escaping the maintainer's words* forbids, since a quoted heredoc's delimiter is in-band. § 4 now says to write every draft (title, body, comment) with the surface's own file-writing tool and points at that PROCESS section rather than restating it (zheref/hatsu#345). A title-file flag is still owed to nen (zheref/nen#444); until it lands the title is typed single-quoted, as § 4 and § 5 now both show. Plugin bumped to 0.128.3. (landed by [#375](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/375).)
- PR bodies resolve from one section catalogue by repository kind and work family, with a seven-reader cap, glyph headings, kind-specific verification tables and a warning above 150 prose words per section ([#351](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/351)). Templates now live per section; footer blocks remain outside the cap and Delivery remains reserved for #321.
- Configuration fill writes every default matrix cell and its section-purpose annotation while preserving consumer edits. Interim resolver and body-section guards have a hermetic focused lane; source skills and all surface mirrors use the same catalogue. Release rows retain the written matrix as the maintainer clarified on 2026-10-20.
- The mirror fixture now verifies that CI calls the shared regeneration script and refuses a copied hook-root table. Its prior assertion expected the retired YAML copy and failed on unchanged main; per-surface generation parity remains covered by the regeneration fixture.
- New interim branching logic and fixtures are TypeScript (`BC-11`); existing configuration Python is moved unchanged behind a catalogue adapter, and the old composition Python only loses its migrated section checks. Adversarial review also covers literal/escaped comment openers, thematic breaks, dangling declarations, terminal-safe diagnostics and the three-dot PR path range. (landed by [#364](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/364).)
- **`hatsu:bankai-rule`** ([#355](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/355), child 1 of the porting epic [#354](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/354)). A port ruling — what a source stack's rule or concept becomes on the target: `mirror` · `map` · `drop with note` · `target-only addition` — recorded once as a stable-id row in a directed pair file of `zheref/bankai-handbooks`' `handbooks/ports/` family (rendered from `templates/ports/`), the id reserved against the tip, the run's own branch, every pushed or local sibling rulings branch and every open same-repository PR, with a `--check` duplicate pass at every catch-up (`scripts/port_rule_reserve.sh`, the `port-rule-guard` lane's fixture with a `gh` stub), one rulings PR per port run through `aka → mukai → en` to the G4 prompt. The pair is parsed with scenario slots enumerated from `handbooks/stack-matrix.md` and the ruling split off before the parse (the probe, refusal included, in `docs/ab/bankai-rule.md`). Shared pieces the later children use: `file` § 4's one clause (a composite's typed call or a ruled picker answer stands as the confirmation), the `port-drift` decision row (one batched picker call per run), the catalogue rows `workflow.port.handbooksRepo` and `workflow.port.rulingsDir` under the new `port` domain, `bankai-handbooks` § 2 reading a pair file through `--always-load` until nen resolves it (`limbo` mirrors none at 0.22.0). Owed to nen and filed: [zheref/nen#456](https://github.com/zheref/nen/issues/456) (an open-PR id-reservation read), [zheref/nen#457](https://github.com/zheref/nen/issues/457) (pair resolution and mirroring — no mirror carries a pair file at 0.22.0, so the limbo half of criterion 5 is owed there) and [zheref/nen#458](https://github.com/zheref/nen/issues/458) (a first-anchored trailing clause for the ruling split). The rule prefix stays unnamed (`<prefix>` in the template) until [zheref/bankai-handbooks#4](https://github.com/zheref/bankai-handbooks/pull/4) rules it. README counts corrected to fifty-four skills; plugin bumped to 0.125.0 (both manifests); the mirrors regenerated. Incidental: `nen/contract.json`'s duplicate `toolchain.node` key is folded into one whose reason names its three consumers (the effective value is unchanged). (landed by [#367](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/367).)
- **`hatsu:kamui` — one feature written once as the product's spec, then ported to the siblings you pick** (plugin `0.125.0`; zheref/hatsu#356, child 2 of the cross-platform porting epic zheref/hatsu#354). `hatsu:kamui <CODE>-IS-#<N> | <CODE>-PR-#<N> [--dry-run]` writes the source issue (or a merged PR and its closing issues) as a `github/spec-kit` set in the product family's **spec repository**. The set is pinned at `v1.2.2`, read live on 2026-10-20: `specs/<nnn>-<slug>/` with `spec.md`, `plan.md`, `research.md`, `data-model.md`, `quickstart.md`, `contracts/`, `tasks.md` and `checklists/requirements.md`, plus `.specify/memory/constitution.md` once, which cites `CON-{n}` and never overrides it. The spec repository is searched first for the source and its linked objects, each matched as a fixed string on word boundaries, so a later port of the same feature amends its folder. The spec PR runs `ren → aka → mukai → en` to the **G2** merge prompt, and nothing is built before it merges: the wait is `nen watch until` on the PR's state; `CLOSED` ends the run *spec declined*, and an observation-error streak is a G5. Then the sibling picker asks which configured siblings get a port issue (new `nen/decisions.json` row `port-siblings`). It is never starred, pages in fours, and asks even for a lone sibling. Its answer is the G1 for one port issue per pick through `file` §§ 3–5, written by `nen issue file --assignee` and read back explicitly. Next, a start picker runs the picks as `hatsu:build` one after another. Kamui holds build's register and wrap-up, and a build's G5 stops the chain. Missing setup is asked per item, in order; creating the spec repository is a G5 hand-over line, given only when gh says the repository does not resolve. A member whose visibility reads public never receives the family.
- **`docs/config/ports.md`**: the checkouts file (tab-separated, each path's `origin` verified before use) and the run record `${XDG_STATE_HOME:-$HOME/.local/state}/hatsu/ports/<run-id>.json` (`schema` `hatsu.port.run/v0.1`). `hatsu:kamui resume <run-id>` validates the id's shape before building any path, and recomputes the step from live state, so a record can never skip the merge wait. `hiraishin` (#357) and `bankai-rule` (#355) cite this page.
- **`families[]` and the `spec` role, documented on the Hatsu side**: `docs/config/repos.json.md` § `families[]`, with three redacted fixtures under `scripts/fixtures/port-family/` (the spec repository's registry, a member's, a registry with no family). All three exit `0` through `nen schema check` at nen `0.22.0`, with `families[]` tolerated but not validated until zheref/nen#453, and an absent `gates.json` warning. The spec repository lists itself under its own `consumers[]`, so it reads **G2**. A family is declared only in its members' and spec repository's own registries, never in Hatsu's. Owed to nen: zheref/nen#453 (the key and the role), zheref/nen#454 (`watch until --until merged`), and zheref/nen#444, amended with `issue file --title-file`. Cited: the prose pack, zheref/nen#450. The slug-shaped `fixture-*` placeholders join `docs/PUBLIC-REDACTION.md`'s legend.
- **A ninth catalogue domain, `port`**, with `workflow.port.specKit.pin` (`v1.2.2`) and `workflow.port.specKit.layout` (`specs/{nnn}-{slug}`), both gated `when: nen/workflow.json#port` so `fill` never writes them into an unrelated repository; documented in WORKFLOW § 2 → `port` and `docs/config/workflow.json.md`.
- **`docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-19/20 — cross-platform porting***: the epic's 21 intake rulings, recorded verbatim.
- `jutaisho` § 4 names every ask that stars none (`publish-offer`, `missing-maintainer-choice`, `port-siblings`) and `kamui`'s start picker, which stars only the item on this surface. It adds the paging rule (pages of four, plus *none of them* under three) and stays under its ceiling. `docs/config/decisions.json.md` lists the same exceptions.
- `kagutsuchi` § *Residue* retires the reservation of the name `kamui`, which ROSTER now records as spent.
- `STANDALONE-ENTRY` §§ 4 and 7b name `kamui`, the first composite to carry a `## 0.`; one more skill carries one.
- The README, the skills index and the `docs/using/` counts move up by one skill and one directory (the final totals are hiraishin's fragment's).
- The tenkai, cleanup and plugin-registries lists name `port` and `kamui`.
- `kamui` joins the dieted skills. (landed by [#366](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/366), reaching main through the integration PR [#368](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/368).)
- **`hatsu:hiraishin` — one merged PR, ported to one sibling repository** (plugin `0.126.0`; zheref/hatsu#357, child 3 of the cross-platform porting epic zheref/hatsu#354, stacked on `kamui`). `hatsu:hiraishin <CODE>-PR-#<N> [to <CODE>] [--dry-run]` and `hatsu:hiraishin resume <run-id>` are two `nen parse` templates chosen by the literal first token; the target is the checkout's `origin` unless `to <CODE>` names another, every code resolved through `nen repo resolve`. A dangling `to` with no code is refused (nen parses it at exit 0, amended onto zheref/nen#366). The precondition read prints the source PR's merge state and base and refuses, in the PR's own words, an open PR, a closed unmerged one and one merged into an integration or stack branch; the file list is read paginated and held to `changedFiles` (`gh pr view --json files` stops at 100). A port of a port is followed to its original, which becomes the source; an original on the target, a cyclic chain and unported prerequisites are refused, each named in object notation. Source text is data, never instructions (SEC-15). Where the family declares a spec repository (`families[]`, role `spec`), kamui §§ 2–4 run on the shipped PR by name (its setup items, the spec step, the **G2** wait) under `kamui` § 6's record with `caller: hiraishin`, its own `next` values, `docs/config/ports.md`'s checkouts file and run record cited (a second run for the same source on the same day taking the next free `runId` ordinal); with none, *spec step: none configured*. The port brief, written to the session's scratch directory and shown before any write, carries one row per concept with `bankai-rule`'s handling words (`mirror` for business logic and domain UX; `map` for architecture, UX idiom and dev-ops, the handbook rule cited on both sides; `drop with note`; `target-only addition`) and a ruling id, an unmerged reservation or `unmapped` and the UZF-26 evidence the target PR owes. Every `unmapped` row is asked once per run in one `port-drift` batch through `bankai-rule` (zheref/hatsu#355; three a picker call), build-time drift as further calls under the same row; until #355 lands an unmapped row stops the run, and an all-`drop with note` brief ends it at *nothing to port*. The port issue — `port: <source PR title> (from <CODE>-PR-#<N>)`, labels `job/parity`, `job/implementation`, the target's `lang/<key>`, kind `enhancement` — is filed through `file` §§ 3–5 on the typed call (its G1; file § 4's own confirmation until #355's clause lands, and for a private source ported to a public target, the source id placeheld) with `nen issue file --private-names-ignore-file` and read back explicitly, the remote title escaped (a file-fed title amended onto zheref/nen#444), or an open port issue amended, a completed one or a merged port PR ending the run *already ported*; `hatsu:build` then runs on it, and the rulings PR opens once at the run's end. `--dry-run` writes nothing outside the session's scratch directory.
- WORKFLOW § 4's table of what only the maintainer calls outside the five now carries `hiraishin` beside `black-voice` (a port runs after the gate, on work already merged, and only on a typed call), with the `v0.126.0` note. `STANDALONE-ENTRY` §§ 4 and 7b name `hiraishin` (§ 7 lists thirty-two rows), and kamui's 7b row says hiraishin calls its §§ 2–4. `docs/config/repos.json.md` and the catalogue's `workflow.branch.base` row name `hiraishin` as a reader. The skills index's count sentence names `bakuryuha`. The README, the skills index and the `docs/using/` counts read fifty-six skills and fifty-seven directories with `ten` (bankai-rule, kamui and hiraishin over the fifty-three at `v0.124.0`) (the two "ordinary words" counts in `docs/using/codex.md` and `cursor.md`, which still read fifty-four, are corrected with them); `hiraishin` joins the dieted skills, and `prose_size_check.sh`'s header names twenty-seven. (landed by [#365](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/365), reaching main through [#368](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/368).)
- Run focused machinery fixtures in a read-only unit-tests GitHub Actions job on pull requests. (landed by [#378](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/378).)
- Register an unprivileged unit-tests workflow ahead of installation, with a trusted runner and pinned Vitest fixture for focused machinery tests. (landed by [#359](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/359).)
- The `v0.124.0` release proposal ([#363](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/363)) folded the previous section; its merge commit landed after `v0.124.0` was cut at the proposal's reviewed head, so it is cited here and adds nothing of its own.

## v0.124.0 — hatsu:file routes every filing, emperor-time intake, CI reviewer lanes, and nen's gate path sets

Twenty pull requests landed on `main` since `v0.114.0` (HA-PR-#311 … #360, 2026-10-19 → 2026-10-20), #318 through #311's branch. Their nineteen `changelog.d/` fragments are folded here in the collation order (by fragment name), each entry cited with the pull request that landed it; #313 carried no fragment and is cited last. Every intermediate version from `0.114.1` to `0.123.0` was never tagged and ships inside `v0.124.0`. The section is folded by hand: `nen changelog collate` anchors only on a `### Unreleased` header, and with this file's `##` release headings it reads every earlier release as unreleased content and refuses.

**Net effect at `v0.124.0`:** the plugin reads `0.124.0` on nen `0.22.0`. `hatsu:file` runs `classify` and `direct` on every new filing. `hatsu:emperor-time` is the Specialist's product-intake ceremony. Event-driven CI reviewer lanes have one readiness canon, `AK` (the Akatsuki CI plane) resolves from a Hatsu checkout, and a committed named list lets the private-name guard admit `zheref/akatsuki-ai`, which now says `no ignore file` when none was read. `surface-mirror-check` runs the trusted direct-matrix drift check, and the mirror-regeneration workflow uses the shared script. Futon records its run and binds stacked delivery PRs to Nen's bounded non-main merge guard. A typed futon or kazaana line carries its working-branch publication scope through every handoff (an unruled reading, ROSTER § *Reading of 2026-10-19*). `cleanup` admits worktree paths as paths. The provenance guard proves merges in a throwaway object directory. `contracts/pr-composition.json` declares `zheref/nen`'s gate path sets, and gate derivation hands its file list over as `--files-from`. Antigravity gains the Gemini 4 Argon frontier escalation, and Codex recovers authorized worktree writes through the host review route. The Claude Code permission pack gains release-plan and publication forms; a version-reference helper lists and rewrites old-version mentions; dieted skills warn under 512 bytes of headroom and a version-bumped spec change owes a fragment; and `docs/PROCESS.md` writes down the report data document's defaults and the register desk's shape.

- `hatsu:file` now runs `hatsu:direct` on every new filing, right after `hatsu:classify`: the three picks, resolved from the target's own checkout, kind and role, join the one plan and confirmation; once the issue has a confirmed number and its classification keys travelled in the create call, `direct` § 7 publishes under the target's `direct.publish`, else routing is reported owed. A missing verb, an empty job axis or an undeclared `model/` label is said and never stops the filing (the maintainer's request of 2026-10-19). (landed by [#335](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/335).)
- **The private-name guard says `no ignore file` when none was read** ([#149](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/149)). `scripts/private_name_check.sh` used to report an absent default ignore file as `0 ignored by the maintainer's ignore file`, indistinguishable from a file that was read and ignored nothing; every ok line (`--allow-empty` included) and every refusal now say `no ignore file, so nothing ignored` when none was read, and a dangling default ignore-file link is a wiring fault (exit `2`) rather than a silent absence, and a host with neither `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` nor `HOME` set refuses at exit `2` naming why, where bash's unbound-variable exit `1` read as a finding. The other exit codes are unchanged, and the `redaction-guard` fixture proves each state (188 cases). `docs/PUBLIC-REDACTION.md` § *How it is guarded* records that the `redaction-tree` lane is red by design without the file and that a session hands that refusal to the maintainer, never judging it no leak; the 2026-10-13 #149 row in `docs/ROSTER.md` carries a dated pointer to the *#289 item 10* row that spent its 2(b) deferral. Plugin bumped to 0.117.0 and the three surface mirrors restamped. (landed by [#343](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/343).)
- **#154 Futon delivery merges** ([#154](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/154)). Futon records its invocation before a PR opens and binds stacked delivery PRs to that run. En may merge only a recorded PR with a pinned Ready verdict through Nen's bounded non-main delivery guard. Main-bound PRs remain at the maintainer gate. The Claude Code permission pack admits only the bounded `nen pr merge … --delivery … --require-head … --run` pattern — Nen's delivery guard, not the pattern, refuses protected bases, a non-Ready verdict and a moved head; the Futon record is checked by en § 5's `futon_effort_record.mts verify`, not by Nen or the pattern — keeps direct `gh pr merge` denied, and removes broad `gh api *` access that could bypass the guard. Node 22.18 or newer is required for the TypeScript record helper. Live classifier proof awaits a later eligible Ready Futon stack. (landed by [#325](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/325).)
- Add narrow Claude Code permission-pack forms for Hatsu's declared plugin release plan and publication, with a fixture and explicit host-denial handling. The publication pattern admits any `--repo` (Claude Code patterns carry no root syntax — `contracts/permissions.json` → `scope.enforced_by`); the maintainer's go is mugetsu § 3's recorded go, a rule nothing enforces (§ 5a), and the host may still deny the command. (landed by [#322](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/322), [#156](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/156).)
- Add a conservative version-reference helper that lists every tracked old-version mention, rewrites only unambiguous authored lines, and leaves generated mirrors and PR bodies for their owning steps. (landed by [#320](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/320), [#158](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/158).)
- Warn authors when a dieted skill has fewer than 512 bytes of prose headroom, and require a changed changelog fragment when a version-bumped PR changes a declared spec path. The new decisions run in trusted TypeScript sources. (landed by [#318](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/318) via #311, [#166](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/166).)
- Use the shared mirror-regeneration script in the GitHub builder and retire its duplicated command loop after the trusted policy accepts the script form. (landed by [#311](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/311), [#209](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/209).)
- **#234 trusted direct-matrix CI policy** ([#234](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/234)). The workflow validator now admits the exact TypeScript-backed matrix drift step, while preserving the current no-step workflow during the staged landing. The adapter fences untrusted renderer output and reports its verdict; a focused fixture proves hostile output stays fenced. Plugin bumped to 0.114.6 and mirrors restamped. #324, below, installs the CI step. (landed by [#323](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/323).)
- **#234 direct matrix check** ([#234](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/234)). `surface-mirror-check` now runs the trusted TypeScript matrix adapter against every PR checkout, so data-only registry, taxonomy and model changes fail on a stale `docs/DIRECT-MATRIX.md`. Nen repin PRs must run `--verify` at the new pin and quote its agreement line. Plugin bumped to 0.114.7 and the three surface mirrors restamped. (landed by [#324](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/324).)
- **The report data document's derivable defaults and the `--register` desk's shape rules are written down once** ([#291](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/291)). A new `docs/PROCESS.md` § *The report data document*, verified live against nen `0.22.0`, says what `nen report data` already fills (`repo`, `gate`, `effortStage`, `stageClass`, `worktree`, `generatedAtLocal`, `generatedDateLocal`, `timeZone`, `turnNumber`), what `--graph` and `--variant` inject, a table of every extension key with a mechanical default and how it is derived (`launch` is total and never `""`: a `ren` turn takes the line `amaterasu` §§ 1, 1a or 3 recorded, and a landing — launch is never a mukai step — or any turn amaterasu did not run in takes the last call's line on the effort, else `no launch target declared; skipped` where no target is declared, else `no launch in this session`; `deskCleared` is `""` beside an ask; `architectureCaption` is `""` only on `turn-fast`, since `turn` and `landing` refuse without `--graph`; `mukaiLastTurn` is the examples' boolean, counting En's first landing only when mukai started En in the same sitting and no newer request came; every row with no live source marked *new canon* for the maintainer to confirm), the judgement remainder the caller writes, the two call rules (a `--target` read through `scripts/gh_token_env.sh`; the desk passed by absolute path), the `repo` fallback when `nen repo resolve` also fails (the `--target` slug, else `null` with a blank masthead slot and the missing repo named in `notDelivered[]` and `footerNote`, *new canon*), and the three desk rules the refusals used to teach, each with nen's refusal text and a minimal valid desk carrying a `generatedAtLocal` placeholder. `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § `reports` *Report time* now says the plain `nen report data` fills the clock from `0.22.0` and only a hand-built document or a `--register` desk calls `scripts/report_time.sh --at <generatedAt>`, and *Where the effort is* derives `turnLabel` from `turnNumber`; `PROCESS.md` cites both rather than restating them. `spiritual-message` § 4 cites `PROCESS.md` and `WORKFLOW.md` *Report time* for the verb-filled keys and the clock instead of restating them, and drops from its extension list the keys the verb now fills; `backlog-board` § 3 cites `nen report data --help` for the desk's shape instead of an inline copy, and `rikugan` § 3 cites `backlog-board` § 3 for the desk's judgement and `PROCESS.md` for its shape and clock. `PROCESS.md` § *Residue and owned dependencies* names the defaults derivation and the register pass's clock lag as owed to nen (not yet filed). The checked report examples' `turnLabel` now reads `Turn 3` and `Landing · after turn 3`, and their `launch` is the line a repository with no `project.launch` records (`turn-fast`: the profile deferral) rather than a render command, and the report-markdown fixture's fence case injects its own entity-bearing `launch`. All three skills stay inside the 12288-byte ceiling. Plugin bumped to 0.123.0 and the three surface mirrors restamped. (landed by [#358](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/358).)
- **The provenance guard no longer calls a failed merge proof an old Git, and no longer needs the object store writable** ([#314](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/314)). `scripts/branch_authorship_check.sh --trunk` used to self-merge the trunk with `git merge-tree --write-tree` before judging anything and report every nonzero exit as `--trunk needs git >= 2.38`, so a supported Git whose object store the sandbox would not let it write was sent toward a Git upgrade. The capability is now read off `git merge-tree -h`, which writes nothing; the merge proof runs only for a trailer-less merge whose exemption must be proved, and it writes its tree into a throwaway object directory under `${TMPDIR:-/tmp}` (named by a template, so macOS honours `TMPDIR`; the repository's objects as its alternate; removed on exit), so it adds no object to the repository's store — Git may refresh the mtime of an object the store already holds, and where that is refused the tree is written to the throwaway directory — and a read-only store proves the same merge. Every Git read runs with `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1` and a `GIT_GRAFT_FILE` that cannot exist, so a `refs/replace` entry cannot pass an evil merge off as the clean one and `info/grafts` cannot hide a commit from the range: the guard judges the objects a push sends. The proof runs with `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`, so a partial clone missing a blob it needs fails closed rather than fetching on the caller's credentials (Git 2.44 or later; an older Git ignores the variable and may still fetch). A proof Git still cannot complete — any exit but `0` clean or `1` conflicted (unrelated histories, a shallow clone, a partial clone that would need a fetch, a signal), or exit `0` with no tree id — is exit `2` naming the commit and Git's exit, quoting at most three lines and 300 characters of Git's sanitised stderr as `git said (untrusted):`, read apart from the stdout the tree id comes from; it points at the harness's approval path only when the quoted lines themselves report a permission refusal, naming the directory actually used, and never at replacing Git. Findings are held until the range is through, so that exit `2` prints none. The `publish-integrity-guard` fixture now holds 138 cases (88 on `main`; #314 adds 50): 25 of the 50 fail against `main`'s guard and the other 25 pin verdicts it already gave. Plugin bumped to 0.119.0 and the three surface mirrors restamped. (landed by [#348](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/348).)
- Register `AK` (`zheref/akatsuki-ai`, the Akatsuki CI plane) in `nen/repos.json`, so `AK#<n>` resolves from a Hatsu checkout. Under the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-19 it is the one private repository whose name may be public; its code stays private (zheref/hatsu#329). (landed by [#338](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/338).)
- Add a committed named list, `scripts/private_name_named.txt`. The private-name guard reads it by default and drops exactly the `owner/name` it carries. A bare name is refused, and a same-named repository under another owner is still a hit. The fixture covers it and pins the list's entry set (173 cases). ([#338](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/338).)
- `hatsu:file` § 4 joins the named list with the maintainer's ignore file into the one file nen's guard takes, until zheref/nen#441. `hatsu:file`, `backlog-synthesis`, `mugetsu` and `sharingan` now always pass that file to nen's issue writers. `hatsu:build` § 1 resolves a code against the checkout it stands in, as `classify` does. The private-name guard is BC-11 debt: it is frozen to fixes only until nen ports it (zheref/nen#441). `docs/PUBLIC-REDACTION.md` and `docs/ROSTER.md` record the exception. ([#338](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/338).)
- State the readiness canon for event-driven CI reviewer lanes once, in `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Reviewer rounds and review threads*, *Event-driven reviewer lanes*: a lane's round is its formal review under its App login (under the `bounded` reading, at any earlier head), it is never requested and never owed on its own, its re-run check pending at head holds `checks-green` as the current-head wait, a green check with no review is unavailable, and `round_check_pattern` is only for a lane whose check concludes `NEUTRAL` or `SKIPPED` when it did not review. Installed lanes join `round_quorum.any_of`, with `minimum` 2 once two or more are installed, and `aiReviewers` stays one value for every bot (zheref/hatsu#330). (landed by [#339](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/339).)
- Show the posted-review and check-is-the-round lane entries and the `minimum` rule in `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` § 3, document the Hatsu-owned `ci` block in `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 2, and record the maintainer's eight rulings of 2026-10-19 on the CI reviewers tier in `docs/ROSTER.md`, the Akatsuki CI repository's `OPEN-4` (Hidan's activation) ruled per consumer. ([#339](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/339).)
- **A typed `futon` or `kazaana` line now carries its working-branch publication scope through every handoff** ([#332](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/332)). `aka` § 0 gains a second carve-out: the typed invocation is the maintainer's one standing publish call for the branches that run authors — each effort's initial publication and every maintenance commit and push that delivers it, to the run's target repository and that effort's own branch — scoped by destination, never by commit SHA, and lapsing when the run ends. Another remote, branch, `branch.base`, a force form, a tag, a merge or a release is outside it and stands or falls on its owning skill's own rule (murasaki § 6, futon § 5's integration branch, getsuga § 3/§ 3a, and § 4 for the tag); an act no skill owns is not performed and is reported, and a G5 only where only the maintainer can do it. The scope lapses at the run's end, a halt, or a G5 that stops the run; an effort's own G5 lapses that effort's scope only — never another's — and it resumes when the maintainer answers; and a resumed session holds it only on a fresh typed invocation. The reading is recorded in `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Reading of 2026-10-19 — a typed composite's delegated publication (unruled; the maintainer's to correct at G4)* as this run's reading. Every parent-to-builder and maintenance handoff carries the invocation verbatim with the repository and branch, and no later checkpoint narrows it to an exact commit. A host approval refusal stays the host's, resolved only through its supported approval path, with no wider grant and no alternative route. A representative trajectory (initial push, then a reviewed repair pushed to the same branch) is stated. `murasaki` § 6, `en`'s catch-up row, `mukai` step 6, `futon` § 7, `kazaana` § 3 and its Authority, and the `aka` row of `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` § 7 cite it; standalone `hatsu:aka` stays one call, one run. Plugin bumped to 0.120.0 and the three surface mirrors restamped. (landed by [#350](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/350).)
- **`cleanup` admits a worktree path as a path, never as a Git branch name** ([#333](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/333)). § 4 put every name taken from the repository, worktree paths included, through `git check-ref-format --branch`, which refuses every native absolute path, so a clean merged worktree under `.nen/worktrees/` was always a named row and § 5's worktree removal was unreachable. Branches and remote heads keep the ref rule; a worktree path is now admitted by the new `scripts/cleanup_path_admit.ts` (TypeScript under BC-11, behind the forwarding shim `scripts/cleanup_path_admit.sh`), whose header is the rule: a canonical main-worktree core; a short trunk `--base` that exists (`refs/…`, `heads/main`, `@{-1}` refused); an absolute path in `^/[A-Za-z0-9._/-]+$` with no `.`/`..`/empty component or trailing slash; exactly `<core>/.nen/worktrees/<surface>/<name>` (kagutsuchi's fixed worktree kept); no symlink on the way; listed exactly in `git worktree list --porcelain -z`, read NUL-split so a newline in another path forges nothing; not prunable, holding no other worktree, not on the trunk. A refused path stays a named row with its reason and no command. An admitted one prints facts — `head`, `locked`, `kind` (`reviewer` for `hanten-*`/`prove-*`), `tree` and a `dirt` line per status entry, ignored entries and a status warning included, so an ignored `.env` or an unreadable directory never reads clean — and the one literal `git -C '<core>' worktree remove -- '<path>'`, printed and never run before the maintainer's pick, never `--force`. It prints no class: § 5's table, read from those facts and `nen wc classify`, is the only class owner, with a fall-through (no row matches → proposed) and the ledger rule alone for reviewer checkouts. Its `--self-test` runs as the new `cleanup-path-guard` lane and asserts every refusal's reason; Shizuku cites the rule; the evidence lives in `docs/ab/cleanup.md`. Also: a refusal prints `refused`, a fact, never § 5's class word; § 5 states its precedence (named facts — `locked yes`, an open PR, `tree unreadable`, a refused name — then the reviewer ledger rule, then the table); nesting is compared on the disk's own spelling and any `.git` below the path refuses, so an APFS case variant or a nested clone under an ignored directory is never removed with its host; `--base` must be spelled as `git for-each-ref` prints it; `_` joins the charset. Under the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-20 (ROSTER § *Rulings of 2026-10-20 — worktree-local ledgers are disposable*) an ignored worktree-local `.nen/` is reported as disposable and does not by itself make the tree dirty; core's `.nen/` stays the record. **The admission needs node >= 22.18** (23.x: 23.6), the native type-stripping floor: the shim exits `2` with `node not found` or `node >= 22.18 required (found <v>)`, never `1`, and `project.toolchain` now declares the `node` row `nen shu tools` checks. CONTRIBUTING's copy of the guarded scripts gains the four it was missing. Plugin bumped to 0.122.0 and the three surface mirrors restamped. (landed by [#353](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/353).)
- `contracts/pr-composition.json` → `gateDerive` declares `zheref/nen`'s own policy and process path sets (zheref/hatsu#334), under the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-20 (`docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-20 — zheref/nen's gate path sets*). Every top-level entry of nen's tracked tree at `v0.23.0` and at nen `main` derives `G4`, including `.github/` as a whole, `changelog.d/` and `.gitattributes`, and so do `bunfig.toml` and `.npmrc`, which are listed even though neither exists yet. Only `LICENSE` and `.gitignore` derive `G2`. A top-level entry nen adds later derives `G2` until the row moves, so two things are owed, neither filed yet (`docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue and owned dependencies*): a tree-freshness check at repin, and a home for these sets in nen itself. zheref/hatsu's own row is unchanged. The `pr-composition-guard` fixture pins the guard's refusal of a wildcard row, so another canon repository with no row still asks. It also records the gate each representative nen change derives, asked of `nen gate derive` itself, and that covers both sides of a rename. (landed by [#360](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/360).)
- `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § *Gate derivation* now names the `--files` hand-off exactly. The source, either the PR files API with `previous_filename` or `git diff --name-only --no-renames`, is written to an absolute file and passed as `--files-from`, and only once it has exited `0` with at least one path. Both sources print one path per line, and nen 0.22.0 reads a newline-joined `--files` value as one path, so a real PR under-derived `G2`. `gh pr diff --name-only` lists only a rename's new name and `--range` is rename-blind too, so neither is used. Until a tree-freshness check exists, a `zheref/nen` path outside both sets and `LICENSE`/`.gitignore` stops and asks. `tensho` and `backlog-state` stop restating the old source. The `pr-composition-guard` fixture pins the newline-joined under-derivation, the `--files-from` derivation and an empty set's `G2`. The nen halves, and `scripts/pr_composition_check.sh`'s BC-11 debt, are recorded in `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue and owned dependencies*, none filed yet. The WORKFLOW line for zheref/hatsu's `contracts/` and `templates/` now cites the ruling of 2026-10-13 that confirmed them. Plugin bumped to 0.124.0; the mirrors restamped. ([#360](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/360).)
