- **`hatsu:emperor-time` — the Specialist's product-intake ceremony, with its own entry** (plugin `0.116.0`). Kurapika's Specialist mode was twelve lines of persona prose with no skill behind it; `file` is Manipulator-mode single-issue filing and `nen idea` offers only `file`. The new skill takes one raw idea, in order: `hatsu:direct` on the idea text (`job/discovery` + `job/requirements`, on a product repository cells `FEAT.DEV.001`/`002`, primary `SEMANTIC_FRONTIER` on `claude-code`, recommended `SEMANTIC_MAX`; on a canon one the `aigov` domain, `AIGOV.REPO.001`; within the set one line, outside it one ask, never a block); interview rounds through the surface's picker until the brief is decision-complete (problem, audience, platforms, constraints, observable success criteria, priority, scope boundaries, a Design Direction through Hisoka for anything with UI), a conversation holding several ideas becoming several briefs; adversarial challenge rounds by a raised persona, never the frontier tier and never Kurapika himself; `file` § 3's four `nen issue search` passes; the filing through `nen idea file` (read back, `--forbid-family` where the target declares its stage family) only on the one confirmation; then `hatsu:build` offered, never started. Never a stage or G1 mode label. (landed by [#344](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/344).)

## v0.114.0 — the backlog selector grammar on nen 0.22, the trusted canon preflight, and Cursor for Grok

Two merges landed on `main` since `v0.112.3`: HA-PR-#306, which carries the reviewed stack #307 → #308 → #309 → #310, and HA-PR-#315. Their six `changelog.d/` fragments are folded here in the collation order (by fragment name), each entry cited with the pull request that landed it. The intermediate versions `0.113.0` … `0.113.3` were never tagged and ship inside `v0.114.0`.

**Net effect at `v0.114.0`:** the plugin reads `0.114.0` on nen `0.22.0` (`minimum "0.22"`). `backlog-state` and `backlog-board` take `context`, gate lists, severity bands and label selectors. The trusted canon contracts guard runs in local PR preflight. The shared mirror-regeneration script is admitted as the workflow's next frozen form; #209 stays open until #311 installs the workflow step. Focused fixtures name their silent failures. Cursor is the coding surface for Grok. En routes a published PR's updates through Murasaki's guarded push.

- **The shared mirror-regeneration script is admitted as the workflow's next frozen form**, with its stamp-only behaviour proved, and it is named in Ao's catch-up path ([#310](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/310) via #306, [#209](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/209)). #209 stays open: #311 installs the workflow step, and it is not in this release.
- **Cursor is the official coding surface for Grok models.** Grok Build remains a documented headless CI option, not a fifth interactive Hatsu surface ([#308](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/308) via #306, [#236](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/236), 0.113.1).
- **Focused fixture lanes report silent failures by name**, and failure probes under `set -e` reach their own expected/actual assertions ([#307](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/307) via #306, [#243](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/243), 0.113.0).
- **En continuations route a published PR's updates through Murasaki's guarded fast-forward push**, reserving Aka for first publication ([#309](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/309) via #306, [#292](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/292)).
- **The trusted canon contracts guard runs during local PR preflight**, naming broken candidate contracts before a PR opens ([#306](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/306), [#293](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/293)).
- **`backlog-state` and `backlog-board` take `context`, gate lists, severity bands and label selectors** ([#315](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/315), [#297](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/297); the maintainer's rulings of 2026-10-17, recorded there).
  - The grammar is `<repo|all|context>[@<gate>[+<gate>…] | <severity>[+] | <label>][#<label>[&<label>…]]`, plus `every …` on the board. `+` between gates is OR, `&` between labels is AND, and every filter combines as AND.
  - It parses through `nen parse`'s list slots and three-way alternation, read by `alternative` and `items`.
  - Labels and severity bands filter through `nen backlog fetch`/`nen report data` `--label`/`--severity` against the target's own `nen/labels.json`. A label containing `&` is unsupported, and a target with no `bankai:severity/*` family has no bands. Follow-ups are tracked in zheref/nen#427.
  - `context` covers only the objects named in the conversation, echoed as a per-repository list before a fresh read.
  - backlog-board's § 1 defers to backlog-state's, so the two cannot diverge.
- **nen repinned to `v0.22.0` (`minimum "0.22"`)** ([#315](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/315); zheref/nen#410, released by NN-PR-#423).
  - The list slots and the backlog filters are 0.22's, and the release raises nen's compatibility floor to 0.22.
  - `nen/contract.json` carries the three release digests, and every convenience copy names the new pin.
  - Hatsu's declarations pass `schema check` under 0.22.0 unchanged.
  - A nen older than 0.22 is refused by every 0.22-pinned check and cannot run the two backlog skills' parse or filters: run `nen shu tools --repo <hatsu>`, and `ten` installs the pin.

## v0.112.3 — the 2026-10-13 rulings built, pickers and hand-overs, the redaction tree

Forty-six deliveries merged since `v0.94.0` (HA-PR-#249 … #305, 2026-10-13 → 2026-10-18), folded here from their `changelog.d/` fragments in the collation order (by fragment name), each entry's title cited with the pull request that landed it. Every intermediate version from `0.95.0` to `0.112.2` was never tagged and ships inside `v0.112.3`.

**Net effect at `v0.112.3`:** the plugin reads `0.112.3` on nen `0.21.0`. Every CI guard workflow is frozen by the trusted validator; the three that bootstrap nen (`surface-mirror-check`, `pr-readiness`, `surface-mirror-regenerate`) hash the binary against the contract's sha256 and fail when it matches but does not run; `prose-size-check.yml` holds the prose ceilings on every same-repository, non-draft pull request, which is also where the canon data contracts are checked. Every Hatsu PR declares its three nen inputs once in `contracts/pr-composition.json`; forbidden commit trailers are refused at the required check; `scripts/publish_gate.sh` is the one gated push; overlapping efforts land as stacks, retargeted before a merged branch is deleted. Pickers carry one short question with starred options and every hand-over is a runnable block; the question budget is three per picker call; a subagent keeps its role's tier. On an on-device act the later ruling stands over the earlier entry: it is a **G5 stop** (#298, `nen/decisions.json` row `on-device-act`), not the refusal #280 first recorded. On #165 the amendment stands over the first record: the toggle alone ships and the issue is closed. Skill blocks run in worktree-isolated sessions; the Rikugan register and every report have Markdown templates beside the HTML ones; `hatsu:kazaana` draws in a surface-matched backlog. The tree names no private repository and the `redaction-tree` lane is the check; no skill, skill index or agent file cites `docs/SURFACES.md`. Removed: the `osascript *` permission row (replaced by `scripts/bell_notify.sh`), and `scripts/plugin_cache_check.sh` with its `plugin-cache-guard` lane (nen's `surface mirror check --installed` took its job).

- **`reports.sections` is required for a render, and canon no longer promises a default nen refuses** ([#253](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/253), [#108](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/108)). `spiritual-message` § 3's parameter row reads *required, no default* and cites `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 2 → `reports`, which now states once what nen 0.21.0 does — an undeclared variant or a file with no `sections` is exit `2`; a variant without `template` or a non-empty `blocks` makes nen refuse the whole file, exit `1` (verified three times by hanten) — and the one adoption sentence: a repository that has never declared `sections` sets up the five-variant block `docs/config/workflow.json.md` § `reports` documents as `missing-configuration` at the first phase that will render, landing as WORKFLOW § 4 step 3 says. The false claim that a variant declaring no blocks renders every block is gone from WORKFLOW § 2, `docs/config/workflow.json.md` and the skill; `en`'s parameter table no longer gives `.sections` a default either. § 6, § 7, § 8 and two hard limits were shortened to carry the row under the 12288-byte ceiling, the Antigravity write permission keeping its `brain/<conversation-id>/` scope. Plugin bumped to 0.94.4; mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

- **Step one of the prose-diet guard in CI: `prose-size-check.yml` is registered in the workflow-policy validator and ships inert at `templates/`** ([#270](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/270), [#135](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/135)). The validator judges every pull request from the base, so the workflow's job, trigger, draft-skip guard, check creation and frozen guard step are learned one PR before the file moves under `.github/workflows/` (#80's order); a workflow pending in `PENDING_HOSTED_WORKFLOWS` is judged from `templates/` as if installed. The frozen guard step refuses to measure without a fresh fence token, and reads any exit but `0` and `1` as a wiring refusal. **The exact-head check each guard workflow creates and finishes is now frozen at both ends** in `plugin-bump-check.yml`, `surface-mirror-check.yml` and `prose-size-check.yml` alike, so its conclusion mapping cannot be edited into a green (QA-18). Every per-workflow table of the validator is checked at load to cover exactly its workflows, so a registration that misses one fails loudly. `scripts/prose_size_check.sh` resolves a symlink's whole chain before its root check, so a link to an in-root link that leaves the root is refused, with a fixture case. Plugin bumped to 0.107.0 (`templates/` and a shipped runtime script); mirrors restamped.

- **`prose-size-check.yml` runs the trusted `scripts/prose_size_check.sh` against every pull request's checkout** ([#271](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/271), [#135](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/135)). A file over its ceiling fails the `prose-size-check` check, the guard's `OVER` row naming the file, its size and its ceiling, and `nen pr ready` reads the pull request not-ready. It is a check of its own because `pr-readiness.yml` concludes `success` by construction. The guard used to run only by hand and in its focused lane, so `hanten/SKILL.md` reached `main` over its ceiling with #129; it is under again on main (12286 bytes), and this effort leaves it untouched. The workflow landed in the #80 order (registered in the validator while it shipped inert at `templates/`, then installed), now stated once as the new-workflow rule in `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md`, with `PENDING_HOSTED_WORKFLOWS` kept as a permanent tier, empty when nothing is pending. Its guard step is frozen (`PROSE_FROZEN_GUARD`), and its exact-head check is frozen at both ends, as in the other guard workflows. The check is advisory and ready to be required, which is the maintainer's ruleset edit. Plugin bumped to 0.106.0; mirrors restamped.

- **A comment can no longer disarm a hostile fixture in `scripts/workflow_runner_policy_check.rb`** ([#266](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/266), [#136](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/136)). The self-test built its fixtures with `String#sub`, which edits the first occurrence of a literal, so on #134 a comment spelling the trusted contract path took the edit meant for `surface-mirror-check.yml`'s pin read, and a trailing comment could take it while the suite stayed green. Every edit of a real workflow now goes through `fixture_sub`: it tries each occurrence and keeps the first that changes what the parser and the shell see (YAML comments are not in the parse; `fixture_code` drops a run's comment text, trailing comments included, reading `'…'`, `"…"` and ANSI-C `$'…'` quotes and passing heredoc bodies over as data), inside the one step the fixture names or outside every step when it names none. `fixture_code` is a reader, not a parser, and its comment names what it still misreads: a `<<` shift inside `$(( ))`, a `case` pattern's `)` inside `$( )`, a heredoc delimiter quoted in part or starting with a digit, and backquotes; no workflow here uses any of them. A fixture no occurrence satisfies, or one whose scratch tree is refused before its edit, stops the self-test as a **BROKEN FIXTURE** naming the case. The two property cases (a sibling `.trusted` directory and a traversal out of it) also assert the refusal's reason, not only a refusal. The hand-built fixture tables check through `fixture_changed!`. Every existing case is still refused for the same reason, and `surface-mirror-check.yml` no longer warns authors against spelling the path. No shipped surface changes.

- **Codex installs from a committed, clean marketplace snapshot** ([#273](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/273), [#147](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/147), 0.108.1)

  - `hatsu_surface_link.sh --surface codex` archives the served commit before registering its marketplace. Codex's copied plugin slot excludes `.git`, ignored settings, worktrees, `.nen/` and `Reports/`. An existing direct-checkout marketplace is migrated to the snapshot.
  - `hatsu_plugin_update.sh --codex` resolves the source checkout from the snapshot's adjacent record, updates it, re-registers the new committed snapshot and reads back the installed version. A changed snapshot refuses installation; the fixture suites cover migration, excluded local files and tampering.
  - The Codex guides explain what is copied and how to remove an old marketplace registration, snapshot and cache slot.

- **#149's ruling of 2026-10-13 is recorded: the listed issue bodies were rewritten to the legend's placeholders on the maintainer's go per edit** ([#287](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/287), [#149](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/149); `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-13*). Twelve closed issues (and #102's title) now carry `<product-repo-A>`, `<product-repo-B>`, `<reference-repo>`, `<ci-plane-repo>` and the `RA` product code where they named a private repository; three were left as they are on the maintainer's word; #57 was already clean. Two of the twelve still trip the guard on one dictionary-word name until the maintainer's ignore file carries it, every rewritten issue's pre-rewrite revisions stay in GitHub's public edit history until the maintainer deletes them, and #102's rename event keeps its old title: the row records each as the maintainer's own. The guard (v0.81.0) and `nen`'s exit-4 refusals delivered criteria 2(a) and 3 before this ruling; criterion 2(b), the tree check, stays deferred and **the issue stays open**. The nen request (the pre-filing check honouring the same ignore file, and nothing wider) goes through the harvest. Plugin bumped to 0.107.10 (a shipped doc changed); the mirrors restamped.

- **Preflight trusted pull request guards before opening a PR** ([#274](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/274), [#157](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/157), 0.108.5)

  Hanten and Mukai now run the base revision's `pull_request_target` guards against the proposed tree before PR creation. The guard set comes from the trusted workflows themselves. A mismatch names the two-step landing path in `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md`.

- **`claude/agents/kurapika.md` § *The exit codes, and what you do on each* now says that `$?` after a pipeline is the last stage's status, never the verb's**, with the exact remedy — redirect to a file and read `$?` after the verb alone — and the two approximations, `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` (bash) or `$pipestatus[1]` (zsh) on the very next command in the same shell, or `set -o pipefail`, each answering `141` (SIGPIPE, not the verb failing) under a reader that closes early (`docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` § 5), with one of the three wrong readings recorded on [#161](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/161) as the evidence. `tenkai` § 2 and `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` are unchanged and named in `kurapika.md` as instances of the general statement; `aka` and `ao`'s two-stream rule is named as a different rule. Plugin bumped to 0.94.2 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); the mirrors regenerated at that stamp, since the agent file is mirrored. ([#250](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/250))

- **The ruling of 2026-10-13 on #165 is amended to option (b), toggle only, and #165 closes** ([#296](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/296), [#165](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/165); `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-17*). The maintainer answered the register's DECIDE through the picker on 2026-10-17: the preflight read is not built and no secret is held; criteria 1 and 2 are withdrawn by the amendment, 3 and 4 were delivered earlier; the 2026-10-13 row opens with the amendment. The same section records the three readings of that sitting (#176, #223, #149) as accepted. Plugin bumped to 0.111.2 (a shipped doc changed); the mirrors restamped.

- **#165's ruling of 2026-10-13 is recorded as partly delivered; the issue stayed open until the amendment of 2026-10-17 (HA-PR-#296, the next entry)** ([#284](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/284), [#165](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/165); `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-13*). The picker's option (a) asked for the repository toggle plus an in-job preflight; the build found the toggle already on, the header note and the docs line delivered by HA-PR-#193 and HA-PR-#211 (moved by HA-PR-#241), the cleanup step by HA-PR-#211, criterion 2 not met (the toggle is named after the push, not before, with no fixture), and the preflight read needing an Administration (read) permission `GITHUB_TOKEN` cannot carry — the 2026-10-02 ruling that kept #165 open. The row returns the remainder to the maintainer: a scoped token and the preflight, an amendment to option (b) and a close, or the issue kept open. Plugin bumped to 0.107.6 (a shipped doc changed); the mirrors restamped.

- **The three inputs every Hatsu PR hands nen are declared once, in `contracts/pr-composition.json`** ([#262](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/262), [#171](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/171); the nen half is zheref/nen#239). Until now each run retyped them from prose. **Body check:** `contracts/pr-body.requirements.json` is the `{name, pattern}` array `nen pr body-check --requirements-from` reads, derived from `templates/pr-body.md` — eight required headings, `The flow` and `Economics` optional, `## Agent attribution` required last — and `shibari` § 4 passes it from `$hatsu_root`. **Gate sets:** `gateDerive."zheref/hatsu"` holds the two `nen gate derive` path sets, its process set now carrying `contracts/` and `templates/` so the declaration, the array and the template derive `G4` as `docs/WORKFLOW.md` did when it held the sets (this run's reading, the maintainer's to confirm at the `G4` merge); a canon repository with no row has none declared, the run asks, and the row is written by a Hatsu PR at Hatsu's `G4`. **Fragment inputs:** `changelogFragment` holds `fragment-required`'s `specPaths`, `fragmentDir` and `headChangelog`; a PR that adds its `changelog.d/` fragment reads `fragment-present` with no opt-out line, and `directSection.optOut` is the one opt-out, carried only by a PR whose own diff changes `CHANGELOG.md`. That condition is unenforced by nen, which honours the phrase anywhere, over any diff, inside an HTML comment or a code fence too: a Hatsu-side raw-body check is owed, and the nen half is zheref/nen#239's scope. **Citations:** `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § *Gate derivation*, `shibari`, `tensho`, `jujisho`, the template and `CONTRIBUTING.md` cite the file and carry no copy. **Drift guard:** `scripts/pr_composition_check.sh`, the new `pr-composition-guard` lane (35 fixture cases), fails on a literal set copied into the canon prose (a leading `/` included), a Gate derivation section naming a directory prefix of any depth that no declared set carries or a file path no set covers (the section cut with fenced lines masked), a requirements array that drifts from the template, the fragment opt-out phrase anywhere in the template, a reader that does not cite the declaration, a declared path that escapes the root, and a scanned file that is not UTF-8. **Template hazard:** the template's top comment no longer spells out the fragment opt-out phrase, which nen honours anywhere in a body.
- **`scripts/plugin_bump_check.sh` no longer honours `no plugin bump:` over a shipped path** ([#171](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/171)). The line used to skip the version check for any diff — HA-PR-#233 carried one over `docs/WORKFLOW.md` and `nen/contract.json` and passed. It is now accepted only where no `PLUGIN_SURFACE_GLOBS` path changed, and, when the version is not bumped, refused by name otherwise, listing the shipped paths; beside a bump it is inert and the pass line says it was ignored; the script header, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, the README, `getsuga` § 3 and `docs/ab/plugin-bump-guard.md` say the same. The required check runs the trusted revision, so the rule binds PRs opened after this merges. Plugin bumped to 0.102.0; mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

- **The question budget is one rule, stated once: no per-run cap, at most three questions per picker call** ([#279](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/279), [#175](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/175); the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-13, recorded in `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-13*). `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 4 *Ask, set up, continue*, step 2 now states the budget: one question per missing item per run, however many are missing; at most three per picker call (the Codex picker's cap); a typed `missing-maintainer-choice` item counts like any other; more than three missing items means more calls, never a bundle and never a refusal on the count. `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` § 3 P4 cites that paragraph instead of restating a rule of its own, `hatsu:jusshin` § 2 and `hatsu:tenkai` cite it instead of restating a cap of their own. Plugin bumped to 0.107.1 (a shipped skill and shipped docs changed, wording only); the mirrors restamped.

- **An on-device act is a refusal, never a G5** ([#280](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/280), [#176](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/176); the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-13, recorded in `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-13*). `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 4 *What is not a gap* states it once: a trust prompt, a security setting, a UAC consent, a Login Items approval or a password typed into an elevated window is the maintainer's own act — no `nen stop` banner, no picker, the five G5s stay five; a skill may raise such a prompt only through a launch the harness itself asks the maintainer to approve (jusshin's Windows path), otherwise it prints the act and where the operation resumes (jujutsu; jusshin's Linux and macOS paths). `nen/decisions.json` → `on-device-act` leaves the `human-gate` class: it lands as `autonomous` with a `default` that spells the refusal, because nen 0.21.0's decisions schema admits no `refusal` class (requested of nen by the run's harvest). `claude/rules/hatsu.md` and `README.md` drop the trust prompt from their G5 examples, `docs/config/decisions.json.md` moves the row to its autonomous table; `hatsu:jusshin` § 5 and `hatsu:jujutsu` cite the row as a refusal; the ROSTER rows that called the reading open are amended. Plugin bumped to 0.107.2 (a shipped declaration, the rules file and two shipped skills changed); the mirrors restamped.

- **`docs/ab/ten.md` records that a host one patch behind the pin reads `BEHIND` (exit `7`, `present-but-behind-pin`) and that ten § 1 routes it to § 2's install** ([#252](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/252), [#182](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/182)). The row is nen's since zheref/nen#327; the record reproduces the pre-0.20 `ok` beside it and the unaffected host at the pin. No shipped surface changes.

- **Every step of `surface-mirror-regenerate.yml` except its pull-request step is admitted only in its exact form** ([#267](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/267), [#190](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/190)). The job holds `contents: write`, and its only push guard was lexical: `git -C . push origin HEAD:refs/tags/v9.9.9`, or a `gh api -X DELETE` of another branch, added to any run step passed `main`'s validator. `REGENERATE_FROZEN_STEPS` now holds each step's keys, `env`, `if`, `id`, `shell`, the checkout's `with` and the run byte for byte, comment lines included. The pull-request step is held short of that (`REGENERATE_PR_WITH`): its exact keys and `with` inputs, and every input's value but `body`'s free prose; no `with` value reaches a shell. **A `${{ }}` expression in any workflow's run is refused**, comment lines included, because Actions substitutes it into the shell source; that covers `github.event.*` and every other context. Sixteen new refused cases, none caught by an earlier check, and one showing `body` stays free. A change to a builder step's body is now a two-step landing (`docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md`, `docs/using/mirrors.md` § 4). **This delivers part of #190: its `v*` tag ruleset is a repository setting, the maintainer's step, on the desk as a DO ask;** `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` carries the ruleset's body and the `PUT` shape for changing one. Plugin bumped to 0.105.0 (shipped docs); mirrors restamped.

- **`bakuryuha` § 5 now says, in its own words, that the host `nen` link is bound to the NEW pin as part of re-proving from the new body, before anything in § 6 switches** ([#256](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/256), [#201](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/201)): the new `ten` § 2 (its host default, exit table and session-bind fallback) and § 2c's `host-nen-link` offer run then, never after `/skills reload`, and the `nen-global:` line reaches § 7 inside the ten line — cited, not restated. Where the session-start hook has not bound the link — on a mirrored copy (Antigravity, Cursor) it binds only from a verified `HATSU_PLUGIN_ROOT` — this step is the binding, which is the gap the v0.74.0 → v0.75.0 upgrade on Antigravity hit. Plugin bumped to 0.96.1 (wording in a shipped skill); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

- Every self-hosted runner pool now requires its own `pool-<id>` label in the workflow policy and readiness rendering ([#204](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/204)). Tenkai accepts both Nen declaration spellings, derives a single declared pool, asks for an explicit choice when several are declared, and uses hosted readiness until a matching online runner is verified. It refuses GitHub's default labels alone and undeclared selectors. The runner preflight fixture proves that a defaults-only rendering fails. Jusshin names `nen runner relabel` for existing runners and retires the same-architecture desktop overlap question. ([#272](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/272))
- The existing `zheref/nen` `windows-x64` pool (NZ-NNR1..3) and `zheref/<product-repo-E>` `windows-x64` pool (NZ-KWIR1..5) still need their declarations, registered runner labels, preflight workflows, and readiness workflows migrated before their jobs are enabled. This Hatsu change does not alter either consumer.
- Plugin bumped to 0.108.0; generated surfaces restamped.

- **On Codex the one chat line names the ACTIVE worktree's `current.html` by absolute path, with the worktree and the branch, and a render from another worktree moves that line to its own file** ([#254](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/254), [#205](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/205)). The rule is stated once in `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Publishing a report* (a new Codex bullet; `nen report render --out` writes only inside `--repo`, so each worktree holds its own page) and `spiritual-message` § 7 cites it, with `worktree` derived as WORKFLOW § *Where the effort is* says. A browser tab still showing an older worktree's page is never presented as current, and a pending product decision is a `DECIDE` ask with `ask.gate: null` and no `ask.blocker` that reaches `request_user_input` in the same turn (jutaisho § 1); which class a non-gate stop belongs to stays an open handbook-question. The two-worktree scenario is documented in `docs/ab/spiritual-message.md` as the manual check to walk on Codex. Five passages were shortened to pay for the rule under the 12288-byte ceiling (§ 0, § 4's companions and usage sentences, § 5's `<details>` note, § 7's title line and § 8's repeated fallback line), the canonical `missing-argument` sentence kept. Plugin bumped to 0.95.0 (a reporting behaviour); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

- **hanten runs each declared guard-lane fixture once per head SHA and hands every reviewer the result** ([#260](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/260), [#208](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/208)). § 4 now says: before the raise, `nen shu test --lane <lane> --effort <branch>` inside a phase for every lane the changed paths map to (WORKFLOW's gate step 3 mapping; nen has no path selector; a prose-only diff reads not applicable), and the brief carries per lane the head SHA, the command and the exit routed as kurapika.md's table (`0` handed over, `1` red and carried, `3`/`4` not run with nen's reason, `2`/`5` a wiring stop); a reviewer re-runs a fixture only where the head it reads differs from the record, naming it, a lane the brief leaves out that the reviewer judges applies is run and named or declared unread, and a not-run row is never clean (§ 2a). Five reviewers on HA-PR-#202 had each re-run the same two fixtures, about ten minutes of identical work on one head. The skill was compressed in about a dozen places to carry the rule under its 12288-byte ceiling, the load-bearing clauses kept. Plugin bumped to 0.100.0; mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

- **Every report now has a Markdown template beside its HTML one, filled by `nen report render` from the same data document** ([#263](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/263), [#215](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/215); its predecessor [#145](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/145) fixed where the artifact goes). `templates/spiritual-message.md` covers `turn`, `turn-fast` and `landing`; `templates/rikugan.md` covers `final` and `register`. Each mirrors its HTML template block for block, from the same `reports.sections.<variant>.blocks`: one starred option per ask, the `nen pr ready` verdict quoted, the G5 evidence in `g5-blocker`, the delta as a `mermaid` fence, and commands and multi-line values in `<pre>`. On Antigravity the artifact is no longer composed from a description, so its structure no longer drifts from turn to turn. It is rendered to `<reports.dir>/current.md` with `--graph` always passed (nen's `--out` stays inside `--repo`), copied verbatim into `write_to_file`, and read back with `cmp`; one rewrite is allowed, and a second difference yields the turn unpublished. `docs/PROCESS.md` § *The Markdown report* states the surface-neutral rule, the one step no verb performs, the checked example per variant, and a caller-side interim: no newline in a value outside a `<pre>` slot. § *Escaping and validation of report data* now says plainly that nen escapes HTML only, so every value in the Markdown twins is live Markdown outside `<pre>` and fences. Link and image syntax in third-party titles is a named gap, and `graphMermaid` is a raw slot that is safe only on the `--graph` path. `spiritual-message` § 7, `rikugan` §§ 4–5, `backlog-board` § 3 and rules item 9 point at the section; `docs/surfaces/antigravity.md` records the three steps and their provenance. A new `report-markdown-guard` lane renders every variant in both formats from `templates/report-examples/` (36 fixture cases). It holds block order, starred option lines, escaping placement (spans, fences, indented code), token parity both ways, no data-carried `graphMermaid`, no stray newline, and the checked examples. **Owed to nen** (§ *Residue and owned dependencies*, not yet filed): render-time Markdown escaping, and a render to stdout or outside `--repo`. **On the desk, G4:** `scripts/report_markdown_check.sh` is embedded Python that BC-11 reads as owed to a nen verb (ROSTER § *Rulings of 2026-10-03*); port it or waive it. Until then it takes fixes only. Plugin bumped to 0.103.0; mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

- **The maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-03 is recorded: BC-11's embedded-Python freeze binds `scripts/hanten_cycle_ledger.sh`, and the ledger ports to a nen verb (zheref/nen#346)** ([#251](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/251), [#216](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/216)). `docs/ROSTER.md` gains § *Rulings of 2026-10-03 — BC-11 binds the hanten ledger*; `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue and owned dependencies* replaces its open question with the ruling and names zheref/nen#346 as the owned dependency; the script's header says it is frozen — fixes only, no new subcommand or verdict — until the verb lands; the ledger's 2026-10-02 growth (#169) is named as the BC-11 finding and zheref/nen#346 as its remediation. PROCESS also records that zheref/nen#339 shipped (NN-PR-#378, nen 0.21's `surface mirror check --plugin --installed`), so `scripts/plugin_cache_check.sh` is owed a retirement of its own. The issue stays open for its last step: when zheref/nen#346 ships, `hanten` §§ 1–4 and `breath` § 3b call the verb and the script retires. Plugin bumped to 0.94.3 (a shipped doc and a shipped runtime script changed); the mirrors restamped.

- **BC-11 binds `scripts/report_markdown_check.sh` as it binds the hanten ledger: frozen to fixes only, its verb owed to nen** ([#285](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/285), [#216](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/216), [#215](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/215); the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-13, recorded in `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-13*). The script's header says it is frozen — no new check, verdict, flag or subcommand — until nen carries the template-conformance verb, zheref/nen#406; `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue and owned dependencies* names it beside the ledger's zheref/nen#346. #216 stays open for the ledger's retirement at that verb. Plugin bumped to 0.107.7 (a shipped runtime script's comments and --help, and shipped docs); the mirrors restamped.

- **murasaki's gated push ships as one command, `scripts/publish_gate.sh`** ([#286](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/286), [#218](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/218) (i), [#188](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/188); the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-13). The six gates of the `&&` chain — the catch-up re-probe reads `noOp: true`, `HEAD` is the proved HEAD, `@{upstream}` is the branch's own name, every declared iteration check in order, the authorship check over the outgoing range, then (`HEAD` re-read) `nen wc publish` — run in that order inside the script, a dry run probing with nen's own `--dry-run` and an interrupt ending the chain before the push, each printed with its exit, so the skill block is one plain call the worktree-isolation guard admits; lane `publish-gate-guard` seats `scripts/publish_gate_fixture.sh` (a stubbed nen and a real git remote prove each gate refuses the push), and `scripts/publish_gate_check.sh` counts a call of the script as murasaki's full gated form.


- **The skill-shell writing rule lives in `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § *Writing a skill block*** ([#218](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/218) (iii)): one plain command or a shipped script per call, the refused-shape table and its replacements, the `GH_TOKEN`/`PATH` form and the chained-gate rule moved whole from `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue*, which now cites it, as does `claude/rules/hatsu.md`.
- **hanten's read-only reviewer fallback is ratified** ([#218](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/218) (ii)): on an observed refusal of the reviewer's `git worktree add` by the harness's isolation guard, the reviewer reads the effort checkout read-only at the printed HEAD, disclosed — the one exception to the worktree ruling of 2026-09-30, recorded in ROSTER and cited from `hanten` § 4 and the writing rule's row (a). Plugin bumped to 0.107.8 (one shipped script, two shipped skills, the rules file, the contract and shipped docs changed); the mirrors restamped.

- **Skill blocks run in a worktree-isolated Claude Code session** ([#264](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/264), [#218](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/218)). The guard there refuses compound shell, so every faithful run used to write throwaway helper scripts. Each refused shape, (1)–(8) and (a)–(d), now has a plain single-command form or a shipped script in the block that owns it: `scripts/gh_token_env.sh` replaces every `export GH_TOKEN=$(gh auth token)` (and takes `--path <dir>` for a session-bound nen); `scripts/nen_reach_probe.sh` is ten § 2c's login-shell probe; en § 6 watches through `nen watch until --pr`; hanten § 4 names the read-only reviewer fallback for harness-isolated sessions and what protects the author's tree; `scripts/surface_mirror_regenerate.sh` is great-hiker § 4's one-call regeneration, with a fixture proving parity with the drift check. Three lanes guard the scripts (`gh-token-guard`, `reach-probe-guard`, `mirror-regenerate-guard`). `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue* owns the refused-shape table, and `docs/ab/worktree-isolation.md` records this session's refusals verbatim — the Write and Edit tools refused the effort path while Bash refused nothing, which is recorded, not called accepted. The `&&` gates on squash, publish and public comments stay chained (#170, #188's `publish-integrity-guard`). `docs/using/mirrors.md` § 3 runs the regenerator too, which now sources the drift check's own table rather than copying it; both scripts read a bare `nen` on `PATH` only (a Hatsu root's `nen/` directory used to pass for the binary); `gh_token_env.sh` never traces the token under `bash -x`. Blocks still carrying a refused shape (`murasaki` § 6, `susanoo` § 5a) are listed, and a refusal of any unlisted block is a named stop. **Three G4 questions on the desk:** #188's required push chain in `murasaki` § 6 holds a refused `$(…)` — split it, ship it as a script, or rule it exempt; hanten § 4's read-only reviewer fallback, on an observed refusal only, is an exception to the 2026-09-30 worktree ruling; and which page owns the skill-shell writing rule (WORKFLOW, `claude/rules/hatsu.md` or PROCESS, where it sits now). Owed to nen, filed at the run's third-hand harvest: `commit format --body-file`, `issue file --title-file`, `shu test` over several lanes in one call (judged needed), and a verb generating every declared surface. Plugin bumped to 0.104.0; mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

- **Overlapping efforts are delivered as a stack, never raced against the trunk** ([#259](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/259), [#219](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/219); the maintainer's words of 2026-10-12, recorded in `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-12*). `docs/WORKFLOW.md` gains § *Stacked pull requests*, stated once and cited by futon § 5 and breath § 3: when to stack (a common path; the manifests and mirrors count, fragments do not collide; the default on overlap, futon § 5's integration branch the typed alternative), how to cut (each effort from the previous one's published branch, a local branch of that name needed, a delegate from the stack's tip), one mechanical commit per PR at stack time (bump per CONTRIBUTING, `changelog.d/` fragment, stamp-only mirror churn; a catch-up over it conflicts on the manifests and stamps and is resolved by regenerating), one PR per issue based on the previous effort's branch with only the bottom one on `branch.base` and presented nowhere as mergeable (ruling 2 of 2026-09-29), bottom-up merging with `--merge` and the head branch deleted so GitHub retargets the next PR to `branch.base` — else `nen pr retarget` — with en § 5's checks re-run before the one `MERGE` ask, and the order said as a plan line on the register desk and in every PR body. Plugin bumped to 0.99.0; mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

- **Skill prose no longer asks for a CI plane Hatsu does not have** ([#261](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/261), [#220](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/220); the trace that made `build` request a nonexistent routing mid-epic and forced [HA#200](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/200)'s amendment). `senkei`, `backlog-state`, `jujisho`, `tensho`, `file`, `sharingan`, the skills README, `kurapika.md`, `gon.md` and ROSTER § *The human gates* drop `bankai:agent/*` routing, `bankai:wake/iterate` fires, "Roy's lane" and `bankai:observation-fix`. A routed-but-unstarted row reads *mode-approved, not yet released*, with the building label as the release (WORKFLOW § *Releasing an issue into `building`*). A sub-PR on an `integration/*` base is the epic's own merge into its integration branch (CON-5's carve-out, which Hatsu has no actor to exercise), and only its integration → trunk PR is gated; the CON-34 `bankai:observation-fix` exception is kept, scoped to a legacy CI-stamped PR, since the constitution still names that merge as the maintainer's. A stalled PR goes to `sharingan`'s channel, never a label fire (futon § 7). Each prohibition line says *no routing label*. `sharingan` § 7 keeps its one wake, for a legacy CI-authored PR, under the label the target's `nen/labels.json` declares (none declared is a G5 naming the gap), its ladder limited to that row; every other mention cites WORKFLOW § *Building an issue with no CI plane* rather than restating it. The live consumer taxonomy (`bankai:severity/*`, `bankai:stage/*`, `bankai:epic`, `bankai:handbook-question`) is untouched; `docs/PUBLIC-REDACTION.md`'s namespace example and the dated records under `docs/ab/` and `docs/history/` are left as they are. Plugin bumped to 0.101.0 (wording in shipped skills and agents); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

- **The Rikugan register's data document is the verb's** ([#255](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/255), [#221](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/221), zheref/nen#276): `backlog-board` § 3 and `rikugan` §§ 3–4 call `nen report data … --register <desk>` and pipe its document into `nen report render --variant register|final`; the hand-built register builder is retired. The desk a run composes is the judgement half only (variant, title, scope, gate, the asks per gate with their lettered options and one `star`, optional row marks), every fact — notation, verdict, labels, checks, threads, links, head, the five tallies and the spend from the ledgers — is nen's, and a desk that carries a `verdict`, or any key nen does not read, is refused; rikugan reads the facts in a plain pass first and composes the desk from them. Canon now writes down the five tally definitions nen 0.21.0 implements: `tallyScope` (the objects in scope), `tallyNeedsYou` (the asks across every gate), `tallyBlockers` (open PRs with a red check, an unresolved thread or a `DIRTY` merge state), `tallyReady` (open PRs whose verdict is `ready` and that are not blockers), `tallyInFlight` (the remaining open PRs). `black-voice` § 6's per-criterion rows are not objects `--register` reads, so that caller keeps the hand-built document until nen carries criterion rows (a nen follow-up). The end-to-end futon run rendered this way — the run that lands this stack — is owed to `docs/ab/futon.md` on this branch before its PR opens. Plugin bumped to 0.96.0; mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

- **A subagent's tier is its role's, never its caller's: reviewers stay `deep` when `direct` sends the session lower** ([#281](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/281), [#222](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/222); the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-13, recorded in `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-13*). `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § `models` states it once and drops the "a delegate that outranks its caller has inverted the delegation" rationale: `hatsu:hanten` raises each reviewer at its scope's declared tier (`models.roles.reviewer`'s `deep` unless `review.scopes.<scope>.tier` says otherwise) whatever tier `hatsu:direct` recommended for the maintainer's own session, since adversarial review wants the deepest tier its scope allows; the frontier-tier rule is unchanged. `hanten` § 4, `direct` §§ 4–5, `claude/agents/kurapika.md` and the registry's companion comment cite it, and WORKFLOW § `direct` no longer calls the question open; `nen/workflow.json` → `models.roles.$comment` names the rule. Plugin bumped to 0.107.3 (shipped docs, two shipped skills and the shipped workflow declaration changed); the mirrors restamped.

- **The canon data contracts are checked by `surface-mirror-check` on every same-repository, non-draft pull request** ([#283](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/283), [#223](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/223), step two of the two-step landing). The workflow gains the frozen `Canon data contracts check` step after the drift step: on a drift-clean tree, the trusted checkout's `scripts/canon_contracts_check.sh` runs the trusted, digest-checked nen's validators over the pull request's `contracts/direct.registry.json`, `contracts/classify.taxonomy.json` and `nen/labels.json`, its output fenced; an invariant nen 0.21.0 states that breaks fails the `surface-mirror-check` check (the two phase-coverage invariants are nen's to add, requested through the harvest). The step first runs live on the first pull request opened after it reaches that pull request's base. `scripts/workflow_runner_policy_check.rb` narrows `EXPECTED_STEPS` to the new shape (one shape admitted again) and its self-test refuses a file without the step, the step moved, doubled or re-bodied; `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` records both steps landed. Plugin bumped to 0.107.5 (the validator, the contract and shipped docs changed); the mirrors restamped.

- **A canon data contract owes a guard lane, and the guard is nen's own validators** ([#282](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/282), [#223](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/223); the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-13, recorded in `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-13*). `scripts/canon_contracts_check.sh` runs `nen direct registry` over `contracts/direct.registry.json`, `nen classify labels` over `contracts/classify.taxonomy.json` and `nen classify install --dry-run` for `nen/labels.json` parity with the taxonomy, re-implementing no invariant (BC-6, BC-11); `scripts/canon_contracts_check_fixture.sh` breaks one invariant per case and proves each read refuses; lane `canon-contracts-guard` seats it (`nen shu test --lane canon-contracts-guard`). Both verbs exist at the pinned nen `0.21.0`, so the lane is green now. CI runs the guard as a `Canon data contracts check` step of `surface-mirror-check.yml`, landed in two pull requests under the frozen-step rule: this one teaches `scripts/workflow_runner_policy_check.rb` the step beside the current shape and freezes its one form (`CANON_CONTRACTS_FROZEN`: the trusted script on the trusted nen over the pull request's data, the output fenced), with self-test cases for the admitted shape and nine hostile edits; the next adds the step to the workflow. The registry's `$comment` names its validators and the lane; `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` records the landing. Plugin bumped to 0.107.4 (a shipped guard, the validator, the contract and shipped docs changed); the mirrors restamped.

- **`build` now states the three things a run had to guess** ([#258](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/258), [#229](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/229)). § 4: what build itself writes to the ledgers — `nen phase begin|end` around its four phases (`author`, `publish`, `pr`, `readiness`) and `nen usage record` at every report — is owed on every target; on a target whose `nen/labels.json` declares no delivery stages WORKFLOW § *A target repository with no delivery-stage taxonomy* holds as written, cited, and build adds only that the release into building is the typed invocation and that § 1's handoff of a PR to `en` holds. § 1: P1 `hatsu:ten build` is never skipped, and on a self-hosting build a host `nen` behind the pin is ten's `BEHIND`/`WRONG` row and install, while a checkout ahead of every release keeps a `nen` that satisfies the pin on `PATH` (ten § 2's `current` or `kept-newer`) and exercises the code under build from the checkout itself, said in every brief. § 3: one checkout per concurrent effort (WORKFLOW § *Where worktrees live*, step 2) — a second issue never starts in an occupied worktree, and a tree a reviewer is reading is never switched under them (hanten § 4's detached checkout, or, where the isolation hook refused it, waiting for the review to return; a moved ref is hanten § 4's G5). § 7 names every way the run ends, with the fall-through a G5, and § 8 names its residue. No new verb: the cut stays breath's `nen shu warmup`. Several passages (§§ 1, 2, 2b, 4, 5–9) were compressed to pay for it under the 12288-byte ceiling, the authority and hard-limit items kept. Plugin bumped to 0.98.0; mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

- **Step one of the CI nen-digest check: the workflow-policy validator learns the digest step that sits beside each bootstrap, and the bootstrap form that goes with it** ([#268](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/268), [#230](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/230)). The validator judges every pull request from the base, so the step is registered one PR before the live workflows take it. Beside the digest step the bootstrap no longer runs the binary: `nen --version` moves into the digest step, after the match, and a bootstrap that still prints the version beside it is refused as the old order. The frozen body tests the contract before reading it, fails a hash it cannot take by name, and reads the value whole, so a trailing newline cannot pass as a digest; it is proven under bash against a scratch binary and contract, eleven cases per body, the binary running only once it matched. Where the root holds `templates/pr-readiness.yml` (Hatsu itself), tenkai's rendered readiness form is refused, so no pull request can trade the checked bootstrap for the consumer's. No workflow, contract or shipped surface changes in this step.

- **`surface-mirror-check.yml`, `pr-readiness.yml` and `surface-mirror-regenerate.yml` hash the binary the bootstrap printed and compare it with the trusted checkout's `dependency.bootstrap.sha256["linux-x64"]`** ([#269](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/269), [#230](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/230)). Each fails closed on a mismatch, a missing or malformed key (a trailing newline included) or a missing contract, naming expected and observed. Nothing runs the binary before the match: the bootstrap step now prints only its path, and `nen --version` runs last in the check. The bootstrap script verifies the binary against `SHA256SUMS` from the same release, so a moved tag or replaced assets verified against themselves. The change landed in two steps: the validator learned the step and the bootstrap's next form first, then the live workflows took them and Hatsu's own forms were narrowed to carrying them, frozen (`NEN_DIGEST_FROZEN`, `NEN_BOOTSTRAP_NEXT`); in Hatsu's own repository tenkai's rendered readiness form is refused. The live step matches the published v0.21.0 linux-x64 binary and fails a one-byte tamper without running it. **A repin moves the linux-x64 digest with `pinned_ref`, in the same pull request,** or every pull request after it fails closed: `hatsu:ten` § 1, `docs/NEN-CONTRACT.md` and `docs/config/contract.json.md` say so, and `scripts/nen_global_fixture_check.sh` case 21c refuses a pin whose record was left behind. Tenkai's rendered readiness form carries no digest step yet, and the optional bootstrap-script digest is not recorded: both are owed, filed by the run's harvest. Whether this check is BC-11 bootstrap-class shell is an open G4 question, recorded in `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md`. Plugin bumped to 0.105.0; mirrors restamped.

- **Register forbidden commit trailer guard ahead of its required check** ([#275](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/275), [#235](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/235), 0.109.0)

  The authorship guard can read `commits.forbiddenTrailers` from trusted configuration and inspect every PR commit. The workflow policy admits the upcoming required-check step while retaining the current shape during the two-step landing.

- **Enforce forbidden commit trailers at the required PR check** ([#276](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/276), [#235](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/235), 0.110.0)

  The required plugin-bump check now runs the trusted authorship guard over every PR commit, reading the forbidden keys from the trusted workflow configuration. It catches commits made outside Kokusen.

- Keep the authorship guard’s own exit as the squash and publish gate, and document the maintainer’s lease-protected repair for published provenance failures. ([#277](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/277))

- **A consumer's first opt-in to `direct.publish` is two steps, never one PR** ([#257](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/257), [#240](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/240)): `direct.publish` and the `model/` declaration are read at the default branch's tip, never the checkout's head (as `aiReviewers` is, SEC-6); where the key reads `none` there (absent, `none`, any other value) nothing publishes, § 8's typed **B**/**C** sweep answer being the maintainer's one exception; the labels are declared and the key set in the consumer's own PR and merged — the registry's `publish.label.declaredIn` and ROSTER's *the declaration PR first, GitHub after* (ruling of 2026-10-04), cited — and the sync and the applies follow from the next sitting. No ad-hoc waiver: the one the 2026-10-05 opt-in PR carried is history, not canon. **The label ledger's expected path is `<checkout>/.nen/direct/label-ledger.jsonl`**: every `nen label apply` under § 7 and the § 8 sweep passes `--ledger` with it after `mkdir -p` (nen creates no parent, and its default lands beside `changelog.d/` in the tracked tree — the registry's `publish.label.verb` carries the flag now), a Hatsu-side default until zheref/nen#391 moves the verb's. A reader can now tell from the skill alone what to do when the key is absent. Six passages were shortened to pay for the rule under the ceiling. Plugin bumped to 0.97.0; mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

- **No skill, skill index or agent file cites `docs/SURFACES.md` any more** ([#249](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/249), [#242](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/242)). The shared policy preamble that 21 skills carry now lists `docs/using/README.md` and `docs/using/mirrors.md` in place of `docs/SURFACES.md`; the nine section citations name the page and section that hold the content today — `docs/using/README.md` § *The four surfaces, compared* (`mukai` § 1's permissions row, `bakuryuha` § 1's install rows, `jutaisho` § 4's native-picker row), `docs/surfaces/README.md` § *The skeleton* (`great-hiker` § 1's frontmatter budget, each surface guide's § 2) and § *Shared rules* (`breath` § 2b's `info/exclude` rule), § *Targeting a local checkout* (`bakuryuha` § 5's inlined pin), `docs/using/mirrors.md` § 1 (`pr-state` § 2's mirror note, `claude/skills/README.md`'s authority line), `ten` § 0 and § 5's two short-name citations; `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` points at `mirrors.md` § 3 for the regenerate loop. `limbo` takes the one-line `**Shared policy:**` preamble seven dieted skills already use, and `breath` shortens two phrases, so both stay under their 12288-byte ceiling; the Claude marketplace description points at `docs/using/mirrors.md` too. `docs/SURFACES.md` stays as the redirect stub: the fixture `scripts/surface_link_check_fixture.sh` and the dated evidence under `docs/ab/` still name it, and removing it is a separate decision. Plugin bumped to 0.94.1 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); the three mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

- **#289's follow-ups, step one** ([#300](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/300), [#289](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/289); `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-17*). The workflow validator refuses the PR-root script spellings its self-test names (the original pattern kept whole, command-position spellings added), refuses an empty workflow file with a policy line, holds the *Surface-mirror drift check* step's body frozen, and admits the digest step's next form — the closing `nen --version` echo made fatal — beside the current one for the two-step landing; WORKFLOW's stacked-PR rule says retarget first and delete after, naming the trusted revision as `main`'s head; `nen/repos.json` declares Hatsu's scenario; `surface_mirror_check.sh --installed` and PROCESS read `nen surface status` / `nen surface mirror check`; hanten, direct, build and ten take a second diet; the decisions docs drop `nen stop --options`; jutaisho § 5's fallback rings through `scripts/bell_notify.sh` (values after `--`, the title prefixed, 240 characters, Glass fallback; lane `bell-guard`); `file` § 8 carries the edit-history read-back and deletion hand-over and `mugetsu` § 7 cites the rule. Minor bump to 0.112.0 (behaviour changed and a shipped script removed); the mirrors restamped.


- **`scripts/plugin_cache_check.sh`, its fixture and lane `plugin-cache-guard`** (#289 item 6): nen 0.21.0 owns the comparison (zheref/nen#339). **The `osascript *` allow row** of `contracts/permissions.json` (#289 item 9, the ruling of 2026-10-17): an installed Claude Code pack keeps the row in `settings.local.json` until the maintainer removes it by hand; Cursor's pack is overwritten on install.

- **The tree's older private-repository mentions read as legend placeholders** ([#305](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/305), [#289](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/289) item 10; `docs/PUBLIC-REDACTION.md` § *Legend*, `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-17*). A public repository whose name contains a private one keeps its own name and is never composed with a placeholder; the predecessor generator's marker stays as the exempt exact shape limbo matches. Three entries that are ordinary English words stay as words, handed to the maintainer's ignore file by index, and the tree check lands as the `redaction-tree` lane (#149 criterion 2(b)).
