- **The validator's self-test accepts a tree that already carries the digest step's fatal echo** ([#304](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/304), [#289](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/289) item 3, step one's follow-up; `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` § 6 *Adoption*, the two-step-landing note of 2026-09-20). Step one built the self-test's refused third form from whichever digest form the tree lacked, so on a tree carrying the fatal `--version` echo the old form was substituted unchanged and accepted, and `main`'s trusted copy refused step two's PR by its own self-test. The third form is now built from the fatal form whichever one the tree carries; the self-test passes over `main`'s tree and over step two's. Plugin bumped to 0.112.1 (a shipped script changed); the mirrors restamped.

- **#289 step two** ([#303](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/303), [#289](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/289); `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-17*). The three guard workflows' digest steps make the closing `nen --version` echo fatal, so a binary that matches its sha256 but cannot run fails the step; the validator's frozen digest form is that body alone and the old echo is refused as a shape change in each workflow. Plugin bumped to 0.112.1 (three workflows and the validator changed); the mirrors restamped.

- **The handbook questions of #290 and two items of #289, ruled through the picker on 2026-10-17 and built** ([#298](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/298), [#290](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/290), [#289](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/289); `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-17*). **An on-device act is a G5** (reversing 2026-10-13's #176): row `on-device-act` is `human-gate` G5, WORKFLOW § 4 lists it as a recorded kind and drops its not-a-gap entry, and the rules file, lead persona, `jusshin`, `jujutsu`, PROCESS, README and docs/config read the same. **G5 is an open class** — what only the maintainer can do — and the "five G5s stay five" sentences are gone; the list is the recorded kinds, now seven. **`secret-shape` stays a G5**; the not-a-gap entry covers only a secret typed into chat. **BC-11 is enforced as written**: the three shell verdict scripts are interim until nen ships the verbs. The `osascript *` row keeps a note; the drift-check step is to be frozen (both built with #289). Plugin bumped to 0.111.3 (shipped canon, the rules file and skills changed); the mirrors restamped.

- **Pickers carry one short question; every hand-over command is a runnable block** ([#299](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/299), [#294](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/294), [#295](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/295); `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-17*). `jutaisho` § 4 says where a stop's four parts go — the banner, link and commands in the chat message, the picker carrying one question of at most two sentences and options of at most eight words with the ⭐ one first, a free-text answer being the maintainer's word; WORKFLOW § 4 states the hand-over rule — complete, one command per fenced `bash` block, where to run it said, the Claude desktop app's Run button the hand-over there and the fence the copy source elsewhere; PROCESS, the rules file, the lead persona, `en`, `tenkai` and the four surface guides cite both, `classify` and `third-hand` the shape, `file`, `bakuryuha`, `mugetsu` and `getsuga` the hand-over; every report template (`rikugan` and `spiritual-message`, HTML and Markdown) renders an ask's command as a block only when one is given. Plugin bumped to 0.111.4 (shipped canon, skills, a template changed); the mirrors restamped.

- **`hatsu:kazaana <repo> [on <line>]` — the surface-matched backlog, drawn in and delivered** ([#278](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/278), the maintainer's request of 2026-10-13, recorded in `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-13*). One `futon` run under the hood — `hatsu:futon <repo>@model/<line> then getsuga`, the line read from `contracts/direct.registry.json` as the session's surface's `deep`-tier alias's `line`, a typed `on <line>` overriding it — with five differences the skill owns: issues carrying no `model/` label are directed first (`hatsu:direct` over exactly those, one confirmation for the batch, published under `direct.publish` as read at the default branch's tip) and the backlog refetched; each queued issue is authored and published by a delegate in its own worktree under `<core>/.nen/worktrees/<surface>/` on `direct`'s primary pick — never the frontier tier — up to 7 at once, `ren` (or `great-hiker`'s authoring on a canon repository, in the delegate at its tier) then `aka`, while the main session runs `mukai` and `en` per effort (a subagent raises no subagent); efforts stack only on overlap (WORKFLOW § *Stacked pull requests*) and otherwise target `branch.base`, the final register carrying the merge order bottom-up; once every PR is merged or closed by the maintainer, `getsuga` runs with its release proposal driven by `en` and merged by § 3a — **never the maintainer's to merge**: transient conditions are held through the observation hold, and a gate the verb refuses on is a G5 halt handing the exact `--run` command, never a `MERGE` ask; then one unstarred picker question (row `publish-offer`, process and library repositories only) whether `mugetsu` should publish the tag, whose affirmative answer is the maintainer's go — form (4) of `mugetsu` § 0. The typed `hatsu:kazaana` is the advance go for the `then getsuga` step it composes. `getsuga` § 3a and `mugetsu` §§ 0, 1 and 3 cite the ruling; `nen/decisions.json` gains the row. **Owed to nen, named as residue**: `nen pr merge --release-unit` reading `aiReviewers`, so a proposal failing only on `rounds-owed` under `optional`/`off` can merge inside § 3a's bounds.
- **Shizuku activates from the Genei Ryodan bench as cleanup, and `hatsu:cleanup [for <scope>]` is her skill** (ruling 2026-10-13; `OPEN-3` closes further — Machi, Kortopi and Pakunoda remain bench only). `claude/agents/shizuku.md` lands on the `fast` tier at effort `medium` with `Read, Grep, Glob, Bash`, listed in `plugin.json`, header `🧹 **Shizuku · cleanup**`, closing line `Shizuku-Sweep: clean ✅ | partial ⚠️ | nothing to clean ⚪ | unread ❌`. In two passes — inventory, then the picked rows — she reads what the effort used and what prior sessions left uncleaned (`nen wc worktrees`, the merged and closed PRs per branch, the remote heads origin still carries, `Reports/current.html` and captures, the bootstrap cache against the pin and the host links, `nen runner inventory`, draft releases, Actions caches and artifacts, `du -sk`) and returns a plan in three classes — **deterministic** rows (a mechanical fact decides: a merged PR's clean worktree and merged branch, its remote head where the SHA is the PR's `headRefOid`, a prunable entry, this sitting's returned reviewer checkouts, a stale tracking ref, `current.html`), **proposed** rows (uncommitted work listed, a closed-unmerged or PR-less branch, a prior session's checkouts, a cache binary below the pin with its spelled command) and **named** rows (plan only: billed resources, the surface's own worktrees, a locked or open-PR worktree) — with every repository-sourced name validated before it enters a command. One multi-select picker (row `cleanup-plan`), nothing removed before the answer, `.nen/` ledgers never a leftover, every removal read back with the bytes freed, the marker at `.nen/cleanup/<slug>.json`. **Owed to nen**: `git worktree remove|prune`, `git branch -d`, `git push origin --delete`, `git remote prune`, `du -sk` as `nen wc prune` (named residue). The permission pack gains a `du *` row for the inventory.
- **The wrap-up is a picker, and `cleanup` is required after a completed session** (ruling 2026-10-13, 5). At every session delivery — a PR at `en` § 5's merge prompt or merged, a composite's run ending — the caller asks once (row `wrap-up`): ⭐ `cleanup` · `cleanup` and `third-hand` in parallel · continue iterating. `third-hand` is optional from this ruling and is offered nowhere else; the wrap-up names no human-called phase and is never a merge prompt. `en` § 8, `build` phase 6, `futon` § 10, `backlog-loop` § 10, `third-hand` §§ 0–2, `kurapika.md`, `claude/rules/hatsu.md`, WORKFLOW § 4, STANDALONE-ENTRY, DISCOVERY and both READMEs say so (with one-line Shizuku additions in `claude/agents/netero.md`, `claude/commands/kurapika.md` and `docs/PROCESS.md`'s residue rows); ruling 1 of 2026-09-09 is amended in place. `kazaana` and `cleanup` join the dieted skills (`scripts/prose_size_check.sh`), both authored under the ceiling. Plugin bumped to 0.108.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp with nen `v0.21.0`.

- **Five small rulings of 2026-10-13, recorded in `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-13* and built here.** ([#288](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/288)) (1) In the four report templates `nen report render` fills (`templates/rikugan.{md,html}`, `templates/spiritual-message.{md,html}` under this checkout, by real path), a `{{…}}` token of its language is a nen data-document key: `scripts/private_name_check.sh` exempts it in that exact shape only, on the line as written (prose, comments, spaced or unbalanced braces, a split or entity-spelt token and any other file still hit, the fixture proving each), the legend heading was renamed `Symbol`, and two template comments that named a consumer repository now say "a consumer repository's"; a dictionary-word private name stays the maintainer's ignore file, and the nen request — the pre-filing check reading that ignore file, and nothing wider — goes through the harvest. (2) `contracts/` and `templates/` stay in zheref/hatsu's process gate set: `contracts/pr-composition.json`'s `$comment` records the confirmation. (3) A stop that is not a gate is a refusal, never a G5: `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Reporting a phase* says so, citing WORKFLOW § 4. (4) A stacked effort's local slot frees at PR-open: already WORKFLOW § *Stacked pull requests* rule 4; the `nen loop slots` request goes through the harvest. (5) The CI nen-digest step is workflow glue under BC-11's bootstrap allowance: `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` records the ruling in place of its open question. Plugin bumped to 0.107.9 (a shipped guard, three shipped templates, a contract and shipped docs changed); the mirrors restamped.

## v0.94.0 — nen v0.21.0, the gates keys retired, prompt on hanten, per-consumer AI reviewers, opt-in direct publishing and the README redesign

Three deliveries merged since `v0.93.0`, folded here from their `changelog.d/` fragments, newest first. `v0.93.1` and `v0.93.2` were never tagged and ship inside `v0.94.0`.

**Net effect at `v0.94.0`:** Hatsu is pinned to nen `v0.21.0` (minimum `0.21`), and its `nen/gates.json` carries only keys nen reads (#245). A Hatsu run on a PR into `main` reads `nen/workflow.json` → `aiReviewers` at the base and default tip, and Hatsu's own value is `optional`: bots are requested but never waited on (#245). A `hatsu:direct` verdict on a Hatsu issue publishes as a `model/` label plus one marker comment (#244). The README is the cheatsheet, and the long-form surface and configuration guides live under `docs/using/` and `docs/config/` (#241); the pin text there reads `v0.21.0` after #245.

### `nen` v0.21.0, the two Hatsu gates keys retired, prompt on hanten, and AI reviewers per consumer ([#245](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/245), 0.94.0)

- **nen is pinned at `v0.21.0` (minimum `0.21`), and Hatsu's `nen/gates.json` carries only keys nen reads** — the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-04, *retire 2, adopt 2* (zheref/nen#310; [#227](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/227); [#245](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/245)). nen `v0.21.0` refuses every gates key it does not read, so the repin and the cleanup land together:
  - **`check_exclusions` → nen's `checks.excluded`** (`{name, match, reason, ruled, until}`, zheref/nen#249). Hatsu's list was empty and is declared empty in nen's form. `nen pr ready` reads it at the pull request's base and applies it itself, so `pr-state` § 2, `sharingan` § 4 and `en` § 6 pass no `--exclude-check` for a declared ruling and quote the verdict as printed. `jusshin` § 8 edits the new key.
  - **`reviewer_fallback` deleted**, superseded by `round_quorum` and zheref/nen#361 (a met quorum fulfils an exhausted member's round). Unavailability is now observed, never declared: `nen pr request-reviews` exit `9`, the bot in `unrecordedBots` (zheref/nen#277), stated once in `sharingan` § 6. When every quorum member is unavailable at the judged head — the five-part predicate stated once in `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Reviewer rounds and review threads*, *The sixth stop*: a declared quorum, `rounds-owed` the only failing row, every member at exit `9` or with a round-check run at the judged head that completed without a round (never "no run at head"), each exit `9` bound to its head and recorded verbatim, and no reviewer outside `any_of` owed — reachable only where `reviewers[]` ⊆ `any_of`, since nen 0.21's `--json` lists no owed reviewers — the PR goes to `en` § 5's merge prompt on hanten's rounds with the not-ready verdict quoted verbatim, never called `ready` — the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-11, *Prompt on hanten*, recorded in `docs/ROSTER.md`. That is `en` § 6's sixth stop and `nen/decisions.json` row `reviewer-exhausted`'s default; PROCESS, WORKFLOW and the decisions guide say the same.
  - **`round_policy.minRounds`/`.maxRounds` stay, now nen's keys** (zheref/nen#240). `nen pr request-reviews` refuses a bot's request past `maxRounds` at exit `2`, and `pr ready --explain` prints requested and posted counts. nen counts `minRounds` against posted rounds, while `sharingan` § 6 counts resolved ones. The `$comment`, `sharingan` § 6, `cap-reached` and `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` § 3 say so.
  - `scripts/tenkai_adopt.sh`: one observation item, `gates/retired-keys`, replaces `gates/check-exclusions` and `gates/reviewer-fallback`. It names the move for either retired key and leaves nen's keys to `nen schema check`. Its self-test covers it. `cap-reached` reads exit `2` with a `refused:` line as the bound and any other exit `2` as usage.
  - The rest of v0.21.0's consumer notes, checked against Hatsu. `pr open`'s new exit `1` for a PR `gh` created before failing is covered by `shibari`'s existing rule (exit `1` names a PR; it opens once). `jusshin` names the `pool-<id>` label every pool now ends in, and `scripts/runner_preflight_fixture_check.sh` expects it in a rendered `runs-on` under nen 0.21+ (the declared set alone under an older binary). `deploy.defaultTarget` needs `project.targets.<name>.nonProduction: true` from nen 0.21: `scripts/config_values.sh set` now refuses a default target without it (self-test added), and `kagutsuchi`, PROCESS, the contract and workflow guides and the catalogue name the typed mark. Hatsu declares no runners and no `select` block, and its callers pass `--requirements-from`.
  - `nen/contract.json` repinned: `minimum` `0.21`, `pinned_ref` `v0.21.0`, the bootstrap URL, and the three release digests from v0.21.0's `SHA256SUMS`. The bootstrap commands in `install_paths.nen_absent` and the halt template pass `--source zheref/nen`, as does `fetch_must_be_two_step.right`. The convenience copies follow it (`docs/NEN-CONTRACT.md`, `docs/WORKFLOW.md`, `docs/config/contract.json.md`, the marketplace descriptions, `surface-mirror-check.yml`, `futon`, `classify`, `templates/runner-preflight.yml`). The `round_policy` default file and its guide say the caps are nen's keys now. The base-read claim for `checks.excluded` is qualified for stacked and integration bases (SEC-6).
  - The plugin is bumped to 0.94.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay). The mirrors are regenerated at that stamp with nen `v0.21.0`.
  - **`aiReviewers` — AI reviewers per consumer** ([#245](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/245), the maintainer's rulings of 2026-10-11). A top-level `nen/workflow.json` key, `required | optional | off`, for every automated reviewer in `nen/gates.json` → `reviewers[]`, gating both their use and their expectation. Absent reads `required`, today's behaviour. `optional` requests within the caps and never waits; `off` never requests, so there is zero AI-reviewer spend. Under both, a PR failing only on `rounds-owed`, with hanten settled, goes to the merge prompt on hanten's rounds with the not-ready verdict quoted verbatim. This returns the off switch that deleting `reviewer_fallback` removed. The rule is stated once in `docs/PROCESS.md` (*AI reviewers per consumer*); `sharingan`, `en`, `pr-state`, WORKFLOW, the new `nen/decisions.json` row `ai-reviewers` and ROSTER cite it. It is catalogued as `workflow.aiReviewers` (enum, default `required`, asked at adoption), and `config_values.sh` sets, diagnoses and refuses it, with self-tests. Hatsu's own value is `optional`. The value is read at the PR's base and the default branch's tip, the stricter applying and never the PR's own head (SEC-6), so a change takes effect once merged. The hanten prompt needs every `reviewers[]` entry to be in `round_quorum.any_of` with an anchored `login_pattern`, since nen exposes no reviewer kind. `round_policy.maxRounds: 0` was never an off switch.

### Hatsu's own `direct.publish` is `label+comment` ([#244](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/244), 0.93.2)

- **Hatsu's own `direct.publish` is `label+comment`**, on the maintainer's request of 2026-10-11 ("Open a PR setting direct.publish to label and comment"), written with `config_values.sh set`: a `hatsu:direct` run here — under `build`, standalone on an issue, or the `backlog` sweep — now applies the primary pick's `model/<line>` label through `nen label apply` and posts one marker-keyed issue comment with the three picks, tier, surface, effort dial and the live-read version, closing with the `Direct:` trailer; `ren` stays report-only. The default in the catalogue, `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 2 and `docs/config/workflow.json.md` is unchanged (`none`); the config page records Hatsu's own value beside it, as `mukai.autoEn` is. The first sweep ran the same day with the key still `none` and, on the maintainer's word, applied its seven labels as if it were already set (`model/gpt` on #243, #238, #236, #235, #234; `model/claude` on #242, #240), ledgered under `.nen/direct/label-ledger.jsonl`; the comments wait for this value. ([#244](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/244))

### The README as a cheatsheet, with surface and configuration prose under `docs/using` and `docs/config` ([#241](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/241), 0.93.1)

- **The README is a cheatsheet, and the surface and configuration prose moved where a reader expands into it.** `README.md` (399 lines, from 1,266) carries a start-here table, a per-surface quickstart, the request flow and the skill composition graph as Mermaid, a *Workflows* section (each with its prerequisites, its prompt per surface and the chain that runs), the roster, every skill with the skills it calls, the model tiers per surface with `docs/DIRECT-MATRIX.md` linked, the gates and the Nen contract in six lines. New: `docs/using/` (the surface hub, one guide per surface in the same order, requirements → setup → usage → maintenance → uninstall → caveats, and `mirrors.md` for the generated-mirror mechanics), `docs/config/` (one page per expected configuration file, key by key, plus the plugin-owned registries), `docs/NEN-CONTRACT.md` (the D10 section whole), `docs/history/release-highlights.md` (the README's former release notes, verbatim) and `CONTRIBUTING.md`. **`docs/SURFACES.md` is superseded** by `docs/using/README.md` and `docs/using/mirrors.md` and remains as a redirect stub whose `§ 3` and `§ 4` anchors still land, so every path citing it in the skills, the mirrors and the evidence keeps resolving. Plugin bumped to 0.93.1 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay) for the stub; mirrors regenerated at that stamp, bodies unchanged. ([#241](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/241))

## v0.93.0 — the registry rebalanced for cost, three picks per cell, Grok on Cursor, and the model matrix as a readable page

Two deliveries merged since `v0.92.0`, folded here from their `changelog.d/` fragments, newest first. `v0.92.1` was never tagged and ships inside `v0.93.0`.

**Net effect at `v0.93.0`:** `contracts/direct.registry.json` carries twelve aliases and three picks per cell (primary, fallback, recommended) by #237, and the matrix page #233 introduced now renders that registry — #233's entry below describes the page as it first landed (ten aliases, winner and runner-up), superseded inside this same release; its generator and guard are unchanged in shape and gained the registry's invariants.

### The registry rebalance: Sol as the execution default, Grok on Cursor, three picks per cell, opt-in publish and the backlog sweep ([#237](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/237), 0.93.0)

- **`contracts/direct.registry.json` — the registry rebalance of 2026-10-05.** The execution default moves from the frontier tier (GPT Astra) to the deep tier (GPT Sol) as `EXECUTION_FRONTIER`; Astra becomes the `EXECUTION_MAX` escalation, mirroring `SEMANTIC_MAX`; Terra becomes `EXECUTION_VALUE` on the fast tier. Grok on Cursor enters as `EDITOR_FRONTIER` (Cursor's deep tier): the primary on the eight editor-shaped TypeScript cells and the cross-provider fallback for execution work. Every cell now yields **three picks** — primary (cost-aligned best), fallback (on another provider or surface in every cell; 66 cells moved in all, 60 of them to satisfy `picks.fallbackRule`) and recommended (cost-agnostic, by `picks.recommended`). Each alias carries its model **line** (`claude | gpt | gemini | grok | composer`) and **escalation**; the snapshot is re-read live with list prices and served ids per surface; Codex's dial gains `max`, Cursor's `xhigh`; Grok Build and Copilot are listed as non-actionable surfaces. Standalone `review` cells route to the semantic reader. The realised saving lags the price sheet where a surface still serves an older id: Codex serves `gpt-5.6-sol` at $4 / $20 today, recorded in `snapshot.served`. (the maintainer's rulings of 2026-10-05, `docs/ROSTER.md`; [#237](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/237))
- **`hatsu:direct` reports three picks on five axes** — line, tier, version and effort dial (read live), surface — compares the session against the whole set (`within the registry's set`, one line, never asked), keeps companion jobs (`review`, `delivery-ops`, flagged in `contracts/classify.taxonomy.json`) out of the verb call that decides and reports them from a second call, names the other-pool aggregate fallback where the verb's runner-up shares the primary's pool (zheref/nen#389), and **publishes only where `nen/workflow.json` → `direct.publish` opts in**: `label` applies one `model/<line>` label through `nen label apply`; `label+comment` adds one marker-keyed issue comment with a `Direct:` trailer; `hatsu:direct backlog [<repo>]` sweeps every open classified issue — one table, one confirmation, then label and comment per issue (never under `ren`, which cannot leave the machine). The five `model/` labels are declared in `nen/labels.json` (sync after this lands). `build` § 2b and `ren` § 2 run the compare; `nen/decisions.json` row `direct-mismatch` reads the set.
- **`docs/DIRECT-MATRIX.md`** re-rendered: the alias table gains line, escalation and list price, a *Primary, fallback, recommended* section, served ids, the non-actionable surfaces and the live effort-dial column; `scripts/direct_matrix_doc.sh --self-test` now checks the registry's own invariants over every cell; `--verify` agrees on 2,019 printed claims at nen `v0.20.0`.
- **`docs/surfaces/cursor.md`**: the live `grok` resolve matches `grok-4.7-*` as well as `cursor-grok-*` (the served 4.7 ids carry no prefix, read 2026-10-05); Cursor's model sheet quoted.
- `contracts/config-catalogue.json` gains `workflow.direct.publish`; `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 2 gains the `direct` key block and its consequence paragraph. The plugin is bumped to 0.93.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay) and the mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

### `docs/DIRECT-MATRIX.md` — the model matrix, readable without running `hatsu:direct` ([#233](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/233), 0.92.1)

- **`docs/DIRECT-MATRIX.md` — the model matrix, readable without running `hatsu:direct`.** One page answers which model, surface and effort `direct` will recommend for any work:
  - the ten aliases, each with its provider, family, surface, the name you type and the dated snapshot model;
  - a grid of all 39 jobs × 5 domains naming each winner, with per-language winners inline, fallback routes marked, and the two cells no issue can reach (`parity` in feature and maintenance) shown as `—`;
  - five job × language matrices, one per domain as the registry routes them, each cell giving that pair's winner, runner-up, phase and interactive tool;
  - the domain rules, the fallback sentence and the aggregation rule, quoted from the data; the effort rule with job weights and each surface's dial;
  - seven worked examples with their restart lines. ([#233](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/233))
  - **Rendered, never hand-written.** `scripts/direct_matrix_doc.sh` builds the page from `contracts/direct.registry.json`, `contracts/classify.taxonomy.json` and `nen/workflow.json` → `models`, the files `nen direct resolve` reads.
    - `--check` fails on a stale or missing page.
    - `--self-test` proves the guard's negative paths on hermetic fixtures, then runs `--check`. It is the argv of the new focused lane `direct-matrix-guard` in `nen/contract.json`.
    - `--verify` asks `nen direct resolve` about **every claim the page prints**: 2,017 at nen `v0.20.0`, all agreeing. A planted renderer bug turns it red with 93 disagreements.
    - The rendering re-implements the verb's rules to work offline, and that re-implementation is named as residue in the script. Running both checks automatically, in CI on data-only edits and at every nen repin, is zheref/hatsu#234.
  - Linked from the README's introduction and from `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § *`direct` — the model, surface and effort*. The WORKFLOW paragraph and the new lane are shipped surfaces, so the plugin is bumped to 0.92.1 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay) and the mirrors are regenerated at that stamp.
Release unit for `v0.92.0..v0.93.0`: [#233](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/233) (the model matrix page, v0.92.1), [#237](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/237) (the registry rebalance, v0.93.0) and [#239](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/239) (this release proposal). `v0.92.1` was never tagged: its section ships inside `v0.93.0`. This line is where the range's PRs are cited, which is what `CON-33(c)` reconciles against.

## v0.92.0 — classify and direct route every issue, one bot reviewer suffices, and nen v0.20.0 is the pin

Six deliveries merged since `v0.86.0`, folded here from their `changelog.d/` fragments, newest first. `v0.87.0` through `v0.91.0` were never tagged and ship inside `v0.92.0`.

**Net effect at `v0.92.0`:** nen is pinned at `v0.20.0` (minimum `0.20`) by #231, which supersedes #226's `v0.19.0` pin inside this same release. The `nen classify` and `nen direct` families and `round_quorum`'s *fulfils* half — described in the #224, #225 and #228 entries below as waiting on the nen release after `v0.19.0` — ship in nen `v0.20.0` and run on that pin. Those entries are kept as written.

### nen repinned to v0.20.0, adopting classify and direct ([#231](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/231), 0.92.0)

- **nen repinned to v0.20.0, and the classify, direct and round-quorum canon now runs on the pinned build** ([zheref/hatsu#168](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/168)).
- **`nen/contract.json` → `dependency.minimum` moves `0.19` → `0.20`, and `pinned_ref` moves `v0.19.0` → `v0.20.0`** ([zheref/nen v0.20.0](https://github.com/zheref/nen/releases/tag/v0.20.0), released by [zheref/nen#384](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/384), published 2026-10-05 UTC).
  - The minimum moves for a feature and for the floor. Hatsu adopts the `nen classify` and `nen direct` families ([zheref/nen#370](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/370), [#371](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/371), carried to `main` by [#380](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/380)) that `hatsu:classify` and `hatsu:direct` execute, and `round_quorum` fulfilling its members' rounds ([zheref/nen#359](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/359)). v0.20.0 raises the compatibility floor to `0.20` for breaking consumer notes, so every 0.20+ build refuses a `0.19` pin at exit 5.
  - `pinned_ref_semantics` records the release and its darwin-arm64, linux-x64 and windows-x64 checksums, and `bootstrap.sha256` carries the same three.
  - The top `$comment`, the bootstrap `url` and `fetch`, the two-step `wrong`/`right`, both `install_paths` and the halt message point at `v0.20.0`.
  - The convenience copies follow: the pin line in the README's § *The range*, `docs/WORKFLOW.md`, the marketplace descriptions, `futon`'s pin line and `surface-mirror-check.yml`'s comment.
- **The canon that waited on this pin no longer does:** `classify` and `direct` residue, PROCESS § *Residue and owned dependencies* and the round-quorum paragraph name `v0.20.0` as the release that ships them; ROSTER's dated note of 2026-10-04 is marked superseded; `classify` reads bodies through `status --with-body` and syncs with `classify install --sync`.
- **v0.20.0's breaking consumer notes are absorbed:**
  - **ten § 1 and § 2c** — `nen shu tools` reports a host nen that meets the minimum but sits below `pinned_ref` as `BEHIND`, exit `7` ([zheref/nen#354](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/354)). § 1 routes it to § 2's install; § 2c reads it as installed, not reachable, never as a failed shell. `nen_global.sh` reads only `satisfied` on a strictly newer host and is unchanged.
  - **ao § 3** — `nen wc catch-up` exits `3` when every conflicted path is in a declared `mechanical` set, printing the commands and running none ([zheref/nen#368](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/368)). The table carries the row; § 4 takes over with nen's classes quoted. Hatsu declares no `mechanical` block, so its own catch-ups still stop at `1`.
  - **file § 4** — `nen issue file`, `comment` and `edit-body` refuse a private repository's name bound for a public target at exit `4`, writing nothing ([zheref/nen#367](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/367), closing [#329](https://github.com/zheref/nen/issues/329)). nen reads no default ignore file, so every such write passes the guard's own with `--private-names-ignore-file` where it exists; `private_name_check.sh` stays the first guard, and a nen `4` after the guard read `0` is the two guards disagreeing — stopped and reported, never redacted around. nen's exit `1` is read by what it shows: its guard's refusal, `issue file`'s label or title refusal, or an uncertain `edit-body` write read back through `--json`'s `outcome`. `--body-file` resolves against `--repo`'s root. `mugetsu` § 7, `sharingan` § 7 and `backlog-synthesis` point at it; PROCESS § Residue and PUBLIC-REDACTION no longer call #329 owed.
  - **murasaki § 6** routes catch-up exit `3` to ao; **ao § 4** reads `conflicted[]` at exit `1` or `3`. **breath § 2c** and the **kurapika** persona's `shu` exit table read `shu tools`' exit `7` as a host behind the pin, not a red build.
  - **direct** passes `--repo` to `nen direct answer`, which refuses without it; `classify` and `direct` name ten § 2's install, not a repin, when the verbs are absent. `nen/gates.json`'s `round_quorum` comment, GATE-CONFIGURATION and ROSTER's quorum ruling name `v0.20.0` as the release that ships the *fulfils* half.
  - **WORKFLOW § `reports` and PROCESS § Residue** — `nen report data` derives `repo` (the hosted `owner/name`, or `null`), `worktree`, `effortStage`, `gate` and `stageClass` ([zheref/nen#375](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/375)); the caller fills only what stays `null` and `turnLabel`/`generatedAtLocal`.
  - `stage triage --nul` and `issue edit-body --json`'s `v0.3` touch no Hatsu caller; the contract's `minimum_semantics` records both.
- **`nen/labels.json`** takes the taxonomy's `job/parity` description through `nen classify install --write` — the first build that could read the drift — so `classify install` reads all 44 labels declared and `--sync` is not refused on Hatsu itself. `templates/runner-preflight.yml`'s header names the minimum as 0.20.
- **Fan-out:** `nen/repos.json` registers no consumer repository, so no consumer's CI or bootstrap cites the old ref; the host link `~/.local/bin/nen` follows this pin at the next `ten` § 2 once the installed Hatsu carries it.
- Plugin bumped to 0.92.0, a minor: a new minimum and pinned ref, and skill behaviour changes (Claude manifest and Codex overlay). Mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

### One bot reviewer suffices — the round quorum, in canon ([#228](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/228), 0.91.0)

- **One bot reviewer suffices — the round quorum is canon in Hatsu's own words.** The maintainer's ruling of 2026-09-29 (nen `docs/USAGE.md` § `round_quorum`: *Copilot OR Cursor (or both)*), restated on 2026-10-04, is recorded verbatim in `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-04 — one bot reviewer suffices*; `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Reviewer rounds and review threads* states the behaviour once, in nen's own terms (nen's `round_quorum`: at least `minimum` of `any_of` must have a round; once met it fulfils an unavailable member's owed round and never one whose round check is still in flight — zheref/nen#361, in the nen release after v0.19.0; until that repin the pinned binary reads the unavailable member as owed and the existing rules route it); `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` § 3 documents the key's shape and its cost when wrong (anchored, bot-only member patterns). Hatsu's `nen/gates.json` declares `round_quorum` over Copilot alone until Cursor Bugbot is enrolled here, and names that reviewer `bugbot` in the fallback chain, as nen does. Plugin bumped to 0.91.0. ([#228](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/228))

### nen repinned to v0.19.0, and the runner preflight proves an interactive pool ([#226](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/226), 0.90.0)

- **nen repinned to v0.19.0, and the runner preflight proves an interactive desktop pool** ([zheref/hatsu#206](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/206), [#226](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/226)).
- **`nen/contract.json` → `dependency.minimum` moves `0.18` → `0.19`, and `pinned_ref` moves `v0.18.2` → `v0.19.0`** ([zheref/nen v0.19.0](https://github.com/zheref/nen/releases/tag/v0.19.0), released by [zheref/nen#358](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/358) and [#362](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/362), published 2026-10-04 UTC).
  - The minimum moves for a feature and for the floor. Hatsu adopts nen's interactive runner mode ([zheref/nen#333](https://github.com/zheref/nen/issues/333)), and v0.19.0 raises the compatibility floor to `0.19` for breaking consumer notes, so every 0.19+ build refuses a `0.18` pin.
  - `pinned_ref_semantics` records the release and its darwin-arm64, linux-x64 and windows-x64 checksums, and `bootstrap.sha256` carries the same three.
  - The top `$comment`, the bootstrap `url` and `fetch`, the two-step `wrong`/`right`, both `install_paths` and the halt message point at `v0.19.0`.
  - The convenience copies follow: the README's Requirements line, `docs/WORKFLOW.md`, the marketplace descriptions, `futon`'s history line, `surface-mirror-check.yml`'s comment and STANDALONE-ENTRY's `jusshin` row.
- **v0.19.0's breaking consumer notes are absorbed:**
  - **kokusen § 5** — `nen commit write` reads the written commit's trailers back itself, through git's own parser, and exits `3` with `injected[]` when a hook added a refused key ([zheref/nen#357](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/357)). Exit 3 means the commit exists. It goes to the same `injected-attribution-trailer` stop as before: drop the tip with `reset --soft HEAD~1`, then ask the switch. The raw tip read-back is retired, and so is its PROCESS owned-dependency line (zheref/nen#273, closed).
  - **aka § 4** maps `nen wc squash` exit `3` the same way: undo with the line nen prints (`reset --soft ORIG_HEAD`, named as residue), ask the switch, squash again. It also says that `wc squash` now loads `nen/workflow.json` on every run. § 7's step-0 read of the whole outgoing range stays, because a clean catch-up merge is written by `nen wc catch-up`, which reads no trailers back.
  - **ao § 5** maps `commit write` exit `3` on its conflict-resolved merge commit. The commit exists and is never re-committed, and the stop goes to the maintainer, because the printed undo would drop the merge's second parent. kokusen and ao also read an exit `1` whose stderr begins *"committed"* as "the commit exists, unchecked" (any other exit `1` committed nothing), and kokusen drops a tip with the undo line nen prints (a root commit's differs). kokusen and shibari stay under the dieted-skill ceiling (`prose_size_check.sh --headroom`: 132 and 6 bytes left). WORKFLOW § `commits` and PROCESS now describe the verbs' own read-back, not the retired one.
  - **shibari § 4** — a draft is never Ready (`nen pr ready` row 1, [zheref/nen#342](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/342)), so shibari **never opens a draft**: `pr open` runs without `--draft` (the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-04). For a draft someone else opened, the transition is `nen pr mark-ready --require-head` ([zheref/nen#355](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/355)) at the maintainer's word, never a raw `gh pr ready` (ROSTER § *Rulings of 2026-10-04*).
  - **jusshin § 7** — `nen runner enable` certifies only a default-branch `workflow_dispatch` run ([zheref/nen#334](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/334)). The branch's push run is the pre-merge proof, never the go.
  - The codex `ten` mirror's quoted `summary:` ([zheref/nen#343](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/343)) arrives with this release's regeneration.
- **`templates/runner-preflight.yml` proves an interactive (desktop) pool** ([zheref/hatsu#206](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/206)).
  - **`MODE`** — the eighth placeholder. The header names eight, and says a 0.18 binary refuses the template.
  - **A new step, "Desktop session present (interactive Windows pool)".** It is gated `runner.os == 'Windows' && '@@MODE@@' == 'interactive'`, which renders as a constant, so a service pool never runs it. It reads its own process's session id through `powershell.exe`, accepts digits only and compares them as a number, and fails session 0 or anything unknown (padding, a BOM, a second line, `00`), naming the remedy: register from an interactive plan, and sign the account in. It prints the id and never an account. It proves the job is not in session 0, not that the session is unlocked.
  - **Mode-aware remediation.** The toolchain and Windows steps take `MODE`. For an interactive Windows runner the restart is signing its account out and back in, which restarts its logon task. A per-user (AppData or profile) match is a `::warning::` for it, because a logon task runs with its own account's environment. The warning says the file is writable by every job the runner takes (Feitan). Not-found, refused and broken matches still fail.
  - **WHAT ONE GREEN RUN PROVES** names the interactive pool as warn-only too, with a sign-in, not a service restart, covering a sibling logon task.
  - **The `runs-on` comment** says an interactive Windows pool already carries its own label (`desktop`). A service pool still waits on zheref/hatsu#204.
- **`scripts/runner_preflight_fixture_check.sh` holds it.** A third fixture pool, interactive Windows, renders `runs-on: [self-hosted, Windows, X64, desktop]` with `MODE: 'interactive'` on both steps; a service pool renders `MODE: 'service'`.
  - The probe's `if:` is checked exactly on the template and as a constant on all three renderings.
  - The probe, lifted and run against a stubbed `powershell.exe`, passes session 2 and fails session 0, no output, and a failed `powershell.exe`, printing no account.
  - The Windows step, lifted and run with `MODE=interactive`, warns and passes on AppData and profile matches, and still fails a missing match and a refused exec, naming the sign-in.
  - The toolchain step, lifted and run with stubbed tools exiting 127 and 126, carries the sign-in only for `MODE=interactive` on Windows, and the service restart for macOS, `MODE=service` and an unset `MODE`.
  - The service rendering's Windows step, run with `MODE=service`, still fails a per-user match.
  - The probe's `powershell.exe` command is held exactly, and the probe is run with the account planted in `USERNAME`, `USERPROFILE` and `USERDOMAIN` and a stubbed `whoami`, printing none of them. The log-hygiene check also refuses `whoami`, `$env:USERNAME` and the profile and home variables.
  - It runs 173 conjuncts, up from 106, and refuses a nen older than 0.19 at exit 5 by name. Mutants were each refused: session 0 passing (old and numeric forms), the probe ungated by `MODE`, an interactive per-user match failing, any declared `MODE` read as interactive, the toolchain restart fixed to the service form, the probe's failure fallback removed, the probe reading explorer's session, and the probe printing `whoami` or the profile leaf. Phinks's red test reproduces green on every case it can still apply.
- **`jusshin`** learns the mode:
  - § 2 asks for it with its cost and takes the four-label set for an interactive Windows pool. It also says a `desktop` runner matches the plain set's jobs too, and beside a same-arch service pool that overlap is said and asked once (Feitan SEC-16, Chrollo; the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-04).
  - § 3 relays the plan's `note:` lines. `dailyAccount` is the maintainer's typed `--accept-daily-account` or a re-asked identity, never a default, routed through § 3's table, § 5's argv and exit-2 row, and § 9's report.
  - § 4 has an interactive Windows row, and § 5 says the script asks no password and that the tasks wait for logon.
  - § 6 reads `offline` as a signed-out account or a stopped logon task (a locked screen keeps a runner online), and § 7 adds J8 (session 0), J9 (a per-user warning, writable by every job) and the sign-in restart. W1, the proof paragraph, the controlled dispatch, `--labels` and J6 read an interactive pool too, and nen#312 is marked done.
  - The hard limits never pass `--accept-daily-account` on the session's own reading.
- **The maintainer's rulings of 2026-10-04** are recorded in ROSTER: the overlap is asked once, `missing-maintainer-choice.governs` gains `jusshin <accept-daily-account>` and `jusshin <desktop-overlap>` (WORKFLOW § 4 names them), and shibari opens no draft.
- Plugin bumped to 0.90.0, a minor (0.87.0 to 0.89.0 were taken on main by #214, #224 and #225): a new minimum and pinned ref, a template step, and skill behaviour changes (Claude manifest and Codex overlay). Mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

### `hatsu:direct` — the model, surface and effort for a build ([#225](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/225), 0.89.0)

- **`hatsu:direct`** (new, `claude/skills/direct/SKILL.md`; the second skill from the maintainer's rulings of 2026-10-04, stacked on v0.88.0's `classify`): from an issue's `lang/` and `job/` labels (or an inline effort classified on the spot), resolves deterministically — `nen direct resolve` over the new `contracts/direct.registry.json` (`hatsu.direct-registry/v1`) — and reports, for the winner and the runner-up: **Provider · Model family · Latest recommended version · Recommended surface · Recommended effort**, each with its **interactive equivalents**.
  - **Stable aliases, replaceable versions.** The registry's ten aliases (`SEMANTIC_FRONTIER`, `SEMANTIC_MAX`, `BALANCED_AUTHOR`, `EXECUTION_FRONTIER`, `EXECUTION_VALUE`, `VISUAL_ORCHESTRATOR`, `VISUAL_FALLBACK`, `MECHANICAL_EDITOR`, `PR_QUALITY_REVIEW`, `PR_SECURITY_REVIEW`) each name a provider, a model family, their Hatsu surface and the `nen/workflow.json` → `models.<surface>` tier the consumer spells it as. No version is in an alias or a routing cell: the latest is read live (the surface's own lookup, then the provider's model page, quoted with its date, never inside a command), with the document's dated `snapshot` as the stated fallback.
  - **95 job × domain routes (204 cells with their language overrides)**, transcribed from the document's five tables (the document's digest is in `source.documentSha256`); a cell's surface is always its alias's (`surfaceRule`), so the actionable winner and runner-up name one of Hatsu's four surfaces — or a review product beside its actionable `also` alias — and the document's Cursor and native-IDE preferences, with GitHub Copilot, are the interactive rows. The aggregation of several pairs, the empty-axis answer (`undirectable`) and the structured `domains.rule` are stated in the file.
  - **Effort is a rule**: the highest job weight plus one for each of three or more jobs, two or more code languages, the `aigov` domain — `low | medium | high | max`, mapped onto each surface's own dial (a collapsed top on Cursor and Antigravity reads `high`, said).
  - **Persistence is local**: `.nen/direct/<effort>.json`, keyed by the issue identity (`<CODE>-IS-#<N>`), known before any branch exists; no issue comment, no label.
- **`hatsu:build` follows the full canonical protocol**: `ten` → `classify` (when the issue is unclassified) → `direct` → `ren` → `aka` → `mukai` → `en` → `rikugan` (En's own terminus render, never a second one) → `third-hand`. A mismatch between direct's verdict and the session's surface, model alias or effort (compared in the surface's dial) **asks once** (new `nen/decisions.json` row `direct-mismatch`, continue starred) and **never blocks**; an absent verb at the pin is one line and the build continues unrouted.
- **`nen/decisions.json`** gains `direct-mismatch` (ask once per run) — 35 rows with v0.88.0's `classify-undecidable`.
- **Canon:** `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 4 *direct — a configuration consequence*; `docs/PROCESS.md` names the residue (the live version read is prose; the session's own model and effort are the harness's fact; the verbs land in the nen release that ships the direct family (an owned dependency: zheref/nen#371 (branch `sonnet/kurapika/direct-verbs`), cited in `nen/contract.json`; `v0.19.0` shipped on 2026-10-04 without it)). **Two questions the rulings did not settle are returned to the maintainer** (`docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-04*, *Open questions*): whether reviewers stay at `deep` when the session runs lower, and whether a canon data contract owes a guard lane. `direct` joins the dieted skills (twenty-two) and the skill indexes (fifty skills, fifty-one directories with `ten`; STANDALONE-ENTRY § 7b, twenty-eight). Plugin bumped to 0.89.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp. ([#225](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/225))
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten: Nobunaga, Feitan, Chrollo, Phinks, all opus): 23 routing cells that put an Anthropic alias on the Cursor surface against WORKFLOW § `models` now route on the alias's surface with the Cursor IDE as the interactive equivalent; the runner-up aggregation rule is stated in full and every reviewer runner-up carries its `also`; an empty job axis is the answer `undirectable`; the ledger key is the issue identity everywhere; the effort compare is in dial space with `unread` never a mismatch; restart placeholders are filled from `models` only; `build` § 2b fails soft at the pin and no longer restates direct § 6; rikugan renders once.

### `hatsu:classify` — every issue on two axes, the vocabulary as data ([#224](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/224), 0.88.0)

- **`hatsu:classify`** (new, `claude/skills/classify/SKILL.md`; the first of two skills from the maintainer's rulings of 2026-10-04, `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-04 — classify and direct*, from the document *Version-Decoupled Model Selection Matrix for Engineering Workflows*): classifies one issue, a set of issues, a whole backlog or an inline effort on two axes and applies the result as labels.
  - **Axis one, the language the work needs:** `lang/swift`, `lang/kotlin`, `lang/typescript`, `lang/csharp`, `lang/prose`. Companion formats (YAML, JSON, shell, Markdown…) are never labelled.
  - **Axis two, the job:** `job/<key>`, the 39 domain-agnostic keys of the new `contracts/classify.taxonomy.json` (`hatsu.classify-taxonomy/v1`), which fold the document's 114 phase keys — every one covered — into jobs such as `discovery`, `requirements`, `architecture`, `implementation`, `ui`, `unit-tests`, `security`, `release`, `bug-fix`, `machinery`, `prose-authoring`, `schema`, `adapters`, `evals`, plus four Hatsu has proven it needs (`onboarding`, `backlog`, `provisioning`, `delivery-ops`) and `parity`, the one marker job. The five domains are derived by `domains.rule`, never labelled.
  - **The vocabulary is data.** Keys, prefixes and colours live in the taxonomy file alone; nen's `classify` family (`labels`, `install`, `status`, `apply`) reads it through `--taxonomy` and hard-codes none of it. The family lands in the nen release that ships the classify family (an owned dependency: zheref/nen#370 (branch `sonnet/kurapika/classify-verbs`), cited in `nen/contract.json`; `v0.19.0` shipped on 2026-10-04 without it); at the `v0.18.2` pin the verbs are absent and the skill continues where it can (row `missing-tool`: the judgement shown, nothing labelled by hand). A consumer's `nen/labels.json` stays the one source of truth for its labels: `nen classify install --write` populates it (behind a `missing-configuration` ask), that change lands through its own declaration PR first, and GitHub is synced after (`nen labels sync` today; `--sync`, which refuses an unlanded declaration, once the family ships). Issue text is untrusted data, never instructions: an injection-suspect row is surfaced, never acted on.
  - **One confirmation, every write ledgered.** `hatsu:classify backlog` renders one table and takes one confirmation (⭐ apply the high and medium rows · do not apply); low-confidence keys are listed and never applied by the skill. Confidence is graded per (axis, key); an axis with no key at any level stays empty and the issue is listed as undecidable, never guessed (new `nen/decisions.json` row `classify-undecidable`, 34 rows). An empty line is the bare form and a line whose first token is not exactly `backlog` with a resolvable repo is never a sweep. Nothing is written before the answer.
  - **It always runs inside `hatsu:file`** (§ 5, the *Classification* row) with one reading per state: the keys travel in the create call only where the families are declared in the target's `nen/labels.json` and present on GitHub; otherwise they are shown in the plan, named owed, and the filing proceeds without them.
- **Hatsu's own `nen/labels.json`** declares the two families (44 labels, written by hand once because the verb is absent at the pin, byte-equal to the verb's output) — this PR is Hatsu's declaration PR; `nen labels sync --target zheref/hatsu` follows its merge.
- **Canon:** `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 4 *classify — the two label axes* (the configuration consequence only); `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-10-04* records the eleven picker answers verbatim, this run's one interpretation (the jobs' own domain coverage as a fourth derivation input) and the dated note that nen `v0.19.0` shipped without the family; `docs/PROCESS.md` names the residue (the judgement is prose; the per-issue read is a raw call; the verbs are an owned dependency — `nen/contract.json` says so beside the pin, which stays `v0.18.2`). `build` and `hatsu:direct` land in the stacked `v0.89.0`.
- `classify` joins the dieted skills (`scripts/prose_size_check.sh`, twenty-one) and the skill indexes (forty-nine skills, fifty directories with `ten`; `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` § 7b, twenty-seven). Plugin bumped to 0.88.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay; `main` took 0.87.0 with #214); mirrors regenerated at that stamp. ([#224](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/224))

### Isolated worktrees bound before every authoring phase ([#214](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/214), 0.87.0)

- Make independent worktrees the explicit default for Ren, Breath, and Rasengan on Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, and Antigravity. Select and bind the effort checkout before warm-up mutations, recheck isolation on continuations, and accept core authoring only when the maintainer explicitly requested it in the original effort prompt. Preserve Antigravity's mandatory `Workspace: "branch"` instruction from Hatsu PR #163.
- Keep isolated Ren turns from falling back to core for automatic launch; render the deferred plan against core with `--dry-run` only and leave core available for the maintainer's own testing. Guard plugin-source refresh and skip incidental read-only placement. An explicit launch or swap is separate from permission to author in core. ([#214](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/214))

Release unit for `v0.86.0..v0.92.0`: [#214](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/214), [#224](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/224), [#225](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/225), [#226](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/226), [#228](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/228), [#231](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/231) and [#232](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/232) (this release proposal). This line is where the range's PRs are cited, which is what `CON-33(c)` reconciles against.

## v0.86.0 — every PR into main ends at the maintainer's merge prompt, and closing issues are linked both ways by a guard

Two efforts from the `hatsu:futon hatsu@bug` run of 2026-10-02. Each was hanten-settled on its own branch and merged locally into the run's integration branch `opus/kurapika/futon-bug-2026-10-02` (no sub-PRs). This release unit carries both.

- **#169 and #122 landed in v0.85.0 (HA-PR-#212); this unit's competing implementations were dropped on the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-03.**
- **The merge-authority ruling of 2026-09-30 is canon** (#207). `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-09-30 — En never merges* quotes the maintainer and states the rows that follow:
  - En never merges. Every PR into `main` ends at en § 5's merge prompt: the PR link and the inline `gh pr merge <n> --repo <o/r> --merge --match-head-commit <sha>` line. Its picker never carries the merge act; the maintainer merges outside the session.
  - The one PR into `main` an agent merges is getsuga § 3a's own release proposal, through `nen pr merge --release-unit`, after `hatsu:en` takes it to its G2 readiness gates.
  - Futon may cut one integration branch per run. Hanten-settled efforts land on it by a **local** `git merge --no-ff` (no sub-PR, no `gh pr merge`), pushed with `nen wc publish`. The integration PR into `branch.base` is the run's terminus, carries the bump and the CHANGELOG, and ends at the merge prompt. `git merge --no-ff` is named residue in `claude/rules/hatsu.md` § 4.
  - futon § 8 holds `then` until every PR the run authored is merged into `branch.base`; "done" means merged, not Ready.
  - "main" reads as `branch.base` — recorded as the run's G4 interpretation, the maintainer's to correct.
  - `en`, `futon`, `getsuga`, `sharingan`, `backlog-loop`, `kurapika.md`, `claude/rules/hatsu.md`, WORKFLOW, README's gate tables and `nen/decisions.json` (`own-pr-merge`, `merge`, `canon-merge`, `reviewer-exhausted`) agree. Ruling 3 of 2026-09-29 is marked superseded in place.
- **A guard verifies every closing issue is linked both ways in GitHub's Development field** (#203). New `scripts/pr_development_link_check.sh` (`--pr`, `--body --target`, `--base`, `--fixture`, `--self-test`; bash 3.2, jq, awk under `LC_ALL=C`).
  - The expected closing set comes from the body's *Associated issues* table, read through the template's closed verdict vocabulary, matched exactly as a leading bold span; only `completes it`/`closes it` rows link. Each needs its own keyword line, and a keyword GitHub would act on anywhere else is refused.
  - Live, both directions must match exactly: the PR's `closingIssuesReferences` and each issue's `closedByPullRequestsReferences`.
  - It **fails closed** on anything its parser does not model (fences, comments, code spans, lookalike headings and verdicts, escaped pipes, a second table, control bytes, more than 50 references) and exits `0`/`1`/`2`.
  - Applicability is the PR's own base first: a PR into the default branch is verified fully whatever `--base` says. Off the default branch it prints `retarget-pending`, never a pass; where `branch.base` is not the default branch either, `not-applicable`, quoted at the merge prompt in place of its `0`.
  - `templates/pr-body.md`'s *Associated issues* contract changes with it: the *Merging this* verdict is a leading bold span from a closed vocabulary; one canonical issue URL per Issue cell (shorthand only on keyword lines); a tableless section reads exactly `No associated issue.`; a `Closes` line per closing row, under the table; and check (d), the guard, in the completion comment.
  - Wired into shibari § 4 (before open and after every body edit), en § 4's Ready predicate and § 5's prompt checks, sharingan § 4's veto pass, and getsuga § 3/§ 3a (a proposal's section reads `No associated issue.`). WORKFLOW states the readiness rule once. Lane `development-link-guard` (178 cases); `scripts/plugin_bump_check.sh` guards the script as plugin-shipped.
- **Residue named** in `docs/PROCESS.md`: the Development guard's raw `gh` reads, an owned dependency on a `nen pr ready` row (not yet filed), BC-11 a G4 question until the verb lands; and futon § 5's local `git merge --no-ff` (no verb, not yet filed).
- **The integration pass's findings are settled** (Nobunaga, Feitan, Chrollo, Phinks; one fixup commit on the integration branch):
  - WORKFLOW § *Build accountability and issue associations* states the Development guard's readiness as three mutually exclusive cases: the PR's own base first — a PR into the default branch, whatever `branch.base` declares; stacked off it with `branch.base` the default branch; and neither the default branch. en § 4, § 5 and § 6's hold row, getsuga § 3/§ 3a, ROSTER and `nen/decisions.json` (`own-pr-merge`, `reviewer-exhausted`) mirror them.
  - The third case is the merge prompt, the guard's `not-applicable` quoted in place of its `0` — a G2/G4 merge stop, not a G5; getsuga § 3a falls back to the declaration gate there. A closed integration PR ends the run with its efforts named as stranded, not a G5. The genuine stops stay five (the run's G4 interpretation, the maintainer's to correct).
  - ROSTER: the two readings ("main" is `branch.base`; keywords act only on the default branch) are one row; an effort catches up from the integration branch with `nen wc catch-up --base <bare branch> --strategy merge`, its merge commits carrying no trailer; the pack's deny row covers `gh pr merge` only, the open merge paths deferred to #154 under *What it does not rule* (no permission or hook change).
  - `build` Phase 3 and `claude/rules/hatsu.md` rule 7 name the Development guard and where `Closes #<N>` sits. `nen/contract.json` lists `development-link-guard`. Three ROSTER citations carry the section's full title.
  - `scripts/pr_development_link_check.sh` refuses a body over 65536 bytes at exit `2` before parsing; `scripts/plugin_bump_check_fixture.sh` no longer flakes on SIGPIPE under `pipefail` (here-strings in place of `printf | grep -q`).
- **Copilot's round on the integration PR is settled:** `pr_development_link_check.sh` decides applicability from the PR's `baseRefName` (above), refuses `#N`/`owner/name#N` in an Issue cell, a verdict that is not a leading bold span, the retired verdict aliases, and a tableless `None.`.
- **Caught up with `main` at v0.85.0** (HA-PR-#211, HA-PR-#212): v0.85.0's `hanten_cycle_ledger.sh ensure` and `plugin_cache_check.sh` are kept as they landed, with every prose line, lane and comment about them.
- Plugin bumped to 0.86.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.85.0 — hanten opens a missing review ledger itself, and ten tells a stale Claude Code plugin cache from a fresh one

- **A branch breath did not cut gets its review ledger** (#169). The desktop app's worktree, or a hand-made branch, had no `.nen/hanten/<branch>.cycle.json`, and hanten's fail-closed ledger refused it. New `scripts/hanten_cycle_ledger.sh ensure --repo <path> --branch <name> [--pr <n>] [--base <trunk>]` opens it with the same `init` breath uses, and hanten § 1 runs it on every entry, passing `--pr` once the effort has a PR. It returns `opened` only when no trace of an earlier ledger survives, and says so. It returns `present` for breath's ledger or an earlier ensure's, left byte for byte, and for the current PR's own PR-keyed ledger. It exits `3` on the trunk (`--base`, else `nen/workflow.json` `branch.base`, else `origin/HEAD`, else `main`), and `4` for a **lost** ledger, which is never re-opened: hanten § 1's recovery path owns it.
- **The trace outlives `.nen/`.** Every `init` (breath's, ensure's, recover-first's, the PR-keyed one) also writes `<git-common-dir>/hatsu/hanten/<key>.opened`. `git clean` never touches it, and every worktree of the clone shares it. It is read without `--path-format`, so git older than 2.31 answers too. `ensure` reads as lost:
  - that marker, or another PR's (exactly `<key>-pr<digits>`, so `x/ten-preflight` is never `x/ten`'s);
  - another PR's ledger in this checkout;
  - the same key's ledger or lock in any other `git worktree list` checkout;
  - a lock older than 30 s, its age read before ensure waits and never dropped.

  A younger lock is an init in flight, waited on. 30-trial race tests pin both. **Not traces, by design:** a pushed branch or an open PR, because hanten first runs on an already-pushed branch (mukai). **Not covered:** a fresh clone reads `opened`. **Fail closed:** a branch name reused after its branch was merged and deleted reads lost and is settled through recover-first (`docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 4). `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` S4 names the branch ledger's two openers. The ledger self-test (79 cases) fails the `ledger-guard` lane on any failure.
- **`ten` § 5 tells a stale Claude Code plugin cache from a fresh one** (#122). New `scripts/plugin_cache_check.sh --root <source> --cache <dir|auto>` compares the shipped trees byte for byte: `claude/skills`, `claude/agents`, `claude/rules`, `hooks`, `templates`, `contracts`, `scripts`, `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` and `marketplace.json`. `auto` judges every recorded copy: each `hatsu@` installPath in `installed_plugins.json`, and `skills/hatsu`. Exits:
  - `0` identical, or served by link to a git checkout named independently;
  - `1` different, every path named;
  - `2` wiring, including an unreadable file or directory;
  - `3` not installed;
  - `4` not comparable;
  - `5` broken install (no usable installPath, a stale record, a dangling or looping link, a control character).

  **A copy is never its own evidence:** where the served copy is `--root`, the source must come from `HATSU_PLUGIN_ROOT`, a `directory` marketplace, or the checkout the caller stands in, and the last only on its trunk or the served copy's own branch (a feature worktree is the change being authored). Otherwise it is not comparable. A symlink is compared by target and never opened. A control character is never printed raw. **jq is optional**: without it the record is reported unread, never a wiring stop. `surface_mirror_check.sh --installed` hands a cache-shaped path to it (a missing or non-executable comparator is wiring). `docs/PROCESS.md` maps every exit to ten § 5's `mirrors:` word. New lane `plugin-cache-guard` (79 hermetic cases).
- **Residue, and a question for you.** The cache comparison is deterministic work that belongs to nen: an owned dependency, zheref/nen#339 (`docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue*). **BC-11, a G4 question, not decided here:** the new shell verdict machinery and `hanten_cycle_ledger.sh`'s growing embedded Python. Does BC-11's embedded-Python freeze bind the ledger? The README now says `python3` is required and `jq` is optional.
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten: Nobunaga ×2, Feitan, Chrollo, Phinks):
  - **Phinks (fail ❌).** Five red tests, ported and now green: a cleaned ledger re-opened with a fresh budget; a race read as lost; broken installs read as not installed; a split installPath; an unrunnable comparator read as drift.
  - **Chrollo (divergent ❌).** The self-comparison, the hard jq dependency and the missing residue entry.
  - **Feitan.** Control characters and followed symlinks.
  - **Nobunaga's second pass.** A prefix glob that misread sibling branches; the current PR's ledger read as lost; a probe race that dropped the stale-lock trace; a feature-branch working directory used as the source.
  - **Corrected facts.** The base self-test already exited 1 on a failure; `ten` shrank 5 bytes, not 20.
  - **Verification.** The last fixes had no reviewer pass of their own; fixtures and mutants are their verification.
- **What Copilot's round changed** (HA-PR-#212): a copy whose manifest names another plugin is no longer judged as Hatsu. An explicit `--cache` refuses it as wiring (exit 2), and an auto-mode `hatsu@` record pointing at one is a broken install (exit 5), never "different, refresh it". The README now states `python3` as a prerequisite rather than something every host ships. Its copy of the guarded-script list mirrors `PLUGIN_SURFACE_GLOBS` again: it had dropped `nen_global.sh`, `branch_authorship_check.sh`, `private_name_check.sh` and `plugin_cache_check.sh`.
- `hanten` stays within its 12,288-byte ceiling (12,286). Plugin bumped to 0.85.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.


## v0.84.0 — readiness pins the event head and regenerate cleans up after itself, every readiness step frozen

- **Step two of the two-step landing for #160 and #165** (`docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md`, 2026-09-20; step one was HA-PR-#193, v0.78.0). `pr-readiness.yml` drops its separate freshness step: the verdict step calls `nen pr ready … --require-head "$EVENT_HEAD" --exclude-check readiness`, so a verdict is about the event's own head and never reads `ready` off this workflow's prior `readiness` check, and nen's exit `8` titles the check **superseded** (nothing asserted about that SHA). Publish runs `if: always()` with no `STALE` branch. `surface-mirror-regenerate.yml` gives its PR step `id: cpr` and adds the failure-only cleanup step that deletes only `refs/heads/bot/surface-mirror-regenerate` and names the PR-creation toggle when the delete itself fails; a failure while updating an already-open regeneration PR also deletes its branch, which the next run reopens, and the step comment and `docs/SURFACES.md` say so. `templates/pr-readiness.yml` carries the same frozen steps.
- **The validator is narrowed to the frozen shapes, and every readiness step is frozen.** `scripts/workflow_runner_policy_check.rb` admits one shape per workflow: all seven `pr-readiness.yml` steps are exact constants (`with:` compared too), and every workflow it checks has exact allowlists for job keys (`container`, `services` and job `env` refused), step keys, `uses:`, `shell:` (exactly `bash`) and step env keys — so no unfrozen step can run PR-checkout code or assemble an env write before the frozen verdict. `refuse_env_writes` stays as a tripwire for literal `GITHUB_ENV`/`GITHUB_PATH`/`_runner_file_commands`/`${!` mentions and step env `BASH_ENV`/`ENV`, and says it catches literal mentions only. The validator prints a PR-controlled path with its control characters escaped. The old shapes are rejected test cases.
- **Tenkai re-renders a stale consumer.** `ReadinessWorkflow.detect` compares the verdict and Publish steps whole and flags the retired freshness step, so an old rendering is `drift` and `apply` re-renders it with both flags; `GuardRegistration` accepts the validator's current types table, so a process repository carrying it reads as registered. Tenkai's self-test runs the rendered verdict body against a stub nen answering as nen v0.18.2 documents, with a rollup holding only a prior green `readiness` check: without the flags it titles the check `ready` (the false READY #160 reports), the shipped body titles it `not-ready: no checks reported`. That proves the workflow half; nen's half is its own test at v0.18.2, cited.
- **What consumers do** (tenkai § 5d): a process repository carrying its own validator lands the narrowed validator and re-renders at once — validators 0.78.0 to 0.81.x admit both renderings, the narrowed one refuses the old. A repository whose only reporting check is readiness now reads `not-ready: no checks reported` on every event, by design: add a real CI check first.
- **#165 stays open** (the maintainer's ruling, 2026-10-02): its criteria 1 and 2, reading the toggle before pushing and failing early, need the Administration permission `GITHUB_TOKEN` cannot hold, and an App token or PAT is declined (SEC-9). This PR delivers the cleanup and the documented prerequisite and refers to it.
- **Deferred to a step three**, because main's validator compares them byte for byte: the SIGPIPE-safe title extraction (`| head -1` can kill the verdict step on an explain above the pipe buffer, failing closed as `undetermined`), and splitting the verdict (read-only) and Publish (`checks: write`) into two jobs. `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` records step three.
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten: Nobunaga, Feitan, Phinks): every readiness step frozen instead of a text check (Phinks' and Feitan's assembled-name bypasses — `printenv`, `eval`, a glob — refused), tenkai's drift check (Phinks), the #160 repro, comments scoped to `pull_request_target`, escaped validator output, the cleanup caveat, § 5d's version wording, the only-check consequence stated. These last fixes had no reviewer pass of their own; 13 of 13 validator mutants die and Phinks' two red tests pass.
- **What Copilot's round changed** (HA-PR-#211): freezing Hatsu's own readiness steps refused the workflow tenkai renders into a process repository at its first step, because the rendering pins `actions/checkout` by SHA and its policy and pin steps carry the `TENKAI_*` placeholders and their guarded bodies, so a repository following § 5d would have failed its own trusted policy step. The validator now admits exactly two whole-job forms, Hatsu's own and the rendering (`READINESS_RENDERED_STEPS`), selected by the head checkout and never mixed, with the placeholders held to the values and role pairs tenkai writes and the SHA-pinned checkout and `TENKAI_*` keys admitted in `pr-readiness.yml` only. The self-test builds its readiness fixtures from the constants rather than the checkout, so it passes on either form, and renders `templates/pr-readiness.yml` for both roles and validates it. Tenkai's self-test also validates its own rendering with the shipped validator; that check fails on the previous validator.
- Plugin bumped to 0.84.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.


## v0.83.0 — the surface link guard runs in CI, and judges a pull request's tree without trusting it

- **The generated-surface link guard runs on every pull request** (#72). `scripts/surface_link_check.sh` proves what the drift check cannot: that the generated bodies' relative links resolve. It now runs in `.github/workflows/surface-mirror-check.yml`'s existing *Surface-mirror drift check* step, from the trusted revision (`.trusted/`), against the PR checkout as the root it judges, once the drift verdict is clean. It stayed out of CI while it was red on main by design: until nen v0.17.0 (zheref/nen#270, fixed by zheref/nen#285) the generator copied relative links verbatim, so they dangled at every mirror depth, and a context red on every pull request would have read every one not-ready for a defect none of them made. The pin now carries the fix and 2,931 of 2,931 links resolve. Folding the guard into the existing step needs no two-step landing: the trusted validator freezes the step names and their order, not this step's body, and main's validator accepts the change. `pull_request_target` runs main's workflow, so the step takes effect on pull requests opened or updated after this one merges.
- **The guard reads a pull request's tree as untrusted input.**
  - **File list:** listed NUL-delimited. A name outside `surfaces/` or carrying a control character is refused (exit `2`) and printed escaped with `%q`.
  - **Fenced output:** both guards' output, stderr included, sits between `::stop-commands::` and a fresh per-run token printed on its own line, so nothing in the tree is read as a workflow command.
  - **Path folding:** `.` and `..` are folded as text, and a trailing `/` is kept.
  - **Never stat'ed:** a target that climbs above the root, or whose folded path passes through a symbolic link, is reported dangling without a stat, so the guard cannot probe the runner's filesystem.
  - **Reference definitions:** judged even in a file with no inline link.
  - **NUL bytes:** a file holding one is read as text (`grep -a`), never skipped as binary.
- **#72's criteria:**
  - Met as written: the guard and its fixture, the exit contract, the companion nen issue, and 0 dangling links.
  - Met by supersession, three times:
    - The guard runs *inside* surface-mirror-check, not beside it.
    - Exit `3` is reserved and never used, because the guard needs no nen.
    - `scripts/antigravity_mirror_sync.sh` is retired, and nen's generator now emits the correct depth.

  `docs/ab/surface-link-check.md` ticks each criterion with its evidence.
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten: Chrollo, Leorio, Feitan, Nobunaga ×2).
  - **Chrollo:** confirmed the fold over a separately named step. The workflow header, the frozen step name's scope, the drift-clean ordering and SURFACES § 4 now say so.
  - **Leorio:** charted about +1.3 s per run and about +9.5 runner-minutes a month. That is USD 0 as configured, on a public repo with a standard runner.
  - **Feitan:** newline file names, `..` escapes, and a `set -e` gap that skipped reference definitions.
  - **Nobunaga, two passes:** symlinked paths, the stderr stream outside the fence, NUL-holding files, a trailing slash, and an invalid shellcheck directive.

  Nobunaga's second-pass fixes had no reviewer pass of their own. Each carries a fixture case that is red on the previous guard or on a mutant of the new one.
- Plugin bumped to 0.83.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.82.0 — align build skill to canonical Akatsuki lifecycle and worktree isolation

- **Align `build` skill to canonical Akatsuki lifecycle**: Replaced legacy Bankai CI builder agent routing (`bankai:agent/*`), CI stage labels, chain-position mappings, wave coordinators, and wake ladders with Hatsu's canonical 5-phase delivery sequence (`ren` -> `aka` -> `mukai` -> `en` -> `third-hand`).
- **Worktree isolation**: Builds run in isolated worktrees under `<core>/.nen/worktrees/<surface>/<name>` preserving core checkout stability on `main`.
- **Diet & mirrors**: Kept `build/SKILL.md` within the 12,288-byte ceiling (12,008 bytes); regenerated Codex, Cursor, and Antigravity surface mirrors at stamp 0.82.0 using Nen 0.18.2.

## v0.81.0 — a run-time guard refuses private repository names before Hatsu writes to a public repository

- **New `scripts/private_name_check.sh`** (#149, criteria 2 and 3; the maintainer's ruling: *guard only, no rewrites*). It compares text against the **live** list of every private repository the maintainer's credentials can see (`gh api 'user/repos?visibility=private' --paginate`; `--owner` can only add, never narrow) — never committed, never cached in the tree; `--names-file` is the fixture and CI form. A name is a hit bare, case-insensitive and whole-word (`_` and `.` bound a word), as an `owner/name` slug or in a URL, and again after a normalised reading of each line: URL-encoding, HTML entities, inline tags and HTML comments, backslash escapes, `*` and backtick markup, NFKC, format characters (zero-width, soft hyphen) and dash variants. **It never prints a name**: hits, paths and labels show `<private #k>`, a path or label that names one only once normalised is withheld whole, the list size appears only under `--verbose`, and `--resolve <k>` prints an entry to a terminal's stderr only. `--tree` reads tracked and untracked-not-ignored files, path strings and symlink targets, decodes UTF-16 with a BOM, and counts what it skips. **It fails closed**: an empty live list, an empty names file without `--allow-empty`, an ignore file that removes every name, a truncated list, a missing `gh`, perl or perl module, an unreadable file and any perl failure all exit 2. The one exemption is the predecessor's own generated-file marker, read at run time from limbo's marker line, in its exact shape; any other name in that shape, or the predecessor's name spelt again on the same line, is a hit. An optional `~/.config/hatsu/redaction-ignore` (outside the tree) lists names too generic to police, keyed bare or by `owner/name`; its count is printed on every exit, and no agent ever writes it.
- **Every public write Hatsu authors runs it first.** `hatsu:file` runs it on every drafted title, body and comment **before** § 4's plan, so the plan shows redacted text (the title through a quoted heredoc file); § 8 forbids writing text the guard has not passed or skipped, and forbids writing the ignore file. `backlog-synthesis` (per group, plus a hard limit), `mugetsu` § 7 and `sharingan` § 7 run it before their `nen issue` writes. Callers outside these skills are named in `docs/PUBLIC-REDACTION.md` as unguarded until zheref/nen#329 (nen's own `issue file`/`comment`/`edit-body` check) lands; `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue* cites it.
- **Criterion 2(b), the tree check at kokusen or as a lane, is deferred, and said so.** The tree is not clean today (173 mentions raw, 60 with two ordinary-word names ignored, counted by the guard itself, names never printed), and kokusen has no room under its prose ceiling. The new lane `redaction-guard` runs the hermetic fixture (115 cases, also under `/bin/bash` 3.2); `scripts/plugin_bump_check.sh` ships the guard as a runtime row; `nen/contract.json`'s `hosts.$comment` names it and its perl modules. `docs/PUBLIC-REDACTION.md` § *How it is guarded* is new.
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten: Feitan, Nobunaga ×2). Feitan: an empty list failed open; only the maintainer's own 50 of 72 visible private repositories were listed; a path or label could print a name; `--tree` skipped paths and symlinks; markdown escapes, entities and zero-width characters bypassed the matcher; four skills wrote issues unguarded; the marker exemption covered any name; the ignore file could silence a hit unseen. Nobunaga: `_name_` emphasis passed; the guard's own header and fixture contained two live names (now invented); the check ran after the plan the maintainer confirmed.
