Nobunaga's second pass found a **critical**: an empty or comment-only ignore file (awk's `NR==FNR` read the list as the ignore set) disabled the guard — fixed, with the marker, markup, withheld-path, `--resolve`, perl-exit and sharingan findings, in the last commit without a further review pass; verified by the fixture (17 of its 25 new cases fail on the previous head) and mutants (each new arm, removed, turns it red). `sharingan` sits at its 12,288-byte ceiling. **What Copilot's round changed** (HA-PR-#196): `mugetsu` § 7 and `sharingan` § 7 chain their comment to the guard with `&&`, so a refused check writes nothing; `--target`'s skip line and its visibility errors no longer echo the target or `gh`'s message, since either may name a private repository (3 fixture cases, red before).
- Plugin bumped to 0.81.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.80.0 — the runner preflight admits no tag push, judges the service's own PATH, and says what one green run proves

- **`templates/runner-preflight.yml` — `push` is filtered to branches and to the file** ([zheref/hatsu#198](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/198) item 1; Phinks QA-17 and Feitan SEC-16 on the first consumer rendering, zheref/<product-repo-E>#99). GitHub evaluates no `paths` filter on a tag push, so `paths` alone woke a runner of the pool on every tag (zheref/nen run 36801489069 on the `v0.18.2` tag, a commit touching only the changelog and the version). `branches: ['**']` beside `paths` admits every branch and no tag ref, and the header's *"costs nothing on any other change"* now says why. `scripts/runner_preflight_fixture_check.sh` holds push to exactly `branches` and `paths`, `branches` to exactly `'**'`, and refuses a copy whose push carries `paths` alone (the previous shape) or a `tags:` filter beside them — nen renders both at exit 0.
- **The Windows machine-wide verdict is judged on the PATH the service hands a job, not Git Bash's** (item 2; Phinks QA-18, Nobunaga). `shell: bash` is Git for Windows, whose bash puts its own `usr\bin` and `mingw64\bin` first, so a tool present only there read machine-wide and would fail a `powershell` or `cmd` step (<product-repo-E> run 36958789239: `curl 8.18.0 (x86_64-w64-mingw32)`, Git's build, while the machine PATH carried no Git directory). A `shell: cmd` step now records `set PATH` under `RUNNER_TEMP`; the bash step reads the `Path=` line back with built-ins, exports it as `SERVICE_PATH`, and calls `where.exe "$SERVICE_PATH:<tool>"`. No match reads *not on the service PATH*; a missing recording fails the step by name; nothing prints the PATH. The cmd step sets the UTF-8 code page first (Phinks on this change: cmd writes a redirected file in the OEM code page, so a machine-wide tool in a non-ASCII directory read as absent — a false red), and the fixture holds the step's two lines exactly. A machine-wide match is then RUN, output discarded (Copilot on #202: where.exe locates and never executes, and Git Bash's own copy can answer the toolchain step while the service's match is refused or broken): a 126 reads *machine-wide, refused on exec*, any other non-zero *machine-wide, exited N*, both failing with the ACL or reinstall remedy; the harness runs a real stub executable for every machine-wide answer and holds both shadowed failures. The fixture's `where.exe` stub answers the `$SERVICE_PATH:` form only when the recording is in the EXPORTED environment (`printenv`, as the real where.exe reads it — Nobunaga: a variable-reading stub passed a step that dropped `export`) and answers `$PATH:` with Git Bash's own hits, and adds the Git-only case, the service-decides case (a per-user tool Git Bash shadows machine-wide), the no-recording case and an unexported mutant — every one fails against the previous template, which the same guard pointed at `main`'s template shows: 18 conjuncts fail there, exactly the new ones.
- **The header says what one green run proves** (item 3; Nobunaga CON-49(c), narrowed by Chrollo on this change): the runner it landed on, for the declared tools — its service's PATH resolves each machine-wide and its account may execute them — and nothing broader. A sibling on that host shares the stored machine PATH and the ACLs but holds the PATH it was started with, so it is covered only once every service on the host was restarted after the last PATH change; a runner on another host is proven by nothing until a run lands there; a Linux or macOS user-scope hit only warns; `nen runner enable` reads one run as the pool's go. [`jusshin`](claude/skills/jusshin/SKILL.md) § 7 carries the paragraph — a pool spanning hosts is dispatched again, at most three unsteered times, since GitHub hands a run to any idle runner and nothing steers it to a host; each host still unseen gets one controlled dispatch of its own (the maintainer stops every proven host's services, § 7 dispatches once, the services are started again), and a host that cannot be reached that way stops the run before § 8 with the proof reported incomplete — never enabled on it, never steered by a per-host label (item 6; Copilot on #202 showed the earlier "accept the unproven host" contradicted § 8) — and § 8 runs `enable` only once § 7 has a green run per host, naming the case of a variable already on (a host added to a live pool serves jobs from registration; switching it off for the pass or accepting the window is the maintainer's call). J4's *Seen* cell carries the Windows verdict *not on the service PATH*.
- **The queue comment states the 24-hour cancellation** (item 4; Phinks QA-3) and its readiness effect: a PR check left pending by a queued preflight reads cancelled after a day, and neither is Ready under CON-32(a).
- **The pin comment names the exact tag** (item 5; Nobunaga, Feitan SEC-14): `# v4.4.0`, the tag `11d5960a…` is, in place of the moving `# v4`; the fixture's regex accepts a dotted `# vN.N…` comment and refuses a bare `# vN` on purpose — the issue's box wrote `# vN(.N)*`, which would admit the moving alias the finding is about.
- **`templates/pr-readiness.yml` pins `actions/checkout` by SHA — `3d3c42e5…` `# v7.0.1`** (the issue's comment of 2026-10-02: zheref/<product-repo-E>#98 requires SHA-pinned actions and had pinned its rendering by hand, which is drift by the template's own rule, and a `hatsu:tenkai apply` of the floating template would have reverted it). `scripts/tenkai_adopt.sh diagnose` now reports a `uses:` set that is not exactly the template's — every action, ref and comment, compared to the rendering rather than to a shape (Feitan and Nobunaga on this change: a shape check on the checkouts alone passed an impostor SHA with a lying comment, a stale pin of another tag, a quoted `uses:` scalar, and a floating action in any other step) — as DRIFT, so `apply` re-renders every earlier `@v7` rendering to the pinned shape; eight self-test cases hold it, one proving `apply` restores the SHA and one rendering a private consumer on `[self-hosted, Windows, X64]` (224 assertions). [`tenkai`](claude/skills/tenkai/SKILL.md) § 5's drift list carries the limb and the consumer re-render step. The checkout lines now match <product-repo-E> main's hand-pinned copy; its re-render also moves `runs-on` to the derived self-hosted labels and the Nen fallback ref to the current pin. Hatsu's own `.github/workflows/*.yml` still float on `@v7`; they are not renderings of this template and are not in this change.
- **Owed re-renders, not done here** (Chrollo): nothing diagnoses a stale `runner-preflight` rendering — tenkai covers `pr-readiness` only, and the release fan-out intersects changed `.github/workflows` with `consumes`, which a `templates/` change never hits — so the two known renderings keep waking on tag pushes until re-rendered through jusshin § 7: zheref/nen's and zheref/<product-repo-E>'s `runner-preflight-windows-x64.yml`. Whether a `templates/` bump is a CON-22 fan-out, and whether BC-11's embedded-Python freeze binds `tenkai_adopt.sh`, are G4 handbook questions this change does not answer.
- **Item 6 is ruled: a pool carries a label of its own** (the maintainer's ruling of 2026-10-02, asked through the picker on this build: GitHub's defaults alone — `self-hosted`, the OS, the arch — are what every runner of that OS and arch gets at registration, so a later registration joined a pool unproven). Not yet renderable: nen's schema refuses a fourth label at exit 2, citing Hatsu's own runner-policy guard, and tenkai's derivation holds the same three-label set, so the template's `runs-on` comment and `jusshin` § 2 state the ruling as pending [zheref/hatsu#204](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/204) — the policy guard, tenkai's derivation, the template's conjunct and jusshin's registration follow once nen's schema admits the label — and the fixture writes the conjunct as *not yet held* rather than as a conjunct that cannot run. #198 stays open on item 6's application.
- Plugin bumped to 0.80.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay; 0.74.0 on the branch until main took 0.74.0–0.79.0 through #189, #195, #192, #194, #193 and #197); mirrors regenerated at that stamp. On a win32 host `scripts/surface_mirror_check.sh` reads the three hook mirrors as hand-edited on this branch and on pristine `main` alike: the bytes match a fresh generation and only the file mode differs (Node reports `0666` there), which nen fixes in `v0.18.3` (zheref/nen#324); the Linux CI job reads the mirrors as it always has.
## v0.79.0 — a review reply names a commit only once it is on the PR's head chain

- **A reply that names a commit names one on the pull request's head chain** (#139: on HA-PR-#124 a thread reply said *fixed in `<sha>`* for a commit the prose ceiling, then a missing message file, had refused to write, and two corrections followed). `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Reviewer rounds and review threads* states the rule once. The commit is the `sha` this invocation's `nen commit write --json` reported (exit `0`, `sha` not null), read **after** kokusen § 5's tip read-back settles, so a commit that read-back dropped and re-ran is replaced by the re-run's `sha`; an earlier commit (a delegate's, or one pushed before a session died) is read from the commit that carries the fix in the fetched head's history (the judged head read and fetched first, then `git -C <path> log <head> -- <path of the fix>`, since the local `HEAD` can be stale), never from memory. After the push, the PR's head comes from `nen pr ready`'s *judged head* line (`(unread)` is a failed read), then `git -C <path> fetch` and `git -C <path> merge-base --is-ancestor <sha> <head>` must exit `0`. Where nothing may land on top, `nen pr ready --require-head <sha>` pins the head — **the test is the judged-head line naming `<sha>`, never the exit code** (exit `8` holds; exit `1` is also a matched-but-not-ready or unread head). A `sha` not yet on the chain right after a push is read once more after the push registers. Only then is the reply composed and posted.
- **Every other outcome holds the reply** — a refused write (missing message file, red proof, hook refusal), a write that made no commit (`--dry-run`), a refused push, a `sha` not on the chain, a read that could not be made — and reports the step, its exit and the SHAs it read. A held reply is released by a later clean read-back, or once the held `sha` is found on the head chain (the resume case).
- **One statement, three pointers.** `sharingan` § 6 points at the rule instead of restating thread hygiene; `kokusen` § 5 drops its restated clause (its residue line now reads `git -C <path> add -- <file>`); `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § *Verified delivery* step 3 points a delegate's reply at it; `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue* names the raw `fetch` and `merge-base --is-ancestor` calls, since nen reads a PR's head but not the ancestry of a commit in it.
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten: Chrollo, Nobunaga ×2, Phinks): the first draft compared a literal `ls-remote origin` tip that never matched once anything landed on top; the rule now requires ancestry of the PR's head, names the `sha` source (the write's own `--json`, after the read-back settles), and makes every failing outcome hold the reply. Nobunaga's second pass found that `--require-head` exits `1` both for a matched-but-not-ready head and for an unread one, so the pin is tested by the judged-head line, not the exit; that fix had no further review pass.
- Plugin bumped to 0.79.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

- **What Copilot's round changed** (HA-PR-#197): a resumed or delegated session found the fix commit with `git log` on its local `HEAD` before fetching the PR's head, so a fix pushed from another checkout was invisible and an older commit touching the path could be named. The head is now read and fetched first, and the fix commit is found in that head's history.

## v0.78.0 — the trusted validator learns the next readiness and regenerate shapes, frozen

- **Step one of the two-step landing for #160 and #165** (`docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md`, 2026-09-20: main's trusted validator judges every PR, so it learns a workflow's next shape before any workflow flips). `scripts/workflow_runner_policy_check.rb` now accepts, beside today's shapes, **one exact next form** of each: no workflow changes here, and step two flips `pr-readiness.yml`, `surface-mirror-regenerate.yml`, the template and tenkai's self-test, then narrows the table to the new shapes (the plan is kept with the effort).
- **Readiness (#160) — the freshness step goes, the pin moves into the verdict.** The next `pr-readiness.yml` drops its separate freshness step; the **Readiness verdict** step is frozen whole (`READINESS_NEXT_VERDICT`: its keys, `id: verdict`, `shell: bash`, its env — including `EVENT_HEAD: ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.sha }}` — and its run, compared byte for byte). The run calls `nen pr ready … --require-head "$EVENT_HEAD" --exclude-check readiness` and routes nen's exits: `0` ready, `1` not-ready, `8` **superseded** (the head moved while the run was evaluating; nothing is asserted about that SHA), anything else *readiness undetermined — nen exited <rc>*; nen's output is fenced with a random `::stop-commands::` token and written to `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` under a random heredoc delimiter. **Publish** is frozen too (`READINESS_NEXT_PUBLISH`, `if: ${{ always() }}`, no `STALE` branch).
- **Regenerate (#165) — a failure-only cleanup step.** The next `surface-mirror-regenerate.yml` may add one step, frozen to its two-line body (`REGENERATE_CLEANUP`), that runs only on `failure() && steps.cpr.outcome == 'failure'` and DELETEs only `refs/heads/bot/surface-mirror-regenerate`, naming the PR-creation toggle when the delete itself fails; the PR step must carry `id: cpr`. **The toggle cannot be read first:** its endpoint needs the Administration (read) permission, which `GITHUB_TOKEN` cannot be granted, and an App token or PAT is declined (SEC-9). The workflow header and `docs/SURFACES.md` now name *Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests* as the job's prerequisite, and the hand cleanup until step two lands.
- **What the validator refuses, by name:** any byte off the frozen bodies (a whole-run comparison, so a `#` line after a `\` continuation cannot end the `pr ready` call before its pins); an extra `--exclude-check`; a dropped exit-8 arm; `continue-on-error`, `id` or `working-directory` on the cleanup step, `shell: sh`, a folded `run: >-`; a `<<:` merge key or an `*alias`; workflow- or job-level `env:` (a `BASH_ENV` there would make bash source a PR-checkout file before even a frozen body); a changed step set or order. `scalar()` fails with a named message instead of raising.
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten: Nobunaga ×2, Feitan, Leorio): the per-invocation argv matcher (Feitan M1/M2, Nobunaga H1/H2) was replaced by freezing the whole step, which closes the comment, echo, substring, reassignment and continuation escapes at once; the cleanup's drift condition (Nobunaga) and the toggle wording (Feitan, SEC-9) were settled. The freeze itself had no reviewer pass of its own, because every budget was spent; it is verified by the self-test (each fixture fails if it equals its base), validator mutants (disabling any freeze, the key or value check, or strict `scalar()` fails the self-test) and 48 adapted Nobunaga/Feitan bypass cases, all rejected, with the controls accepted.
- **What Copilot's round changed** (HA-PR-#193, 2 findings, both fixed): main's trusted validator runs `--self-test` against the PR checkout, and the self-test read its current-shape baseline from that checkout, so a step-two checkout would have failed it (the readiness freshness step gone; the regenerate step given a second `id` and a second cleanup). The self-test now builds every fixture from independent baselines: the current readiness tail held as `CURRENT_READINESS_TAIL`, and the regenerate workflow reduced to its current shape. Each baseline must validate, and the missing-`id` case now tests a missing id. Verified on a scratch step-two checkout, where the self-test and validation exit 0; the old validator exits 1 there.
- **Still open, for step two:** a `$GITHUB_ENV` write in an earlier, unfrozen step could set `BASH_ENV` before the frozen body; no workflow writes it today, and a text check cannot close it reliably, so step two freezes those steps too.
- Plugin bumped to 0.78.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.77.0 — getsuga fits the description cap, and re-checks the changelog at the judged head before its merge

- **getsuga's `description` fits the 1,024-character cap** (#186). It was 1,092 characters in the source (1,087 in each mirror), over the Agent Skills limit; Codex 0.154.0 truncates an over-cap description to 1,021 characters plus `...`, and Copilot CLI drops the skill (github/copilot-cli#3494). It is now 865. `docs/surfaces/codex.md` records the cap and both behaviours.
- **The cap is guarded.** `scripts/prose_size_check.sh` now fails any source or mirror `description` over 1,024 characters, counted as code points and measured whole in the forms the generator writes — plain, plain-continued, folded, literal, single- or double-quoted, CRLF. Everything else is refused by name as UNMEASURABLE, never passed: invalid UTF-8 (checked exactly by perl's strict `Encode` decoder; macOS `iconv` rejects a valid multibyte character straddling its buffer, and a missing perl is an exit-2 wiring refusal), a duplicate key, a quoted key or a space before the colon, an alias or anchor value, a BOM before `---`, an unlistable skill or mirror directory. A dieted skill is reported once. The fixture (lane `prose-guard`) carries Phinks' red cases R1–R7 and one row per refusal; each new guard arm, removed in turn, turns it red. **The guard should not grow further:** a parser in shell is residue owed to a nen verb (BC-11, the shape of zheref/nen#298).
- **getsuga § 3a re-checks completeness at the merge, at the judged head** (#140). § 3a records `<preflightTip>` when the proposal opens and, immediately before its merge or its fall-back ask, reads `<baseNow>` once and runs `nen changelog completeness --range <preflightTip>..<baseNow> --json` from a worktree detached at the judged head (`--repo` and `--changelog` both there, the worktree removed after) — because `--fragment-dir` defaults to `changelog.d/` under `--repo`, and a PR with a fragment in the checkout the check ran from would otherwise count as reconciled. It routes on `ok`: `ok:true` continues; `ok:false` with PRs in `missing[]` routes back; anything else stops at the declaration gate, quoting the output. `changelog.d/` empty and the tag matching the manifest are re-asserted at the same moment.
- **Route back has one order:** bring the recorded `<baseNow>` SHA itself into the release branch (murasaki, as en step 3 does — never `origin/<baseNow>`, which is no ref), collate (§ 3 item 1), then item 3, which must exit 0 before the push; `<preflightTip>` advances only after that pass. Two laps at most, each absorbing one base move; the body is rewritten whole, its live read named as `gh` residue (`gh pr view <N> --repo <owner/name> --json body -q .body > <file>`, then `nen pr edit-body --body-file <file>`); § 4 stays the backstop.
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten: Nobunaga ×2, Feitan, Chrollo, Phinks): the guard measures block scalars and CRLF whole instead of skipping them, refuses invalid UTF-8, duplicate keys and unlistable directories, and stops double-reporting; the re-check is scoped to `<preflightTip>..<baseNow>` (a whole `<vPrev>..` range re-reports PRs § 3 already reconciled). Nobunaga's second pass found no critical or high open; his four mediums and two lows — the judged-head re-check, route back's order, the non-canonical forms, exact UTF-8 — are settled in the last commit without a further review pass, verified by the fixture and a scratch repo (a fragment in the cwd no longer masks a missing PR: `ok:false, missing:[5]`).
- **What Copilot's round changed** (HA-PR-#194, 2 findings, both fixed): route back merges the recorded `<baseNow>` SHA itself, never `origin/<baseNow>`, which names no ref; and a `|` or `>` description is measured with YAML's chomping — clip keeps one final line break, strip none, keep every trailing one — so a clipped literal of 1,024 visible characters reads 1,025 and is over (24 header and tail combinations match PyYAML; three fixture rows, two of them red on the previous guard).
- Plugin bumped to 0.77.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.76.0 — a push after review is gated on catch-up, checks and provenance, in one `&&` chain

- **The post-review push is one gated chain, written once** (#188: `mukai` step 6 pushed while its own catch-up had exited 1 mid-merge, because the rule lived only in prose). `murasaki` § 6 now spells the push as one `&&` chain: `nen wc catch-up --json` must read `"noOp": true`, `HEAD` must equal the `<proved>` SHA that steps 3–5 proved, `@{upstream}` must resolve (both the published precondition and the range base), the lane's declared check runs, then `scripts/branch_authorship_check.sh`, then `nen wc publish` (never `--set-upstream` there). `mukai` step 6 points at it. `aka` § 7's two push forms are chained to the check with `&&`, and `aka` § 4 runs it before the squash, routing exit 1 (every SHA reported, nothing squashed, the maintainer decides) and exit 2 (a wiring stop) as § 7's step 0b does.
- **A reviewer's commit is caught before it is pushed** (#170: a reviewer's repro commit reached the branch because nothing read `base..HEAD`). New `scripts/branch_authorship_check.sh --repo <path> --base <ref> [--require-agent <persona>] [--trunk <ref>] [--allow-email <e>]` reads every commit in the outgoing range. With `--require-agent` it reads each `Hatsu-Agent` trailer against the run's persona, so a reviewer's persona or a missing trailer is a finding; trailers were chosen over a per-SHA ledger because they survive the squash and rebase that break one. `--trunk` exempts only the base's own history and a merge whose tree is exactly git's clean merge (`git merge-tree --write-tree`, git ≥ 2.38, probed once, exit 2 naming the version otherwise), the trailer-less commit `nen wc catch-up` writes. Exit 1 names SHA, author and file count; exit 2 is wiring. **A commit that forges the run's own persona passes this test**, and murasaki § 6 and aka § 7 say so; `hanten` § 4's printed-SHA comparison (HEAD and the branch ref printed before raising, re-read on every return, then the check over that range) and the `<proved>` pin are what catch a commit made during a review.
- **Each call site judges only its own outgoing range** (#159): `@{upstream}` in murasaki § 6; in aka §§ 4 and 7 the SHA `git ls-remote` printed, or `git merge-base origin/<base> HEAD` for a first publish, as aka's own table says. A published web-flow commit is never re-judged.
- **The chain is guarded, not trusted.** New `scripts/publish_gate_check.sh` fails any documented catch-up/publish block that is joined by `;`, `||`, a lone `&`, a newline or a pipe (glued or spaced), that negates any step from the catch-up or the authorship check to the publish, that carries a second ungated publish, or that hides the publish in `~~~`, nested or unterminated fences; whitespace runs collapse before matching and `#` comments are stripped only outside quotes; an unreadable or non-regular file is an offence. New lane `publish-integrity-guard` (`scripts/publish_integrity_fixture.sh`, 73 hermetic cases, Phinks' RED-1..RED-7 ported) holds both scripts; `scripts/plugin_bump_check.sh` ships the authorship script as a runtime row.
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten: Nobunaga ×2, Feitan, Chrollo, Phinks): provenance by `Hatsu-Agent` trailer replaced author-email matching, which stays the weak half; the outgoing range per call site; HEAD pinned to `<proved>`; aka's check moved before the squash; the gate check's negation, glued-pipe, whitespace and comment escapes closed; the forged-persona limit stated instead of overclaimed; the git floor probed. Nobunaga's second pass found the criteria of #188, #170 and #159 met with no critical or high open; its mediums and lows are settled in the last commit without a further review pass, verified by the fixture (13 new cases, 7 of them confirmed passing on the previous script and failing on the new one).
- **What Copilot's round changed** (HA-PR-#192): blocks were read one at a time, so a file could pair one gated chain with a separate bare `nen wc publish` block, and a publish placed ahead of its authorship check passed. The skills that own a push now carry a file-level policy — in `murasaki` and `mukai` every publish needs its own catch-up and authorship check, in `aka` its own authorship check — and anywhere a publish ahead of the check its block names fails; five fixture cases, three of them confirmed passing on the previous script.
- `hanten` stays within its 12,288-byte ceiling (12,273). Plugin bumped to 0.76.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.75.0 — every settable value is written out, and asked for before a skill needs it

- **One list of what a consumer can set** ([`docs/ROSTER.md`](docs/ROSTER.md) § *Rulings of 2026-10-01*). `contracts/config-catalogue.json` holds 33 values Hatsu skills read from a consumer's `nen/workflow.json`, `nen/gates.json` and `nen/contract.json`, grouped by domain (development, versioning, testing, review, reporting, notifications, deployment, publishing). Each row carries:
  - the default canon already states, with its `source`;
  - the options, for a fixed set (`enum`) or a list drawn from one (`subset`);
  - its readers and the skills that cannot run without it;
  - any step on an outside platform: a store record, an API key exported under an environment-variable name, a reviewer app, a runner host.
- **Exclusions are named, not implied.** The catalogue's `$comment` names every key it leaves out, with the reason. A written snapshot or a guess would change behaviour for each of them:
  - derived at run time: `iteration.lane`, `reports.timeZone`, and `profile.default`, which nen derives from `profile.allowed`;
  - resolved from a Hatsu canon file only when absent: `round_policy`;
  - also read by nen, whose own default may differ: the `commits` trailer keys, `futon.advanceGo`;
  - structural blocks that are the repository's own: `models`, `reports.sections`, the gates identity blocks.

  `check-catalogue` holds 15 defaults against `docs/WORKFLOW.md`'s key tables, so the two statements of one default cannot drift apart.
- **`scripts/config_values.sh` is the one reader.** It follows `tenkai_adopt.sh`'s pattern: a bash wrapper over embedded python3, with a hermetic `--self-test`. Its commands:
  - `diagnose` reports every value by domain.
  - `fill` writes each absent defaulted value, plus a `$<key>` note naming the options (`"$autoEn": "one of: true | false"`, `any of:` for a subset). nen reads `$`-prefixed keys as metadata. Text is inserted in place, so a file's hand formatting and line endings survive. `fill` is idempotent, never creates a declaration, and never rewrites a set value or a `$<key>` note of the maintainer's own.
  - `need --skill <s>` lists what one phase cannot run without. It refuses an unknown skill.
  - `set` writes one answered value. It refuses a value outside its options (`1` is not `true`), a target its `candidatesFrom` does not declare, a production `deploy.defaultTarget`, and any credential-shaped string inside the value.
  - `check-catalogue` validates the catalogue.

  Read and write alike refuse a declaration that is a symlink, sits under a symlinked `nen/`, or resolves outside the repository. A duplicate key, a deep nest or an unwritable directory is `blocked` or exit 2, never a traceback. `fill` and `set` hold an advisory lock on `nen/`, so a concurrent write is never lost.
- **Values only some repositories need never block adoption.**
  - An `on-use` value (self-hosted `runners`, `project.targets`) is offered and may be deferred.
  - An `optional` value is offered, and nothing stops for it.
  - A `null` marked `nullIsAnswer` is a deliberate answer: "ask on each call" for `deploy.defaultTarget` and `launch.default`.
  - `deploy.defaultTarget` stays *typed, never picked* (WORKFLOW § 4, row `missing-maintainer-choice`): its candidates are listed for reference, none starred.
  - A new `project.targets` entry lands through its declaration PR before the phase that asked runs on it.
- **`tenkai` § 6d writes the values out and asks the rest up front.**
  - Its engine gains `config/defaults` (Hatsu-owned, repaired by `apply` through `fill`) and `config/needed` (consumer configuration, routed to the owner's answer). Either reads `blocked`, never "every value is set", when a declaration cannot be read.
  - § 1's Apply picker names the values `fill` will write.
  - § 3's nen row now scopes nen's ownership to the file, with the values inside it the consumer's.
  - § 6d's state table covers every state the engine emits.
  - The description, Hard limits and Authority name both engine writes, `fill` and `set`.
  - `tenkai_adopt.sh`'s header states the exception.
- **A missing value is asked, never a hard stop.**
  - `ten` § 6 gains step 5b in every phase row: `"$hatsu_root/scripts/config_values.sh" need --repo . --skill <phase>`. Exit 1 asks, sets up and resumes; exit 2 stops, quoted. A declined answer falls back to the phase's own ask.
  - § 0a keeps its `missing-argument` / `missing-configuration` citation.
  - To fit the 12,288-byte ceiling, `ten` quotes ruling A by date and letter and drops 32 emphasis-only bold markers and a ⚠️ (counted against main: 178 `**` → 114). No rule was removed.
  - `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 4 names the catalogue as what counts as required, and step 3 names `fill`'s answer.
- **Residue and owned dependency.** No nen verb writes a declaration value, and the `$`-metadata convention is followed by nen's loaders but stated in no published contract. Both are named in tenkai's Residue and `docs/PROCESS.md` § Residue as an owned dependency on `zheref/nen`, not yet filed.
- **Pre-PR review settled** (hanten, first cycle: Feitan 4, Chrollo 16, Nobunaga 15, Phinks 13).
  - Every finding is fixed except two deferred to named records: coverage measurement (zheref/hatsu#148) and the nen owned dependency (tenkai's Residue). Phinks's red reproductions now pass, the race 3/3.
  - `.gitleaksignore` names the one fingerprint of a fake `sk-live-…` fixture in the already-published `29fac9e2`, which is never rewritten.
  - Hatsu's own `nen/` is not filled here: that is a G4 change to this repository's own declarations.
- **Hatsu's own `mukai.autoEn` is `true`**, on the maintainer's request of 2026-10-01 ("Let's configure this repository for autoEn (let's make it enabled)"): a `hatsu:mukai` here now starts `hatsu:en` on the PR it opened instead of offering it. Written with this PR's own `config_values.sh set`, which added the `$autoEn` options note beside it.
- **Copilot's round settled.** `config_values.sh` no longer imports `fcntl` on Windows: there the lock is `msvcrt` on a file under `.nen/`, as `hanten_cycle_ledger.sh` does it, while POSIX keeps `flock` on `nen/`. `check-catalogue` now fails when a WORKFLOW-sourced default has no key-table row to check against, instead of passing silently. The four defaults documented in WORKFLOW's prose declare `sourceTable: false` and are held to a line naming the key.
- **New focused lane `config-values-guard`** (`nen shu test --lane config-values-guard`). `tenkai-guard` now has 222 assertions.
- Plugin bumped to 0.75.0 after catching up with v0.73.0 (HA-PR-#183) and v0.74.0 (HA-PR-#189) (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.74.0 — Antigravity writes each report as one artifact per conversation

- **The Antigravity report contract is complete** (zheref/hatsu#145, criteria 1 and 2). `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Publishing a report* now holds the Antigravity half of the one-address rule: one `<report-name>.md` per conversation, keyed by `<conversation-id>`, written with `Overwrite: true` whenever it already exists, never a new file per turn; an effort that continues in a new conversation starts its own file. `spiritual-message` § 7 points there rather than restating it, and its block list now cites `reports.sections.<variant>.blocks` instead of copying it (the old copy had drifted).
- **A per-turn rule in the always-loaded rules** (`claude/rules/hatsu.md` item 9): on Antigravity, every turn that renders a report (docs/PROCESS.md § *Reporting a phase*) writes it as that report's native artifact before yielding — `spiritual-message.md` for a turn or landing report, the report's own name otherwise — the same file each render, `Overwrite: true` once it exists; a chat summary never stands in. It is keyed on PROCESS's predicate, not a list of skills, so `aka` and `mugetsu` are covered and `en`'s quiet polls are not forced to write. This deliberately replaces the issue's literal "every ren, en, mukai turn" and narrows "every turn of an effort" to "per conversation", because the artifact path is conversation-scoped.
- **`docs/surfaces/antigravity.md`** records `write_to_file`'s `TargetFile` and `Overwrite` parameters with a dated provenance row, marked third-party and unverified on any `agy` here.
- **Not closable here:** criterion 3, agents reliably writing the artifact every turn, is host model behaviour; the repository can only state the rule.
- Plugin bumped to 0.74.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.73.0 — `hatsu:rikugan` renders the final state upon delivery, the session binds the pinned nen on the host, and every worktree lives under `.nen/worktrees/<surface>`

Release unit for `v0.67.0..v0.73.0`: [#163](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/163) (Antigravity's builder efforts take `Workspace: "branch"`), [#172](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/172) (the Codex install guide in the README), [#173](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/173) (the subagent Mukai lifecycle and staging discipline), [#174](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/174) (`hatsu:jusshin`, v0.68.0), [#177](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/177) (the runner preflight's AppData check, v0.68.1), [#178](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/178) (its first-match classification, v0.68.2), [#179](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/179) (the per-user remedy wording, v0.69.1), [#180](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/180) (the reviewer-round caps' canon default, v0.69.0), [#181](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/181) (`ten` § 2c, nen reachable by the maintainer, v0.71.0), [#183](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/183) (this section, v0.73.0), [#184](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/184) (nen repinned to v0.18.2, v0.70.0), [#185](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/185) (a direct `hatsu:mugetsu` is the go, v0.72.0), [#187](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/187) (jusshin's first live run recorded) and [#191](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/191) (this release proposal). `v0.68.0` through `v0.72.0` were never tagged: their sections below ship inside `v0.73.0`, the first tag after `v0.67.0`. This line is where the range's PRs are cited, which is what `CON-33(c)` reconciles against.

- **`hatsu:rikugan` — the name returns as a skill, for the Rikugan page** (the maintainer's request of 2026-09-30: *"How come Rikugan is not available as a callable skill? I tried to call Rikugan as a suffix after "then" clause on a futon skill invokation and it rejected it saying the skill DOES NOT exist. It should! And it should work upon any completed workflow to summarize the final state of work upon delivery."*). Since the names swapped at v0.42.0 the Rikugan was only `templates/rikugan.html` — the `final` and `register` variants — reached through `backlog-board` § 3's render path, and the skill once called `rikugan` had become `spiritual-message`; `futon` § 8 resolves a `then` step against the installed skills, so `then rikugan` resolved to nothing and the clause fell to prose. `hatsu:rikugan [for <CODE>#<N>[,…] | <owner/name>] [as final|register]` renders the final state of a just-completed workflow — the `final` variant for a delivered one-effort scope (the desk cleared, one register row per issue and PR the workflow touched with the `nen pr ready` verdict quoted verbatim, spend, legend), `register` for a stopped workflow, a wider scope, or on request — through `backlog-board` § 3 **by name, never restated**, written to `<reports.dir>/<YYYY-MM-DD>-<effort>.html` (the one report kept on disk) and published as the surface's artifact. The scope is derived in one order, total — handed over by the composite that names it (a futon `then`-chain step's scope is the run's objects plus every earlier step's output), typed, the session's last completed workflow's (futon's selector run, en's PR, backlog-loop's cycle, black-voice's PR, read from the run's record), the checkout's (the branch's PR and the issues its body closes — also the fall-through for an unlisted workflow), else asked as free text (`missing-argument`), never the whole backlog — and stated with its source in the first line. `final` is only for a delivered, one-effort scope (the desk cleared, as backlog-board § 3, spiritual-message, en § 8 and `reports.retain: final-only` already define it; a typed single PR takes its branch slug as the effort); a workflow that stopped short, a typed multi-object scope or a repository scope renders `register` at `current.html`, not retained. The rows come from one `nen report data --base origin/<branch.base> --prs <n,...> --issues <n,...>` call (`--base` is required — exit 2 without it; verified at nen 0.18.1 against `zheref/hatsu`: PR 177 `MERGED` and issue 170 returned together at exit 0). The grammar is `for [<scope>] as [<variant:final|register>]`, verified at nen 0.18.1 (2026-09-30) — a leading bracketed `[<scope>]` is refused by `nen parse` (*its leading slot is bracketed but nothing introduces it*), hence the `for` anchor; `nen parse futon` classifies `then rikugan` and `then getsuga+rikugan` as skill steps, and refuses `then tag+rikugan` because `tag` is the terminal's own word. Read-only: never labels, merges, pushes or comments; readiness is the quoted verdict. `futon` § 8's `then rikugan` and § 10's final report are one render, after the `then` outcome exists; `en` § 7 row 7 and § 8, `backlog-loop` § 10, `spiritual-message` § 2, `backlog-board` § 3's *Other callers* and the `/kurapika` command point at it as the callable entry, backlog-board § 3 staying the render path it composes — `build` and `getsuga` are not wired to it. Distinct from its pre-v0.42.0 meaning — `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-09-30 — rikugan is callable; nen is bound on the host at session start; worktrees live under `.nen/worktrees/<surface>`*. Lean (12,283 bytes), on the diet's list in `scripts/prose_size_check.sh`, so the 12,288-byte ceiling is enforced.
- **The session binds the pinned nen on the host, at session start** (the maintainer's request of 2026-09-30: *"When a new session starts, it is expected global NEN binary to be updated and made available on global profile both for agentic and human-manual usage (on PATH or bash/zsh profile/rc as applicable)."*). `ten` § 2's host link at `~/.local/bin/nen` was *the maintainer's word only* and the session-only `mktemp` bind the default, so every session ran the pin while the host's `nen` stayed where it was (0.14.0 on the maintainer's host against a v0.18.1 pin). The ruling inverts it: **the host is the default.** New `scripts/nen_global.sh --root <hatsu_root> [--bin-dir <dir>] [--rc auto|none|<file>] [--dry-run] [--quiet]` reads the contract's `pinned_ref`, runs nen's own two-step bootstrap only when `~/.local/bin/nen --version` is not the pin, verifies the binary's SHA256 against the new structured contract key `dependency.bootstrap.sha256.<platform>` **before** linking, links atomically (tmp + `mv`; a real file at the link is refused, exit 3; a linked nen newer than the pin that nen accepts is kept, `kept-newer`, never downgraded), passes `--source` explicitly with `NEN_SOURCE`/`NEN_CACHE_DIR` unset, and appends an idempotent marked `# >>> hatsu nen-global >>>` PATH block to the shell's rc and profile (`$SHELL` zsh → `~/.zshrc` and `~/.zprofile`; bash → `~/.bashrc` and `~/.bash_profile`; else `~/.profile`) — **prepending `~/.local/bin` deliberately so the pinned nen wins over any other on `PATH`** (Feitan's precedence finding, settled as a documented trade-off), and never re-adding a block the user removed (a marker under `~/.config/hatsu/`). Opt-out `HATSU_NEN_GLOBAL=0|false|no|off`, or `${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-$HOME/.config}/hatsu/nen-global` containing `off`; a win32/MSYS host is skipped. Exits: 0 ok (`current`, `linked`, `kept-newer`) or `skipped (<reason>)`, 2 usage / contract unreadable / a bootstrap URL that is not `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/<dependency.source>/<pinned_ref>/<script_path_in_source>`, 3 a real file at `<bin-dir>/nen`, 4 the host write refused (`mkdir`/`ln`/`mv` — the harness or a sandbox), 5 bootstrap or verification failed (fetch, the bootstrap's code, a non-absolute or out-of-cache path, a digest or version mismatch); its one summary line reads `nen-global: nen <pin> at <link> (<current|linked|kept-newer>) · rc: <what each profile file got>`. New lane `nen-global-guard` (`scripts/nen_global_fixture_check.sh`, hermetic under a throwaway `HOME` with a stub bootstrap) holds it, and `scripts/plugin_bump_check.sh` ships it as a plugin-surface row. `hooks/session-start.sh` runs it as step 0 **on the source copy always, and on a mirrored copy only after `HATSU_PLUGIN_ROOT` (or Codex's `PLUGIN_ROOT`) is verified** — fail-open, bounded at about 50 s by a watchdog even where no `timeout` exists — and on Claude Code appends its summary line to the additional context. `ten` § 2 runs it as the host default, with one total row per exit: 0 re-probes `nen --version` through `command -v nen` (exporting `PATH="$HOME/.local/bin:$PATH"` in the block when the session's `PATH` misses the link) and quotes the summary line in § 4; `skipped`, 3 and 4 fall back to the session bind (3 and 4 hand the maintainer the verified path through row `host-nen-link`'s offer, § 2c); 2 is fixed and rerun once, else the session bind, reported; 5 is still § 3's halt. `nen_global.sh`'s shell two-step is named the sanctioned exception to *shell only when nen is absent*. `mugetsu` § 7's re-pin after publishing nen itself leaves the host link to the contract's pin, which follows once the installed Hatsu carries the repin (`ten` § 4b, `bakuryuha`); `bakuryuha`'s never-list is reworded for it. The README's requirements box, `docs/SURFACES.md` § 1's session-start row, `docs/surfaces/claude-code.md`'s hook rows, `docs/surfaces/README.md` and `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 6 name the binding; `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue* records that no nen verb owns a host's `PATH` or shell profile.
- **Every worktree Hatsu creates lives at `<core>/.nen/worktrees/<surface>/<name>`** (the maintainer's request of 2026-09-30: *"I saw worktrees being generated as sibling folders of the core checkout directory. I don't like this approach and as possible we should generate a git-ignored folder (maybe for each surface) where surfaces can install the worktrees and safely work within a contaned environment without creating bad hygiene outside the actual workspaces. ... it would be great to make it canon and clear on all surfaces."*; the ruling, *"go with .nen/worktrees/<surface> as canon"*). Rule 6 required a worktree per effort and named no path, `hanten` § 4 put its reviewer at `.claude/worktrees/hanten-<persona>`, the stash-and-restore proof said `<tmp>`, and a sibling folder was the easiest answer to a rule with no path in it. Now one path on every surface, `<core>` resolved as `nen wc worktrees --repo <target> --json` → `core` — `claude-code`, `codex`, `cursor`, `antigravity`, the slugs `ten` § 5 already passes — git-ignored (Hatsu's `.gitignore`; tenkai's `dirs/nen-state` row in a consumer), inside the core checkout, never a sibling, never `/tmp`; `hanten-<persona>` for the reviewer, `prove-<n>` for the proof, the branch slug for a lead-session effort, and kagutsuchi's fixed `.nen/worktrees/kagutsuchi` kept as the one purpose-named path. The harness-native exceptions are named once: Claude Code's own isolation stays at `.claude/worktrees/` ("By default, the worktree is created under `.claude/worktrees/<name>/` at your repository root", fetched 2026-09-30; a `WorktreeCreate` hook may relocate it only outside any repository), git-ignored and listed by tenkai as `dirs/claude-worktrees`; Codex's app worktrees under `$CODEX_HOME/worktrees`, Cursor's under its machine worktree root and Antigravity's `Workspace: "branch"` are accepted where the tool puts them. A Hatsu-made worktree on Claude Code is `git worktree add` then `EnterWorktree` by `path`, its one approval prompt answered. Containment is both ways: a nested worktree survives `git clean -fdx` (*Skipping repository*, verified locally) but `git clean -ffdx` deletes `.nen/` wholesale, so `-ff` is never run in a core checkout that holds worktrees; a worktree is removed when its effort lands, on Hatsu's own checkout without exception, because Codex's plugin install copies ignored worktrees too. The full rule with each surface's line quoted is `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § *Where worktrees live* — the one place it lives; rule 6 of `claude/rules/hatsu.md` keeps the path shape, never-a-sibling and Claude Code's `.claude/worktrees` exception and cites that section for the rest; `hanten` § 4 (resolving `<core>` first), `breath` § 2b, the stash-and-restore shape's *Prove* step, `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` § 1, the four `docs/surfaces/*.md` identity tables and `antigravity.md` § 11.1 name the path; `docs/ROSTER.md` records ruling C; `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue* records that `git worktree add|remove` stays hand-spelt because nen has no worktree verb that writes.
- Counts: forty-eight skills, forty-nine directories per surface with `ten` — `README.md`, `claude/skills/README.md` (the way-of-working table reads twenty-five, twenty-one atomic), `docs/SURFACES.md` § 2, `docs/surfaces/{codex,cursor,antigravity}.md`, `docs/surfaces/README.md`, the marketplace description; `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` § 7 carries rikugan (twenty-six, fourteen deriving state; § 6's terminal list names it) and `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue* its by-hand scope read. `ten` is trimmed to make room for § 2's exit table (12,281 of 12,288 bytes after the review below), `futon` for its one-render `then rikugan` line (12,263 of 12,288), `hanten` for resolving `<core>` (12,280 of 12,288).
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten on `7e747498`: Feitan 8 findings, Chrollo 16, Nobunaga 16, Phinks 8). Fixed: `ten` § 2's host/session axis renamed and made total — one row per `nen_global.sh` exit, § 1's non-`ok` rows routed to § 2 as a whole, the read-only reporters given their own `4 · 5` row in § 6 and the two "any other" rows made exclusive; `rikugan`'s `nen report data` lines carry the required `--base origin/<branch.base>` and take the scope's PRs and issues in one call (so does `backlog-board` § 2's), its § 2 is ordered and total with the handed-over scope first and the checkout as the fall-through, `final` is reserved for a delivered one-effort scope, the blocks and the desk rule are cited to backlog-board § 3 instead of restated, § 6 lists only the callers that name it, its § 0 declines P1b and says what is not running, Residue is narrowed to scope derivation, and every verification is restamped at nen 0.18.1; `build` and `getsuga` — never wired — are removed from rikugan § 6, the README row, the ROSTER *Reached three ways* row and the scope table; `en` § 7 row 7 names `hatsu:rikugan` (one owner for the step); futon's typed `then rikugan` and § 10 are one render; `hanten` § 4 and the WORKFLOW isolation row resolve `<core>` through `nen wc worktrees` before `git worktree add`; rule 6 cites WORKFLOW § *Where worktrees live* instead of restating it; every "step 0 runs on every copy" claim now reads *on the source copy, and on a mirrored copy once its plugin root is verified*, WORKFLOW § 6 names the host binding as the hooks' third purpose, and the Integrity probe path reads `.nen/worktrees/claude-code/wt1`; `mugetsu` § 7's host link follows the repin once the installed Hatsu carries it, not the PR landing; the README requirements box carries the opt-out list, the deliberate `~/.local/bin` precedence (Feitan's PATH finding, a documented trade-off), the never-re-added block and the `skipped` fallback; `scripts/nen_global.sh` verifies the SHA256 against `dependency.bootstrap.sha256.<platform>` before linking, links atomically, passes `--source` explicitly, exits `4` on a refused host write, and `hooks/session-start.sh` runs it only through a verified plugin root under a watchdog.
- **What Copilot's round changed** (HA-PR-#183 at `565297ed`, 6 findings, all fixed): the contract records the `linux-x64` digest, so a Linux host binds instead of exiting `5` (the guard asserts every published platform has one); a failing post-link probe with no previous link removes the new link; the never-re-add record is written before the block and rolled back if the block fails, so no block exists without it; `rikugan` § 7 stops instead of falling back to `backlog-state`, which reads the open backlog rather than the scope; and the README headline, its skill row, this section and ROSTER ruling B state the variant rule once — `final` for a delivered one-effort scope, `register` for a stopped one or a wider scope.
- **The digests follow the pin.** Main's v0.70.0 repinned nen to `v0.18.2` (#184) while this branch was in review, so `dependency.bootstrap.sha256` carries that release's three digests — darwin-arm64 `73a003f1…`, linux-x64 `4c355aac…`, windows-x64 `c3bd7d8a…` — read from its `SHA256SUMS` and identical to the record in `pinned_ref_semantics`; every future repin moves this key with the pin, or `nen_global.sh` refuses the new binary at exit `5`.
- **Reconciled with #181 (the maintainer's ruling of 2026-09-30, picked at the G5 on HA-PR-#183: *A · Reconcile*).** Both land, in one division of labour — **the hook acts, `ten` § 2c verifies.** `scripts/nen_global.sh` keeps linking `~/.local/bin/nen` at session start by default (ruling A above; `ten` § 2's host default, with its exit table); #181's `ten` § 2c — the login-shell probe `"${SHELL:?}" -lic 'command -v nen && nen shu tools --repo "$1"' nen-probe "$hatsu_root" </dev/null`, its three readings `reachable at <path>` / `installed, not reachable (<row | no nen>)` / `not read (<rc>, <first stderr line>)`, and § 4's `host: …` token beside § 2's `nen-global:` line — stays, run after § 2 every time (§ 1's `ok` row now goes to § 2c, not § 4), as the proof that the maintainer's own shell resolves the binary. `nen/decisions.json` row `host-nen-link` (ask-once-per-run) becomes **the host's fallback offer**, by `ten` § 2c's predicate, verbatim: offered where the login shell reads installed, not reachable; never where it reads reachable; an opt-out skip is no offer (host: opted out), win32 is not read, and a removed-by-user rc entry withholds the PATH half of option A. Option A's halves follow `nen_global.sh`'s state — where it bound the host (`current`, `linked`, `kept-newer`) A is the one `PATH` line only, never a relink (a `kept-newer` link is never downgraded; what the login shell resolved instead is named); where it did not (exit `2` unfixed after one rerun, `3`, `4`, `skipped (another session is binding)`) A is the link plus the `PATH` line; on exit `3` the statement names the real file (its path, `ls -l`, its `--version`) and that A replaces it, and the star moves to B. The session always resolves the pin — through the host link (`PATH` exported in the block) or, where the host was not bound, the session bind. `ten` § 2's exit-3/4 row hands the row's offer instead of a bare `ln -sfn` line (option A *is* that command); `mugetsu` § 7 keeps main's `session nen:` token beside `host:` for the login shell's verdict; `bakuryuha`'s never-list, `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue* (both residue items: no nen verb owns a host's `PATH` or shell profile, and the login-shell probe), the README's requirements box and `docs/ROSTER.md` (row *A · Reconciled with #181*) say the same. `ten` is re-trimmed for § 2c (12,281 of 12,288 bytes).
- **What the review of the reconciliation changed** (hanten on the PR's ledger at `8bda2da5`: Chrollo 12, Nobunaga 12). The prose half: one offer predicate, written once in `ten` § 2c and carried verbatim by row `host-nen-link`, the README requirements box, the skills index's ten row, `mugetsu` § 7, ROSTER row *A · Reconciled with #181* and the reconcile bullet above — closing the cases the merged prose left open (an opt-out skip, win32, a `removed-by-user` rc entry), binding option A's two halves to `nen_global.sh`'s state (bound: the `PATH` line only, never a relink; not bound: link plus `PATH` line), and on exit `3` naming the real file A would replace with the star on B. `ten` § 1's `ok` route runs `nen_global.sh` first and then § 2c, its exit feeding only § 2c's predicate (`5`/`2` quoted in § 4's `nen-global:` token, never § 3, no session re-bind), so § 3 is reached from § 2 only; § 2's exit-0 row re-binds the session when the re-probe is still not `ok`, then goes to § 2c; § 3 restores the contract pointer — a non-zero bootstrap halts only after `dependency.bootstrap.retry_policy` (`4` retried once; `2`/`7` fixed and re-run), `rc` quoted. *The session binding stays unconditional* is replaced everywhere by *the session always resolves the pin — through the host link or, where the host was not bound, the session bind*; the three trimmed phrases are back (`--source` shape-checked, § 4 naming which `nen` § 2 bound on every route, § 6's *Suggested next steps: ruling 7*); the ROSTER cite reads *§ Rulings of 2026-09-30 — nen is bound on the host at session start, ruling A*, and `mugetsu` § 7, `bakuryuha`'s never-list, ROSTER and this section cite *ten § 2's host default* (§ 2's own words); `getsuga` § 7's hand-forward reads `session nen: … the host link follows the contract's pin (ten § 2)`; the rikugan count above reads what `wc -c` reads. To fit, `ten` cites `docs/PROCESS.md` for detail it restated — § 4's field mapping and § 4b's line contents (§ *Reporting a phase*), § 5's mirror verdicts, the pre-skill carve-out, Cursor's listing and the model matrix (§ *Surfaces and pickers*), § 6's `gh`/`git` reads and the `then aka+mukai` hint (§ *Residue*) — and drops four asides as deliberate removals: `Registry: nen/repos.json` (§ 0), *the SessionStart hook's step 0* beside the ROSTER cite (§ 2), the `mktemp -d` mode `0700` (the private directory stays), and *once the installed Hatsu carries it* after *the host link follows the repin* (§ 4b and `bakuryuha` say when). The machinery half: `nen_global.sh` rolls the never-re-add record back on grep's own exit (`0`/`1` replaced, `2` left untouched and reported `record not rolled back`), names a failed restore as `restore failed; <link> now points at <verified>` instead of claiming one, and no longer leaks a raw redirection error for an unwritable rc file; the fixture (57 cases) gains 21d (rollback), 21e (an unreadable record keeps another file's tombstone) and 21f (an in-range link kept on a failed fetch), and 21c derives the platforms from `published_binaries` and requires each `sha256` value inside the current pin's own record in `pinned_ref_semantics`, so a repin that leaves the digests behind goes red; the stale `ten § 2 (a)` cites in the script, the hook, `hooks.json`, `plugin_bump_check.sh` and the contract's lane comment read `ten § 2 (the host default)`.
- Plugin bumped to 0.73.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.72.0 — a direct `hatsu:mugetsu` is the go, as ruled

- **`mugetsu` reads the maintainer's own typed `hatsu:mugetsu [<target>]` as the go, by name.** The basis is the G3 row in [`docs/ROSTER.md`](docs/ROSTER.md) § *The human gates, and who may cross them*: *"publishing runs only on the maintainer's typed go — by name, or as a `mugetsu` step of their own `futon` chain"*. Ruling 2(a) of § *Rulings of 2026-09-26/27/28* adds that no version or target is needed: *"Mugetsu should always default to latest unpublished cut tag. It should never be mandatory, not even when called directly"*. § 0 and P4 had said *"The invocation is not the go … half the authorisation"*. On 2026-09-30 a session read that literally and refused a bare `/hatsu:mugetsu` as "no go"; the maintainer answered *"That's not true per latest Hatsu specification. Should you get up to speed with latest mugetsu updates?"*, now recorded in ROSTER as a dated clarification of ruling 2(a).
- **§ 0 names the go's three forms, and a fall-through.** By name; in the maintainer's own words in this session (the form § 1 always named); or the advance go, only for a `then` step `nen parse futon` parsed as `mugetsu` with `gate.allowed` true. "By name" is one observable thing, a user turn the surface rendered from the maintainer's own typed command. It is never a Skill-tool call the session made, a scheduled, looped or cron-fired prompt, a headless `claude -p`, a hook-injected line, a post-compaction or resume summary, or on Codex a `$mugetsu` merely mentioned in a sentence. A go does not survive its run. Every other reach prints the plan and stops. The Hard limits carry the same list.
- **The one ask is the destination**, on a call naming none where several are declared. P4, § 1, `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` § 7 and `nen/decisions.json` → `missing-maintainer-choice` had tied it to a call that names a target, where nothing is missing. "mugetsu go" is no longer listed as an askable value there or in `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 4: the go is typed, never asked.
- **Residue 8: the surface may refuse a valid go.** Claude Code's auto-mode classifier has denied the bare `nen shu release` on a go that held, under more than one label. The exact command is handed over, nothing is retried another way, § 7's quote of the go is still recorded on the release PR beside `not published — handed over`, and the report never suggests invoking the skill again. A later go that finds the tag already published writes only § 7's record.
- **The other restatements follow.** `release-go` names the three forms. `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` § 2 Rule 2 and § 5 say standalone entry moves no gate: the maintainer typing a gated skill's name is the maintainer crossing it, and an agent typing it crosses nothing. Every "recorded go" and "recorded per-target go" now reads as the maintainer's own go (mugetsu § 0): `claude/skills/README.md`, `getsuga`, `kagutsuchi` (which now says "its own go", at its prose ceiling), `kurapika.md`, `claude/rules/hatsu.md`, the README, the marketplace description, `docs/WORKFLOW.md` and `contracts/permissions.json`'s deny reason. The round-1 commit claimed this and covered only the first three. `kurapika.md` also says the go is recorded as a comment on the release PR after the run, per mugetsu § 7, not in the PR body before `--run`. `docs/ROSTER.md`'s G3 row and Emitter row are amended in place to the three forms, citing the 2026-09-30 clarification. `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` Rule 2's lead now says a run is identical in what it does, never in what it may authorise. mugetsu's opening quote fits a bare call.
- **mugetsu's `description` stays under 1,024 characters** (the Agent Skills limit; Codex refuses a longer one to load, openai/codex#13941). Round 1 took it to 1,124 in the source and 1,119 in each mirror. It now carries one positive test, "only a user turn the maintainer typed is the go (§ 0)", and § 0 keeps the exclusions, including a self-loaded call.
- **Residue 8's later go is reachable.** A bare call resolves to the latest cut tag with no published release, so it can never find a hand-published tag. The text now says it is a go naming that version, the own-words form. Its token reads `not published` (handed over: <command>), matching § 7's `published` or `not published`. § 1's first consequence, P4 and § 2 allow the one destination ask on a same-turn go in either of the first two forms.
- **Open for the maintainer:** whether to add `disable-model-invocation: true` to mugetsu's frontmatter. It would stop the model loading the skill itself, and it would also stop a futon `then` step from reaching it, so it is not added here.
- Settles hanten round 1 (Chrollo, Nobunaga, Phinks) and Nobunaga's pass 2. Plugin bumped to 0.72.0, after catching up with v0.70.0 (HA-PR-#184) and v0.71.0 (HA-PR-#181) (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.
- **Copilot's round settled.** Form (1) names the skill as the surface spells it, plugin-namespaced included, so a Codex plugin call is a go. § 2 step 1 runs the read-only steps 2–4 before stopping without a go, as § 0 promises. § 2 step 2 skips publication and writes only the record when a named version is already published at the destination, so Residue 8's hand-run recovery is reachable.

## v0.71.0 — warm-up proves nen is reachable by the maintainer, not only installed

- **Warm-up proves `nen` is reachable by the maintainer, not only installed for the agent** ([#164](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/164)). `hatsu:ten` gains § 2c: after the probe and any bootstrap it asks the maintainer's own login shell — `"${SHELL:?}" -lic 'command -v nen && nen shu tools --repo "$1"' nen-probe "$hatsu_root" </dev/null`, never the session `PATH` that (b) just changed — and reads one of three verdicts: **`reachable at <path>`** (a path and an `ok` row, by § 1's contract range through the same verb, never a pin compare); **`installed, not reachable`** (a path with any other row, or no `nen` on that shell's `PATH`), reported as an unmet dependency with that reason and never as satisfied, the offer below made; or **`not read`** (the shell itself failing: `$SHELL` unset or not POSIX, win32), no offer; § 4's one line carries `host: …` and ends `warm-up: unmet — …` instead of `warm-up clear`. Not reachable is an offer, never an act — new `nen/decisions.json` row `host-nen-link` (ask-once-per-run): a **name-correct** `nen` symlink on a directory already on the login shell's `PATH` (`~/.local/bin/nen` → the checksum-verified binary, repointed on a bump so the shell line never changes), plus the one `PATH` line only where that directory is missing from it; the change is stated before it is taken and taken on the maintainer's word only. The trap is named: the versioned directory holds only the platform asset, so putting it on `PATH` leaves a bare `nen` unresolved. Evidence from the sitting that raised it, the agent shell answering `command not found: nen` beside a login shell resolving `~/.local/bin/nen` at the previous pin: `docs/ab/ten.md`.
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten on `4b8d4668`, first cycle: Nobunaga 13, Chrollo 7, Phinks 1 proven 3/3 by a release dry-run). Fixed: the skills index's H1 had taken the sentence meant for the ten row; § 2c's link operand was undefined on § 1's `ok` route and now names the real path of the binary § 1 ran; the host is judged by § 1's range through `nen shu tools`, not by an exact pin; a third reading, `not read`, covers an unset or non-POSIX `$SHELL` and win32 with no offer made; § 4 says what replaces `warm-up clear`; option A creates `~/.local/bin`, names the `PATH` read and the rc file, and the probe is asked again once A is taken; `--quoted` and the ROSTER pointer restored in ten §§ 0 and 6; mugetsu's `host nen:` token renamed `session nen:` so `host` means the login shell everywhere; the stale `ten § 2's (a)` cites repointed. Pushed back: rewriting the published commit's message (row `rewrite-published-history`); the Cursor/Codex `summary:` truncation and the oversized Codex `AGENTS.md` are pre-existing generator facts, routed.
- Plugin bumped to 0.71.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.70.0 — nen repinned to v0.18.2

- **`nen/contract.json` → `dependency.pinned_ref` moves `v0.18.1` → `v0.18.2`** ([zheref/nen v0.18.2](https://github.com/zheref/nen/releases/tag/v0.18.2), released by [zheref/nen#322](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/322), with [zheref/nen#318](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/318), the windows-x64 runner preflight workflow, in the same unit and no verb change, published 2026-10-01 UTC). The patch carries the two `hatsu:futon nen@bug` fixes. [zheref/nen#315](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/315) makes `nen pr fetch` and `nen pr next-blocker` read a pending check whose conclusion gh writes as `""` instead of exiting 1 ([zheref/nen#304](https://github.com/zheref/nen/issues/304)). [zheref/nen#316](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/316) stops `nen shu warmup` reading a finished rebase as in progress through a stale `REBASE_HEAD` ([zheref/nen#307](https://github.com/zheref/nen/issues/307)). The compatibility floor stays `0.18`, so `dependency.minimum` stays `0.18` and no consumer pin breaks. `pinned_ref_semantics` records the release and its darwin-arm64, linux-x64 and windows-x64 checksums, and the top `$comment`, the bootstrap `url`, `fetch`, the two-step `wrong`/`right`, both `install_paths` and the halt message point at `v0.18.2`. The convenience copies follow: the README's Requirements line, `docs/WORKFLOW.md`, the marketplace descriptions, `futon`'s history line and `surface-mirror-check.yml`'s comment.
- **`ao` § 3's catch-up exit table is total against nen v0.18.2** (hanten: Chrollo, Nobunaga). Exit `1` with a `conflicted[]` report goes to § 4; exit `1` with no report (a failed fetch, status or HEAD read, a rebase or merge failing without a conflict, a failed `--continue`) stops, quoting nen verbatim, and never enters § 4. Exit `2` has three rows: a rebase paused at a `break` or a failed `exec` is quoted with git's own `--continue` / `--abort` and never acted on; an unanswered rebase probe is quoted with no git command named, because there may be no rebase at all; anything else is a refusal before any mutation, quoted. Any other exit stops. `breath`'s exit-`2` row already reads "refused before any mutation — fix, re-run" and stays true for warmup's matching refusals.
- **`ao` § 2 asks git, never `.git/` on disk, for an operation in progress** (Chrollo). The old check read `.git/MERGE_HEAD` and `.git/rebase-merge`, which a linked worktree's `.git` file hides. It now asks `git rev-parse -q --verify MERGE_HEAD` and `git rebase --show-current-patch`. An operation stopped on a patch (a conflict, or an `edit` stop) or a resolving `MERGE_HEAD` is reported and nothing is started, as before; it is never re-run from here, which would commit a merge with git's default message and no re-proved build (Nobunaga, reproduced). A rebase paused with no current patch (probe exit `1`) is reported with git's own `--continue` / `--abort` and stops in § 2 itself, because a host still on nen 0.18.0 or 0.18.1 has no catch-up refusal for it (Copilot, zheref/hatsu#182).
- **`pinned_ref_semantics` also records the linux-x64 checksum** (Feitan), the binary CI's bootstrap runs, beside darwin-arm64 and windows-x64.
- Plugin bumped to 0.70.0, a minor: a new pinned ref and a skill behaviour change (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.69.1 — the runner preflight's per-user remedy names no install path

- **`templates/runner-preflight.yml`'s Windows step "Resolution must be machine-wide (Windows)" no longer tells the maintainer to add `C:\Program Files\<tool>` to the `PATH`** (Copilot's review of the rendering on [zheref/nen#320](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/320), comment 4150839702). Git for Windows exposes `git.exe` and `bash.exe` from subdirectories of `C:\Program Files\Git`, and GitHub CLI does not install under `C:\Program Files\gh`, so that remedy could leave the preflight failing. The `::error::` line is now layout-agnostic and carries the toolchain step's exit-127 remedy word for word: install it machine-wide, put its directory on the MACHINE `PATH`, then restart the runner services. A first draft kept a parenthetical saying a machine-wide installer puts its files under `C:\Program Files`; Copilot's review of this PR ([zheref/hatsu#179](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/179), comment 4150885649) pointed out that a valid machine-wide tool can live elsewhere, so the parenthetical is gone. The step's logic is untouched. `scripts/runner_preflight_fixture_check.sh` adds a `remedy` property on the live template and on both renderings: no `::error::` line carries a tool-templated path segment (`\$tool`, `/$tool`, `<tool>`) or names `Program Files`, and every per-user `::error::` line still names the tool and the MACHINE `PATH`. Against the v0.68.2 template the first two conjuncts fail on all three, six conjuncts in all.
- Plugin bumped to 0.69.1 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.69.0 — the reviewer-round caps have a canon default, so no repository is unbounded

- **`contracts/round_policy.default.json` is the canon default for Hatsu's two reviewer-round keys** ([`docs/ROSTER.md`](docs/ROSTER.md) § *Rulings of 2026-09-30 — reviewer-round caps by default*). `zheref/nen`'s own `nen/gates.json` declared no `round_policy`, so a run driving [NN-PR-#316](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/316) read no ceiling and requested four Copilot rounds where Hatsu's own caps allow one resolved round. The new file carries `minRounds` and `maxRounds` only, with the same values as Hatsu's own `nen/gates.json`. `stallMinutes` is nen's key with nen's own built-in default, so it is not in the file, and `contracts/reference.gates.json` is unchanged: it stays the frozen reference repository's identity file.
- **`sharingan` § 6 states the rule once, per key.** A target value that is absent, not a non-negative integer, or leaves `minRounds` above `maxRounds` takes the default from `$hatsu_root/contracts/round_policy.default.json`, an absolute path because the run stands in the target's checkout. The report names that file as the source. An unreadable default is a stop, never unbounded. A malformed value is reachable: nen validates only `stallMinutes`, and `nen schema check` reads `ok` for a string, negative or inverted cap (hanten: Chrollo, Nobunaga, Feitan SEC-7).
- **To stay inside sharingan's 12,288-byte prose ceiling**, three edits made room without dropping a rule. § 5's request-channel bullet now points at § 6, where the channel is stated, instead of restating it. § 6 dropped the `monitor.maxCycles` aside, which `nen/gates.json`'s `round_policy` comment still states, and a repeated "plus this run's requests", which § 6's own *requested* definition carries. "never copied into prose" now reads "never in prose".
- **`docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` § `round_policy`** points at the default file and at § 6's rule rather than restating it. The same caps are declared on `zheref/nen` itself in [NN-PR-#321](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/321).
- Plugin bumped to 0.69.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.68.2 — the runner preflight classifies the first match, and the whole profile is per-user

- **`templates/runner-preflight.yml`'s Windows step "Resolution must be machine-wide (Windows)" classifies the resolution a job actually runs** (Copilot's review of the rendering on [zheref/nen#318](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/318#discussion_r4150505974), comment 4150505974, two correct readings). (1) It equated "not under AppData" with machine-wide, so a per-user install elsewhere in the profile — `%USERPROFILE%\scoop\shims\gh.exe`, `%USERPROFILE%\bin\gh.exe` — passed. (2) `where.exe` prints every match in `PATH` order and the step failed a tool when *any* line was under AppData, so a tool whose first, effective match is under Program Files failed on a stale later match. The step now reads only the first `where.exe` line (CR stripped, `IFS= read -r`), normalises it and `$USERPROFILE` with the two-backslash `${x//\\//}`, and under `nocasematch` answers `not found` (no line), `per-user (AppData)` (an `/appdata/` segment, which also covers a profile `USERPROFILE` does not name), `per-user (profile)` (under `$USERPROFILE/`, when set) or `machine-wide`. It still prints `<tool>: <verdict>` and never the path or the profile; every failing tool gets one `::error::` naming the remedy (install under `C:\Program Files\<tool>`, put that directory on the MACHINE `PATH`, restart the runner services), and the step exits `1` after classifying every tool. `scripts/runner_preflight_fixture_check.sh` now asserts both normalisation lines, stubs `where.exe` as a function printing several CRLF lines, and adds six whole-step cases to the three it kept, each on the live template and on the `windows-x64` rendering: Program Files first with a later AppData match exits `0`; a scoop shim under `USERPROFILE=C:\Users\x` exits `1` per-user (profile); with `USERPROFILE` unset an AppData path still fails and Program Files still passes; `USERPROFILE=c:\users\X` against `C:\Users\x\bin\gh.exe` is per-user (profile); no line at all is `not found`; and no output line carries `Users\x`, `Users/x` or `users/x`. Against the v0.68.1 template the first-match and both profile cases fail, seven conjuncts in all. Copilot's second finding, on this PR ([zheref/hatsu#178](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/178), comment 4150618465), is settled too: a bare `where.exe <tool>` searches the current directory first, where the job has just checked the repository out, so a checkout-local `gh.exe` would become the first match and hide a per-user one. The step therefore calls `where.exe "\$PATH:$tool"`, which searches the PATH directories only, and the fixture's stub prints a checkout-local `C:\a\checkout\gh.exe` first whenever it is called without that prefix.
- Plugin bumped to 0.68.2 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.68.1 — the runner preflight's AppData check matches, and its checkout is pinned by SHA

- **`templates/runner-preflight.yml`'s Windows AppData check failed open, and now fails a per-user tool** (Feitan BC-9 and Nobunaga QA-18/BC-9, both on the rendering `zheref/nen` got from this template). The step normalised `where.exe`'s output with `norm="${line//\//}"` — one backslash, so the pattern was a literal `/`: it deleted forward slashes and left every backslash standing, `*/appdata/*` never matched, and the step could only pass. It now carries `norm="${line//\\//}"`, the two-backslash form nen's own fixture (`src/runner/fixtures/runner-preflight.template.yml`) and its golden already had, so the two stay byte-aligned. `scripts/runner_preflight_fixture_check.sh` asserts that exact line and the one-backslash form's absence, then lifts the whole step out of the live template and out of a rendering and runs it against a stubbed `where.exe` printing one CRLF-terminated line: `C:\Users\x\AppData\Local\Programs\gh\gh.exe` and `C:/Users/x/AppData/Local/gh.exe` exit `1` as per-user, `C:\Program Files\GitHub CLI\gh.exe` exits `0` machine-wide, and no path is printed. Against the v0.68.0 template the same cases read exit `0` for both AppData paths. Nobunaga's two nits ride along: the toolchain step now names every tool's verdict and exits with the first non-zero code instead of stopping at the first, and the header claims the guard for this template only, as `zheref/hatsu:scripts/runner_preflight_fixture_check.sh` — a consumer's copy carries no such guard, which is why it is re-rendered, never edited.
- **`actions/checkout` is pinned by commit SHA** (Feitan SEC-14): `actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4`, the commit `v4` resolved to on 2026-09-30. The job runs on a persistent physical host with a `GITHUB_TOKEN`, a movable tag is not acceptable there, and the old advice — a SHA-pinning consumer re-pins the line by hand — contradicted the rule that a rendering is never hand-edited. A consumer now moves the pin by re-rendering after a template bump. The fixture asserts every `uses:` in the template and in both renderings is a 40-hex SHA with a `# vN` comment; the v0.68.0 template fails it on all three.
- Plugin bumped to 0.68.1 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.68.0 — `hatsu:jusshin` raises a consumer's self-hosted runners, and the Nen minimum moves to 0.18

- **`hatsu:jusshin` — the fleet, raised by hand once and standing on every boot** (the maintainer's request of 2026-09-30: bring back `zheref/nen`'s skipped Windows `check` leg on this Windows host, and make the provisioning a skill any consumer can run). `hatsu:jusshin [for <owner/repo>] [pool <id>] [x <count>] [on <machine-code>] [--dry-run]` reads the pool from the target's own `nen/workflow.json` → `runners` declaration (`naming`, `pools[]`: label set, enable variable, tools, preflight workflow, per-OS root), lists what is registered (`nen runner inventory`), plans the names `<machine>-<consumer>R<slot>` at the lowest free slots with the download's checksum and one service name per runner (`nen runner plan`), and renders one host script per OS (`nen runner script`) **outside the runner root**, where only the maintainer can write (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\nen\jusshin\` on Windows, `~/.local/state/nen/jusshin/` elsewhere). The maintainer consents on the host — the harness's own permission prompt on the Windows launch line, then the UAC prompt and the service-account password typed into the elevated window; `sudo bash <script>` run by the maintainer on Linux; the Login Items approval on macOS — and the session runs only the script's own `launch` line, quoted, never typed, and reads only its one-line summary file, **never its transcript**. `nen runner verify` reads the runners online with their labels; then the pool is **proven from inside a job**: `nen runner workflow` renders a preflight workflow onto the effort's branch, it lands through `mukai` at the target's declaration gate (**G4** canon / **G2** consumer, as `nen repo classify` answers), and `nen runner preflight` dispatches it and waits. Only a green preflight run's id lets `nen runner enable --after-run <id>` set the pool's repository variable, and a `check_exclusions[]` row that excluded the check while the pool was offline is reconciled at that gate. Every control-flow table maps every exit and message nen's released tables name, and ends in one named fall-through.
