Four decisions, each with its reason in the skill: (1) **the three host rules from <reference-repo>'s runner runbook are carried as rules, not as its machinery** — W1 a service's tools must be machine-located **and** machine-readable, W2 `126` ≠ `127`, W3 registration is not capability — with its lessons as a remedy table under local ids J1–J7 (a local-only service account, never a Microsoft account, `NETWORK SERVICE` only on the maintainer's typed choice; traverse `(RX)` on every parent; restart the services after a `PATH` change, per OS; the macOS Background Task Management hold), and none of bankai's routing probe, App or opt-in input; (2) **the target, the machine code and the service identity are the maintainer's word**, typed, never picked (`missing-maintainer-choice`; the maintainer's ruling of 2026-09-30, recorded in `docs/ROSTER.md`), while the pool and the count are asked through the picker and the consumer code and the runner root are derived and stated; (3) **no password, registration token or `gh` token ever passes through the session** — the elevated script mints each token itself; (4) **provisioning is not selection**: `tenkai` § 5b still derives which runner a readiness workflow targets, and jusshin only registers the pools a consumer declares. Seven residues are named, the first being that the elevated launch has no verb by design, and one of them nen's to close ([zheref/nen#312](https://github.com/zheref/nen/issues/312)). Registered in [`claude/skills/README.md`](claude/skills/README.md), the README, `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` § 7b (twelve), `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue*, `docs/ROSTER.md` § 1 and § *Rulings of 2026-09-30 — jusshin*, `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 4 and its runner-selection paragraph, `nen/decisions.json`'s citing list and the marketplace. **Off the prose diet**: the totality its tables need does not fit 12,288 bytes, as `tenkai`'s does not. The verification record [`docs/ab/jusshin.md`](docs/ab/jusshin.md) is opened: its offline rows ran at nen `0.18.0` (the grammar, the refusals, `nen runner workflow` and `script` dry runs, a live-read `plan` that registers nothing), and every row that acts on the host or GitHub is to be filled by the live `zheref/nen` run — pending at v0.68.0. The live skill surface is **forty-seven**, forty-eight directories per surface with `ten`.
- **`nen/contract.json` → `dependency.minimum` moves `0.16` → `0.18`, and `pinned_ref` to `v0.18.1`** ([zheref/nen#305](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/305); the pin is the patch [zheref/nen#313](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/313)/[#314](https://github.com/zheref/nen/pull/314), which keeps the registration secrets off `cmd.exe`'s argv, locks the runner root down before any download and writes the `.summary` line § 5 reads, after Feitan's review of this very change — floor unchanged at `0.18`). jusshin is the first skill that executes the `nen runner` family — `inventory`, `plan`, `script`, `verify`, `workflow`, `preflight`, `enable` — and reads the `nen/workflow.json` `runners` block, which exist in no tag before `v0.18.0`; a `0.17` binary refuses `nen runner` as an unknown command. `v0.18.0` moves the compatibility floor to `0.18` (the maintainer's ruling of 2026-09-30: `nen commit format`'s new exit 2 on an unreadable commitlint config, zheref/nen#302, is breaking). **The repin crosses two floors**: `v0.17.0` raised it from `0.7` for breaking consumer notes, so every `0.17`+ build already refused Hatsu's `0.16` pin by name (`nen shu tools`: *Repin to '0.17'*). Hatsu absorbs them here, one clause each: `nen wc publish` on a branch whose upstream names another branch exits `2` unless `--set-upstream` (and `nen shu warmup` cuts with `--no-track`); a round check counts only a `SUCCESS` conclusion; `nen commit format` and `write` read `nen/workflow.json` on every run (malformed exit `1`, nonexistent `--repo` exit `2`, `nen: warning:` lines at exit `0`); `nen pr request-reviews` exits `9` for an unrecorded bot; `nen pr ready --exclude-check` may repeat and splits on commas outside brackets; case-colliding product codes are refused at exit `2`; a non-string `scenario` on a `maintained_tools` row stops registry loading; `nen surface mirror check` reads a mirror generated by an older build as `stale`; `changelog completeness` and `release preflight` exit `0` on a range whose only uncited PR is the release PR; `nen coverage --touched` exits `6` when nothing matched a non-empty touched set — `byakugan` maps it: nothing measured, not a pass and not 0%. `minimum_semantics`, `zero_major_caveat.rule`, `pinned_ref_semantics` and the top `$comment` record the adoption; the bootstrap `url`, `fetch`, the two-step `wrong`/`right`, both `install_paths` and the halt message point at `v0.18.0`; and the convenience copies follow — the README's range and Requirements row (`>= 0.18`: it stated the current minimum, stale since `0.13`), `docs/WORKFLOW.md`, the marketplace, `futon`'s history line and `surface-mirror-check.yml`'s comment.
- **`templates/runner-preflight.yml`** — the preflight workflow `nen runner workflow` renders for one pool (seven placeholders: slug, `runs-on`, OS, tools, pool id, workflow file, rendering build). It proves a pool from inside a job running as the service: the OS it landed on, every declared tool's `--version` with `126` (found, refused on exec: an ACL) named apart from `127` (not on the service's `PATH`), and where each tool resolves — an `AppData` hit fails on Windows, a `$HOME/.local` or `$HOME/bin` hit warns on Linux and macOS. `workflow_dispatch` plus a `push` filtered to the file itself, `pull_request` deliberately absent, the job bound to the canonical repository, `contents: read`, five minutes. Nen carries the renderer and Hatsu the template, the split `pr-readiness.yml` already has. **The job prints whether a service account is set, never its name, and each tool's name and verdict, never the path it resolved to** — on a public consumer the log is public (Feitan, hanten F5) — so the body is no longer byte-identical to nen's own test template, and what holds it is the guard below. `actions/checkout` is pinned by tag: a consumer whose policy requires SHA pins re-pins it in the PR that adds the file.
- **`scripts/runner_preflight_fixture_check.sh` and the `runner-preflight-guard` lane** (hanten C19, N7, Phinks P1: nothing here held the template, and a hostile copy adding `pull_request_target` and dropping the repository binding rendered through nen at exit 0). It reads the live template and a rendering of it for a Windows and a macOS fixture pool, and asserts each conjunct on its own line: the event set exactly `workflow_dispatch` and `push`, `push.paths` only the file itself, no `pull_request` or `pull_request_target`, the one job bound to the slug, `runs-on` exactly the pool's labels, `contents: read`, `persist-credentials: false`, a `timeout-minutes` bound, no step printing an account name or a resolved path, no placeholder left — and it refuses the hostile copy, a bare `self-hosted` `runs-on` and a leftover placeholder (nen's exit `1`, nothing written). Properties, not bytes. Hosts darwin and linux like every guard lane (it ran green on win32 under Git Bash at nen `0.18.0`; nen's Windows `PATH` story for a lane run is unproven). `nen/contract.json` names it in the plugin lane's `test.unsupported` and in `hosts.$comment`; it is a CI- and maintainer-only fixture, so `scripts/plugin_bump_check.sh`'s runtime list does not carry it.
- **`contracts/permissions.json` gains no allow row for jusshin, and two deny rows.** The Windows launch line (`Start-Process powershell -Verb RunAs …`) is asked by the harness every time: its permission prompt, showing the exact string, is the maintainer's advance consent — an allow row with a trailing `*` inside the `-Command` string admitted any continuation, elevated or not (Feitan F1, Nobunaga N1). `gh variable` and `gh workflow run` are nen's to spawn, under the existing `nen *` row, never the session's (F4, N6); beside `gh release create *` stand `gh variable delete *` and `gh variable set RELEASE_HOLD*`. No row runs `config.cmd`, `config.sh` or `svc.sh` from a session, and `sudo` stays denied.
- `byakugan`'s exit codes name `6` (hanten C20). `docs/surfaces/{cursor,antigravity,codex}.md` count thirteen agent files, twelve personas plus the preamble include (N8, stale since v0.67.0); the marketplace names the skills its parts left uncounted, so they sum to forty-seven (N13). `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` § 7b (twelve), `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue* and `docs/ROSTER.md` § 1 carry jusshin; `scripts/plugin_bump_check.sh`'s shipped-surface comment counts forty-eight skill directories. Forty-seven skills, forty-eight directories per surface.
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten on `b904dd66`: Nobunaga 15 findings, Chrollo 23, Feitan 5, Leorio 1, Phinks 3). Fixed: the RunAs, `gh variable` and `gh workflow run` allow rows (above); the skill's tables made total against nen's released exit tables (`x 0` defined, the checkout row, pool OS ≠ host, the unobservable Windows exit read from the summary file, verify's rows, workflow's exit `1` split by message, enable read from the declaration first, Next's two missing clauses); every path argument single-quoted; `$hatsu_root` set in the block that uses it; the template's log hygiene and its guard. Deferred, each tracked: the root lockdown, secrets off `config.cmd`'s argv and the summary file to [zheref/nen#312](https://github.com/zheref/nen/issues/312); the question budget and the reading of an on-device act to two handbook questions.
- Plugin bumped to 0.68.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.67.0 — Leorio charts what a change costs, and never blocks it

Release unit for `v0.66.0..v0.67.0`: [#162](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/162) (Leorio, the economics reviewer — this section, v0.67.0) and [#167](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/167) (this release proposal). This line is where the range's PRs are cited, which is what `CON-33(c)` reconciles against.

- **A new advisory `hanten` reviewer, Leorio (the maintainer's ruling of 2026-09-30).** He reads a change for its economics — what it does to billing, to a user's cost or to the maintainer's run cost — and hands the maintainer a report to decide from. `nen/workflow.json` gains the `economics` review scope (persona `leorio`, tier deep, budget 1 per effort) beside the other scopes.
- **The scope is raised by path or by content, never on every diff.** The path rows live only in `nen/workflow.json` → `review.scopes.economics.paths`: CI workflows, the consumer readiness-workflow template, and the generic product globs (billing, pricing, price, subscription, paywall, entitlement, purchase, payment, invoice, quota, Stripe) in lower-case and PascalCase and as directories, because matching is case-sensitive and a bare `*name*` matches the last path segment only — plus `*.storekit` and `*.tf`. The content rows live only in `docs/ECONOMICS.md` § 1 and are config-shaped: a `model:`/`effort:` pin or tier-map entry, a review budget, a cap/limit/poll/retry/timeout/interval value in configuration, a schedule, a price/plan/trial/quota constant, or a newly paid host, API, SDK or runner class — never a number mentioned in prose.
- **The rules are ECON-1..ECON-8 in `docs/ECONOMICS.md`**: price and plan surface, metered consumption, maintainer run cost, provider and store terms (the four delta rows), and bounded consumption, revenue integrity, disclosure and method (the four finding classes). They resolve from the ECONOMICS.md path `hanten`'s prompt names, never from memory; absent, `ECON-{n} not resolved on this host`.
- **He never rejects.** An economic delta is report content, and a change that costs more is never a finding. A finding exists only where the maintainer cannot decide informed: an unbounded consumption (ECON-4), money granted or charged wrongly (ECON-5), an undisclosed user-facing change (ECON-7), a number the change carries with no method (ECON-8). No vote, no verdict, no recommendation; the closing line is `Leorio-Ledger: no delta | delta charted [· <n> findings open] | unread`, where a count is never a rejection. His tools are Illumi's read-only set plus Bash and the web: he writes no file.
- **The report returns in his reply, and `hanten` writes it per effort.** Summary, delta table, Mermaid charts (one bar series per chart — `xychart-beta` overlays series, mermaid-js/mermaid#7392 — each followed by its data table), the case for landing, the case against, what would flip the call, what is unestimated, and a method block per number, a fetched price sheet appearing only as its number, URL and read date. It lands at `<reports.dir>/hanten/<branch-slug>[-pr<N>].economics.md`, keyed like the cycle ledger; a delta pass appends `## Delta <old-head> → <new-head>` rather than replacing it; the findings record gains an additive `reports[]` of `{scope, persona, invocation, head, path}`; and `templates/pr-body.md` carries it as an optional `## Economics` section with a third checklist reading for a scope raised but not run. `shibari` composes the body's parts as the template's own comments say, with no count to drift.
- **The cycle ledger hydrates a persona added after it shipped — only in a ledger that provably predates him.** `scripts/hanten_cycle_ledger.sh` gains `leorio: 1` and `LATE_PERSONAS`; `init` now stamps `personasAtOpen`, and a hydration is recorded in `lateHydrated`. A pre-0.67.0 ledger with no `leorio` row loads with him at `used: 0` marked `hydratedAs: persona-added-after-ledger-opened`; a lost, deleted or re-cased row in a ledger that already knew him refuses at exit `2`, like any other persona's. `scripts/tenkai_adopt.sh` reads the same rule, and its persona sets are now constants its self-test proves equal to the ledger script's.
- **New lane `ledger-guard`**: `scripts/hanten_cycle_ledger_late_persona_fixture.sh` runs the ledger's own `--self-test`, then the lost-row cases, with a 0.66.0-shaped ledger as the control. The ledger script had no lane before this.
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten on `265c2533`, first cycle: Nobunaga 13, Chrollo 10, Feitan 2, Phinks 1 proven 3/3 by a red fixture, Leorio 0 with his own report charted). Fixed: the late-persona hydration re-minted a spent budget when his row was lost in a ledger opened after he existed (Feitan, Phinks, Nobunaga and Chrollo, the fixture now green); the path globs missed PascalCase billing files (4 of 11 synthetic paths matched, now 11 of 11) and `templates/*.yml` claimed the colour vocabulary; the report had no rule for a delta pass or a second PR; the content predicate was restated in seven places that already disagreed; the severity table had a class with no ECON id; the landing-report destination was claimed and not wired; persona counts, the marketplace roster and the routing list lagged. Deferred to the maintainer at G4: where the ECON rule family lives (Chrollo, CON-13 — Hatsu canon or a canon-lane PR to `bankai-handbooks`).
- **Plugin `0.66.0` → `0.67.0`** on the Claude manifest and the Codex overlay, `leorio.md` listed in `agents`, the marketplace descriptions name him (and now Nobunaga and Shalnark) beside the other independents, and the generated surfaces are regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.66.0 — every surface installs Hatsu first-party from your own checkout, and bakuryuha switches it

Release unit for `v0.61.0..v0.66.0`: [#150](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/150) (no private repository named in the tree, v0.63.0), [#152](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/152) (kagutsuchi gated on freshness, rebuilt only when stale, `deploy.defaultTarget`, v0.64.0), [#153](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/153) (the mirror check accepts Codex's next hooks root, v0.65.0) [#151](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/151) (first-party installs and `hatsu:bakuryuha`, this section, v0.66.0) and [#155](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/155) (this release proposal). The v0.63.0 to v0.65.0 sections below describe the first three; this line is where the range's PRs are cited, which is what `CON-33(c)` reconciles against.

- **Claude Code is served in place, never from the cache.** `~/.claude/skills/hatsu` links to the maintainer's checkout, so Claude Code loads it as the skills-directory plugin `hatsu@skills-dir`, which "loads in place and is never copied". Observed live (2026-09-29, `claude` 2.1.284, an isolated config): a local-directory marketplace install **copies** into `plugins/cache/` despite the loading page, a link under `skills/` loads the checkout with the link as `installPath`, and an installed `hatsu@hatsu` shadows it until uninstalled (`docs/surfaces/evidence/surfaces.md` § 10). An update is now the checkout moving, then `/reload-plugins` or a new session; `claude plugin update` is no longer part of it.
- **Codex installs Hatsu as its own plugin.** Codex reads the checkout's `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` as a legacy-compatible marketplace; the new authored overlay `.codex-plugin/plugin.json` hands it the canonical `claude/skills/` (listed `hatsu:<name>`, invoked `$hatsu:<name>`, the spelling the bodies already carry) and the generated `surfaces/codex/hooks.json`. The generated Codex hooks resolve their scripts through `${PLUGIN_ROOT:-${HATSU_PLUGIN_ROOT:-./.codex}}`, the installed slot first, so a plugin hook never runs a live-checkout or working-directory script; the legacy placement gets a copy rewritten to `${HATSU_PLUGIN_ROOT:-./.codex}` (the two-step bullet below). The plugin carries no personas: `surface_bootstrap.sh --install-all --plugin` places `AGENTS.override.md` and the persona files and removes the skill copies and hooks file a placement left, so nothing is listed or fired twice. Codex copies the whole checkout per version and notices a newer source only on `codex plugin add` (observed, codex-cli 0.154.0).
- **Antigravity's global plugin link is the install.** `~/.gemini/config/plugins/hatsu` → `<checkout>/surfaces/antigravity`, in place; `agy plugin install` copies into the same directory, so it is never run over the link.
- **`scripts/hatsu_surface_link.sh --surface claude-code|codex|antigravity`** makes, re-points or reports (`--status`) each first-party install, refuses a destination it did not make, retires a shadowing `hatsu@hatsu` together with the marketplace and enable declarations settings keep of it (left in place, a reload reinstalled the cached copy on the maintainer's host, evidence § 10 F9), and reads the result back; fixture lane `surface-link`. **`scripts/hatsu_plugin_update.sh`** learns the Claude Code link (fast-forward the linked checkout, name a shadowing install), `--codex` (fast-forward the marketplace checkout or upgrade a Git snapshot, then re-add the plugin), and a guard that never runs git in a surface's plugin cache. **`scripts/plugin_bump_check.sh`** holds `.codex-plugin/plugin.json` at the Claude manifest's version, because Codex keys its cache slot on it.
- **`hatsu:bakuryuha [--channel auto|trunk|release] [--dry-run]`** does it all per surface — first-party install where a legacy form stands, the checkout updated **without `--auto`** (a dirty tree or an authoring branch refuses and is asked about), the new version's `ten` run from disk, the served version read back — and **every run ends with a Next block**: exactly what to type or open to finish (`/reload-plugins`, a new chat or conversation, a hook to trust in Codex's `/hooks`), never an app restart and never a picker option. Until then the session follows the new skill bodies from disk.
- **`hatsu:great-hiker` writes for four readers.** § 3 gains the rules that keep one body whole on every surface (tools named through the picker/delegate adapter, `hatsu:<name>` invocations, `$hatsu_root` paths, frontmatter within the smallest budget, links only into what every install carries); § 5 verifies the Codex overlay's version and `agy plugin validate surfaces/antigravity`.
- Every surface guide gains an *update and activation* row and dated checklist rows (2026-09-29); `docs/SURFACES.md` compares the installs and names the first-party command; `ten` § 4b passes `--claude|--codex` and § 5 `--plugin`. `bakuryuha` joins the prose diet (nineteen skills, with `limbo`), `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` § 7b (eleven), `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue* and `docs/ROSTER.md` § 1. Forty-six skills with `limbo`, forty-seven directories per surface.
- **`hanten` is back on its diet** (13,442 → 12,285 of 12,288 bytes; over since v0.57.0, so the prose guard read red on `main`). Nothing it does changed: the ledger-recovery doctrine is cited to `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 4, which already states it, with every command and stop kept; the security scope's four rows are cited to Feitan's own table, which carries the same values and failure rules; the PR-keyed ledger is stated once, in § 2b; the rest is wording.
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten on `e9c5d8ef`, first cycle opened by the maintainer's confirmation: Feitan 4, Nobunaga 5 plus 6 notes, Chrollo 15, Phinks 6 each proven by a bats test failing 3/3). Fixed: the updater read Claude Code's registry in a shape it never writes, so it missed a shadowing `hatsu@hatsu` (high, two reviewers); `surface_bootstrap.sh --plugin` skipped the parent guard and could delete through a symlinked `.agents/skills` or `.codex/hooks` (high), and removed Antigravity-stamped skills from the shared `.agents/skills`; a failed `ln` inside `$(...)` reported "linked" on bash 3.2 and the handover ran anyway; a `{}` settings file was refused; a tracked last persona exited silently (older than this change); a refused catch-up of the served checkout exited 0; the Codex read-back was not compared to the manifest; the Codex hook root put a live override and a working-directory fallback ahead of the installed slot; a missing CLI exits 5, as PROCESS says; bakuryuha §§ 2–7 now route every reachable state (a surface asked when no signal holds, every exit of both scripts, the Next cases in a stated order); `ten` § 5 says when `--plugin` applies; hanten raises `security` on a consumer's workflow change. Deferred with a tracked item: a nen verb owning host installs (zheref/nen#298, BC-11 — a G4 question), a clean tree for the Codex marketplace (zheref/hatsu#147, SEC-8), shell line coverage (zheref/hatsu#148, UZF-19). Pushed back: the reviewers' `nen` hand-off named the binary rather than the `nen` symlink `ten` § 2 binds (the orchestrator's slip, not the canon's); the plugin-mode SessionStart taking the reminder branch is deliberate (its refresh branch would re-place the copies the plugin replaces).
- **Landed in two steps, with a guard change first.** `surface-mirror-check` runs `main`'s `scripts/surface_mirror_check.sh` under `pull_request_target`, and that copy read this branch's Codex `hooks.json` (the plugin root first) as `hand-edited`. So the guard learned the next root first in [#153](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/153) (0.65.0), as `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` rules. That review found one thing for this step: under the legacy `.codex/` placement Codex sets no `PLUGIN_ROOT`, so a stray one in the user's environment would outrank `HATSU_PLUGIN_ROOT` (CWE-426, low, Feitan). `surface_bootstrap.sh` now places that copy rewritten to `${HATSU_PLUGIN_ROOT:-./.codex}`, as the Antigravity placement already rewrites its own, and the bootstrap fixture asserts it. This PR is step two: `scripts/surface_mirror_check.sh` loses `hooks_root_next_for` and its re-check (back to one streamed check per surface), `scripts/surface_mirror_check_fixture.sh` keeps the clean, drift, refusal and Cursor cases, and a Codex mirror regenerated at the old root now reads `hand-edited: hooks.json` again (checked with nen 0.16.0 on a copy of this tree). `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` records that step two landed.
- **What #152 needed under this architecture.** `kagutsuchi` now runs `scripts/send_freshness_check.sh` and `scripts/kagutsuchi_worktree.sh` from `$hatsu_root/scripts/` ([#152](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/152)), and neither was a plugin-shipped row in `scripts/plugin_bump_check.sh`. Codex installs Hatsu by copying the checkout into a slot keyed on the version, so an unbumped change to either would never reach it. Both are shipped rows now, with `scripts/hatsu_surface_link.sh` (which `bakuryuha` § 3 runs), each with a fixture case. #152 adds no skill, so the counts stay forty-six skills and forty-seven directories; its two focused lanes join the lane list.
- **What review after the PR opened changed** (Cursor Bugbot on the PR, then a hanten delta round on `f77c411d` with fresh PR-keyed budgets: Feitan 1 low + 1 nit, Nobunaga 2 medium + notes, Phinks 1 high proven by a bats test failing 3/3 — the same defect as Feitan's). Fixed: `hatsu_plugin_update.sh --claude` now names a `settings.json` that still declares `extraKnownMarketplaces.hatsu` or enables `hatsu@hatsu` as a reload risk, since the next `/reload-plugins` reinstalls the shadowing copy (medium, Bugbot; the readers are copied verbatim from `hatsu_surface_link.sh` and the fixture holds the copies identical); the warm-up's `permissions_pack.sh` copied `.codex/hooks.json` verbatim after `surface_bootstrap.sh`, undoing the placed-copy rewrite and, under `--plugin`, putting back hooks the plugin serves (CWE-426; Feitan, Phinks) — the pack now applies the same rewrite, refuses one that did not take, and takes `--plugin`, which `ten` § 5 passes; both writers refuse a rewrite that did not take; `permissions_pack_fixture_check.sh`, which no lane ran, is lane `permissions-pack-guard`; `surface_link_check.sh` and `prose_size_check.sh`, which `hanten` runs from `$hatsu_root`, are shipped rows; the `surface-mirror-guard` fixture checks each hooks root is written the same in the check, the regenerate workflow, `docs/SURFACES.md` and bootstrap's sed; a stale changelog clause and the PR body were brought current.
- Plugin bumped to 0.66.0 (Claude manifest and Codex overlay); mirrors regenerated at that stamp. This branch claimed 0.62.0 and then 0.64.0; 0.63.0 ([#150](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/150)), 0.64.0 ([#152](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/152)) and 0.65.0 ([#153](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/153), step one of this landing) landed first, so this passes above them. The first catch-up also found `.github/workflows/surface-mirror-regenerate.yml` still rooting Codex hooks at `${HATSU_PLUGIN_ROOT:-./.codex}`: the check script had moved to `${PLUGIN_ROOT:-…}` and the workflow had not, so its first regenerate on `main` would have failed its own in-job check. It now matches.

## v0.65.0 — the mirror check accepts Codex's next hooks root, step one of a two-step landing

`surface-mirror-check` runs `scripts/surface_mirror_check.sh` from `main` under `pull_request_target`, so it judges a pull request's `surfaces/` with the hooks roots `main`'s copy knows. [#151](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/151) regenerates the Codex mirror at `${PLUGIN_ROOT:-${HATSU_PLUGIN_ROOT:-./.codex}}` (the Codex plugin's hooks run the installed slot first, its review's CWE-426 fix), and `main`'s copy reads that `hooks.json` as `hand-edited`, which no commit on #151 can change. `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` (2026-09-20) already rules the path: the trusted copy judges the PR, so the guard learns the new shape first.

- **`hooks_root_next_for`**: a surface may name a next root. When a surface's mirror drifts at its current root, the check re-runs it at the next root; a mirror that matches there passes with one line saying so (*"codex matches a fresh generation at its NEXT hooks root …, accepted while that root lands in two steps"*), and anything else prints the first run and fails as before. Only Codex has one. The per-surface call is factored into `check_one`, unchanged in its flags.
- **When the re-check does not pass, it says why.** Drift at both roots keeps the current root's report and adds that the next root drifts too, so neither explains it; a refusal at the next root keeps its own exit code and is named, never read as drift. A refusal at the current root is never re-checked.
- **`scripts/surface_mirror_check_fixture.sh`, lane `surface-mirror-guard`**: a stub `nen` answers each check by surface and `--hooks-root`, offline, and the six cases above run on every change (clean, next root accepted, drift at both, current refused, next refused, Cursor never re-checked). It fails against `main`'s script and against this branch's first commit. Also verified with the real generator (nen 0.16.0): this tree at the current root, exit 0; #151's head `37919f42`, exit 0 with the acceptance line; `surfaces/codex/hooks.json` hand-edited to a third root, exit 1.
- **Step two is #151**: it makes the next root current everywhere the root is written (the script, `surface-mirror-regenerate.yml`, `docs/SURFACES.md` § 3, `docs/surfaces/README.md`) and deletes `hooks_root_next_for` with its fixture cases. `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` records the rule for a hooks root, and `docs/SURFACES.md`'s root comment names the transition.
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten on `a73f77d7`: Nobunaga 2 findings plus 7 notes, Feitan 1, Phinks 2 with a 27-case adversarial suite). Fixed: no committed test drove the new pass path (BC-9 high, QA-18 medium; now the fixture and lane above); a refusal at the next root was reported as drift (low, both); the final line now names a surface accepted at its next root; the header, `docs/SURFACES.md`'s root comment and the step-two list in `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` say what step two must change; the wording outside the acceptance line never repeats it, so a check for acceptance matches that line only. Deferred to step two, which introduces the precedence: under the legacy `.codex/` placement Codex sets no `PLUGIN_ROOT`, so a stray one in the user's environment would outrank `HATSU_PLUGIN_ROOT` (CWE-426, low, Feitan); #151 settles it for that placement. Pre-existing and kept: `scripts/prose_size_check.sh` reads `hanten` over its diet on `main` (#151 fixes it).
- Plugin bumped to 0.65.0 (`scripts/surface_mirror_check.sh` is a shipped runtime script); mirrors regenerated at that stamp, at the current roots.

## v0.64.0 — the send is gated on freshness, a stale archive is rebuilt in a clean worktree, and the target may come from configuration

Closes [#146](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/146) — the maintainer's rulings of 2026-09-29 from the <product-repo-A> sitting where `hatsu:kagutsuchi testflight` would have sent a `.ipa` built nine days earlier from a commit **95 commits behind `origin/main`**, on a dirty tree, with every plan row `ok`: canon protected the *tag* (cut after the send) and not the *send*. **No new skill**: mid-run the maintainer ruled *"I'd like to make it so that kagutsuchi by defaults runs susanoo for a fresh build ONLY when latest build is not up to date with target branch (usually main). Let's reserve Kamui for another thing later on"* — so the surface stays forty-five and `kamui` is reserved.

- **`kagutsuchi` § 3a — the freshness gate, before every `--run`** (item A). New `scripts/send_freshness_check.sh --repo <path> [--base <branch>]`, run after the plan: it reports at exit `2`, in order, a dirty working tree (captured and tested — a failing `git status` never reads clean), a `tags.identity.nameFrom` that is absent, symlinked, out-of-tree or carrying no raw build SHA on line 2, a build SHA that is not the checkout's `HEAD`, and — after `git fetch origin <base>` — one that is not the tip of `origin/<branch.base>`, both SHAs named with the ahead and behind counts; exit `3` is a repository declaring no `tags.identity` (tree proven clean, the send proceeds, the report reads `freshness: unverified`, never `fresh`); exit `0` prints the four lines the report quotes. Hermetic self-test on a bare-origin fixture, 33 assertions, as the focused lane `freshness-guard`. New `nen/decisions.json` row `stale-send` (autonomous — a rebuild, never a question, never a send as it stands).
- **`kagutsuchi` § 3b — the fresh build, ONLY when owed** (item B, folded into kagutsuchi by the ruling above). When § 3a's gate reports a dirty tree, no archive, or an archive not built from `HEAD` at the trunk's tip, kagutsuchi runs `susanoo` first — in **one** detached worktree at `<core>/.nen/worktrees/kagutsuchi` at `origin/<branch.base>` through `scripts/kagutsuchi_worktree.sh` (steps 1 and 2 of the proposed flow collapsed and idempotent: absent → created, present and clean at the tip → reused, present and clean elsewhere → moved, dirty → refused listing every path and **never discarded**, a stray directory, a sibling registration or a symlinked parent → refused, a vanished registration → dropped and re-created), copying every `nen/contract.json → project.fromCore[].path` **from the core checkout only** — each proven untracked, gitignored, present and a regular file in core **and at the trunk's tip**, landed through a temp file and a rename so a trunk commit can never decide where a credential goes; nothing synthesised; each copied path listed, a `core-ignore:` line saying whether core ignores the worktree — then runs the gate again in the worktree and sends from there, never from the stale checkout. A `fresh` gate skips all of it. Self-test, 58 assertions, as the focused lane `fresh-worktree-guard`. Verified live against zheref/<product-repo-A> up to the worktree cut and reuse ([`docs/ab/kagutsuchi.md`](docs/ab/kagutsuchi.md) § *Dated 2026-09-30*); <product-repo-A> owes a `project.fromCore` row for `<product-repo-C>/Config.xcconfig` at its own G2 before its archive precondition reads `ok` in the worktree.
- **`project.fromCore`** — the copy list, declared in `nen/contract.json` beside `project.preconditions` as `[{ "path", "why" }]`, documented in `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 3; nen's loader preserves it and still asserts the precondition in the worktree.
- **`deploy.defaultTarget`** (item C) — `nen/workflow.json` → `deploy.defaultTarget` supplies `kagutsuchi`'s target when the invocation omits it, one row per state: `null` or absent asks as free text as before; a declared key whose `why` reads non-production is the target; one whose `why` does not is refused, never asked; a name that is no declared key is refused naming the declaration PR; a typed target always overrides; the report states the target **and its source** (`typed` or `workflow.json → deploy.defaultTarget`). `nen shu deploy` is unchanged — the skill always passes `--target` explicitly. Hatsu's own value is `null`. Documented in `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 2 → `deploy`; nen neither validates nor reads the key, and the per-stack default in `nen shu detect`'s reference pack is nen's to add (`docs/PROCESS.md` § *Residue and owned dependencies*).
- **Canon and surfaces** (item D): `kagutsuchi` rewritten — §§ 0, 1, 2, 3a, 3b, 5, Residue and hard limits — and kept on the diet at 12 287 bytes; `susanoo` § 1 names kagutsuchi § 3b as a caller and § Residue 2 is re-pointed at the gate; `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` § 7a (kagutsuchi's row: S2, the target's source, susanoo's `## 0.` held under § 3b); `docs/PROCESS.md` residue and two owned dependencies for nen (a worktree verb, the reference-pack default); `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § 4's kagutsuchi row and per-target paragraph read the new rule; `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-09-29* records rulings 4–7 verbatim; `kurapika.md` and `/kurapika` name the gate and the rebuild. Two focused lanes in `nen/contract.json`; plugin bumped to 0.64.0 (0.62.0 before #150 took 0.63.0 on the trunk); every surface regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.63.0 — no private repository is named in the tree

`docs/PUBLIC-REDACTION.md` rules that no private repository is named anywhere in this repository's content, and six were: the live `gh repo list zheref --visibility private`, matched against the tree by **bare name, case-insensitive, whole-word**, found fifty-five lines across twenty-three files naming the Xcode consumer, the Compose consumer, the Apple TV app, the résumé site and the CI plane, plus one workflow comment naming the predecessor. The first pass matched `owner/name` slugs and missed the bare names; hanten's review caught it, and the sweep above is the corrected one.

- **Placeheld by the legend's letters, one per repository, read off the tree before a new one is given.** The SwiftUI/TCA consumer is `<product-repo-A>` (`swiftui-tca-uzf-v2` in `docs/ab/bankai-handbooks.md`), the Compose consumer `<product-repo-B>` (`compose-uzf-v2`), the Apple TV app `<product-repo-D>` — `docs/ab/drive.md`'s `<product-repo-D>#22` was open on 2026-09-02, which only that repository's #22 was — and the résumé site takes the new **`<product-repo-E>`** (its #22 was created 2026-09-18). The legend and the object-id rule now run A … E and say a letter is never reassigned. Object ids keep their numbers — `RA-PR-#577`, `RA-PR-#576`, `RE-PR-#22` — and the one link into a private repository is unlinked. A placeholder outside code is written in backticks so markdown does not read `<…>` as a tag.
- **The CI plane is "Akatsuki"**, which the policy already exempts as the system's name; the hyphenated form that stood in three places spelled its private repository (`shalnark`, `black-voice`, ROSTER). The workflow comment in `.github/workflows/surface-mirror-regenerate.yml` says *the predecessor's* `sync-canon`; `scripts/workflow_runner_policy_check.rb` self-test and `--root` are green after it.
- **What tied the product to a placeheld repository is placeheld too**, as the legend's family-name rule asks: the bundle id and the two product-named simulators in transcripts, and a test-target name in `docs/WORKFLOW.md`. **Personal device identifiers leave the transcripts** (SEC-8): two Android hardware serials, three physical-device UDIDs and a third party's name in a device name become numbered placeholders, so each transcript still tells its devices apart; the U+2019 device names the byte-matching evidence depends on stay, as does the maintainer's own name. The legend gains the row.
- **One thing kept, and the policy now says why** (§ *What is deliberately not redacted*): the predecessor generator's marker where `hatsu:limbo`, its mirrors and `docs/ab/limbo.md` quote it — limbo matches it byte for byte in consumer files, so a placeholder would match nothing. The bullet says plainly that the marker makes `<reference-repo>` identifiable, so nothing relies on that placeholder for secrecy.
- **Deferred, tracked**: GitHub issue bodies are in the policy's scope and still name private repositories, and nothing guards the tree or a filing against a relapse — [#149](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/149), numbers only. Git history, past commit messages and PR bodies are out of scope by § *Scope*.
- Plugin bumped to 0.63.0. 0.62.0 is claimed by the in-flight `opus/kurapika/amaterasu-worktree-launch`: if that lands first, this passes as is; if this lands first, that branch re-bumps above 0.63.0 and retitles its section. Whichever lands second regenerates its mirrors. Mirrors regenerated at this stamp.

## v0.61.0 — native artifacts on Antigravity, and the release unit for `v0.58.0..v0.61.0`

Release unit for `v0.58.0..v0.61.0`: [#134](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/134) (the Nen repin), [#138](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/138) (the futon run record), [#142](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/142) (`hatsu:limbo`), [#143](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/143) (this section's delivery) and [#144](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/144) (the release proposal). The v0.59.0 and v0.60.0 sections below describe #134 and #142; this line is where the range's PRs are cited, which is what `CON-33(c)` reconciles against.

- **Spiritual Message publishes a native artifact on Antigravity** (#143). `docs/PROCESS.md` and `docs/surfaces/antigravity.md` define the report as native Markdown written through `write_to_file` with `ArtifactMetadata` into the session's artifact store, so a turn or landing report lands in the artifact viewer with Generative UI and GFM callouts instead of an unviewable file or a raw chat recap. The skill resolves its template through `$hatsu_root` (`ten` § 0) rather than a relative path, so a consumer repository resolves it whatever directory the run starts in. `scripts/hanten_cycle_ledger.sh` gains Python 3.9 compatibility (`from __future__ import annotations`), 38 self-tests green. Plugin bumped to 0.61.0 with all three mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

> release's own version existed only in the manifest until now. That is the gap this section closes;
> the delivery-PR-carries-the-bump convention still holds.

## v0.60.0 — `hatsu:limbo` keeps a consumer's canon mirror current, and the Nen minimum moves to 0.16

- **`hatsu:limbo` — the canon, projected into every surface a consumer declares** (the maintainer's request of 2026-09-29: *"prepare nen to sync handbooks as proper rules for each supported surface on consumer repos … add a new skill to handle this automatically (ensuring to keep given consumer repo up to date)"*). `hatsu:limbo [for <path>] [to <tag>] [as check|sync]` reads the consumer's recorded canon pin (`nen canon pin`), checks the public `zheref/bankai-handbooks` out at that tag, derives the stack's `rules/` directory (`nen canon resolve --leaf rules`), classifies every declared surface's committed mirror (`nen canon mirror check`: `ok / missing / extra / stale / hand-edited / foreign`, with the `--markdown-out` drift table), and — on the maintainer's answer, or `as sync` — regenerates it (`nen canon mirror generate`) into **exactly the surfaces `.claude/canon-values.yml` declares**, then cuts the branch through `breath`, commits through `kokusen`, publishes (`nen wc publish`), opens one PR (`nen pr open`) at the gate `nen repo classify` names — `G2` for a consumer — and hands it to `en`. Four decisions, each with its reason in the skill: (1) **it opens the PR**, because a sync that stops at a dirty tree keeps nothing current, and the gate is honoured because limbo never merges — the merge at the PR's terminus is `en` § 5's (`own-pr-merge`), not limbo's; (2) **a pin behind the canon's latest tag is reported with the exact `to <tag>` line and never moved on limbo's own authority** — which canon governs a repository decides which rules its reviewers cite — and `to <tag>` moves the pin and regenerates in **one commit**, since a repin without a regenerate reads `stale` on every surface (verified); (3) **`hand-edited` is a G5 before any write** — new `nen/decisions.json` row `hand-edited-canon-mirror`: file the edit upstream, move it to the consumer's own file, or `git rm` a predecessor generator's mirror (the `sync-canon` marker every existing consumer carries today reads as unmarked, and `generate` refuses the whole run over it, exit `2`, nothing written — verified); (4) **the surface set is the consumer's `surfaces:` declaration, never all four** — nen refuses to default it and so does limbo, deriving a recommendation from disk and asking once when the key is absent. Every claim is verified live at nen `0.16.0` against a fixture consumer ([`docs/ab/limbo.md`](docs/ab/limbo.md)). Six residues are named, two of them nen requests (a pin-writing verb; `--markdown-out` creating its parent directory). `bankai-handbooks` § *CON-13* now points at limbo as the skill that renders and checks the mirror. Registered in [`claude/skills/README.md`](claude/skills/README.md), the README, `docs/SURFACES.md`, `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` § 7b and both manifests; on the prose diet (`scripts/prose_size_check.sh`, 12 288 bytes). The live skill surface is **forty-five**, forty-six directories per surface with `ten`.
- **`nen/contract.json` → `dependency.minimum` moves `0.15` → `0.16`.** The rule the contract states is that a minimum names the oldest release carrying every verb Hatsu *executes*; limbo is the first skill that executes `nen canon pin` and `nen canon mirror generate|check --surfaces`, which exist in no tag before `v0.16.0` — a `0.15` binary refuses the first as an unknown subcommand and the second's flag as unknown — so the minimum now says so. `pinned_ref` was already `v0.16.0` (v0.59.0 moved it first, on purpose, so limbo could be built against a released binary); `minimum_semantics`, `zero_major_caveat.rule` and `pinned_ref_semantics` record the adoption, and the convenience copies in the README, `docs/WORKFLOW.md` and both manifests follow the contract. `nen shu tools` on this checkout at `0.16.0`: `ok nen 0.16.0 pinned >=0.16.0 <0.17.0`, `compat floor: 0.7`.
- **`docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` § 7 reconciled**: `tenkai` has carried a `## 0. Standalone entry` since it was authored and stood in neither table; it and `limbo` are listed in § 7b, and the counts read what the files say.
- Plugin bumped to 0.60.0; mirrors regenerated at that stamp — the regeneration also re-renders `surfaces/antigravity/rules/hatsu.md`, the one file `scripts/surface_mirror_check.sh` read as `hand-edited` on clean `main` at 0.59.0.

## v0.59.0 — the Nen pin moves to v0.16.0, and the minimum deliberately does not

- **`nen/contract.json` pins `v0.16.0`** (zheref/nen#272/#274/#278, released by #281). That release renders the canon mirror into **every surface a consumer declares** — `AGENTS.md` as one managed block, `.cursor/rules/*.mdc`, `.agents/rules/*.md`, `.claude/rules/*.md` — with the generated-from marker as the ownership claim, so an unmarked destination refuses the whole run before the first byte lands on any surface. It also adds `nen canon pin` and the `nen/repos.json` `maintained_tools[].pinned` field it reads.
- **The feature minimum stays `0.15`, on purpose.** A minimum names the oldest release carrying every verb Hatsu *executes*, and no skill on `main` executes `canon pin` or `canon mirror --surfaces` yet — `hatsu:limbo` is the skill that will, and the change that lands it raises the minimum to `0.16` with it. Raising it now would refuse a `0.15` binary that runs everything Hatsu actually does. The pin moves ahead of the minimum so limbo can be built against a **released** binary rather than an unpublished tag: `bootstrap/nen.sh` resolves the binary and `SHA256SUMS` from the GitHub *release* at `--ref`, and a pin whose release is unpublished goes red on every pull request, not only the one that moved it.

## v0.58.0 — the round ceiling is two declared numbers, a check-exclusion ruling has a home, the host re-pins after its own cut, a consumer's canon mirror is read before the commit

Release unit for `v0.55.0..v0.58.0`: [#127](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/127) (this section, plugin 0.58.0 — the three rulings of 2026-09-29 ride here), [#126](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/126) (the v0.56.0 section, effort 5), [#129](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/129) (the v0.57.0 section: guided review setup and audited ledger recovery), [#132](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/132) (`docs(loop)`: the futon hatsu@bug run record — no section of its own) and the reconciling [#137](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/137). `v0.56.0` and `v0.57.0` were never tagged: their sections below ship inside `v0.58.0`, the first tag after `v0.55.0`.

Delivered by the `hatsu:futon hatsu@enhancement then getsuga+mugetsu` run of 2026-09-29, effort 6, stacked on v0.56.0's branch (zheref/hatsu#126; run record `docs/Loop/hatsu-futon-enhancement-2026-09-29/`): [#102](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/102), [#104](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/104), [#90](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/90) and [#111](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/111).

- **The reviewer-round ceiling is two numbers in one declared home, and the request is the verb** (#102). `nen/gates.json` → `round_policy.minRounds` (N) and `.maxRounds` (M) — Hatsu's own keys since v0.41.0, preserved by nen as raw data and **not validated at 0.15.1** (a string `minRounds` reads `ok`; owned by no issue yet); counting them in `--explain` is zheref/nen#240 — are what `sharingan` § 6 and `en` § 2 read: a round is requested only while requested < M **and** resolved < N, only through `nen pr request-reviews` (never a raw GraphQL `requestReviews`, never `gh api` — a hard limit in both skills and in PROCESS § *Reviewer rounds*), and **never again once N resolved rounds exist**: *owed at head* after the run's own push or request is not a reason. M reached with fewer than N resolved is the reported blocker, `nen pr ready --explain` quoted, never a ceiling raised in-session. `en` § 6's observation table says so at the row that used to re-request; `docs/ab/en.md` cites the repro (RA-PR-#577: eight requests under a two-round ruling); `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` § 3 gains `round_policy` with post-#214 `bounded` semantics for a consumer, naming the stale *every push re-opens…* comment as pre-#214.
- **A maintainer's check-exclusion ruling has a declared home, and readiness reads it** (#104). `nen/gates.json` → `check_exclusions[]` (`name`, `reason`, `ruled`, `until`; empty here, an empty array being a decision). `pr-state` § 2 passes every live row as `nen pr ready --exclude-check <a,b>` beside the identity flag; `sharingan` § 4 and `en` § 6 carry it; a ruling with no row is `missing-configuration` (WORKFLOW § 4 *Ask, set up, continue* names it: written into `nen/gates.json` at that repository's gate, never carried in prose or a brief); a name the flag cannot carry (zheref/nen#243) keeps the PR not-ready — the verb's verdict quoted verbatim, the excluded check named beside it — never `ready`. `scripts/tenkai_adopt.sh diagnose` gains `gates/check-exclusions` (observation only, never rewritten): none or every row live is `satisfied`; a row lapsed, a condition row unconfirmed past 90 days from `ruled`, a near-miss date in `until`, a `ruled` in the future, a missing field, or a name the flag cannot be handed (a comma, a quote, a metacharacter, a control byte — SEC-7) is `drift`, named, and **a refused row is never put on the call**; the day is the declared `reports.timeZone`'s, else the host's, said; a non-object or non-UTF-8 gates file routes to `nen schema check` instead of a traceback (twenty-six self-test assertions). `docs/GATE-CONFIGURATION.md` § 3 documents the key; validating it in `nen schema check` is zheref/nen#249.
- **A sitting that publishes `nen` itself re-pins the host after the assets attach** (#90). The executable step is `mugetsu` § 7's — the phase still running when the assets its § 5 attached exist: when the target's `origin` resolves to the plugin contract's `dependency.source`, ten § 2b's verb re-binds the session's `PATH` to **the tag this run published, read back from the release** (never a tag listing; a mismatch halts) before any further `nen` invocation; assets not attached or the ref refused, it stays on the current binary and says so; the report line names the version now on `PATH` (`host nen: <version> on PATH (re-pinned from <old>)`) and its absence is the signal the step was skipped; the script at the new tag is fetched outside the contract's pin, the one call site where it is, and the pin follows in the repin PR; `~/.local/bin/nen` stays the maintainer's word. `getsuga` § 7 hands forward in one line (mugetsu never runs from inside it), `ten` § 2 points at mugetsu.
- **A consumer's canon mirror is read in the local verification gate, before the commit** (#111, the Hatsu half). WORKFLOW § *The local verification gate* step 2 (the gate is five numbered steps now): where the delta touches `.claude/canon-values.yml`, the argv `nen/workflow.json` → `iteration.canonMirror.command` declares (Hatsu's own key, preserved by `nen schema check`; README or `$comment` only while zheref/nen#262 is open and the key is absent) runs locally (never the weekly cron, never a dispatch that carries the branch into a PR against trunk), a non-empty `git status --porcelain .claude/rules` after it is a red gate (row `red-lint`: the regenerated files ride the commit or nothing does), and a `grep` for the superseded value across `.claude/rules/` is the correctness read drift cannot give — a hit is a finding. `kokusen` § 3 counts the step; `tenkai` § 6c has adoption read the key, a consumer with bindings and no key being a routed gap. The deterministic half is zheref/nen#262, an owned dependency.
- **The reviewer fallback chain — the ruling of 2026-09-29** (a ruling, no issue; the maintainer's words are in `docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-09-29*). At this run's G5 stop — Copilot's round owed on HA-PR-#126 and #127 while `nen pr request-reviews` registered nothing, the credits exhausted — the maintainer ruled that the review falls back to Cursor, and past that limit to the local hanten rounds, and the PR merges. `nen/gates.json` → `reviewer_fallback` declares `chain` (`copilot`, `cursor`), `terminal` (`hanten`, never requested) and the dated exhaustion; PROCESS § *Reviewer rounds and review threads* states the whole rule once (*The fallback chain*: a step `reviewers[]` lacks is passed over, a fallback request counts toward `maxRounds`, the terminus outranks `cap-reached`), and `sharingan` § 6, `en` § 5/§ 6 (the sixth stop, the bell at a terminus), `pr-state` § 2, `nen/decisions.json` row `reviewer-exhausted`, ROSTER and GATE-CONFIGURATION cite it. A PR presented this way carries **the verb's verdict quoted verbatim** (`not-ready: … (CON-32b): copilot (no round at head)`) **and one Hatsu line beside it** (`Hatsu: reviewer copilot declared exhausted (…); nen does not read this — zheref/nen#275`), never a string the verb did not print, never `ready`; the merge stays the maintainer's. `scripts/tenkai_adopt.sh diagnose` gains `gates/reviewer-fallback` (a lapsed or malformed row, a chain step without an identity, a terminal that is not `hanten`). GATE-CONFIGURATION states the Hatsu-own-key convention once. nen reads none of these keys: zheref/nen#275 is the owned dependency.
- **No merge prompt off the trunk — the ruling of 2026-09-29 (2)** (`docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-09-29*): a merge prompt exists only for a PR whose base is `branch.base`; a stacked PR is retargeted through `nen pr retarget` and its base read back the moment its delivery merges, before `en` § 5's bell exists — getsuga § 3 had said it for release proposals only; `futon` § 5's *prompted* means prompted on `branch.base`.
- **The merge is the run's — the ruling of 2026-09-29 (3)** (`docs/ROSTER.md` § *Rulings of 2026-09-29*: *"The merge is not mine. It is yours and it has been. And if it is not clear spec-wise, let's make it so during this very session."*). `en` § 5 states it once: the PR the run authored is merged by en at its terminus — `CON-32` Ready, or the declared reviewer-exhaustion stop — on `branch.base`, through five gates in order (whose PR, the base read back, the verdict, the body, the head pinned) and `gh pr merge --merge --match-head-commit` in the verb's own argv shape, the state read back (`nen pr merge` merges a release unit only; the delivery form is zheref/nen#286). The gate tables in ROSTER, README and WORKFLOW, `nen/decisions.json` (`merge`, `canon-merge`, new row `own-pr-merge`), `futon` § 5/§ 11 (*done* is merged), `sharingan` § 6, `kurapika.md`, `jutaisho` § 4 and the seven skills' shared gate note now say so; ruling 1's *and merge* always meant this. Another's PR and a `gh` refusal stay the maintainer's.
- Plugin bumped to 0.58.0; mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.57.0 — guided review setup and audited ledger recovery

- Tenkai diagnoses missing review scopes and the active effort's Hanten ledger. Hanten guides a missing ledger through review-history inspection, records a maintainer-confirmed first-cycle recovery, and preserves prior reviewer counts when restoring history. A genuinely lost cycle remains a safety stop.

## v0.56.0 — the effort keeps its own ledgers, the generated surfaces and every routing table get a reviewer, and headroom is a number

Delivered by the `hatsu:futon hatsu@enhancement then getsuga+mugetsu` run of 2026-09-29, effort 5, stacked on v0.53.0's branch (zheref/hatsu#124; run record `docs/Loop/hatsu-futon-enhancement-2026-09-29/`): [#100](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/100), [#73](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/73), [#113](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/113) and [#119](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/119).

- **An effort driven by `build` leaves a phase ledger with the phases build itself ran, and a usage ledger written as the spend happens** (#100). `build` § 4 wraps `release`, `build`, `hanten`, `remediate` and `open` in `nen phase begin|end`, runs every `nen shu` step with `--effort <branch>` so it lands under the open entry, and records usage at every § 7 report from the surface's own readout — never transcribed at the end of the sitting. `hanten` § 4 records each reviewer's spend as it returns (`nen usage record … --source "hanten § 4: <persona>, <readout>"`, the Agent tool's result totals; `--not-reported` where a surface exposes none), so five reviewers in the cycle ledger are five usage entries. Neither template changed; no new verb.
- **The generated surfaces and every routing table have a named reviewer, or a named gap** (#73). `nen/workflow.json` declares a `surfaces` scope (`surfaces/**`, Phinks, budget 1): the two guards — `scripts/surface_link_check.sh` and `scripts/surface_mirror_check.sh` — run first and are handed to him as the security rows are handed to Feitan. What a path cannot raise, `hanten` § 2 raises by content: a diff adding or editing a `## 0.` section, or any table that routes control flow, raises a **totality pass** on the architecture scope — every reachable input state matches exactly one row and the fall-through is named — Chrollo's (`claude/agents/chrollo.md` names it a `high`), a § 3 gap when he is spent, never silence. `docs/STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` § 7 points at the rule rather than restating it; one persona, one budget, so release and surfaces raised together are one Phinks raise carrying both.
- **A live worktree on the same topic is surfaced before a new effort cuts** (#113). `breath` § 2d reads `nen wc worktrees --repo <path> --json` and treats a row whose `branch` or `lastSubject` shares a word of the rendered descriptor as an overlap: reported with its path, branch, `ahead`, `dirty` and subject, and **asked** — continue there (`amenotejikara`) or cut anyway by name — never resolved or ignored silently, never merged, closed or reassigned. Name-level only; diff-level overlap is a separate Nen ask.
- **Headroom against a prose ceiling is a number the guard prints** (#119, option b). `scripts/prose_size_check.sh --headroom` lists every measured file with its size, its ceiling and the bytes (or characters) left, smallest first; an unknown flag is exit 2; the ceilings are unchanged. The guard gains its focused lane, `prose-guard` (`scripts/prose_size_check_fixture.sh`, hermetic: the clean tree, the report and its order, the ceiling edge, over by one, characters, a missing diet entry, the two refusals), the one-lane-per-executable-guard pattern. `hanten` § 6 has a fix that edits a dieted file quote that file's line. Measured on this very effort, from the report itself: the first draft of `hanten` read 1,315 bytes over its ceiling and `chrollo.md` 2 over, and the trims that followed were read off the report rather than rediscovered — the head readings are the ones the guard prints (`sh scripts/prose_size_check.sh --headroom`).
- **What the pre-PR review changed** (hanten on this effort at cb3ab3c5: Feitan 4, Chrollo 15, Nobunaga 12, Phinks 6 — 37 findings, every one settled; two shared shapes). The two surface guards in hanten § 2 take the checkout under review as their root (both default to the tree they ship in); the phase and usage ledger mechanism has **one owner**, `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § *The effort's ledgers* (argv, flags, files, the branch key, `--not-reported`), which `ren`, `build`, `hanten` and `spiritual-message` now cite; a content-raised persona joins the ledger's `--applicable`, the totality trigger covers a removal and the fall-through statement beneath a table, and a spent Chrollo is § 2b's `skipped-exhausted` while an absent one is § 3's gap; `scripts/hanten_cycle_ledger.sh` keeps one budget per persona as the **smallest** declared, naming every scope, never last-writer-wins; breath's overlap read moved to § 3a after the descriptor is rendered, matches on kebab segments of four letters or more (never `fix`, `feat`, the model or the persona), reports a `null` `lastSubject` as unread, and offers the worktree's path for the maintainer's own session (amenotejikara is the build swap, not a continuation); `chrollo.md` keeps only the pass, `phinks.md` carries the two surface rows in Feitan's shape and names his third route; `docs/WORKFLOW.md` counts seven scopes in both places, the `hosts` comment names the eleventh fixture. The guard: a symlink that leaves the root, a dangling one, an unreadable file, an **unlistable directory** and a rules file that is not valid UTF-8 are each an offender by name (SEC-7; QA-16/QA-18 — at the candidate an unlistable `claude/rules` read green and an unreadable file crashed on arithmetic), an empty or second root and a single-dash flag are refused, `--` ends the flags, the report header uses the rows' widths, and the fixture proves each with the byte helper built on awk. Phinks's red test for the unlistable directory (`ichigo/prose-guard-unlistable-directory-reads-green`, f35755ef) is folded into the fixture.
- **Cursor Bugbot's round at `3c776daf`** (1 medium): the worktree-overlap read had taken § 3a and moved the ledger open to § 3b, and two pointers — § 0's *after the restore, § 3b's `init` runs once* and the hard limit on resetting a ledger — still said § 3a, so a cold start could skip opening `.nen/hanten/<branch>.cycle.json`; both fixed.
- Plugin bumped to 0.56.0; mirrors regenerated at that stamp.

## v0.55.0 — canon lives in `zheref/bankai-handbooks`

Release unit for `v0.49.0..v0.55.0`: [#125](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/125) (this section, plugin 0.55.0), [#120](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/120) (`docs(tenkai)`: the lane-less adoption path, and three shapes that read as drift — plugin 0.54.0, no section of its own), [#128](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/128) (the `v0.49.0..v0.53.0` reconciliation, whose line under v0.53.0 lists [#121](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/121), [#123](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/123) and [#124](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/124)) and the reconciling [#130](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/130). `v0.50.0` through `v0.54.0` were never tagged: every section below ships inside `v0.55.0`, the first tag after `v0.49.0`.

- **Canon is read from `zheref/bankai-handbooks`, not from the frozen reference implementation.** The Bankai handbooks, `CONSTITUTION.md` and the shared agent conventions moved on 2026-09-28 into the **public** [`zheref/bankai-handbooks`](https://github.com/zheref/bankai-handbooks) (MIT; handbook set `v0.6`, tagged `v0.6.0`), and `CON-13` as rewritten there names it the single canonical source: a consumer keeps a **generated, pinned mirror** rendered from a **tag** of that repository by Hatsu/Nen, per agent surface — the mirror concept was re-pointed, not removed. `hatsu:bankai-handbooks` now resolves against a local checkout of that tag: `--repo` names **the registry** that records the target's `scenario` (`nen canon resolve` derives the stack path from the string and opens no handbook file — `src/canon/resolve.ts`), and the resolved paths are opened in the `bankai-handbooks` checkout. Verified live at nen `0.15.1`: the public registry records **no consumers** (private products are not named there), so `nen repo scenario --repo <bankai-handbooks checkout> --target zheref/hatsu` refuses with *"only a consumers[] entry carries a 'scenario' field"* (exit `1`), while a target whose own `nen/repos.json` records it under `consumers[]` with a `scenario` resolves (exit `0`). The skill tries the canon registry first, then the target's own, and treats a target neither records as `missing-configuration` — **which registry is canonical for a private consumer is an OPEN question for the maintainer.** `--repo` omitted on `canon resolve` is exit `2` at this pin (the port's "silently falls back to the cwd" residue is retired). `hatsu:bankai-quality` reads `quality-baseline.md` from the same checkout; the machinery scenario is **`bankai-machinery`** (renamed at v0.6 from the frozen repository's own name — the **`BC-` prefix is unchanged**, so no `BC-{n}` citation moves). A handbook gap is a `bankai:handbook-question` landing as a canon-lane PR the human merges at `G4` in `zheref/bankai-handbooks`, never authored by a resolver, never against the frozen predecessor.
- **Registered and pinned.** `nen/repos.json` gains `zheref/bankai-handbooks` under `maintained_tools` (`nen repo classify --target zheref/bankai-handbooks` → `role canon · gate G4`) with `pinned: "v0.6.0"` — the tag the resolvers read; nen carries the key on a `maintained_tools` entry as prose and does not model it — and the product code `BH`, the code that repository gives itself. `nen schema check --repo .`: every row `ok`.
- **The public-redaction policy and the content agree again.** `docs/PUBLIC-REDACTION.md` said no private repository is named anywhere, while the frozen reference implementation was named in full in 80 lines across 52 files. Every naming that meant *where canon lives* now names the public `zheref/bankai-handbooks` directly (no placeholder — it is public); every naming that meant *the frozen predecessor* — its rulings, its backlog transcripts, its `attention_signal.sh` port in `hooks/stop-bell.sh`, its `sync-canon` in `docs/SURFACES.md`, its issue link in the discovery ledger (now `RR-IS-#895`, unlinked) — is `<reference-repo>`; and the live G4 canon lists in `susanoo`, `tenkai`, `tsukuyomi`, `byakugan`, `rasengan`, `kotoamatsukami`, `backlog-state`, `tensho` and `STANDALONE-ENTRY.md` now read `nen repo classify`'s `role`, as ROSTER ruling 6 (2026-09-27) requires. The legend lists `zheref/bankai-handbooks` as a named, public canon repository and `bankai-machinery` as a kept stack id; the Copilot round on #125 then found the CI plane's and the scaffolder's repository names in the same rulings, persona prose and transcripts — now `<ci-plane-repo>` (a new legend row) and `<scaffold-repo>`; `contracts/reference.gates.json` is about the frozen repository and is unchanged; dated transcripts under `docs/ab/` and `docs/history/` keep their content with the name placeheld. `docs/AGENT-ATTRIBUTION.md` cites `CON-51(c)`, the clause that now makes Hatsu's trailer rule canon. Mirrors regenerated for codex, cursor and antigravity at stamp 0.55.0.

## v0.53.0 — the injected trailer is caught at the written commit; inside a newer checkout the tree is named, never trusted by itself

Delivered by the `hatsu:futon hatsu@bug then getsuga+mugetsu` run of 2026-09-28, effort 4 of 4, stacked on v0.52.0's branch (zheref/hatsu#123; run record `docs/Loop/hatsu-futon-bug-2026-09-28/`): [#66](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/66) and [#67](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/67).

Release unit for `v0.49.0..v0.53.0`: [#121](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/121) (efforts 1 and 2: v0.50.0 and v0.51.0), [#123](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/123) (effort 3, v0.52.0 — carrying [#124](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/124), effort 4, v0.53.0, which merged into its branch) and the reconciling [#128](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/pull/128). `v0.50.0`, `v0.51.0` and `v0.52.0` were never tagged: their sections below ship inside `v0.53.0`, the first tag after `v0.49.0`.

- **A harness-injected trailer does not remain the commit a run publishes** (#66). Cursor appends `Co-authored-by: Cursor <cursoragent@cursor.com>` at `git commit` time — observed on the raw `git commit --file` path (HA-PR-#64); whether it reaches the `git commit` a nen verb spawns is not verified here, and the guard catches both. `kokusen` § 5 reads the tip back after every `commit write` and `aka` § 7 reads every outgoing commit (`<the SHA ls-remote printed | origin/<base>>..HEAD`, § 4's own refs) immediately before `nen wc publish` — after ao's catch-up and the re-lint, so a squash parent under ao's merge is inspected too (`git -C <path> log -1 --format='%(trailers:only,unfold)'`, named residue, on the repository the verb just wrote). **What is classified is an attribution key, defined once in WORKFLOW § `commits`: a key ending `-by` or `-with`, or listed in `forbiddenTrailers`; `Closes` and its kind are ordinary trailers and never compared.** An attribution key outside `commits.allowedAttributionTrailers`, compared case-insensitively as nen's layer (c) compares (so `Co-authored-by` against the policy's `Co-Authored-By`, and a `Made-with: Cursor` too), is a stop with a default — the new `nen/decisions.json` row `injected-attribution-trailer`: the just-written tip is dropped (`git reset --soft HEAD~1`, unpublished by construction, never an earlier commit) or re-squashed; the off-switch is the new ask row `harness-attribution-switch` (the maintainer toggles their harness; no skill writes that file — `missing-configuration`'s default writes through an owner the harness file has none of); a published commit is never rewritten (row `rewrite-published-history`) and is disclosed by shibari § 3 as its own checklist line, outside the attribution ledger. `docs/WORKFLOW.md` § `commits` names Cursor's own off-switch — Cursor Settings → Git & Pull Requests → *Commit Attribution*, or `attribution.attributeCommitsToAgent: false` in `~/.cursor/cli-config.json` — as forum-sourced (2026-08-21) and not verified live, widens layer (a)'s row to the post-write reads, and says plainly that Claude Code's `includeCoAuthoredBy` does not reach Cursor; `docs/surfaces/cursor.md` § 9 quotes the answer and § 10 records the executor path reported to ignore it. kokusen § 6/§ 7 and PROCESS § Residue name both raw calls; the verb-side read-back is an owned dependency, zheref/nen#273. `docs/ab/kokusen.md` carries the positive transcript on HA-PR-#64's `4ee8ce3`.
- **Inside a newer Hatsu checkout, the tree is named — never trusted by itself** (#67). `scripts/hatsu_root.sh` compares the working tree's manifest, when the git toplevel is a Hatsu checkout, with the resolved candidate's and **keeps the candidate**: a directory's claim about itself never picks the root later steps run scripts from (hanten's security read: a tree naming itself `hatsu` at 999.0.0 must not become the plugin root by being `cd`'d into). stderr says which slot resolved the pin, that this session's skill bodies are the pin's, where to read the tree's protocol, and the single-quoted `export HATSU_PLUGIN_ROOT='<checkout>'` that binds the tree from the next session; with no candidate at all the answer stays NOT INSTALLED with the same line; older, equal, one- or two-field, over-long and pre-release versions are said and change nothing; an absent git or a newline-bearing toplevel reads "tree check not run". The manifest version is read by the same stack-machine parser as the name; `semver_newer` orders exactly three digit fields of at most nine digits. `docs/SURFACES.md` § 1 states the rule and its three limits (the line exists from the first installed pin carrying it — `ten` § 0 runs the bound pin's own resolver, so below 0.53.0 the export is the only form; Claude Code's bodies are always the installed pin's; Cursor's `.cursor/skills/` precedence over the cache is unverified), `ten` § 0 points at it, `docs/surfaces/cursor.md` carries the row and the gap. Fixture: the tree-is-named cases in `scripts/hatsu_root_fixture_check.sh` (newer named with the deferral and the quoted export; a handed skill directory's walk line; a stale `$CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT`; an explicit export given as the root and as a skill directory; a 999.0.0 tree with no candidate; older, equal, four unorderable versions with no shell noise; a path with a space and `;`; a newline-bearing toplevel; git absent; a plain repository), every case run from a neutral directory under `/tmp` with git's ambient environment cleared. #67's first criterion — the surface binding the tree's body — stays open on Cursor and is by design not this script's on Claude Code.
- Plugin bumped to 0.53.0; mirrors regenerated at that stamp (the link sweep reads 408 of 2686: two more generator-produced rows from the new authored links in kokusen and ten, zheref/nen#270). Hanten on this effort (candidate a5ac6f1d): Nobunaga 15, Chrollo 15, Feitan 4, Phinks 9 — the override was withdrawn on Feitan's SEC-14 high and the "inert below the pin" high shared by three reviewers; every other finding is folded above.

## v0.52.0 — a release PR reaches en, a stacked proposal is retargeted, the disclosure step names its layers

Delivered by the `hatsu:futon hatsu@bug then getsuga+mugetsu` run of 2026-09-28, effort 3 of 4, stacked on v0.51.0's branch (zheref/hatsu#121; run record `docs/Loop/hatsu-futon-bug-2026-09-28/`): [#98](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/98), [#103](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/103) and [#101](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/101).

- **A release proposal is never called ready on CI alone** (#103). `getsuga` § 3 hands the proposal to `hatsu:en` the moment `nen pr open` returns; en runs its steps 1–5 and **returns the quoted verdict and its judged head to getsuga — no bell, no `MERGE` ask** (en § 5 and § 8 do not run under getsuga; § 3a is that PR's merge, ROSTER ruling 4); § 3a merges the PR number plus the head en's `ready` verdict names, re-recorded at the hand-back, and a fall-back stop quotes `nen pr ready`'s verdict, never "green" or `gh pr checks`. `docs/PROCESS.md` § *Reporting a phase* states the rule once for every skill and brief: every PR a session opens or causes to be opened — a delegated subagent's included — reaches the maintainer through `en` or with the verdict line quoted (with `nen pr body-check`'s where the body is in scope — `pr-state`'s rule and `sharingan` § 4's conjunction, cited); a brief that opens a PR ends at the verdict with zero unresolved threads; *ready*, *G2-ready* and *G4-ready* appear only beside the quoted line; a `not-ready` on an excluded row stays `not-ready`, naming the row. `en` § 6's observation table names the asynchronous reviewer round as its first row — a requested round not yet posted at the current head is pending, not ready, however green the checks, and spends no cycle — and § 1 names getsuga (verdict returned, no bell), futon's chain and the maintainer among en's callers. The incident (zheref/nen#246, zheref/nen#247) is recorded in `docs/history/2026-09-23-readiness-on-ci-alone.md`, with HA-PR-#121's own verdict lines as the corrected practice.
- **A stacked release proposal is retargeted the moment its delivery merges** (#98). GitHub retargets a PR only when its base branch is deleted, so `getsuga` § 3 now runs `nen pr retarget --target <owner/name> --pr <N> --base <branch.base>` (`<owner/name>` from `nen repo resolve --from <checkout>`), reads the base back with `gh pr view … --json baseRefName` (named residue), and presents the proposal at no gate — nor to § 3a's verb, whose `release unit-check` reads `release.unitPaths` at the PR's base — while that read is anything but the trunk; the G4 ask names the retarget as the blocking step. The retrospective check the issue asked for holds live: zheref/nen#232 and zheref/nen#222 both return a `fable/…` base (`docs/ab/getsuga.md`). One new hard-limit bullet carries both rules (#98's retarget clause and #103's verdict clause). PROCESS § *Residue* gains getsuga's base read-back beside shibari's.
- **mukai's disclosure step names its two layers** (#101). The mukai call is the authorization for a `public-mirror` publish step in Hatsu's terms; the harness's permission classifier is a separate layer Hatsu never overrides, and it reads `-y` as a blind apply. § 1 now says `-y` is passed only because the script's own confirmation would block a non-interactive tool, never as a token of authority, and the run says so before the call; the step itself is step 8's, so `shibari` § 1 says it in the same terms; **the portable grant is the consumer's and the maintainer adds it — no skill writes a permission setting**: on Claude Code a **tracked** `.claude/settings.json` allow row (`Bash(ci_scripts/pr_screenshots.sh:*)` would be `<product-repo-A>`'s; it carries no such file today, so the stop there is expected until it does), the other surfaces' equivalents `docs/SURFACES.md` § 1's permissions row, unverified for this step; never Hatsu's pack, which is itself a per-checkout `.claude/settings.local.json` carrying Hatsu's verbs only. Where the consumer has not declared it, the stop is expected and named in shibari's handover line, not a G5. `docs/ab/mukai.md` records it.
- Plugin bumped to 0.52.0; mirrors regenerated at that stamp. Hanten on this effort: Chrollo 10, Nobunaga 11, Phinks 1 (low: en § 1's new `docs/PROCESS.md` link dangles in the antigravity mirror's nested `skills/` — the generator's depth defect, zheref/nen#270, two more rows in the sweep with mukai § 1's `docs/SURFACES.md` link, 406 of 2674; never hand-edited). The en dieting for the callers paragraph and the getsuga § 3a head re-record are named here as prose changes only; no verb or script changed. `docs/SURFACES.md` § 3's regenerate snippet now passes the guard's codex and cursor hooks roots (`${HATSU_PLUGIN_ROOT:-./.codex}`, `./.cursor`); as written it produced a `hooks.json` the mirror check called hand-edited.

## v0.51.0 — the bump guard covers what ships, and a link guard for the mirrors

Delivered by the `hatsu:futon hatsu@bug then getsuga+mugetsu` run of 2026-09-28, effort 2 of 4 — cut as a stacked branch and folded onto v0.50.0's branch (zheref/hatsu#121) when its first publish landed there through zheref/nen#271; run record `docs/Loop/hatsu-futon-bug-2026-09-28/`: [#74](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/74), [#99](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/99) and the Hatsu half of [#72](https://github.com/zheref/hatsu/issues/72).

- **`scripts/plugin_bump_check.sh` guards every surface an installed copy reads** (#74). `PLUGIN_SURFACE_GLOBS` gains `docs/SURFACES.md` and `docs/PUBLIC-REDACTION.md` — both meet the guard's own criterion and were the two documents its header listed as unguarded — and the four runtime scripts the same criterion had missed: `scripts/hatsu_root.sh` and `scripts/surface_mirror_check.sh` (Phinks proved on the #105/#106 delivery that a resolver-only fix with no bump passed the guard and shipped to nobody), `scripts/permissions_pack.sh` and `scripts/dist_tag.sh`. The refusal message was already generated from the array; README's guarded-surface paragraph now lists the same rows and says it is a copy of the one list. Eight fixture cases prove the new rows (unbumped fails naming the path, bumped passes, a fixture script alone does not apply).
- **A bump beside lagging mirror stamps fails by name** (#99). After a real version increase the guard reads the stamp off each `surfaces/<s>` marker in the head tree (the directory the head manifest sits under — CI's PR checkout, so no workflow argument changed) and, when any lags the new version, fails naming the surface, the old stamp, the new stamp and the one owner of the regeneration command (`docs/SURFACES.md` § 3). What this adds is the CAUSE on the required check: `surface-mirror-check` reports the same drift in the same CI round with no cause attached, and mukai's `scripts/surface_mirror_check.sh` is the local catch before a PR opens (Chrollo corrected the "before the push" reason first written here).
